I can’t see how Miss Forrest had anything to do with it. As to Costock, you know about him and I don’t.”
“Yes,” said Sir Clinton, “I know all about Costock.”
But he volunteered no further information and waited for Wendover to proceed.
“That leaves Torrance, then. It’s as plain as print that Torrance might have been the murderer. He was in the Maze at the time. He arranged to part from the girl at the entrance. He’s had plenty of time to learn the Maze while he’s been down here at Whistlefield. He might have been the person Vera Forrest heard running in the Maze just after the murder—quite easily.”

===Chinese===
“这就轮到案发当时确在迷宫里的人，或者我们知道案发后立刻进去过的人了：托伦斯、福雷斯特小姐，还有科斯托克那家伙——你那位干非法钻石买卖（I.D.B.）的朋友。我看不出福雷斯特小姐跟这事能有什么牵扯。至于科斯托克，他的底细你知道，我不知道。”
“是啊，”克林顿爵士说，“科斯托克的底细我全知道。”
可他不再主动多说一个字，只等温多弗往下讲。
“那就剩托伦斯了。托伦斯可能是凶手，这白纸黑字一样明白。案发时他就在迷宫里。是他安排好在入口处跟姑娘分手的。他住在惠斯特菲尔德的这些日子，有的是工夫把迷宫摸熟。案发后维拉·福雷斯特听见迷宫里有人跑动，那很可能就是他——轻而易举。”

===Original===
“He didn’t take an airgun into the Maze with him,” Sir Clinton objected.
Wendover had his answer ready this time.
“No, but he might have had it hidden there beforehand.”
“And no airgun was found afterwards.”
“He may have chucked it on to the top of one of the hedges. Your constables couldn’t have spotted it there without ladders.”
“That’s quite true,” said Sir Clinton. “Well?”

===Chinese===
“他可没带气枪进迷宫。”克林顿爵士提出异议。
这一回温多弗早备好了答话。
“是没有，可他可能事先把枪藏在了里面。”
“事后也没找到气枪。”
“他兴许把它甩到了某道树篱顶上。你的那些警员不架梯子，是发现不了它的。”
“这话不假。”克林顿爵士说，“后来呢？”

===Original===
Wendover seemed to have a flash of illumination. His face lit up.
“Now I see what you meant by your map-analogy! Of course, the snag is that on the face of it young Torrance had no motive. But suppose he was Hackleton’s tool? Suppose he was in the pay of Hackleton to do this job for him? Then it would all fit in. But it’ll be the devil of a business to prove it, if it is true.”
Glancing across at his friend he detected a peculiar expression on Sir Clinton’s face. It was only a fleeting one, for almost immediately the Chief Constable resumed his normal mask.
“Go on,” he said again.
Wendover had to confess that he had reached the end of his list.

===Chinese===
温多弗似乎灵光一闪。他的脸顿时亮了。
“这下我明白你那地图的比方是什么意思了！当然，难处明摆着：表面上看，小托伦斯没有动机。可要是他就是哈克尔顿的爪牙呢？要是他拿的是哈克尔顿的钱，替他办这桩差事呢？那就全对上榫了。只是万一当真如此，要证实它可是桩要命的差事。”
他朝朋友那边一瞥，察觉克林顿爵士脸上有一种异样的神情。那也只是转瞬即逝，因为几乎同时，郡警察局长又恢复了平日那副面具。
“讲下去。”他又说了一遍。
温多弗只得承认，他的名单念到了头。

===Original===
“There’s nobody else that I can think of. Sylvia Hawkhurst was paying a visit to some people in the afternoon and didn’t get home till it was all over. Ernest Shandon was off the premises, too, probably sitting by the roadside and cursing the nail in his boot at the very time his brothers were being murdered. And then there’s Stenness. He was up at the house when the affair took place. Miss Forrest found him there when she went to give the alarm.”
“Stenness,” said Sir Clinton reflectively. “Stenness is a very efficient fellow.”
Wendover thought he detected something behind the phrase.
“What do you think?” he demanded.
Sir Clinton looked at him mildly.
“I think it’s about time we were going to bed, Squire. We may have to be up early tomorrow. At least, I may.”

===Chinese===
“再想不出别的人了。西尔维娅·霍克赫斯特下午出门访客，直到一切都收了场才到家。欧内斯特·香农也不在宅子里，多半正坐在路边，咒骂他鞋里那颗钉子——就在他两位兄弟遭人毒手的当口。再就是斯滕内斯。出事的时候他在宅子里。福雷斯特小姐跑去报信，就是在那儿撞见他的。”
“斯滕内斯。”克林顿爵士若有所思地说，“斯滕内斯是个非常干练的家伙。”
温多弗觉得，他从这句话里咂摸出了点别的什么。
“你怎么想？”他追问道。
克林顿爵士温和地望着他。
“我想，咱们也差不多该上床了，乡绅。明天说不定得早起。至少，我说不定得早起。”

## IX The Burglary at Whistlefield / 九 惠斯特菲尔德失窃案

===Original===
When Sir Clinton came down to breakfast on the following morning, Wendover thought that he looked tired and worried, though he was doing his best to show his normal composure.
“You look as if you’d been up all night, Clinton; and yet you cleared me off to bed fairly early.”
The Chief Constable forced a smile, but it was obvious that he had something on his mind which was troubling him.
“Not *all* night,” he said, qualifying Wendover’s suggestion by a slight emphasis. “But I’ve certainly lost a good deal of sleep over this Whistlefield business.”
“I can’t see what you’ve got to worry about just now,” his host retorted. “Until one gets more evidence than we have just now, there’s nothing that can be done, so far as I can see. You practically admitted as much yourself, last night.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，克林顿爵士下楼来用早餐，温多弗觉得他看上去又疲惫又焦灼，尽管他正竭力摆出平日那副从容。
“瞧你这副样子，像是熬了个通宵，克林顿；可昨天夜里你还早早地就把我打发上床了。”
郡警察局长勉强笑了笑，可明摆着他有心事，正为此烦忧。
“不算*一整夜*。”他说道，微微加重语气，给温多弗的说法打了个折扣，“不过为惠斯特菲尔德这桩事，我确实少睡了相当多的觉。”
“我看不出你眼下有什么好烦的。”主人回敬道，“依我看，在拿到比现在更多的证据之前，什么都做不成。昨晚你自己也差不多承认了这一点。”

===Original===
“Last night and this morning are two different things,” Sir Clinton pointed out, rather gloomily. “A lot may happen in six hours.”
“Well, if they have happened, they have happened; and you couldn’t have prevented them happening.”
“That sounds like a truism,” the Chief Constable commented, “and I wish it were one. But it isn’t.”
He seemed almost on the verge of a confidence at last; but to Wendover’s disappointment he contented himself with adding:
“I’ve taken a big risk in this affair, Squire; and if the game goes against me, I’d never be able to forgive myself. It’s as serious as that.”

===Chinese===
“昨晚和今早，是两码事。”克林顿爵士相当阴郁地指出，“六个钟头里，可能出不少事。”
“嗨，事情要是出了，那就是出了；你也拦不住它们不出。”
“这话听着像句不言自明的老生常谈，”郡警察局长评论道，“我还巴不得它真是。可惜不是。”
他似乎终于快要向人吐露心迹了；可让温多弗失望的是，他只满足于补上一句：
“这桩案子里，我担了一个天大的风险，乡绅；这局棋要是输在我手上，我永远也不能原谅自己。事情就有这么严重。”

===Original===
From his tone, it was evident that he was gravely perturbed; and Wendover could find nothing to say which seemed likely to be helpful.
In a moment or two, Sir Clinton broke the silence.
“They’re on the phone at Whistlefield, aren’t they?”
“Yes. Are you expecting a message?”
“One never can tell,” was all that Sir Clinton would vouchsafe. “Can you hear your telephone bell from this room?”

===Chinese===
听他的口气，他分明忧心忡忡；温多弗也找不出一句像是帮得上忙的话来。
过了片刻，克林顿爵士打破了沉默。
“惠斯特菲尔德那边装了电话，是吧？”
“装了。你在等消息？”
“这可说不准。”克林顿爵士肯透露的只有这一句，“在这个房间里，听得见你家的电话铃吗？”

===Original===
“Oh, yes, the machine’s just down the passage from here, as it happens.”
Sir Clinton went on with his breakfast; but Wendover could see that he was listening for the ringing of the bell. Just as they had finished, it rang sharply.
“I’ll go,” said Sir Clinton. “It’s almost certain to be Whistlefield ringing up.”
As he rose from the table Wendover could see a look of acute anxiety on his face. He left the door open as he went out, and the sound of his voice at the telephone came back into the room.

===Chinese===
“哦，听得见，电话机碰巧就在这条走廊过去没几步的地方。”
克林顿爵士继续用他的早餐；可温多弗看得出，他一直在侧耳等着铃响。两人刚一吃完，铃声就尖锐地响了起来。
“我去。”克林顿爵士说，“几乎肯定是惠斯特菲尔德打来的。”
他从桌边起身时，温多弗看得出他脸上那副焦灼的神情。他出去时没有带上门，他在电话里的说话声便传回了屋里。

===Original===
“Driffield speaking … Did you say burglar or burglars? … All right, don’t bother to tell me any more now. I’m coming across at once. Goodbye.”
Sir Clinton came back to Wendover. The anxiety on his face was as deeply marked as ever; but the prospect of action seemed to have raised his spirits slightly.
“Come on, Wendover. Get the car out, will you? There’s been a burglary at Whistlefield last night. I’ll need to go across and look into the affair.”
When they reached Whistlefield, they were shown into the study where they found Ernest Shandon and Stenness waiting for them.

===Chinese===
“我是德里菲尔德……你们说的是一个窃贼还是几个？……好，现在不必再细说了。我马上就过来。再见。”
克林顿爵士回到温多弗这里。他脸上的焦灼依旧深重；但有行动在望，他的精神似乎稍稍振作了一点。
“走吧，温多弗。把车开出来，劳驾。昨天夜里惠斯特菲尔德失窃了。我得过去查究一番。”
到了惠斯特菲尔德，两人被引进书房，欧内斯特·香农和斯滕内斯正在那里等候。

===Original===
“Now you might give me the whole story, Mr. Shandon,” Sir Clinton requested as soon as he had greeted the two. “It may be a case where time means a good deal; and we want to get our hands on these fellows at once, if we can.”
Ernest pulled out his cigarette-case. He seemed to be in a very nervous condition.
“D’you mind if I smoke?” he demanded, perfunctorily. “It soothes one, I always think; gives you a better chance of putting things calmly and not getting mixed up in your story.”
He peered thoughtfully into his case for a second or two before he could make up his mind which cigarette to take; but at last he found one to his mind and set it alight. Wendover fidgeted slightly, but Sir Clinton evidently recognised the uselessness of trying to hasten Ernest in his operations.

===Chinese===
“现在，你可以把事情从头到尾讲给我听了，香农先生。”克林顿爵士招呼过两人，便即请求道，“这也许是桩时间至关重要的案子；这些家伙，只要办得到，我们要尽快抓到手。”
欧内斯特掏出烟盒。他看上去神经紧张得很。
“我抽支烟你不介意吧？”他心不在焉地问了一句，“我总觉得，抽烟叫人镇定；能让你把事情讲得从容些，不至于把话说乱。”
他对着烟盒若有所思地端详了一两秒，才拿定主意抽哪一支；末了总算挑中一支合意的，点着了。温多弗微微有些坐不住，可克林顿爵士显然明白，想催欧内斯特快一点，纯属徒劳。

===Original===
“There’s been a burglary here last night,” he announced at last. “Or rather, when I say last night, I really mean this morning because it was a good deal after midnight when it happened.”
“Can you give me the exact time?” Sir Clinton asked.
Ernest looked at him owlishly, reflected for a moment or two, and then shook his head in a careworn fashion.
“No, I don’t think so. I didn’t look at the clock, you know. It was after midnight, that’s all I can remember.”

===Chinese===
“昨天夜里，这里进了贼。”他终于宣布道，“或者说，我说昨夜，其实是今晨——因为事情发生的时候，早已过了半夜好一阵了。”
“你能给出确切的时刻吗？”克林顿爵士问。
欧内斯特像猫头鹰似的呆望着他，寻思了一两秒，随即一脸苦恼地摇了摇头。
“不，我想给不出。你知道，我没看钟。我只记得是过了半夜，就记得这个。”

===Original===
“Begin at the beginning, then, Mr. Shandon, and give us all the details you can. Anything may turn out to be useful for all we can tell.”
“I usually go to bed quite early,” Ernest began, “but last night, after you went away, I thought I would have another look over Roger’s papers. You interrupted me, you remember,” he said, as though in explanation of his activity. “I got quite interested in some of them. Roger had so many irons in the fire. I hadn’t realised before what an amount of energy he must have had. You’ve no idea of the amount of things he was mixed up in.”
“Yes?” said Sir Clinton, trying to hasten the slow progress of the narrative.
“Such an amount of things,” Ernest went on. “It took me all my time to make head or tail of the papers I looked at. I must have been hours and hours, turning them over and reading bits here and there—files of correspondence and that sort of thing. His chequebook stubs were there, too, and I looked at them. I’d no idea so much cash passed through his hands, no idea at all. By the way, I noticed something funny about his last chequebook. I’ll tell you about that again, though. It was rum, I thought; but I’d better be getting on with the story.”

===Chinese===
“那就从头讲起吧，香农先生，能想起的细节都讲给我们。天知道呢，随便哪一样都可能派上用场。”
“我平素睡得挺早，”欧内斯特开了头，“可昨天夜里，你们走了之后，我想把罗杰的文件再看一遍。你们打断了我的，你记得的。”他说道，像是在为自己这番忙碌辩解，“有几份我看得入了神。罗杰手头张罗的事真多。我从前竟没意识到，他得有多大的一股精力。他搅和在里面的事情有多少，你简直想象不到。”
“是吗？”克林顿爵士说，想催一催这叙述的缓慢进程。
“多得不得了的事。”欧内斯特接着说，“我看的那些文件，光是理出个头绪，就够我全副工夫了。我一定是耗了好几个钟头，翻来翻去，东读一段西读一段——一捆一捆的往来信件，还有那一类东西。他的支票簿存根也在那儿，我也看了。那么多现钱从他手里过，我原先毫无概念，一点都没有。对了，他那最后一册支票簿，我注意到一点古怪。不过那个我回头再讲。我觉得挺邪门；可我还是先把事情讲下去吧。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton nodded patiently and waited for more.
“I’d just been looking over the chequebook when I heard a noise,” Ernest pursued. “Of course, in an old house like this one often hears sounds at night, furniture cracking and doors rattling, and all that sort of thing; so I didn’t pay any attention to it at the time. It was only afterwards that I remembered I’d heard it; and perhaps it had nothing to do with the burglary at all. I just mention it, because you said I was to give you all the details I could, you know.”
“What sort of sound was it?” Sir Clinton asked.
Ernest looked bewildered.
“What sort of sound was it?” he repeated. “Oh, a noise, you know. A … a …” he seemed to find the English language too limited. “It was a sound, you understand.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士耐心地点了点头，等着下文。
“我刚把支票簿看完，就听见了一个响动。”欧内斯特接着讲，“当然，这么一座老宅子，夜里常听见些声响，家具开裂啦，门扇咯咯响啦，诸如此类；所以当时我没大留意。还是后来我才想起自己听见过它；兴许它跟失窃根本不相干。我提上一句，只因为你说过，能想起的细节都要讲给你，你知道。”
“那是什么样的声响？”克林顿爵士问。
欧内斯特一脸茫然。
“什么样的声响？”他重复了一遍，“哦，就是一个响动，你知道。一声……一声……”他似乎觉得英语贫乏得不够用了，“反正是个声响，你明白。”

===Original===
“A voice?” suggested Sir Clinton.
“No, not a voice. A sound, just like a snick or a rap or something of that sort, if you see what I mean.”
“And then?”
“Oh, I paid no attention to it. In a house like this one often hears queer noises at night. It didn’t really draw my attention. I was interested in this thing about the chequebook. So I didn’t trouble about the sound.”
Wendover was surprised at Sir Clinton’s patience, for no sign of boredom appeared on his face. In fact, he seemed keenly interested.

===Chinese===
“是人声？”克林顿爵士提示道。
“不，不是人声。是个响动，就像咔嗒一下，或者笃的一下，那一类的东西，但愿你能明白我的意思。”
“后来呢？”
“哦，我没理它。这样一座宅子，夜里常有些古怪响动。它实在没引起我的注意。我心里惦记的是支票簿那桩事。所以那声响我就没去费神。”
温多弗对克林顿爵士的耐性暗暗称奇，因为他脸上不见一丝厌烦。事实上，他似乎兴味浓得很。

===Original===
“The next thing I remember,” Ernest continued, “was feeling sleepy. I put away the papers, put them all back in the safe again and locked it up. Then I thought I’d go to bed. I always go out for a breath of fresh air before I go to my room at night—if it isn’t raining—so I went to the window and looked out. It was quite dry; so I made up my mind to go for my usual stroll. I don’t go far, you know, just up and down a little near the house. It seems to me that a breath of fresh air clears your lungs and makes you sleep better after you’ve had it. I’m a great believer in fresh air. I hate sitting in a stuffy room—must have the windows open always.”
“So you went out?”
“Yes. I put on a light overcoat and a cap and I opened the front door. It was locked when I found it—I suppose that’s important?”
Sir Clinton made no audible comment.

===Chinese===
“我记得的下一件事，”欧内斯特继续道，“就是犯困了。我把文件收起来，统统放回保险柜里，锁好。然后我想该上床了。夜里回房之前，我总要出去透一口新鲜空气——只要不下雨——于是我走到窗前望了望。天干得很；我便打定主意，照例去遛一圈。我走不远，你知道，就在宅子附近来回走上那么一小段。依我看，透一口新鲜空气，能涤清肺腑，透完了觉也睡得更香。我是新鲜空气的大信徒。我恨坐在闷屋子里——窗户永远非开着不可。”
“于是你就出去了？”
“是的。我罩上一件薄大衣，戴了顶便帽，去开前门。门是上了锁的——我猜这一点要紧吧？”
克林顿爵士没有出声评论。

===Original===
“I went out into the garden and strolled round the house. That took me under the window of the room where Neville—my brother—had been sleeping during his stay here. And, d’you know? I found a ladder sticking up against the wall there and resting against Neville’s windowsill. And when I looked up, there was the window open!”
Sir Clinton interrupted him.
“Was there a light in the room?”
Ernest blinked hopelessly for a moment or two.
“Was there a light? There may have been. Did I say anything about a light to you, Stenness, when I waked you up? No? Well, I don’t think there was a light. There may have been, but now I come to think of it I don’t remember seeing a light. No, I’m almost sure the electric light wasn’t on in the room. I’d have noticed that. I’d have seen that at once. No, there was no light.”

===Chinese===
“我走进花园，绕着宅子散步。这一绕，就走到了内维尔——我兄弟——住在这里期间睡觉的那间屋子的窗下。还有，你猜怎么着？我发现那里立着一架梯子，靠在墙上，搭在内维尔的窗台上。我抬头一看，窗子竟开着！”
克林顿爵士打断了他。
“房间里有灯吗？”
欧内斯特毫无办法地眨巴了一两秒钟的眼。
“有没有灯？也许有。斯滕内斯，我叫醒你的时候，跟你提起过灯的事吗？没有？唔，我看是没有灯。也许有，可现在想起来，我不记得看见过灯光。不，我几乎可以肯定，屋里的电灯没有开着。那个我会注意到的。我会一眼就看见的。没有，是没有灯。”

===Original===
Wendover intervened with a suggestion.
“Perhaps the burglars heard you coming and switched off.”
Sir Clinton had evidently heard all he wished to know about the light.
“And what happened next, Mr. Shandon?”
“When I came to the bottom of the ladder, I said to myself: ‘Burglars.’ You remember you’d been talking about how easy it would be to get into Whistlefield, that very night, in the museum. Then I had an idea. I took away the ladder as quietly as I could. That would prevent them getting out of the window again, you see? And then I went off back to the front door, let myself in, and roused Stenness and young Torrance. I was very nervous, you understand. Anyone might be, after getting a surprise like that.”

===Chinese===
温多弗插进来提了个想法。
“兴许窃贼听见你来了，就把灯关了。”
灯的事，克林顿爵士显然已经问完了想知道的。
“接下来怎么样了，香农先生？”
“走到梯子脚下的时候，我对自己说：‘窃贼。’你记得吧，恰恰就在那天晚上，你们还在陈列馆里谈过，说闯进惠斯特菲尔德有多容易。接着我有了个主意。我把梯子悄没声儿地搬走了。那样他们就没办法再从窗口出来了，你明白吧？然后我回到前门，自己开门进去，叫醒了斯滕内斯和小托伦斯。我紧张得很，你理解。撞上那么一惊，谁都会紧张的。”

===Original===
He took a fresh cigarette and lighted it with care. As he was about to continue his narrative, Sir Clinton arrested him and turned to Stenness.
“Perhaps you could give us your experiences, Mr. Stenness.”
“I’d gone to bed at the usual time, and fell asleep. I was waked up by someone knocking at my door; and when I got up I found it was Mr. Shandon. He said there were burglars in the room that Mr. Neville Shandon’s body was lying in. Mr. Shandon had on a cap and a light overcoat. As soon as he had waked me, he went off to wake Mr. Torrance. I looked at my watch. It was 2:35 a.m. I picked up the poker from my fireside and went out of my room. Mr. Torrance was there, too, by that time. I suggested that he had better get a poker as well, or else go down to the gun-room and get something better. He got a poker. Then all three of us went to Neville Shandon’s room. The door was locked; but we burst it in without making much of a noise. It’s an old door, and the lock fitted very poorly. There was no light on in the room when I got to it.”
“I’m almost sure, now I think over it, that there wasn’t any light at the window,” Ernest began again. “I couldn’t have helped seeing it, could I? Of course, all the rest of the house was dark, so if that window was dark it wouldn’t catch my eye and I wouldn’t remember about it. But if it had been lit up, I’d have noticed it at once.”

===Chinese===
他又取了一支烟，细细点上。他正要接着往下讲，克林顿爵士拦住了他，转向斯滕内斯。
“也许你可以给我们讲讲你的经历，斯滕内斯先生。”
“我按平常的时间上了床，睡着了。有人敲我的门，把我吵醒；我起身一看，是香农先生。他说，内维尔·香农先生遗体停放的那间屋子里有窃贼。香农先生戴着一顶便帽，罩着一件薄大衣。他一叫醒我，就去叫托伦斯先生了。我看了看表。是凌晨 2 点 35 分。我从壁炉边拿起拨火棍，出了自己的房间。那时托伦斯先生也到了。我说他最好也去拿一根拨火棍，要不然就下楼去枪械室，取件更顶用的家伙。他拿了一根拨火棍。然后我们三人一起去了内维尔·香农的房间。门锁着；可我们没怎么费声响就把它撞开了。那是扇旧门，锁也咬合得很松。我赶到的时候，屋里没有开灯。”
“现在细细回想，我几乎可以肯定，那扇窗里当时没有灯光。”欧内斯特又开了腔，“要是有，我不可能看不见，对不对？当然，宅子其余部分全黑着，那扇窗要是也黑着，就引不起我的注意，我也就记不得它。可它要是亮着，我准会一眼看见。”

===Original===
Stenness took no notice of the interruption.
“Somebody had evidently been in the room. Everything was upside down. All the drawers had been ransacked and their contents had been thrown about. Neville Shandon’s attaché case had been treated in the same way. The whole place was in confusion.”
“Did you make out what the thieves had been searching for?”
“Well, his writing-case had been torn open and most of the contents had been strewn about the floor. They’d been in a great hurry over their work. And his pocketbook was pitched over into one corner of the room as if someone had been through the contents and had chucked it away.”

===Chinese===
斯滕内斯不理会这番插话。
“分明有人进过那间屋子。样样东西底朝了天。所有抽屉都被翻了个遍，里面的东西扔得四处都是。内维尔·香农的手提公文箱也遭了同样的对待。整个地方一片狼藉。”
“你看没看出来，那些贼在搜找什么？”
“这么说吧，他的书信夹被撕开了，里面的大部分东西撒了一地板。他们干这活儿的时候匆忙得很。他的记事本被甩到了房间的一角，像是有人翻完了里面的东西，随手扔掉的。”

===Original===
“What about money? His notecase was lying on the dressing-table. I put it there myself when I searched his body yesterday.”
“Some notes were lying on the floor amongst the rest of the stuff. I didn’t count them. In fact I didn’t touch anything. I thought you’d better see things as they were.”
“The window was still open?”
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“钱呢？他的钞夹就在梳妆台上。是我昨天搜检遗体的时候，亲手放上去的。”
“地板上那堆东西里散着几张钞票。我没有数。其实我什么都没碰。我想，东西保持原样让你亲眼看更好。”
“窗子还开着？”
“开着。”

===Original===
“It looks as though the burglar (or burglars) had got away before Mr. Shandon saw the ladder, then. They’d cleared out and left the ladder in position. What about the key of the door?”
“It hasn’t turned up.”
“And what happened after that? Why didn’t you ring up the police at once?”
Stenness suppressed a sardonic smile with evident difficulty.
“Mr. Shandon was to look after that part. I went back to my room, put on some clothes, and kept myself awake by reading till the morning. We hadn’t roused the rest of the people in the house.”

===Chinese===
“这样看来，窃贼（一个也好，几个也罢）早在香农先生瞧见梯子之前就跑掉了。他们撤了出去，却把梯子留在了原处。那房门的钥匙呢？”
“一直没有找到。”
“后来又怎样了？你们为什么没有立刻给警察打电话？”
斯滕内斯好不容易才压下一丝讥诮的笑。
“那一部分归香农先生负责。我回自己房里，穿了些衣服，靠读书撑着不睡，一直挨到天亮。宅子里其余的人，我们都没有惊动。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton turned to Ernest.
“Couldn’t you get through to the police-station, Mr. Shandon? I must see about this. It’s a serious matter for my subordinates.”
Ernest seemed completely taken aback by this view of the question.
“Well, Sir Clinton, I suppose I ought to have rung up the police; but it was very late, you know. I was awfully sleepy; and as I was walking along, I turned into my own bedroom. I was very shaken up by the whole affair. It hasn’t happened to me before, you see. And somehow, I must have begun to undress quite without thinking about it—you know how one does things unconsciously …”
Then, with disarming frankness, he admitted the truth:

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士转向欧内斯特。
“你没能把电话打到警察局去吗，香农先生？这件事我必须过问。对我的下属来说，这可是件严重的事。”
问题被摆到这个高度，欧内斯特似乎全然愣住了。
“哎呀，克林顿爵士，我想我本该给警察打电话的；可是天太晚了，你知道。我困得要命；一路走着走着，就拐进了自己的卧室。这整个事儿把我吓得不轻。你知道，我从前没经历过这种事。也不知怎么的，我准是不知不觉就脱起衣服来了——你知道人做事情，常常是无意识的……”
接着，他以一种令人没法苛责的坦白，承认了实情：

===Original===
“I went to bed. And after a minute or two, I remembered I ought to have rung up the police. But that would have meant getting out of bed again and putting on some clothes to go down to the phone. It would have been a lot of trouble. And it didn’t seem to me that it mattered very much really. So while I was thinking about it, I fell asleep. But I rang you up as soon as I got up this morning.”
Sir Clinton made no comment on Ernest’s methods. He had got all the information he needed apparently, for now he turned to Stenness and suggested that they should go upstairs and look at the scene of the burglary.
Neville Shandon’s room bore out Stenness’s description of it. Everything seemed to have been turned over and left higgledy-piggledy. The floor was littered with a confused mass of clothes, papers, contents of drawers, and other things. It seemed as though the whole place had been searched in frantic haste for some object or other; but whether the seeker had succeeded or not was apparently an insoluble problem.
Sir Clinton stepped over to the still open window and examined the sill.

===Chinese===
“我上床睡了。过了一两分钟，我想起本该给警察打电话的。可那就得再爬起来，穿上衣服，下楼去打电话。那可是天大的麻烦。再说依我看，这事其实也无关紧要。我正琢磨着呢，就睡着了。不过今天早晨我一爬起来，就给你打了电话。”
对欧内斯特的这一套做法，克林顿爵士未置一词。他想要的信息显然已经到手，因为他随即转向斯滕内斯，提议上楼去看看失窃的现场。
内维尔·香农的房间印证了斯滕内斯的描述。样样东西都像是被翻了个底朝天，乱七八糟地撂着。地板上狼藉着一堆混杂的东西：衣物、纸张、抽屉里的物件，还有别的零碎。整个屋子像是被人发了疯似的匆匆搜遍了，就为找某一样什么东西；至于找的人得没得手，看来是个解不开的谜。
克林顿爵士走到那扇仍开着的窗前，察看窗台。

===Original===
“The marks of the ladder-ends are there, clear enough,” he pointed out to Wendover, “and there’s the ladder itself down on the ground.”
He beckoned Stenness to his side.
“One of your own ladders, I suppose?”
Stenness examined it.
“Yes, I happen to recognise it. The gardeners use it, and it’s kept somewhere about the place.”

===Chinese===
“梯子两头的印痕就在那儿，清清楚楚，”他指给温多弗看，“梯子本身就在下头的地上。”
他招手让斯滕内斯到身边来。
“是你们自家的梯子吧，我猜？”
斯滕内斯察看了一番。
“是的，我恰好认得它。园丁们用这架，平日就搁在这园子里的什么地方。”

===Original===
“Some soil on the windowsill,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “They must have picked it up on their boots from the flowerbed where the end of the ladder rests.”
“There’s some on the floor here, as well,” Stenness pointed out.
“So I see,” Sir Clinton confirmed, “but that might have been brought up by Mr. Shandon when you and he came in here. One can’t attach much importance to it.”
He said nothing more, and contented himself with a careful inspection of the room.

===Chinese===
“窗台上有一些泥土。”克林顿爵士指出，“准是从梯脚扎根的那块花坛里，沾到他们靴子上带下来的。”
“这儿的地上也有一些。”斯滕内斯指出。
“我看见了。”克林顿爵士认可道，“不过那也许是香农先生和你进屋的时候带上来的。这一层没法太当真。”
他没有再多说什么，只管细细检视那个房间。

===Original===
“I think I’ve seen all I want to see,” he said at last. “By the way, you haven’t a key of the Maze, have you? I noticed the iron gates at each entrance had locks on them. I want to go down there now and look round.”
“I can get you a key, I think,” Stenness said, doubtfully, “but the place is always left open. It’s never been locked at any time, to my knowledge.”
“Oh, that’s all right then,” Sir Clinton hastened to say. “Now, Wendover, I think we’ll be getting along.”
A thought seemed to strike him at the last moment.
“If you’re afraid of being worried by any more burglars, Mr. Shandon, I’ll detach a couple of constables to look after Whistlefield. But I really don’t think it’s the least likely that you’ll have any further attempts of the sort. They seem to have made a thorough business of this one, to judge by the state they left the place in.”

===Chinese===
“想看的东西，我想都看到了。”他末了说道，“对了，你们没有迷宫的钥匙，是吧？我注意到每个入口的铁门上都装着锁。我现在想下去到那儿转一转。”
“钥匙我想能给你找一把，”斯滕内斯迟疑地说，“可那地方一向敞着。据我所知，从来就没锁过。”
“哦，那就没关系了。”克林顿爵士忙说道，“好了，温多弗，我们该动身了。”
临到最后关头，他像是又想起一件事。
“你要是怕再有窃贼来搅扰，香农先生，我可以拨两名警员来看守惠斯特菲尔德。不过说实在的，我认为绝无丝毫可能再来这样一遭。看他们留下的这副现场，这一票像是干得挺彻底的。”

===Original===
Ernest seemed rather shamefaced at the Chief Constable’s proposal. Quite obviously he recognised that he had not shone as a hero in the business of the night.
“No,” he replied, “I don’t think we need them, Sir Clinton. I think we’ll manage without them, really. Of course, one feels a little nervous. I think it’s quite understandable, when things have been happening all together like this. But still, I don’t think we really need a guard. If you think it’s not likely to happen again, I’m quite ready to take your view of it, quite ready, I assure you. As you say, there’s no reason why they should come back at all. They must have got what they wanted. They’re sure to have got it, I think. No, they’re hardly likely to come back again.”
As they made their way downstairs Sylvia Hawkhurst met them.
“I’ve been looking for you, Sir Clinton. Guess what you left behind you last night.”

===Chinese===
对郡警察局长的这番提议，欧内斯特显得颇有些面皮发臊。很显然，他心里明白，昨夜那场事里自己没显出半点英雄气概。
“不用，”他答道，“我看我们用不着他们，克林顿爵士。没有他们我们也能对付，真的。当然，人心里是有点发慌。事情接二连三赶到一块儿，有这点感觉，我想完全说得通。不过话说回来，我们实在不需要派人守卫。你既然觉得这事不大可能重演，我十分乐意照你的看法办，十分乐意，我向你保证。就像你说的，他们根本没有任何理由再回来。他们想要的东西准是到手了。我想他们肯定到手了。不，他们几乎不可能再来了。”
他们下楼的时候，西尔维娅·霍克赫斯特迎面遇上。
“我正找你呢，克林顿爵士。猜猜你昨天晚上落下什么东西了。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton shook his head doubtfully.
“I never succeed in these guessing competitions, Miss Hawkhurst. What was it?”
“The box of darts! You put it down on the mantelpiece of the museum; and I happened to notice it this morning when I went in.”
Sir Clinton’s face betrayed his annoyance at his blunder. It was so obvious that no one cared to say anything on the subject.
“I’ll get it for you in a moment,” Sylvia said, as she hurried off.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士疑惑地摇了摇头。
“这种猜谜的比赛，我从来就没赢过，霍克赫斯特小姐。是什么呀？”
“那盒飞镖！你把它搁在陈列馆的壁炉台上了；今天早晨我进去，恰好瞧见了它。”
克林顿爵士脸上透出了对自己疏失的懊恼。那副神情太明显了，谁都不好意思就这事再说什么。
“我去给你拿来，一会儿就好。”西尔维娅说着，匆匆走了。

===Original===
Stenness looked at the Chief Constable, and it seemed as if his estimate of Sir Clinton was undergoing revision. Wendover was completely taken aback by the turn of events.
“Here it is,” Sylvia said, as she came back to them again. “It was just where you left it. You’d better count the darts to make sure I haven’t lost any—though I haven’t opened the box at all. I was too much afraid of them.”
Sir Clinton obeyed and found the total correct. He shut the box carefully and stowed it away in his pocket.
“Thanks, Miss Hawkhurst. It was very careless of me. But there’s no harm done, since you’ve taken care of them for me.”

===Chinese===
斯滕内斯望着郡警察局长，他对克林顿爵士的估价，似乎正在修订。这番变故让温多弗彻底愣住了。
“在这儿。”西尔维娅回到他们跟前说，“就在你搁下的地方。你最好数一数飞镖，看看我有没有弄丢——不过我压根儿没开过那盒子。我对它们怕得要命。”
克林顿爵士照办了，数下来一支不少。他把盒子仔细扣好，收进了口袋。
“多谢，霍克赫斯特小姐。我实在太粗心了。不过幸好有你替我照看，总算没出乱子。”

===Original===
And after a few words about the affairs of the night, he took his leave.
“Take the road to the East Gate, Squire,” he requested, as Wendover let in the clutch.
“*You*’re a bright detective,” his friend retorted scornfully. “Here you’ve been racing and chasing to cut off a possible source of curare; and in the middle of the job you leave a whole tin of lethal darts lying about for Tom, Dick or Harry to pick up. The limit, I’d call it!”
“It was very careless,” Sir Clinton admitted, biting his lip.
“Careless!” Wendover echoed, contemptuously. “I can’t think how you managed to do it. My godfathers! Leaving stuff like that on a mantelpiece!”

===Chinese===
又说了几句昨夜的事情，他便告辞了。
“走通往东门的那条路，乡绅。”温多弗松开离合器时，他吩咐道。
“*你*可真是个高明的大侦探。”朋友轻蔑地回敬道，“你这边追的追、查的查，为的是掐断一条可能的箭毒来路；可干到半道，却把整整一盒致命的飞镖撂在那儿，让随便哪个张三李四捡了去。要我说，真是出格到了家！”
“实在太粗心了。”克林顿爵士咬着嘴唇承认。
“粗心！”温多弗轻蔑地应了一句，“我怎么也想不出你是怎么干出来的。我的老天爷！那样的东西，居然撂在壁炉台上！”

===Original===
Sir Clinton flushed.
“Look here, Squire, I can say ‘You’re a damned fool,’ just as often as I need to hear it just now, without your help. You can’t guess how I feel about it. Don’t rub it in, there’s a good chap.”
Wendover had never seen his friend so disturbed before. He stopped his denunciations at once. In a few moments they reached the Maze, and both left the car. Sir Clinton led the way to the entrance through which they had gone on the previous afternoon.
“I’d better take the lead,” said Wendover. “I know the Maze and you don’t. Just follow me.”
Sir Clinton paid no attention but kept in front. To Wendover’s surprise he showed no hesitation, but threaded his way through the labyrinth without difficulty. When he reached the centre he turned to his companion.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士涨红了脸。
“听我说，乡绅，‘你是个十足的蠢货’这句话，眼下我要听几遍，自己就能对自己说几遍，用不着你帮忙。你猜不到我心里是什么滋味。别再戳痛处了，好伙计。”
温多弗从未见过朋友这般失态。他立刻收了声讨。不多时他们到了迷宫，两人下了车。克林顿爵士领头，走向昨天下午他们进过的那个入口。
“还是我打头吧。”温多弗说，“迷宫我熟，你不熟。跟着我走就是。”
克林顿爵士置之不理，仍旧走在前头。让温多弗吃惊的是，他毫不含糊，一路在迷宫里穿行，毫无滞碍。到了中心，他才转向同伴。

===Original===
“That’s merely to show you that anyone can find their way through here if they keep their heads. I memorised the thing as Stenness was guiding us yesterday—first right, third left, and so on. So you see the murderer could have got up it easily enough if he had someone to show him the ropes at the start.”
He glanced into the centre and then passed round to the position of the loophole in the outer hedge. As he did so he gave an exclamation of disgust and passed his hand over his face.
“Ugh! Spider’s web got across my mouth! There’s any amount of gossamer about here. These hedges must be full of spiders. Beastly things!”
Coming to the loophole, he examined it carefully as though to discover the range of view from it. Then he made his way to the loophole commanding the second centre, which he inspected with equal interest.
“Now we’ll go outside and have another look at the track my dog picked up,” he announced, curtly.

===Chinese===
“这不过是向你证明：只要保持头脑清醒，谁都能从这里穿出去。昨天斯滕内斯领路的时候，我就把路线记下了——第一处岔口向右，第三处向左，依此类推。所以你瞧，只要起初有人给他指点门道，凶手想把这套路数学会，容易得很。”
他朝中心瞥了一眼，随即绕到外围树篱上那处暗孔的位置。走着走着，他厌恶地叫了一声，抬手抹了一把脸。
“呸！蜘蛛网糊上我嘴了！这一带游丝多得要命。这些树篱里准是爬满了蜘蛛。讨厌的东西！”
到了暗孔跟前，他细细察看，像是要弄清从那里望出去的视野范围。然后他走向俯瞰第二个中心的那处暗孔，怀着同样的兴致检视了一番。
“现在我们出去，再看看我的狗嗅出的那条踪迹。”他言简意赅地宣布。

===Original===
Wendover followed him once more, and they emerged from the entrance near the river. Sir Clinton walked over to the tree to which the dog had led them; and then, using the scraps of paper scattered on the previous day as his guide, he crossed the grass. Once on the road, he stopped and turned to Wendover. He seemed to be still smarting under the annoyance of his blunder with the darts.
“That’s the murderer’s route, you see? He came out of the Maze obviously. Then he climbed that tree, I think you said. No doubt he was well out of danger there. No one would think of looking for him up amongst the leaves. And after that he came over here, got into his private aeroplane, and flew off—since the trail stops short.”
He glanced up and down the road.
“Just the one place where he could have done it, notice. This bit of the road is concealed from nearly every direction by these banks of rhododendrons round about.”
Wendover took no notice of the irony. He sympathised with Sir Clinton’s feelings; it required no great stretch of imagination to appreciate how a man would feel after making a mistake like that. They walked over to the car and took the road to the East Gate.

===Chinese===
温多弗又跟了上去，两人从临河的那个入口走出来。克林顿爵士走到那棵狗曾把他们引到的树下；然后，拿前一天撒下的纸屑当路标，穿过草地。一上大路，他便停下脚步，转向温多弗。飞镖那桩疏失带来的懊恼，似乎还在他心里作痛。
“这就是凶手的路线，你明白吗？他显然是从迷宫里出来的。然后他爬上了那棵树——我记得你是这么说的。待在那上头，无疑就脱开危险了。谁也不会想到到枝叶中间去找他。再往后他走到这里，钻进他的私人飞机，飞走了——既然踪迹到这里就断了。”
他朝大路两头望了望。
“请注意，整条路上就这一处他干得了这事。路的这一段，四面八方几乎都被周围这一道道杜鹃花堤遮住了。”
温多弗没有理会话里的讥讽。他体谅克林顿爵士的心情；一个人出了那样的差错之后是什么滋味，不用多费想象力也能体会。他们走回汽车旁，上了通往东门的路。

===Original===
As he drove, Wendover began to fit together the new facts in the Whistlefield case. The more he recalled the state of Neville Shandon’s room, the more obvious it grew that the burglar had been searching for a document of some sort. This linked itself in his mind with the torn fragment of Neville’s notes which had been found in his hand after death. And Roger’s room had not been burgled.
“It looks like Hackleton at work,” he uttered, half-unconsciously.
Sir Clinton seemed to come out of a savage reverie at the words.
“Hackleton? Oh, you mean the burglary? It fits neatly in, doesn’t it?”
Then, in a more friendly voice than he had used since the dart incident:

===Chinese===
一路上开着车，温多弗开始把惠斯特菲尔德案里的新事实拼凑到一处。内维尔·香农房间的情形，他越回想越明白：那窃贼搜寻的是某种文件。这在他脑子里同内维尔笔记上撕下的那角碎纸勾连起来——内维尔死后，那片碎纸是在他手里发现的。而罗杰的房间，却无人光顾。
“这像是哈克尔顿的手笔。”他半是不自觉地脱口而出。
这句话似乎把克林顿爵士从一腔恶气的出神里唤了出来。
“哈克尔顿？哦，你是说这桩失窃？扣得倒严丝合缝，是不是？”
随后，他用了自飞镖事件以来不曾有过的、较为友善的口气说：

===Original===
“I’m sorry if I rubbed you on the raw, Squire. But you know how I hate to look like a fool; and that’s exactly what I do look like just now.”
Wendover was eager to accept the advance. He had no desire to irritate his friend. After all, everyone makes mistakes sooner or later. But as they fell into talk again a fresh idea shot through his mind; and this time he did not utter it aloud:
“Clinton hustled me off early to bed last night. He was washed-out-looking this morning. He hinted he’d done something or other that was risky. What if he was the burglar himself?”

===Chinese===
“乡绅，方才要是戳了你的痛处，我抱歉。可你知道我多恨自己看上去像个傻子；而我眼下这副样子，恰恰就是个傻子。”
温多弗巴不得接受这番示好。他无意再惹恼朋友。毕竟，人迟早都会犯错。可两人重新聊起来时，一个新的念头闪过他的脑海；这一回，他没有说出口：
“克林顿昨晚急匆匆把我打发上床。今天早晨他又一脸倦容。他暗示自己干了件什么冒险的事。万一他自己就是那个窃贼呢？”

===Original===
But though he puzzled over this view of the case, it yielded very little help to him. At last he put it to the back of his mind, ready for future reference if needed.
Sir Clinton had one further surprise for him as they reached the Grange:
“Would you mind, Squire, if somebody brings a glass of boiling water, some vinegar, and some washing soda to my room as soon as possible? I’d like to have them now.”

===Chinese===
可这一层案情他翻来覆去地想，也帮不上他多少忙。末了他把它搁到脑子后头，以备将来需要时再查考。
车子回到塔尔加斯农庄，克林顿爵士还有一件叫他意外的事：
“乡绅，能不能叫人尽快给我房里送一杯滚开的水、一些醋，还有一点洗涤碱来？我现在就要用。”

## X The Third Attack in the Maze / 十 迷宫里的第三次袭击

===Original===
When Sir Clinton came down from his room Wendover noticed that he had mastered his vexation. During lunch, both of them avoided the Whistlefield case by tacit consent; but the Squire was relieved to see that his friend’s face showed less anxiety in its expression than had been obvious at the breakfast table. Sir Clinton usually had complete control of his features and showed no more than he wished the world to see; and Wendover guessed that behind the mask the Chief Constable was still too sensitive to make the affair at Whistlefield a safe subject of conversation.

When lunch was over Sir Clinton smoked a cigarette for a minute or two in silence. Then he turned to his host.

“You might lend me your car, Squire. I ought to go down to the police station this afternoon and get some reports from the man in charge. It isn’t worth your while to come with me. They’ll only be formal affairs, I suspect; and if there’s anything striking, I’ll tell you about it when I get back.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士从房间里下楼来，温多弗注意到，他已经把那阵恼怒压了下去。午饭桌上，两人心照不宣，都避开了惠斯特菲尔德的案子；但让乡绅宽慰的是，朋友脸上的焦灼神色，已比早餐桌上那副显而易见的样子轻了几分。克林顿爵士素来完全管得住自己的五官，露给世人看的，绝不多于他愿意示人的一丝一毫；温多弗猜得到，在那副面具背后，郡警察局长对惠斯特菲尔德这桩事仍然敏感得很，拿它当谈话的题目并不稳妥。

午饭过后，克林顿爵士默默抽了一两分钟烟。然后他转向主人。

“把你的车借我用用吧，乡绅。今天下午我该去一趟警察局，从主办的人那里取些报告。不值得你陪我跑这一趟。我料想那些多半是例行公事；倘若有扎眼的东西，我回来再告诉你。”

===Original===
Wendover consented. His tact suggested that Sir Clinton would probably prefer to be alone until the first edge of his irritation had worn off.

When the Chief Constable returned, however, he had little news of importance.

“There’s no sign of the burglars so far,” he admitted. “I rang up the police from Whistlefield in the morning and put them on the alert; but they’ve picked up nothing that looks like the shadow of a clue. One could hardly expect it. Thanks to friend Ernest’s lethargic habits, a burglar could have got to the Midlands before my men even knew of the Whistlefield affair.”

===Chinese===
温多弗应允了。他的分寸感告诉他，在那股火气最伤人的锋头磨钝之前，克林顿爵士多半还是宁愿独处。

然而郡警察局长回来时，并没有多少要紧的消息。

“到现在还没见到窃贼的踪影。”他承认道，“今天早晨我从惠斯特菲尔德给警察打了电话，让他们严加戒备；可他们连半条线索的影子也没摸到。这原本也几乎不能指望。多亏欧内斯特朋友那副懒散的习性，等我这班手下得知惠斯特菲尔德这桩事的时候，窃贼只怕已经跑到米德兰（the Midlands）去了。”

===Original===
“I suppose they’ve done all they can?”

“For a local lot handling a thing of this sort, they’ve really done very well. They’ve made inquiries at all the railway stations in the neighbourhood and drawn blank. No suspicious person can be traced there. They’ve done their best in the matter of motors, too; but that, of course, was rather a washout. One can’t expect them to keep tally of every car that might pass along the road. And they’ve had a regular hunt through the Whistlefield gardens to find out how the burglary was done. But there again they struck a blank end.”

“Footmarks on the flowerbed?” inquired Wendover.

“One or two beautifully rectangular impressions—that’s all. The fellow evidently tied bits of cardboard under his shoes. One hasn’t even an idea of the size of his foot. And of course friend Ernest had been stamping about all over the bed in his efforts to remove the ladder with least trouble to himself. He didn’t exaggerate when he said he was nervous. It seems he just gave the thing a push and let it fall anyhow—smashed some flowers to bits in the collapse. If the burglars were still in the room above the row must have put them on the alert at once.”

===Chinese===
“我想，他们能做的都做了吧？”

“就一帮本地人手经手这样一桩事来说，他们实在干得够好了。他们把左近所有的火车站都查问了一遍，空手而归。查不出任何形迹可疑的人。汽车方面他们也尽了力；不过那一头自然是一无所获。总不能指望他们把沿路每一辆可能经过的汽车都记上一笔。他们还把惠斯特菲尔德的园子实实在在搜了一遍，想弄清这桩失窃是怎么干的。可那一头又是撞上了死胡同。”

“花坛上有脚印吗？”温多弗问。

“只有一两个方方正正、干干净净的印子——就这么多。那家伙显然在鞋底下绑了些硬纸板。连他的脚有多大都无从知道。当然啦，欧内斯特朋友为了自己省事搬梯子，在那花坛里踏得到处都是。他说自己紧张，倒一点没夸张。看样子他把那东西一推，就由它随便倒下去——倒下来的时候把一些花草砸了个稀烂。窃贼们当时要还在楼上的房间里，这一阵响动准会立刻叫他们警觉。”

===Original===
“You think they may have got away into the house and been hidden there while Stenness and Co. were breaking into the room?”

“Well, you can lock a door from either side, can’t you?”

Wendover reflected for a moment.

“It’s a pity Stenness didn’t think of searching the house when they found no one in the room.”

“Much too late by that time, Squire. No hunted burglar would wait on the premises a second longer than he could help. He’d be off downstairs at once and get out of the ground floor windows on the opposite side of the house.”

===Chinese===
“你是说，他们可能溜进了宅子，趁斯滕内斯一伙人撞门的时候藏在里面？”

“这个嘛，门从哪一边都能上锁，不是吗？”

温多弗寻思了片刻。

“可惜他们发现房间里没人的时候，斯滕内斯没想着把宅子搜一遍。”

“到那时候就太迟了，乡绅。任何被追捕的窃贼，绝不会在宅子里多耽搁一秒钟。他会立刻奔下楼去，从宅子另一面的底层窗户出去。”

===Original===
“But then he’d leave an unlatched window behind him.”

“So he may have done. No one can swear that all the windows were made fast yesterday evening. They’re a careless lot up at Whistlefield.”

Wendover’s mind fastened upon the thing which seemed to him of most importance.

“What did the burglar want? What was he after, Clinton?”

Sir Clinton’s face became inscrutable, though Wendover could not help seeing irony in his reply.

===Chinese===
“可那样一来，他身后就会留下一扇没闩上的窗子。”

“也许真留下了。谁也没法起誓，说昨天傍晚所有的窗户都闩得严严实实。惠斯特菲尔德那一家人，全是些粗心大意的。”

温多弗的脑筋钉在了他看来最要紧的那件事上。

“那窃贼要的是什么？他图的是什么，克林顿？”

克林顿爵士的脸变得莫测高深，不过温多弗还是不由得从他这句答话里看出了一丝讥诮。

===Original===
“ ‘What song the Syrens sang, or what name Achilles assumed when he hid himself among women, though puzzling questions are not beyond all conjecture,’ ” he quoted. “Sir Thomas Browne knew what he was talking about. What thing the burglar sought, though puzzling is not beyond conjecture, Squire. The field’s open, if you wish to enter for the competition.”

Wendover accepted the irony as a proof that Sir Clinton had got over his fit of annoyance completely.

“Well, then, I conjecture that the burglar was in Hackleton’s pay—like the murderer—and that he was hunting for more of Neville Shandon’s notes for the case. Look how everything was turned upside down. Look at the fact that the money was left intact. That wasn’t what one expects from a normal burglar.”

“No, it wasn’t,” Sir Clinton agreed. “But I’m not going to be drawn. Go on with your conjecturing, Squire; and if that fails you might take to surmising or speculating as a change of occupation. Thinking exercises the brain, so you won’t really lose in the end.”

===Chinese===
“‘塞壬们唱的是什么歌，阿喀琉斯混迹于妇人中间时用的什么名字，这些问题虽然难解，却并非全然无从猜度。’”他引了一句，“托马斯·布朗爵士（Sir Thomas Browne）的话说得在理。那窃贼寻的是什么东西，虽属难解却并非无从猜度，乡绅。赛场大门敞开着，你要是想报名参赛，请便。”

温多弗把这句讥诮当作凭证，证明克林顿爵士那一阵恼怒已经消散干净了。

“那么好，我猜那窃贼受的是哈克尔顿的雇用——跟凶手一样——他要找的是内维尔·香农为那桩案子留下的更多笔记。看看屋里翻得底朝天的那个样子。看看钱原封不动这一事实。正常的窃贼可不会给人那种印象。”

“是的，不会。”克林顿爵士赞同道，“不过我可不上你套话的钩。接着猜吧，乡绅；猜砸了，你还可以改行去揣度或者悬想，换换营生。动脑筋能锻炼脑子，所以到头来你其实并不吃亏。”

===Original===
“You’re an exasperating beast at times, Clinton,” Wendover affirmed, without a trace of irritation.

“If that’s the first result of thinking, I don’t think I’d take it up as a hobby,” Sir Clinton responded cheerfully. “It might lead to peevishness among the neighbours.”

He walked over to the window, possibly to conceal his expression, before communicating his next piece of information.

“I had time to drop in on your friend Ardsley, too, on my way home.”

===Chinese===
“你这家伙，有时候真叫人冒火，克林顿。”温多弗断言道，语气里却没有一丝火气。

“要是这就是动脑子的第一样成果，我看这爱好还是不沾为妙。”克林顿爵士轻快地应道，“闹得四邻都暴躁起来，可不是玩的。”

他走到窗前，也许是为了掩住脸上的表情，然后才透出他的下一条消息。

“回家路上，我还抽空去看了你的朋友阿德斯利。”

===Original===
Wendover rose to the bait at once.

“Oh, indeed! I hope he showed you his best specimens; a pithed frog, perhaps, or a mangled dog? It’s no good lifting these eyebrows of yours, Clinton. I don’t like the fellow.”

“One could almost guess it from the way you talk. But bear in mind, Squire, that even the meanest of God’s creatures may have its uses. I’ve got a use for Ardsley,” he added carelessly, “so don’t go making things too unpleasant if you come across him any time.”

Wendover gave a half-suppressed growl.

“One rubs up against a lot of queer fish when one begins mixing with the police, it seems,” he complained, half in fun and half in earnest.

===Chinese===
温多弗立刻上了钩。

“哦，当真！我希望他把他那些最好的标本给你看了；一只穿刺毁脑的青蛙，也许，再不然一条血肉模糊的狗？你冲我扬这两道眉毛没用，克林顿。我不喜欢那家伙。”

“听你这口气，差不多也猜得出来。不过记住，乡绅，哪怕是上帝造的最卑贱的生灵，也自有它的用处。阿德斯利，我正有用他之处。”他漫不经心地补了一句，“所以往后什么时候撞见他，可别把事情弄得太不愉快。”

温多弗半压着嗓子哼了一声。

“看样子，人一跟警察这路人打上交道，就免不了撞见许多怪鱼。”他半玩笑半当真地抱怨道。

===Original===
Before Sir Clinton could reply, the bell of the telephone rang sharply.

“Bet you nine to four that’s Whistlefield ringing up,” the Chief Constable offered. “Here, I’ll go myself.”

He left the room and Wendover waited uneasily for the result of the conversation. It took a minute or two and he knew from this that it must be something relating to Whistlefield, for Sir Clinton had no friends in the neighbourhood. When the Chief Constable returned, Wendover looked up with a certain foreboding. News from Whistlefield of late had never been encouraging; and he feared that something more might have happened.

“Did you take that bet?” inquired Sir Clinton. “If so, you owe me a note or two. It was Whistlefield at the other end of the line, just as I expected. If this goes on, we may as well tell the Exchange to leave their plug in our hole permanently and save bother to all hands.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士还没来得及答话，电话铃就尖锐地响了起来。

“跟你打赌，九对四，准是惠斯特菲尔德打来的。”郡警察局长开口道，“来，我自己去接。”

他出了房间，温多弗忐忑地等着这番通话的结果。通话占了一两分钟，他从这一点就知道准是惠斯特菲尔德的事，因为克林顿爵士在这一带没有朋友。郡警察局长回来时，温多弗怀着几分不祥的预感抬起眼来。近来从惠斯特菲尔德传来的消息，就没有一条让人宽过心；他怕的是又出了什么事。

“那个赌你下注了没有？”克林顿爵士问道，“要是下了，你该付我一两张钞票了。电话那头正是惠斯特菲尔德，跟我料的一点不差。再这么下去，我们不如干脆叫电话局把插头永久插在我们的插孔里，省得大家费事。”

===Original===
“What’s happened now?” demanded Wendover anxiously.

“An attempted murder this time. Your friend Ernest rang up to tell me about it. They’ve tried to get him next; but he fled like a lamb from the slaughter and seems to have saved his bacon. But he’s in a pitiable state,” Sir Clinton went on, a tingle of contempt coming into his tone. “Quite blue with funk, I should judge. He nearly wept into the mouthpiece, and I could hear him gasping for breath at the other end of the wire. Quite a shock to his nerves, it appears. We’ll have to go across and comfort him. Come along.”

“You don’t seem much worried over his troubles,” Wendover commented.

“I’ve no great use for a cowardly little beast. You should have heard him on the phone, Wendover. Sounded like one of those things they used to run in the Grand Guignol.”

===Chinese===
“这回又出了什么事？”温多弗焦灼地追问。

“这回是一桩谋杀未遂。你的朋友欧内斯特打来电话告诉我的。他们下一个要收拾的就是他；不过他像逃出屠刀的羔羊一般逃了，看来是保住了性命。可他眼下那副样子可怜得很。”克林顿爵士接着往下说，语气里透进一丝轻蔑，“依我看，是吓得面无人色了。他差点儿对着话筒哭出来，电话线那头直喘粗气，我在这头都听得见。看样子，对他的神经是一大刺激。我们只好过去安慰安慰他了。走吧。”

“他的这番遭难，你好像不怎么放在心上嘛。”温多弗评论道。

“胆小如鼠的小畜生，我实在用不大上。你真该听听他在电话里那副腔调，温多弗。活像大木偶剧场（Grand Guignol）里从前常演的那些戏码。”

===Original===
“Even the meanest of God’s creatures may have its uses,” Wendover quoted, sarcastically.

Sir Clinton’s temporary cheerfulness seemed to have passed away.

“That’s a true word spoken in jest, no doubt. You’re perhaps right after all. We may find a use for even friend Ernest before we’re done. But on the face of it, it doesn’t look probable, does it?”

When they reached Whistlefield they were shown at once into the study where they found Ernest in a state of nervous collapse. A syphon and a decanter stood on a tray at his elbow; and the moving surface of the whisky showed that he had just finished pouring out a drink. As they came in, he poured some more liquor into his empty tumbler.

===Chinese===
“哪怕是上帝造的最卑贱的生灵，也自有它的用处。”温多弗讥诮地引了一句。

克林顿爵士那一阵暂时的轻快劲儿似乎消散了。

“玩笑话里出真言，这话不假。也许你到底是对的。案子了结之前，就连欧内斯特朋友，我们说不定也能派上用场。不过就表面看来，这可能性实在不大，是不是？”

到了惠斯特菲尔德，两人立刻被引进书房，只见欧内斯特已是神经崩溃的一副惨状。他手边的托盘上立着一只苏打水瓶和一只玻璃酒瓶；威士忌晃动未定的酒面表明，他刚斟过一杯。两人进来时，他正往空掉的平底酒杯里再添一些酒。

===Original===
“I think I’d leave it at that, Mr. Shandon,” Sir Clinton suggested, coolly. “We’d better not run any risk of your memory getting confused.”

Ernest took his hand away from the tumbler obediently. Wendover could see that he was trembling, and he seemed to be in a condition bordering on panic.

“Now, let’s have the story as briefly as possible, if you please,” Sir Clinton requested.

===Chinese===
“我看就到此为止吧，香农先生。”克林顿爵士冷冷地说，“最好别冒任何风险，把你的记性搅糊涂了。”

欧内斯特乖乖地把伸向酒杯的手缩了回去。温多弗看得出他在发抖，他那副光景已近乎惊惶失措。

“好了，请你把事情尽量简短地讲给我们听。”克林顿爵士要求道。

===Original===
Ernest looked helplessly round the room for a moment.

“I can hardly believe I’m safe,” he explained. “I’ve had such a time, such a time. Dreadful!”

“Yes, tell us about it.”

“After dinner, I thought I’d go down and have a look at the Maze,” Ernest continued. “I hadn’t been there, you know, since the affair happened; and I thought I might as well go down and look round the place. I wish I’d never had the idea. Such a time I’ve had.”

===Chinese===
欧内斯特束手无策地朝屋里四下望了片刻。

“我简直不敢相信自己脱了险。”他解释道，“我遭了多大一场罪啊，多大一场。可怕极了！”

“是吗，讲给我们听听。”

“晚饭之后，我想下楼去迷宫看看。”欧内斯特接着说，“你知道，出事之后我就没再去过那儿；我想不如下去到那地方转一转。真希望我从没起过这个念头。我遭了多大一场罪啊。”

===Original===
His eyeglasses slipped askew on his nose and he laboriously set them right before continuing.

“Damn these things! I must get a new pair. They’re always dropping off.”

“Yes?” Sir Clinton repeated, patiently. All his levity had vanished, Wendover noticed, now that he had come to real business.

“After lunch I thought I’d go down to the Maze; but it seemed a lot of trouble, going all that distance; and I very nearly gave up the idea. I wish I had. But then I thought of the push-bike I keep in the garage. It would be easy enough to pedal down on it. So I got it out and went off by the road that leads to the East Gate.”

===Chinese===
他的夹鼻眼镜在鼻子上滑歪了，他费劲地把它扶正，才接着往下说。

“这玩意儿真该死！我必须换一副新的。它们老是往下掉。”

“是吗？”克林顿爵士耐心地重复道。温多弗注意到，一旦办起正事，他那一身轻浮之气都消失净尽了。

“午饭之后，我想下楼去迷宫；可是要走那么远一段路，似乎太费事；我差一点儿就打消了这个念头。真希望我当时打消了。可这时候，我想起了我搁在车库里的那辆自行车。骑着它蹬下去，容易得很。于是我把它推出来，顺着通往东门的那条路去了。”

===Original===
He put out his hand tentatively towards the tumbler, but drew it back again at the sight of Sir Clinton’s frown. He looked like an overgrown baby caught in the act of mischief.

“Yes?” Sir Clinton repeated once more.

“I went into the Maze, you know, never thinking that anything could possibly happen there. I never dreamed of anything happening, you understand? And I walked through it to Helen’s Bower—the place where my brother Roger was murdered, you remember? And when I got there I sat down. I’d come a good way, you see. And I felt that I’d like to sit down.”

===Chinese===
他试探着朝酒杯伸出手去，可一看见克林顿爵士皱起的眉头，又把手缩了回来。他那模样，活像个干着恶作剧被当场拿住的大个儿婴孩。

“是吗？”克林顿爵士又重复了一遍。

“我走进了迷宫，你知道，压根儿没想到那里还可能出什么事。我做梦也没梦到会出事，你明白吗？我一路穿行过去，到了海伦亭——我兄弟罗杰遇害的那个地方，你记得吧？到了那儿，我就坐下了。我走了好一大段路，你瞧。我觉得很想坐一坐。”

===Original===
“Did you see anyone in or near the Maze up to that moment?” asked Sir Clinton.

Ernest pondered for a moment or two. His trepidation, far from brightening him, seemed to have made him look duller than ever.

“No,” he said, hesitatingly at last, “I can’t say I did. I don’t remember seeing anyone.”

“And then?”

===Chinese===
“到那一刻为止，你在迷宫里头或是迷宫附近，见过什么人吗？”克林顿爵士问。

欧内斯特寻思了一两秒。他那场惊惧，非但没让他精神起来，似乎反倒叫他比往常更呆了。

“没有。”他末了迟疑地说，“我不能说见过。我不记得看见过什么人。”

“后来呢？”

===Original===
“Where was I? Oh, yes, I sat down. It was rather hot; and I thought I’d like a seat. I meant to sit there and smoke a cigar before looking round the Maze. I sat there for a while, I don’t quite know how long. Some time, at least. And then I may have fallen into a doze. The sun was very hot, even when I was in the shade of the hedge, you understand? It makes you sleepy. I suppose I dozed off. Perhaps I was asleep for quite a while.”

“You can’t give me anything more exact than that, can you, Mr. Shandon?”

“No, I’m afraid I can’t. It was quite a while, though, I feel pretty sure of that.”

He put out his hand towards the tumbler again.

“I really think I’d get on better if I had another drink.”

===Chinese===
“我讲到哪儿了？哦，对，我坐下了。天相当热；我想找个座儿坐坐。我本打算先坐在那儿抽一支雪茄，再把迷宫逛上一逛。我坐了一阵，坐了多久我说不太清。至少有些时候吧。再后来，我也许是打起了盹儿。太阳很毒，哪怕我坐在树篱的荫凉里也一样，你明白吗？那叫人犯困。我大概是迷糊过去了。也许我睡了相当一大阵子。”

“比这更确切一点的说法，你给不了我了，是吧，香农先生？”

“是啊，恐怕给不了。不过那确是不短的一段工夫，这一点我挺有把握。”

他又朝酒杯伸出手去。

“我真觉得，再喝上一杯，我就能讲得顺当些。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton looked at him with unconcealed distaste. Then he picked up the tumbler himself.

“Two fingers then.”

He went across to the window and poured away the surplus from Ernest’s generously filled glass.

“Now, come along, Mr. Shandon. The sooner we get your story the sooner I can get to work. You must pull yourself together.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士毫不掩饰厌恶地看了他一眼。然后他亲自拿起了那只酒杯。

“那就两指高。”

他走到窗前，把欧内斯特那一杯斟得慷慨满满的酒里，多余的都倒掉了。

“好了，快些吧，香农先生。你的故事讲得越快，我就能越早上手办事。你必须振作起来。”

===Original===
Ernest Shandon drank off his whiskey neat and then gave a sigh of relief.

“I feel better now. I’ve really had a terrible time! Where was I? Oh, yes, I woke up.”

“Thrilling!” said Sir Clinton, brutally. “And what next?”

Wendover could not help seeing that Sir Clinton’s temper was wearing thin under the strain of listening to this outpouring of rambling narrative. And this time there was no Stenness who could be turned on to complete the tale. They were dependent entirely on the terror-stricken creature before them.

===Chinese===
欧内斯特·香农把那杯不掺水的威士忌一饮而尽，随即如释重负地舒了一口长气。

“现在我觉得好些了。我真是遭了一场大难！我讲到哪儿了？哦，对，我醒了。”

“真够惊心动魄的！”克林顿爵士毫不留情地说，“接下来呢？”

温多弗不由得看出，听着这一番东拉西扯的滔滔叙述，克林顿爵士的耐性正被磨得越来越薄。而这一回，再没有一个斯滕内斯可以接手把话讲完了。他们全得仰仗眼前这个魂飞魄散的家伙。

===Original===
“I woke up,” Ernest repeated, staring at them with wide-opened eyes as though chronicling some vast convulsion of Nature. “And just after I woke up I seemed to hear steps somewhere near me. I wasn’t very wide awake, you understand? and I sat listening for a moment or two—or it may have been for a little longer than that,” he added with an evident effort at exactitude. “And I thought to myself it might be young Torrance or Stenness. It couldn’t have been the girls, you see? because they had taken the car and gone off to do some shopping in Ambledown. I know that, because they said they were going there and I wondered why they didn’t go to Stanningleigh, which is nearer. But I suppose they wanted to go to some special shop in Ambledown. There are better shops in Ambledown …”

A glimpse of the expression on Sir Clinton’s face brought him suddenly back to his direct narrative.

“So I called out: ‘Who’s there?’ just like that, you know. But nobody replied. So I was wondering who it could be; and I was just going to call again when suddenly I heard the noise of an airgun going off; and something whizzed past me as close as that!”

He indicated a track almost grazing his cheek.

===Chinese===
“我醒了。”欧内斯特重复道，瞪圆了眼睛望着他们，仿佛在记述某场天崩地裂的大灾变，“我刚醒过来不多时，就好像听见附近什么地方有脚步声。我还不十分清醒，你明白吗？我坐着听了那么一两秒——也许比那还要稍长一点儿。”他补了这么一句，显然下了番求准确的工夫。“我心里想，这也许是小托伦斯，要不就是斯滕内斯。不可能是姑娘们，你瞧，因为她们开了车到安布尔当（Ambledown）买东西去了。这个我知道，因为她们说过要去那儿，我当时还纳闷，她们为什么不去更近的斯坦宁利。可我猜，她们是想上安布尔当哪家特别的铺子。安布尔当的铺子好些……”

瞥见克林顿爵士脸上的神情，他猛地回到正题上来了。

“于是我喊了一声：‘谁在那儿？’就是这么喊的，你知道。可是没人应声。我正纳闷会是谁呢；正要再喊一声，忽然听见一支气枪发射的声响；有个东西贴着我飞了过去，就差那么一点儿！”

他比划出一条几乎擦着他面颊的路线。

===Original===
“I jumped up. I didn’t wait to hear any more. I can take the right decision as quick as most people, I assure you, Sir Clinton. I ran as fast as I could to the entrance, and then I heard the fellow reloading the gun! It was dreadful! My blood didn’t freeze or anything like that, but I suffered agonies—agonies!”

“Quite so,” said Sir Clinton soothingly. “You were in a blue funk. We quite understand. An alarming situation. And what happened after that?”

“I ran out into the Maze. Luckily I’d spotted where the fellow was. He was at the same loophole that he’d used when he killed Roger. Oh, I had all my wits about me; I was really very cool, considering the state of affairs.”

“And then?”

“Then I ran through the Maze as hard as I could. Such a time! Fancy having the fellow after me with those darts!”

===Chinese===
“我跳了起来。我没等着再听下文。下正确的决断，我的速度不输大多数人，我向你保证，克林顿爵士。我拼了命朝入口跑，就在那时候，我听见那家伙在给枪装弹！可怕极了！我的血倒没有凝固，也没那一类的事，可我受了大罪——大罪呀！”

“正是如此。”克林顿爵士抚慰道，“你是吓破了胆。我们十分理解。形势确实吓人。那之后又怎样了？”

“我跑出了迷宫。幸亏我看出了那家伙待的地方。他就在他杀罗杰时所用的那同一个暗孔那里。哦，我当时脑筋齐全得很；考虑到当时的局面，我实在是非常镇定的。”

“然后呢？”

“然后我拼尽全力穿过了迷宫。好一场折腾！想想吧，那家伙带着那些飞镖在我后面追！”

===Original===
“He followed you, then?”

“It would be what he *would* do, wouldn’t it?”

“You mean that you didn’t actually hear him?”

“No, I didn’t hear him. I didn’t wait to hear anything. I was so busy getting out of the Maze. Of course, I know the Maze well, but it’s difficult to keep your head in a case like that, very difficult. But I did it,” he ended proudly. “I got away from the fellow. Never as much as saw him.”

His glasses slipped off again in the excitement of his peroration; and he adjusted them painfully.

===Chinese===
“这么说，他追你了？”

“那正是他*会*干的那种事，不是吗？”

“你的意思是，你其实并没有听见他？”

“没有，我没听见他。我没停下来听任何动静。我光顾着逃出迷宫了。当然，迷宫我很熟，可到了那种关头，要保持头脑清醒不容易，非常不容易。可我做到了。”他颇为自豪地收了尾，“我甩掉了那家伙。从头到尾连他的影儿都没见着。”

讲到收尾的激动处，他的眼镜又滑了下来；他吃力地把它们扶正。

===Original===
“These things do give one a lot of bother,” he complained. “I expect it’s the perspiration on my nose, with all that running. I haven’t run for years.”

“You got out of the Maze safely, then. What next?”

“I got on to my bicycle and rode away as hard as I could. What a blessing I had that machine there, eh? If I hadn’t, he might have run me down in the open quite easily. I was quite out of breath.”

“Then?”

===Chinese===
“这东西真给人添不少麻烦。”他抱怨道，“我估摸是跑了那一阵，鼻子上出的汗闹的。我好几年没跑过步了。”

“这么说，你安全出了迷宫。接下来呢？”

“我跨上自行车，拼了命地骑走了。幸亏那辆车就在那儿，真是天大的福气，你说是不是？要不是有它，他在空旷地里撵上我，容易得很。我都完全喘不上气了。”

“然后呢？”

===Original===
“Then I went to the telephone and rang you up at the Grange. I had an idea you’d be there. If you hadn’t been, I’d have tried the police-station.”

“Quite right, Mr. Shandon. Now there are one or two points I must have cleared up. First of all, it seems you met no one either going to the Maze or coming back from it. Did you shout or call for assistance at all on your way to the house?”

“I couldn’t,” Ernest admitted, simply. “I hadn’t any breath left to shout with. You don’t understand what it was like, I assure you.”

“Was there no one about?”

===Chinese===
“然后我就奔了电话机，往塔尔加斯农庄给你打了电话。我料想你会在那儿。你要是不在，我就试警察局了。”

“完全正确，香农先生。现在有一两处疑点，我必须弄清楚。首先，去迷宫和从迷宫回来的路上，你似乎谁也没碰见。回宅子的路上，你喊过人或者呼过救吗？”

“喊不了。”欧内斯特直截了当地承认，“我连喊的气儿都没剩下。那是什么滋味，你不明白，我向你保证。”

“周围一个人也没有？”

===Original===
“No,” Ernest answered after a pause. “Arthur had gone off somewhere. He generally seems to go off by himself, quite often one doesn’t see him for hours. I don’t know where he was. And Torrance was out of the road, too. I can’t tell you where he was. Perhaps he’d walked over to Stanningleigh. Or perhaps he’d gone somewhere else. I haven’t seen him since lunchtime.”

“And Mr. Stenness?”

To Wendover’s surprise the sound of Stenness’s name seemed to galvanise Ernest. His terror appeared to increase again, just when it had seemed to be dying down.

“Stenness!” he repeated. “Oh, Stenness …”

He broke off short, as though afraid he had been heard.

===Chinese===
“没有。”欧内斯特顿了一顿才回答，“阿瑟到什么地方去了。他好像总是一个人独自出去，常常好几个钟头见不着人影。我不知道他在哪儿。托伦斯也不在跟前。他在哪儿，我说不上。也许是步行去斯坦宁利了。也许去了别的什么地方。午饭以后我就没见过他。”

“那么斯滕内斯先生呢？”

让温多弗吃惊的是，斯滕内斯这个名字一出口，就像通了电流似的，把欧内斯特击了一下。他的恐惧本来看着正要平息，这时似乎又重新高涨起来。

“斯滕内斯！”他重复道，“哦，斯滕内斯……”

他猛地住了口，像是怕被人听了去。

===Original===
“Just a moment,” he muttered, and rose from his chair.

Wendover could see that the man’s knees were trembling. Ernest walked across to the door, opened it gently, and peered out with a caution which had in it a touch of the ludicrous.

“Nobody there,” he explained, as he came back again. “You never know.”

“What’s behind this, Mr. Shandon?” Sir Clinton demanded, impatiently. “If you’ve any information, it’s your duty to give it to me at once. Have you anything to tell me about Mr. Stenness?”

Ernest made a gesture, appealing piteously for a lowering of Sir Clinton’s voice.

===Chinese===
“等一下。”他咕哝了一声，从椅子上站了起来。

温多弗看得出，这人两条腿正在打颤。欧内斯特走到门口，轻轻把门打开，探头向外张望，那副小心劲儿里透着几分滑稽。

“外头没人。”他走回来时解释道，“这种事说不好，防着点总没错。”

“这背后有什么文章，香农先生？”克林顿爵士不耐烦地诘问道，“你若是掌握什么情况，立刻报告给我是你的义务。关于斯滕内斯先生，你有什么要告诉我的吗？”

欧内斯特做了个手势，可怜巴巴地恳求克林顿爵士把声音放低一些。

===Original===
“You remember,” he continued, almost in a whisper, “that the other night—I mean last night, the night of the burglary—I was going through Roger’s papers. I think I told you before that I was doing that, didn’t I? And amongst his papers I came across his chequebooks and some stubs. I was looking through these, just to see what things he’d been spending money on—the firms he’d been dealing with, and so forth, you understand? And quite by accident, I noticed something funny. The counterfoil of the last missing cheque had been cut out of the book. I’d never have noticed it if it hadn’t been that I was looking at the numbers. It was cut away very carefully, very neatly indeed, you know. But there was the counterfoil for the cheque before it numbered something like 60072 and the next one in the book was numbered 60074 or some figures like these. There was a missing number in the series. And there was another funny thing. I happened to look at the last bundle of returned cheques in Roger’s drawer. He hadn’t destroyed them, it seems, for some reason or other. I can’t think why, myself. But there they were. And one of them was missing from the series.”

===Chinese===
“你记得吧，”他几乎用耳语接着说，“前几天夜里——我是说昨天夜里，失窃的那天夜里——我在翻检罗杰的文件。我想我先前跟你说过我在干那个，说过吧？在他的文件中间，我碰上了他的几册支票簿和一些存根。我翻看这些，只是想瞧瞧他把钱都花在了什么上头——他打交道的都是哪些商号，等等，你明白吗？纯属偶然，我注意到一桩怪事。最后那张缺失支票的存根，被人从簿子上剪掉了。要不是我正好在看编号，我根本不会察觉。剪得非常仔细，确实非常齐整，你知道。它前面那张支票的存根，编号像是 60072，而簿子里下一张的编号是 60074，或是类似这样的数字。这一串编号里缺了一个号。还有另一桩怪事。我恰好看了罗杰抽屉里最后一捆银行退回的支票。看样子，他出于这样那样的缘故没有销毁它们。我自己是想不出为什么。可它们就在那儿。那一串里面也缺了一张。”

===Original===
“There’s nothing mysterious in that,” Wendover objected. “It may have been a cheque that went abroad, and hasn’t been returned to the bank yet. Your brother had interests overseas.”

Ernest’s dull eyes brightened slightly in triumph.

“That’s where you’re wrong, Wendover. That’s a mistake. I was curious about the thing, it seemed to me so funny. So I looked up the counterfoil of that missing cheque in Roger’s stub; and it was a cheque for some hundreds and it was made payable to his stockbrokers. That seemed funnier than ever, didn’t it? A cheque like that would go right back to the bank with no delay. It would be paid in immediately, I’m sure it would. Wouldn’t it? Of course, sure to be, you know?”

Sir Clinton had been following this with keen interest.

===Chinese===
“这里头没什么神秘可言。”温多弗反驳道，“那也许是一张寄往国外的支票，还没退回银行来。你兄弟在海外有产业。”

欧内斯特那双呆滞的眼睛因得意而微微亮了一下。

“这正是你说错的地方，温多弗。错了。我好奇心起来了，这事在我看来太古怪了。于是我到罗杰的存根里去查那张缺失支票的存根；那是一张好几百镑的支票，抬头开付给他的证券经纪人。这就比先前更古怪了，是不是？那样的支票会毫无耽搁地直接回到银行。立刻就会缴进账去，我敢肯定。是不是？当然是肯定的，你知道吧？”

克林顿爵士一直怀着强烈的兴味听着这一段。

===Original===
“And where does Mr. Stenness come in?” he asked. Ernest looked round the room again as though he feared that Stenness might be concealed somewhere.

“Well,” he said, reluctantly, “Stenness had access to Roger’s papers. He could have got at this chequebook, I’m sure. Roger was a bit careless, sometimes. I’ve seen his chequebook lying about on the table often. I remember I saw it last Tuesday, was it? Or was it Wednesday? It was in the morning, I know that.”

Sir Clinton’s face showed uncommon interest now.

“And you think … ?” he prompted.

===Chinese===
“那么，斯滕内斯先生是从哪儿插进来的呢？”他问道。欧内斯特又朝屋里四下张望，像是怕斯滕内斯就藏在什么地方。

“这个嘛，”他不情愿地说，“斯滕内斯能接触到罗杰的文件。这本支票簿他是够得着的，我敢肯定。罗杰有时候有点儿马虎。我常常见他的支票簿撂在桌子上。我记得上星期二见过它——是星期二吧？还是星期三？是上午，这一点我知道。”

这时，克林顿爵士的脸上显出了异乎寻常的兴味。

“那么你想的是……？”他提示道。

===Original===
Ernest poured out another stiff glass of whiskey, this time unchecked by anyone.

“I can’t say I think anything, really. I shouldn’t like to go so far as that, you understand. That might be going too far. But I let slip to you that I’d found something funny amongst the cheques, last night. I mean I told you this morning what I’d found last night. Or rather …”

“I understand,” said Sir Clinton, rescuing him from his tangle. “And … ?”

“And Stenness was there when I mentioned it. He knew I’d found some hanky-panky.”

===Chinese===
欧内斯特又斟了满满一杯威士忌，这一回谁也没有拦他。

“真的，我说不上自己想了些什么。我不愿把话说到那一步，你明白吗。那也许就说过头了。可是，我把话对你漏了出来，说我在那些支票里发现了蹊跷——那是我昨天夜里发现的。我是说，今天早晨我把昨夜发现的东西对你讲了。或者不如说……”

“我明白了。”克林顿爵士说，把他从一团乱麻里解救出来，“还有呢……？”

“还有，我提这件事的时候，斯滕内斯就在场。他知道我发现了些猫腻。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton leaned back in his chair and thought for a moment or two.

“I see what’s in your mind, Mr. Shandon,” he said at length. “Well, that can be put straight easily enough. So long as you were the only person who knew of this affair, you might be a danger to the fellow who was responsible for what you call the hanky-panky. It might be worth his while to put you out of the way—silence you, eh? and cover the business up.”

Ernest’s starting eyes showed that he had no liking for such plain discourse.

“Then,” continued Sir Clinton, “the remedy’s simple. Just tell whoever it is—we needn’t drag in names, need we?—that you’ve mentioned the matter to me. Then there will be no point in disturbing you further, you see? You’ll be quite safe, once you’ve done that. Doubly safe, in fact, for any further attack on you would be a bit too suspicious. That’s your best course.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士往椅背上一靠，想了一两秒。

“你心里打的什么算盘，我明白了，香农先生。”他终于开口，“唔，这事轻易就能料理妥帖。只要你还是唯一知道这桩事的人，对那个该对你所谓的猫腻负责的家伙来说，你也许就是个危险。把你除掉——叫你永远闭嘴，嗯？再把这摊事掩盖过去，对他或许是划算的。”

欧内斯特那双惊得凸出的眼睛表明，这样露骨的谈话，他可爱不了。

“那么，”克林顿爵士接着说，“对策很简单。只消告诉那一位——我们不必把名字扯进来，对吧？——就说你已经把这件事对我说了。这样一来，再惊扰你就没有任何好处了，你明白吗？这事一办，你就相当安全了。实际上是加倍安全，因为再朝你动手一次，就未免太招人疑心。这是你的上策。”

===Original===
“I never thought of that,” said Ernest, gratefully. “It’s a relief, I can tell you. Such a relief! And you think there’ll be no chance of another attack on me?”

“I’d take almost any odds against it,” Sir Clinton reassured him.

“Well, I shall stay inside the house altogether for a week or two, at any rate,” Ernest decided, his fears returning suddenly. “That ought to be safe enough.”

He applied himself again to the decanter.

===Chinese===
“这一层我可想没想到。”欧内斯特感激地说，“这真是叫人松了口气，我跟你说。太让人宽心了！那么你认为，不会再有人袭击我了？”

“这种事，几乎什么赔率我都肯押它不会发生。”克林顿爵士宽慰他道。

“唔，不管怎么说，往后一两个星期，我索性足不出户，就待在宅子里。”欧内斯特拿定了主意，恐惧又突然涌了上来，“那样总该够安全了。”

他又埋头伺候起那只酒瓶来。

===Original===
Sir Clinton had one last question to put.

“Where was Stenness while you were down at the Maze?”

Ernest stood with his tumbler arrested on the road to his mouth while he pondered over the matter.

“I don’t know,” he admitted finally. “I really can’t say. I left him here, working at Roger’s papers; and I told him I was going to Helen’s Bower. But when I came back again he wasn’t here. He’d put the papers away. I don’t know where he’d gone.”

“Ah, indeed?” said Sir Clinton ruminatively. But he made no further comment.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士还有最后一个问题要问。

“你在下面迷宫里的时候，斯滕内斯人在哪儿？”

欧内斯特端着酒杯站在那里，杯子举到半路停住，一面琢磨这个问题。

“我不知道。”他末了承认道，“我实在说不上。我离开他的时候，他正在这儿料理罗杰的文件；我还告诉了他，我要去海伦亭。可等我回来，他就不在这儿了。文件他已经收起来了。他去了哪儿，我不知道。”

“啊，是吗？”克林顿爵士若有所思地说。但他没有再作评论。

## XI The Squire’s Theories / 十一 乡绅的理论

===Original===
“We’ll have another look at the Maze, Squire, if you don’t mind stopping there.”
Wendover nodded. He had expected the suggestion.
“You didn’t seem to overflow with sympathy for Shandon,” he commented.
“Friend Ernest raises my gorge,” admitted Sir Clinton frankly. “Did you ever see a man in such a state? I never could stand that sort of thing.”
Then, as though he felt he had been too hard on Ernest, he added perfunctorily:
“Of course, he’d had rather a bad half hour of it.”

===Chinese===
“咱们再到迷宫里看上一眼，乡绅，要是你不介意在那儿停一停车。”
温多弗点了点头。他早料到会有这个提议。
“你方才对香农可没显出多少同情。”他评论道。
“咱们的朋友欧内斯特叫我反胃。”克林顿爵士坦率地承认，“你可曾见过谁那副德行？这种人我从来就受不了。”
随后，他像是觉得方才对欧内斯特太狠了些，又敷衍地补了一句：
“当然，那半个钟头他也着实不好受。”

===Original===
“I admire the restraint of your language,” said Wendover with a smile. “But, you know, Clinton, I think you’re a bit hard on the beggar. What could he do but run? I’d have run myself, and I make no bones about it either.”
“Oh, so would I,” Sir Clinton conceded carelessly. “It wasn’t the running that put my back up.”
“You mean that there’s running *and* running, so to speak?”
“Exactly. Look at the case of that girl who was in the Maze when the murders were done—Miss Forrest, I mean. She had just as much right as Ernest to get hysterical. I won’t say she was as cool as a cucumber when we saw her; one couldn’t expect that. But she kept her nerves in order. She didn’t arrive at the house afterwards in a state of whimpering panic.”
“No, that’s true,” Wendover confirmed. “She’s worth a dozen of Ernest Shandon at a pinch, that girl. She kept her head and did exactly what was wanted.”
“Quite so. She wasn’t thinking of her own skin all the time like friend Ernest.”

===Chinese===
“我真佩服你措辞的克制。”温多弗含笑说，“可是你知道，克林顿，我觉得你对这可怜的家伙有点儿狠。他除了跑还能怎么办？换了是我，我也会跑，而且我毫不讳言。”
“哦，我也会。”克林顿爵士漫不经心地让步，“叫我上火的可不是他跑。”
“你的意思是，跑跟跑*大有*分别，可以这么说？”
“正是。看看命案发生时迷宫里那个姑娘的做派——我说的是福雷斯特小姐。论歇斯底里的资格，她一点也不比欧内斯特少。我不能说我们见到她时她泰然自若；那也不能指望。可她把神经管得服服帖帖。事后她回到宅子里，也不是一副呜呜咽咽、魂飞魄散的样子。”
“是啊，这话不假。”温多弗确认道，“紧要关头，那姑娘一个顶得上十来个欧内斯特·香农。她保持着头脑清醒，该做的做得一点不差。”
“正是如此。她可不像咱们的朋友欧内斯特那样，时时刻刻只顾着自己那身皮。”

===Original===
“What’s all this about Stenness?” Wendover demanded. “Is it merely some rot that Ernest’s squirted out in the middle of his funk, or is there anything in it?”
“Here’s the Maze,” Sir Clinton interrupted, cutting him short. “Suppose we postpone discussion till after dinner tonight, Squire. I don’t want to be distracted for the next few minutes if you don’t mind.”
They entered the Maze and made their way towards Helen’s Bower. Near the door into it Wendover stopped suddenly and pointed to the pathway at their feet.
“Hullo! Look, Clinton! There’s a bit of black thread lying on the ground.”

===Chinese===
“斯滕内斯那些事到底是怎么回事？”温多弗质问道，“是欧内斯特吓破了胆、当口喷出来的一通浑话呢，还是里头真有点名堂？”
“到迷宫了。”克林顿爵士插进来，截住他的话头，“咱们把讨论推迟到今晚晚饭之后吧，乡绅。你要是不介意，接下来这几分钟我不想分神。”
两人进了迷宫，一路走向海伦亭。快到亭门口时，温多弗猛地站住，指着脚下的甬道。
“喂！瞧，克林顿！地上躺着一段黑线。”

===Original===
They stooped over it and examined the fibre.
“Ordinary sewing-silk off a reel, obviously,” was all that Sir Clinton vouchsafed.
Wendover thought he had seen more in the matter.
“Don’t you see what it is, Clinton? Ariadne’s clue! It’s a thread that the murderer must have been using to find his way out of the Maze in a hurry.”

===Chinese===
两人俯下身去，察看那根纤维。
“明摆着是线轴上退下来的普通缝衣丝线。”克林顿爵士肯说的只有这一句。
温多弗觉得自己在这上头看出了更多的东西。
“你难道看不出它是什么吗，克林顿？阿里阿德涅（Ariadne）的引线！这一定是凶手用过的线，他准是靠它才能匆匆忙忙找出迷宫的路。”

===Original===
“I showed you before that there’s no difficulty in the Maze if you’ve once been taken to the centre.”
Wendover had his answer in readiness.
“Yes. But suppose you were the murderer, you would have to get out in a hurry, wouldn’t you? And you might lose your head. Anybody might get confused in the flurry. So he took the precaution of laying the thread to the exit; and all he had to do was to follow it and reel it up as he went. And this time a bit of it caught somewhere—see, this end’s tangled in the hedge—and so broke off and he had to leave it behind. When the Shandons were murdered he probably managed to reel up the whole of it and so left no trace behind him.”

===Chinese===
“我从前演示给你看过：只要被人领到过中心一次，走迷宫就毫无难处。”
温多弗早备好了答话。
“是没错。可你倒假设自己是凶手：你不得不赶紧脱身，对不对？你也可能慌了神。那样一阵忙乱里，谁都可能转糊涂。于是他预先留了这一手，把线一路铺到出口；往后他只消顺着线走，边走边把线绕回轴上。而这一次，有一小段在哪里挂住了——瞧，这头还缠在树篱上——于是挣断了，他只好丢下它。香农兄弟俩被害那回，他多半把整根线都收了回去，所以身后没留下一点痕迹。”

===Original===
“Sounds plausible,” Sir Clinton commented curtly. “We may as well collect the specimen, though really there’s nothing distinctive about it. One bit of thread’s very much like another.”
“Sherlock Holmes might have made more out of it than that,” said Wendover, rather resentful at the way his discovery had been treated.
“Doubtless. But as he isn’t here, what can we do? Just bumble along to the best of our poor abilities. That’s what I’m doing, Squire.”
They entered the tiny enclosure of Helen’s Bower, and Wendover’s eye was at once caught by a sparkle from the grass near one of the chairs. He stepped across and picked up a silver cigar-case. Sir Clinton held out his hand for it and glanced at the outside.

===Chinese===
“听着倒是入情入理。”克林顿爵士简短地评了一句，“这个标本咱们不妨收起来，虽说它实在没什么特别之处。线头跟线头，彼此都差不离。”
“夏洛克·福尔摩斯（Sherlock Holmes）或许能从它里头榨出比这更多的名堂。”温多弗说，自己的发现受了这等冷遇，他颇有几分不忿。
“多半是。可他既然不在这儿，咱们有什么办法？只好凭咱们这点可怜的本事跌跌撞撞往前摸。我就是这么干的，乡绅。”
两人走进海伦亭那方小小的围地，其中一把椅子旁的草丛里有一点闪光，立刻勾住了温多弗的眼睛。他跨步过去，拾起一只银质雪茄烟盒。克林顿爵士伸手接过，朝盒面瞥了一眼。

===Original===
“It’s got a monogram, E. S., engraved on it,” he said. “This is obviously friend Ernest’s. You remember he said something about smoking a cigar here. He may have laid the case on his knee and jerked it off without noticing it when he started on his Marathon for safety.”
He held the case in his hand and seemed to give careful consideration to some point. At last he came to a decision and turned to Wendover.
“I think we’ll say nothing about this for a day or two, Squire. I may want to send this case up to London to have it examined, perhaps. I can’t say yet. But in the meanwhile we’ll not mention that we found it. Friend Ernest can take his cigars from the box, in the meantime. That’s no great hardship for him.”
“You think the murderer may have picked it up, and you’ll get his fingerprints from it? It’s a nice smooth surface.”

===Chinese===
“上面刻着花押字母 E. S.。”他说，“这明摆着是咱们朋友欧内斯特的。你记得吧，他说过在这儿抽过一支雪茄。他兴许把烟盒搁在膝头，等他撒腿跑他那场保命马拉松的时候，一颠就颠了下去，自己都没察觉。”
他把烟盒握在手里，似乎对某个要点细细掂量。末了他拿定主意，转向温多弗。
“我想这一两天里，咱们对这件事只字不提，乡绅。说不定我要把这个烟盒送到伦敦去检验。现在还说不好。不过在此期间，咱们不提发现了它。咱们的朋友欧内斯特暂且可以从烟盒里取他的雪茄。那对他算不得多大的苦。”
“你是想，凶手说不定拾起过它，你就能从上面取到他的指纹？这表面又光滑。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton looked up from the case with a gleam of amusement on his features.
“You’re in charge of the Speculation, Surmise, and Conjecture Department of this firm, Squire. I’m only a humble clerk in the Mum and Dumb Section—telegraphic address: ‘Tongue-tied.’ ”
Wendover accepted the tacit rebuke without protest.
“Oh, have it your own way,” he said, “I forgot I wasn’t to expect anything from you.”
Sir Clinton wrapped the cigar-case carefully in his handkerchief and stowed it in his pocket before doing anything further.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士的目光从烟盒上抬起来，脸上闪过一丝被逗乐的神色。
“在这家商行里，你执掌猜测、臆断与悬想科，乡绅。我不过是缄默部里一名小小的职员——电报挂号：‘结舌’。”
温多弗受了这记无言的抢白，没有抗议。
“哦，随你怎么着吧。”他说，“我忘了，我本不该指望从你这里得到什么。”
克林顿爵士用手帕把烟盒仔细包好，收进口袋，然后才做别的事。

===Original===
“Now I think we’d better go and have a look at the loophole again,” he suggested. “Though I hardly think it’s likely to have changed much since I saw it last. But I have a sort of feeling that Sherlock would have found something there, and perhaps you might be able to spot it, even if I can’t. That’s the worst of this detective business: one needs an eye for detail and I never had it.”
With an air of deep solemnity he led the way to the outer side of the hedge, approached the loophole, and peered into it for a time.
“No,” he admitted finally with a crestfallen air, “it seems just the same as it was when I saw it last.”
He put his hand into the hole.
“Not even a bird’s nest or any little thing of that kind,” he announced disconsolately. “Ah, we need Sherlock; we need Sherlock. He’d have found some cigar-ash or something of that sort, no doubt. I can’t see it. Have a look yourself, Squire.”

===Chinese===
“现在我想，咱们最好再去看看那处暗孔。”他提议道，“虽说我不大觉得它自我上次看过之后会有多大变化。可我隐隐有这么一种感觉：换了福尔摩斯，准能在那儿找出点什么；或许你也能瞧出它来，哪怕我瞧不出。干侦探这行，坏就坏在这儿：得有一双对细节敏感的眼睛，而我从来就没有。”
他摆出一副深沉庄严的神气，领头走到树篱外侧，凑近暗孔，朝里面张望了一阵。
“不。”他末了垂头丧气地承认，“它跟我上次看的时候似乎一模一样。”
他把手伸进孔里。
“连个鸟窝都没有，连那一类的小物件也没有。”他郁郁地宣布，“唉，我们需要福尔摩斯；我们需要福尔摩斯。他准能找出一点雪茄灰之类的东西来，毫无疑问。我看不出来。你自己来看一眼吧，乡绅。”

===Original===
Rather irritated by the chaff, Wendover stooped and stared into the loophole. He had to confess, however, that he saw nothing in the slightest degree suggestive.
“No broken twigs where the murderer rested his airgun?” Sir Clinton inquired. “Have a good look; the price is the same for two peeps as for one. Special terms by the hour, if you care to … Ugh! Damn these spiders! That gossamer’s all over the place—filthy, filmy stuff!”
He rubbed his hand on the hedge while Wendover grinned at his annoyance.
“Serves you right, Clinton! It’ll take your mind off all this persiflage business.”

===Chinese===
被这番打趣惹得有些恼，温多弗俯下身，盯着暗孔里看。可他不得不承认，自己看不出丝毫有启发的东西。
“凶手架气枪的地方，没有压断的树枝吗？”克林顿爵士问道，“好好瞧瞧；看一眼是这个价，看两眼也是这个价，按钟点算另有优惠，你要乐意的话……呸！这些蜘蛛真该死！游丝满处都是——又脏又黏的薄玩意儿！”
他把手往树篱上蹭，温多弗瞧着他那副懊恼直乐。
“活该，克林顿！这倒能叫你别再尽耍你那套唇枪舌剑了。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton seemed engrossed in removing the remaining filaments from his hand.
“Pity Sherlock isn’t here,” he let fall in a regretful tone. “He turned into an entomologist of sorts when he retired. Perhaps he could tell us what earthly use spiders can be.”
“They keep down flies,” said Wendover, instructively.
“So they do. Illuminating idea! I wish I’d thought of it myself. They keep down flies!”
“When you’ve quite finished being funny, perhaps you’ll get on with your job, Clinton. You’re supposed to be detecting murderers, not delivering lectures on insects.”
Sir Clinton dropped his jesting tone at once.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士似乎正专心致志地把手上剩下的细丝摘干净。
“可惜福尔摩斯不在这儿。”他语带惋惜地漏出一句，“他退休之后，多少算变成了一位昆虫学家。说不定他能告诉我们，蜘蛛究竟有个什么用处。”
“它们能治苍蝇。”温多弗以开导的口吻说。
“可不是嘛。振聋发聩的见解！可惜我没自己先想到。它们能治苍蝇！”
“等你把俏皮话抖搂够了，能不能干点正事，克林顿。你的差事是缉拿凶手，不是开昆虫讲座。”
克林顿爵士立刻收起了打趣的腔调。

===Original===
“Quite right. We ought to be getting along to the police-station now.”
“Aren’t you going to do any more here?”
“No.”
“What about bringing up that dog of yours and seeing if it can do anything?”
“The dog could do nothing in this case,” said Sir Clinton, definitely. “It would be a mere waste of time.”
“Well, you seem to know your own mind about that,” Wendover said rather wonderingly. “I suppose you know best. But I’d have thought it worth trying.”

===Chinese===
“说得对。咱们现在就该到警察局去了。”
“你在这儿不再干点什么了吗？”
“不干了。”
“把你那条狗牵来试试，看它能不能起点作用，怎么样？”
“这案子狗帮不上忙。”克林顿爵士语气笃定地说，“那纯粹是浪费时间。”
“唉，这一点上你像是胸有成竹。”温多弗颇觉诧异地说，“我想你最清楚吧。不过换了是我，会觉得值得一试。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton made no reply, but led the way through the Maze to the car.
“We’ll call at the police-station on the road home, Squire, if you’ll run us round there. I’m expecting some more reports. And some men must come up here and search for anything left about—not that it really matters. By the way,” he added, casually, “I suppose you know who the murderer is by this time?”
Wendover could only express astonishment at the question.
“Well, you’ve had every chance,” was all that Sir Clinton would vouchsafe.
“If you know who he is, why don’t you arrest him at once?” Wendover demanded.
“There’s a big gap between knowing a thing and proving it,” said Sir Clinton, cautiously.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士没有作答，只领着路穿过迷宫，回到汽车旁。
“回家的路上，咱们在警察局停一停，乡绅，劳你把车绕过去。我还在等几份新的报告。另外，得派几个人到这儿来，搜一搜有没有遗留下什么东西——虽说其实无关紧要。对了，”他随口补了一句，“我猜到这会儿，你已经知道凶手是谁了吧？”
对这个问题，温多弗只能表示惊讶。
“嘿，机会你可是全都有过了。”克林顿爵士肯透露的只有这一句。
“你要是知道他是谁，为什么不马上逮捕他？”温多弗质问。
“知道一件事和证明一件事，中间隔着一大截呢。”克林顿爵士谨慎地说。

===Original===
At the police-station the Chief Constable got out of the car and went in to interview his subordinates. In a minute or two he was back again, with some papers in his hands; and they drove on to the Grange.
“I’ve just time to ring up Ardsley before going upstairs,” he said, when they arrived; and he disappeared in the direction of the telephone. Wendover noticed that this time Sir Clinton closed the door of the room behind him, instead of leaving it ajar as he had done on the previous occasions; so that no sound came out during the conversation.
“I wonder what he’s up to with that confounded Jack-the-Ripper,” Wendover speculated uneasily, as he went upstairs to dress. “Well, perhaps he’ll tell me something after dinner.”
But when they had settled themselves in their easy chairs after dinner, he found that Sir Clinton evidently intended to reverse the roles.

===Chinese===
到了警察局，郡警察局长下车进去见他的下属。不消一两分钟他就回来了，手里拿着几份文件；两人继续驱车回塔尔加斯农庄。
“上楼之前，我还来得及给阿德斯利打个电话。”到达后他说道；随即朝电话机的方向去了。温多弗注意到，这一回克林顿爵士随手把房门关严了，不像先前几回那样虚掩着；因此通话期间，一点声音也没传出来。
“不知道他跟那个该死的开膛手杰克（Jack-the-Ripper）在搞什么名堂。”温多弗上楼更衣时，不安地揣测着，“唉，晚饭之后，他或许会跟我说点什么吧。”
可晚饭后两人各自在安乐椅里坐定，他发现克林顿爵士显然打算把角色对调过来。

===Original===
“Now then, Squire, *you’re* under no restraint of official secrecy. What do you make of the affair so far?”
“I see. I’m to be Watson, and then you’ll prove what an ass I am. I’m not over keen.”
Sir Clinton hastened to reassure him.
“I’m not going to poke fun at you merely for the sake of making you uncomfortable, Squire. It would really be some help if I could see the thing from a fresh point of view. Lord knows, I’m not infallible; and you may quite easily hit on something that’ll put a new light on things and prevent me making a bad mistake.”
The obvious sincerity of this was enough to placate Wendover. He had been cogitating deeply over the Whistlefield affair, and he felt that if he could not suggest a provable solution of the mystery, at least he could bring a reasonable amount of criticism to bear on the available evidence.

===Chinese===
“好了，乡绅，*你*可不受官方保密纪律的约束。到眼下为止，你对此案有何见解？”
“我明白了。我当华生（Watson），然后你再来证明我是头蠢驴。我不大来劲。”
克林顿爵士忙安慰他。
“我不是要拿你逗乐、专门叫你不自在，乡绅。倘若我能从一个新的视角看这件事，那实在会有些帮助。天知道，我并非一贯正确；你很可能碰上一点什么，给事情投来一线新的光亮，免得我犯下大错。”
这番话的真诚一望而知，足以安抚温多弗。惠斯特菲尔德这桩案子，他一直苦苦思量；他觉得，自己纵然提不出一个可以证明的谜底，至少也能对现有的证据施以相当分量的评骘。

===Original===
“What we have to account for,” he began, “are: first, the murder of the two Shandons; second, the burglary; third, the attack on Ernest Shandon; and, fourth, the so-called hanky-panky with the cheque.”
“That’s correct,” Sir Clinton agreed. “But suppose we leave out the cheque affair at present. We really know nothing definite about it yet.”
Wendover was dissatisfied with this ruling.
“It seems to me an essential part in the scheme of things. Let me put the case as I see it. Hackleton is at the back of the whole affair, I take it; but he’s been employing an agent; and that agent has been going beyond Hackleton’s instructions and has been operating on his own to a certain extent. I think that fits everything in the case.”
Sir Clinton seemed inclined to dispute this conclusion, but he restrained himself and merely nodded to Wendover to continue.

===Chinese===
“我们必须解释清楚的，”他开了头，“是：第一，两位香农遭凶杀；第二，失窃案；第三，对欧内斯特·香农的袭击；第四，所谓支票上的那番猫腻。”
“不错。”克林顿爵士赞同道，“不过咱们暂且把支票的事撇开。它到底是怎么回事，我们实在还一无所知。”
温多弗对这条裁定不满。
“在我看来，它是全局中少不了的一环。让我把案子照我的看法摆一摆。整桩事的幕后是哈克尔顿，我这么认为；可他雇用了一个代理人；那代理人越出了哈克尔顿的指令，在一定程度上自行其是。我觉得这样一来，案中种种都扣上了。”
克林顿爵士似乎有意反驳这个结论，但他克制住了，只朝温多弗点点头，让他继续。

===Original===
“I see your objection, I think,” the Squire went on. “You meant to say: ‘Why were *both* the Shandons murdered when it was only Neville’s death that was essential to Hackleton?’ But there’s quite a plausible explanation of that. You can’t hang a man twice. So if a man decides to commit a single murder, he might as well commit two. The punishment’s the same for a quantity. And if he can commit two with equal impunity—as in the Maze—mightn’t it occur to him that two murders would be a stiffer problem than one murder, in these particular circumstances?
