Isn’t it the double murder that’s giving all the difficulty? Of course it is. If either Shandon had been murdered solus, we’d know at once the line to look up. But at present we don’t. Now why shouldn’t the murderer have seen that very point and utilised it?”
Sir Clinton nodded.
“That’s ingenious, Squire. I’m not ironical.”

===Chinese===
“你的异议，我想我明白。”乡绅接着说，“你想说的是：‘既然对哈克尔顿来说，要紧的只是内维尔的死，为什么*两个*香农都遭了毒手？’可这一点有个相当说得通的解释。一个人不能被绞死两回。所以，一个人既然横下心要犯一桩凶案，索性犯两桩也无妨。论抵命，杀得多也是一样一条命。而如果他可以同样逍遥法外地连犯两案——就像在迷宫里那样——在这些特定情势下，他难道不会想到，两桩凶案是比一桩凶案更难解的题目？如今叫我们为难的，不正是这双重凶案吗？当然是。两个香农不管哪个单独被害，我们立刻就知道该沿哪条线去查。可眼下我们不知道。那么，凶手为什么就不会恰恰看中这一点，把它利用起来呢？”
克林顿爵士点了点头。
“这想法很巧，乡绅。我不是说反话。”

===Original===
“I’d rather choose that solution than any of the other possible ones. If you reject it, you’ve got to assume that two independent murderers, both using the same out-of-the-way method, chose to operate simultaneously. The chances against that are miles too big. Or else you have to believe that two cooperating murderers were at work and that each of them thought he had the right victim in front of him. I can’t quite swallow the notion that this was a cooperative affair. The third solution is that the murderer mistook one brother for the other, killed Roger first, and then had to kill Neville to carry out his instructions. He might have had only a general description of Neville Shandon to go on and may have made a mistake in identity.”
“I doubt if Hackleton would have left any loophole of that sort,” Sir Clinton interrupted. “Neville’s portrait could easily have been bought and given to the murderer. But it’s not worth while arguing the point. The murderer knew the two Shandons perfectly well by sight. I’m sure of my ground there.”
“You mean the murderer was a local man?” demanded Wendover. “How did you find that out?”
“I’m not going to tell you at present, Squire. Sorry to play the mystery-man, and all that sort of stuff; but it has to be done.”

===Chinese===
“我宁可选这个解释，也不选其他任何可能的解释。你要是不接受它，就得假定有两个各自独立的凶手，用的是同一种冷僻手法，偏偏又选择同时动手。这种事的机会小得没了边。再不然，你就得相信有两个合作的凶手在作案，而且每个人都以为自己面前站着的是正主。要说这是一桩合伙买卖，我可咽不下去。第三种解释是：凶手把两兄弟认错了人，先杀了罗杰，随后为了完成任务，只好再杀内维尔。他手里也许只有一份关于内维尔·香农的大略描述可作凭据，可能在身份上认错了人。”
“我看哈克尔顿不会留下那一类的漏洞。”克林顿爵士插话道，“内维尔的肖像，很容易弄到一张交给凶手。不过这一点犯不着争论。凶手同两位香农，见面认得清清楚楚。这一点我脚下站得稳。”
“你是说，凶手是个本地人？”温多弗追问，“这个你是怎么查出来的？”
“眼下我不打算告诉你，乡绅。抱歉要摆一摆玄虚先生的派头，来那一套；可不得不如此。”

===Original===
Wendover was plainly distrustful of this point.
“If it *was* a local affair, what was the black silk thread then? The thread we found in the Maze not a couple of hours ago.”
Sir Clinton closed his eyes as though pondering deeply.
“Yes indeed,” he said oracularly, “what was the silk thread?”
He sat up suddenly and beamed on Wendover.
“I should say it was a clue.”

===Chinese===
对这一点，温多弗分明不信任。
“如果那*真是*本地人的案子，那黑丝线又怎么说？才不过一两个钟头之前，咱们在迷宫里发现的那段线。”
克林顿爵士闭上了眼，仿佛在深深运思。
“可不是嘛。”他像宣神谕似的说，“那丝线到底是什么呢？”
他猛地坐直了身子，笑眯眯地望着温多弗。
“依我看，它是一条线索。”

===Original===
“Damn your leg-pulling,” the Squire broke out. “I shan’t go on, if you’re going to make the whole thing into a farce.”
Sir Clinton apologised.
“Sorry. You took the wrong meaning out of what I said. But don’t let’s waste time over it. Please go ahead, Squire.”
Only partly mollified, Wendover continued his analysis.

===Chinese===
“去你的，少拿人逗闷子。”乡绅发作道，“你要是打算把整件事弄成一场闹剧，我就不往下讲了。”
克林顿爵士道了歉。
“抱歉。你把我的话理解岔了。不过咱们别在这上头耗时间了。请接着说吧，乡绅。”
气只消了一半，温多弗继续他的分析。

===Original===
“The next thing is the burglary. That was obviously a case of getting at some document belonging to Neville Shandon. You remember the fragment of notes for his cross-examination that was found in his hand? They got some of his stuff, but clearly they suspected that he might have more notes. So they burgled his room to see if they could find anything further.”
This time Sir Clinton showed no desire to criticise.
“Right! On the face of it, the burglary and the murder of Neville Shandon fit together. But the trouble is that the commission of the burglary would show that it was Neville they were after, and hence make the murder of Roger useless as a blind. I merely point out the snag. I’m not trying to carp, Squire.”
Wendover thought for a minute or more in silence. Then he produced a reply.
“The two murders were part of a pre-devised scheme, as I suggested. But afterwards, the murderer found he hadn’t got the documents complete. He had to get them if possible. So he took the risk of the burglary giving the show away.”
Sir Clinton admitted the possibility of such a case.

===Chinese===
“下一件是失窃。那分明是为了弄到属于内维尔·香农的某份文件。你记得他死后手里攥着的那片交叉质询笔记的残页吧？他们弄到了他的一部分东西，可他们显然疑心他还有更多的笔记。于是他们撬了他的房间，看看能不能再找出点什么。”
这一回，克林顿爵士没有露出要批评的意思。
“对！表面上看，失窃同内维尔·香农的凶案是扣合的。麻烦在于：一犯下失窃，就表明他们的目标是内维尔，这样一来，杀罗杰便起不到障眼的作用了。我只是指出这个坎儿。我不是要挑刺，乡绅。”
温多弗默默想了一分多钟，随后给出了回答。
“正像我方才说的，两桩凶案是预先设计好的方案的一部分。可事后凶手发现，文件他没有到手全。只要办得到，他就非弄到手不可。于是他豁出去了，冒着失窃露马脚的风险。”
克林顿爵士承认有这样的可能。

===Original===
“But now what about the attack on Ernest Shandon. How does that fit in?”
“What’s one murder more or less to a man who has two on his soul already? The attack on Ernest may have been an extra blind, simply, like the murder of Roger Shandon. Suppose they’d got Ernest this afternoon, wouldn’t that have tangled the business up still further?”
“Admitted, of course. And really friend Ernest would hardly have been missed. Is that all the theory you have on the point?”

===Chinese===
“那么现在，对欧内斯特·香农的袭击又怎么说。它怎么嵌得进去？”
“对一个灵魂上已经压着两条人命的人来说，多一桩凶案少一桩凶案算得了什么？对欧内斯特的袭击，说不定不过是又一重烟幕，就跟杀罗杰·香农一样。假定他们今天下午真把欧内斯特办成了，那不是把这潭水搅得更浑了吗？”
“当然成立。再说，咱们的朋友欧内斯特真没了，也实在没谁会惦记。关于这一点，你的理论就这些吗？”

===Original===
Wendover was rather doubtful about putting forward his second choice.
“It might have been a practical joke, of course. Someone with a sense of humour rather out of gear might have had a grudge against the little beast and, knowing he was an arrant coward, they might have stirred him up without meaning to do him any real harm—just used an ordinary airgun dart.”
He looked at Sir Clinton suspiciously.
“You yourself didn’t waste much worry over him, it seemed to me. I thought at the time that you were taking it as a practical joke, somehow.”
“A very practical joke,” Sir Clinton said, but he kept every tinge of expression out of his voice when he made the comment.

===Chinese===
温多弗对要不要端出他的第二套方案，颇有几分踌躇。
“当然，那也可能是一场恶作剧。哪个幽默感出了岔子的人，说不定对那个小讨厌鬼怀恨在心，知道他是个十足的懦夫，存心撩拨他一下，并不真想伤他——只用了一支普普通通的气枪飞镖。”
他狐疑地望着克林顿爵士。
“依我看，你自己也没为他多费什么心事。我当时就觉得，不知怎么的，你是把这事当一场恶作剧看的。”
“一场十分讲实效的恶作剧。”克林顿爵士说道，只是发这句议论时，他把嗓音里的一切色彩都剔除得干干净净。

===Original===
“Now we can go on to the identity of Hackleton’s agent,” Wendover resumed. “You say it was someone who knew the Shandons by sight. It must have been someone who had leave to come and go at will through the Whistlefield grounds, or else someone who landed on the riverbank. That limits things down a good deal. Roger Shandon didn’t encourage strangers to roam about his place. The gardeners had orders to turn out anyone who ventured in, unless they were going up to the house on business. No stranger or neighbour—bar Costock—was on the premises so far as is known. I came across one of the gardeners and he told me that.”
Sir Clinton had no hesitation in confirming this.
“That agrees with all my men have been able to make out.”
“Then,” Wendover proceeded, “we’re limited down to the people at the house, the staff of the place, and Costock.”
“Go on,” Sir Clinton encouraged him.
Wendover pulled a notebook from his pocket and consulted some figures which he had jotted down at the time he heard the original evidence.

===Chinese===
“现在咱们可以接着谈哈克尔顿那个代理人的身份了。”温多弗重新拾起话头，“你说过，那是个见了香农家人认得出的人。那他必定是个可以在惠斯特菲尔德地界里随意出入的人，要么就是个从河岸上登陆进来的人。这样一来，范围就缩小了许多。罗杰·香农可不欢迎生人在他地面上乱逛。园丁们奉了命，凡是擅自闯进来的，一概撵出去，除非是上宅子里办事的。据目前所知，案发时地界内没有任何生人或邻居——科斯托克除外。我碰见过一个园丁，是他告诉我的。”
克林顿爵士毫不犹豫地证实了这一点。
“这跟我手下人查明白的情形全都对得上。”
“那么，”温多弗接着推进，“范围就缩到宅中诸人、庄园仆佣，再加一个科斯托克了。”
“往下说。”克林顿爵士鼓励他。
温多弗从口袋里掏出记事本，翻看当初听取原始证词时匆匆记下的几组数字。

===Original===
“If you take the facts as we know them,” he went on, “it’s clear that Neville Shandon could not have reached the Maze before 3:37 p.m.; and the second murder was over before 4:05 p.m. As a matter of fact, the times really allow less margin than that, for Neville’s body was found at 3:52 p.m. and both were probably dead by that time.”
“I think that’s quite demonstrable on Torrance’s evidence,” Sir Clinton admitted.
“That means then that the murderer left the Maze at some time not much earlier than four o’clock, since Miss Forrest heard him in the Maze after Neville’s body was found by Torrance at 3:52 p.m.”
“Most probable on the face of it.”
“Then if you find someone in such a position that they could not have been in the Maze at 4 p.m., they’re cleared.”
“True.”

===Chinese===
“如果把已知的事实摆在一起看，”他接着说，“那么显然，内维尔·香农最早也要到下午 3 点 37 分才能到达迷宫；而第二桩凶案在下午 4 点 05 分之前已经完毕。实际上，时间上的余隙比这还要小，因为内维尔的尸体是下午 3 点 52 分发现的，到那时，两人多半都已断了气。”
“我想，凭托伦斯的证词，这一点完全论证得出来。”克林顿爵士承认。
“那就是说，凶手离开迷宫的时刻，不会比四点钟早出多少，因为下午 3 点 52 分托伦斯找到内维尔的尸体之后，福雷斯特小姐还听见他在迷宫里。”
“从表面上看，最可能是这样。”
“那么，谁要是处在下午 4 点绝不可能身在迷宫的位置上，谁就洗清了。”
“对。”

===Original===
Wendover produced from a cupboard an Ordnance Survey map of the district.
“Let’s take each person in turn and see if we can establish their positions during the afternoon.”
“I can help you there,” Sir Clinton volunteered. “I got most of it in the police reports. They were busy on that very point.”
Wendover nodded and began without more ado.
“Sylvia Hawkhurst. She was out paying a call, wasn’t she?”

===Chinese===
温多弗从柜子里取出一幅本地区的陆军测量局（Ordnance Survey）地图。
“咱们把每个人挨个过一遍，看看能不能把他们当天下午的行踪确定下来。”
“这方面我能帮上你的忙。”克林顿爵士自告奋勇，“警方报告里，大部分我都拿到了。他们忙的正是这一点。”
温多弗点了点头，不再多话，随即开了头。
“西尔维娅·霍克赫斯特。她出门拜客去了，是不是？”

===Original===
“Yes,” Sir Clinton explained. “She and friend Ernest went off in the car from the house at about eighteen minutes past three. At a quarter to four—just about the moment when the murders occurred—she was in a shop buying shoelaces. That precludes any chance of her having used the car to get back to the Maze at the critical time. After that she paid a call on some friends and stayed with them until she came back home after six o’clock.”
“What about Ernest Shandon?”
Sir Clinton smiled.
“Miss Hawkhurst dropped him at the East Gate as she passed out. It’s about two-and-a-half miles to the East Gate, and she says she was driving about fifteen miles an hour—it’s a narrow road, you remember. That means she dropped him at the East Gate at about 3:30. It’s the best part of two miles back to the Maze. Friend Ernest could hardly have walked it in fifteen minutes, could he? And he’s not much of a runner, to judge by his condition this morning. As a matter of fact, his story’s completely confirmed by other evidence. My men interviewed the driver of the post-cart. At 4:20 he came upon Ernest squatting by the roadside, about a mile along the public road, into which the East Gate leads. It’s a place where there’s a little wood, easily identifiable. Friend Ernest was sitting there with his boot off, damning the nail that had hurt him.”
Wendover looked at his map.

===Chinese===
“是的。”克林顿爵士解释道，“三点十八分左右，她和咱们的朋友欧内斯特一起坐汽车从宅子出发。三点三刻——差不多正命案发生的时候——她正在一家铺子里买鞋带。这样一来，她在关键时刻开车赶回迷宫的可能就完全排除了。之后她去几位朋友家拜客，一直待到六点多钟才回家。”
“欧内斯特·香农呢？”
克林顿爵士微微一笑。
“霍克赫斯特小姐开车出去的时候，顺路把他放在了东门。到东门大约两英里半，她说自己一路的车速在每小时十五英里上下——那条路窄，你记得的。这样算来，她是在三点半左右把他放在东门的。从那儿折回迷宫，足足有两英里。咱们的朋友欧内斯特总不能十五分钟就走完吧？再看他今早那副尊容，他也不是个能跑的主。事实上，他的说法被旁的证据完全证实了。我的人问过邮车的驾驶人。4 点 20 分，他在公路上撞见欧内斯特蹲在路边，离东门通往的那条大路走出一英里开外。那地方有一小片树林，很好辨认。咱们的朋友欧内斯特正坐在那儿，脱了靴子，咒骂那颗硌了他的钉子。”
温多弗看了看地图。

===Original===
“That clears him. I can see the wood; it’s the only one that abuts on the road in that stretch. Now what about Arthur?”
“We’ve only his own word for his movements. He certainly set out for the spinney; but that’s all one can say.”
Wendover scanned his map once more.
“The spinney’s only a mile from the Maze in a direct line. He might have cut across and got away again; and no one would be any wiser. He had all the afternoon for the affair.”

===Chinese===
“这一来他就洗清了。那片树林我看得见；那一段路上紧挨着大路的，就它一处。现在说阿瑟怎么样？”
“他的行踪，只有他自己的一面之词。他确实动身去了那片小树林（spinney）；可能说的也就这么多了。”
温多弗又把地图扫了一遍。
“那片小树林离迷宫的直线距离不过一英里。他满可以斜插过去，再脱身回来；谁也不会比先前多知道半点。整个下午都归他办这件事用。”

===Original===
His face clouded.
“Somehow, I don’t think he was responsible, Clinton.”
Sir Clinton made no direct reply.
“He was hardly a likely agent for Hackleton to fix on, at any rate,” he observed.

===Chinese===
他的脸色阴了下来。
“不知怎么，我不认为是他干的，克林顿。”
克林顿爵士没有直接回答。
“无论如何，他不大像是哈克尔顿会挑中的代理人。”他评说道。

===Original===
“Well, let’s get on. What about the gardeners?”
“Two of them were working in a field about a mile from the Maze all afternoon. Each clears the other.”
“And the third gardener who was on the spot that day—the man Skene?”
“His story is that he was working in the kitchen-garden near the house. There’s no evidence against that.”
“The maids? And the chauffeur?”
“All accounted for. They had nothing to do with the affair.”

===Chinese===
“好，往下走。园丁们呢？”
“有两个整下午都在离迷宫约一英里的一块田里干活。两人互相洗清了对方。”
“那天在场的第三个园丁呢——斯基恩那个人？”
“他的说法是，他在宅子附近的菜园里干活。没有同这说法相抵触的证据。”
“女仆们呢？还有司机？”
“全都交代清楚了。他们跟这案子没有干系。”

===Original===
“And Stenness?”
Wendover looked keenly at Sir Clinton as he brought out the secretary’s name; but the Chief Constable showed no sign of special interest.
“Stenness?” he repeated. “Stenness was undoubtedly at the house at twenty to five, or thereabouts, for Miss Forrest saw him when she came back.”
“Then he’d plenty of time to be down at the Maze at the critical period and get home to the house again while Torrance and Miss Forrest were wandering about in the labyrinth?”
“He had,” Sir Clinton agreed, gravely.

===Chinese===
“那斯滕内斯呢？”
温多弗说出秘书这个名字时，目光锐利地盯着克林顿爵士；可郡警察局长没有露出一丝特别的兴趣。
“斯滕内斯？”他重复道，“五点差二十分前后，斯滕内斯毫无疑问在宅子里，因为福雷斯特小姐回来时看见过他。”
“那么，趁托伦斯和福雷斯特小姐还在迷宫里东转西转的当口，他大可以在关键时刻下到迷宫，再赶回宅子里，时间绰绰有余？”
“绰绰有余。”克林顿爵士郑重地认可。

===Original===
“He’d have been the ideal agent for Hackleton,” Wendover pursued. “And if Ernest’s not got the wind up about nothing—which is always possible, of course—Stenness would be worth watching.”
“He is being watched,” Sir Clinton assured him, and then seemed to regret his confidence.
Wendover, however, seized on the point at once.
“Ah! So after all your criticisms it seems you believe in my original theory!”
“I’ve forgotten which that was, by this time,” Sir Clinton admitted. “What was it?”

===Chinese===
“他本是给哈克尔顿当代理人的理想人选。”温多弗接着说，“倘若欧内斯特不是在那儿自吓自——当然，这也总是可能的——斯滕内斯就值得盯一盯。”
“他正被盯着呢。”克林顿爵士向他担保，随后似乎又后悔交了这句底。
温多弗却立刻抓住了这一点。
“啊！这么说，你挑剔了半天，到头来还是信我最初那套理论！”
“到了这会儿，我已经忘了那是哪一套了。”克林顿爵士承认，“是哪套来着？”

===Original===
The Squire was rather nettled.
“You poured scorn on it at the time. What I said was this: Suppose Hackleton hired a man to put Neville Shandon out of the way. You say that was a local man, according to some evidence which you haven’t divulged to me. Very good. If he was a local man, he might have had access to Roger Shandon’s private papers, his chequebook, and so forth. When he was hired for the Neville Shandon business, he may have decided to make a bit extra by forgery, and cover it up by the second murder. Two murders are as cheap as one, when it comes to pay for them; and Roger’s murder has confused the trail very considerably. It’s only a question of identifying the man who could have managed all that without going too much out of his way and attracting attention.”
Sir Clinton had been listening carefully to Wendover’s exposition.

===Chinese===
乡绅可有点冒火。
“你当时对它大加讥嘲。我当初是这么说的：假定哈克尔顿雇了一个人，要他把内维尔·香农除掉。你说，照某项你还没向我透露的证据，那是个本地人。很好。如果他是本地人，他就有机会接触罗杰·香农的私人文件、他的支票簿，等等。受雇去办内维尔·香农这件事的时候，他可能动了念头，想靠伪造再捞上一笔外快，用第二桩凶案把它盖住。说到抵命，杀两个跟杀一个一样划算；而罗杰的死把线索搅得七零八落。剩下的不过是认出那个人来：他把这一切办妥，又用不着太绕自己的路子，招人耳目。”
克林顿爵士一直仔细听着温多弗这番铺陈。

===Original===
“That’s very neat indeed,” he conceded. “It would certainly hold water, if it fitted all the facts that you know, Squire; but unfortunately it leaves out of account the most interesting fact of all.”
“And that is?” Wendover demanded, with some asperity. He was annoyed to find that he had overlooked something.
“That is the most interesting fact of all,” Sir Clinton assured him blandly. Then, with a change of tone: “And that’s all I’m able to say just now, Squire. I’ve no fault to find with your reasoning. It hangs together beautifully. But sometimes the human mind, if you follow me, is apt to assume connections where no such things exist in Nature. We’ve got an instinctive craving to trace associations between sets of phenomena—and at times we kid ourselves that there is some relationship when really it’s only a case of simultaneity.”
“You’ve been reading one of these shilling manuals lately,” said Wendover suspiciously. “ ‘How to be a Philosopher in Ten Minutes,’ or something like that. All this gay talk about simultaneity and phenomena and association comes straight from there. You can’t deceive me with a veneer of learning.”
“Well, I won’t dazzle you with further extracts. Let’s get back to business. Go on with your list.”

===Chinese===
“这番推论确实漂亮。”他让步道，“倘若它同你掌握的全部事实都吻合，它当然立得住脚，乡绅；可惜的是，它漏掉了所有事实里最有趣的那一件。”
“那是什么？”温多弗语气尖刻地追问。发现自己漏看了一样东西，他心里着恼。
“那就是所有事实里最有趣的一件。”克林顿爵士和颜悦色地向他担保。随后，他换了个口气：“眼下我能说的就这么多，乡绅。你的推理我挑不出毛病。它环环相扣，漂亮得很。可是人的头脑——你听懂我的意思——有时候爱在没有关联的地方臆造出关联来。我们有一种本能的渴求，总想在不同的现象群之间追出些联系——有时我们哄骗自己，说这里头有什么关系，其实不过是碰巧同时发生罢了。”
“你近来读了那种一先令一册的小册子吧。”温多弗狐疑地说，“《十分钟学会当哲学家》，或者那一类的货色。什么同时性啦、现象啦、关联啦，这套花哨话就是打那儿原封搬来的。你蒙不了我，休想拿一层学问的漆皮唬人。”
“好，我不再引章摘句晃你的眼了。回到正事上来。接着数你单子上的人吧。”

===Original===
“Young Torrance,” Wendover continued. “He’s a possible agent. I don’t know about his financial circumstances; he may be hard up, for all I know, and amenable to the cash bait that Hackleton could offer. It would be a pretty big one. Young Torrance was the person who proposed that game in the Maze to Miss Forrest. That would give him a reasonable excuse for being in the Maze at that particular time; and further, it would ensure that he was free from the girl’s supervision at the critical moment. Could you have invented a neater dodge yourself if you’d been set to it?”
“No,” Sir Clinton admitted, frankly, “I doubt if I could.”
“Take another point,” Wendover pursued his line of reasoning with increased interest. “What evidence have we that there ever was a third individual in the Maze at all? Torrance’s statements: but if Torrance was the murderer himself, of course he’d insist that a third person was present. Miss Forrest’s story of someone running in the Maze: but that may have been Torrance himself. You remember that she found it most difficult to tell the direction from which sounds came when she was in the Maze.”
“That’s a theory that might take some upsetting, Squire, if you can explain just one point. What did Torrance do with his airgun after he’d finished with it? No airgun was found in the Maze after the business. The murderer got rid of it somehow.”

===Chinese===
“小托伦斯。”温多弗接着说，“他是个可能的代理人。他的经济状况我不清楚；就我所知，他也许手头拮据，经不起哈克尔顿拿得出的现金诱饵。那诱饵可小不了。在迷宫里玩那场游戏，正是小托伦斯向福雷斯特小姐提议的。这给了他一个说得过去的理由，恰恰在那个时辰身在迷宫；而且更进一步，这还保准他在关键时刻摆脱了姑娘的视线。要是把这差事派给你，你自己能想出比这更妙的招数吗？”
“不能。”克林顿爵士坦率地承认，“我看我够呛。”
“再举一个要点。”温多弗兴致更浓地顺着思路往下推，“究竟有什么证据，能证明当时迷宫里当真有过第三个人？托伦斯的陈述：可要是托伦斯本人就是凶手，他当然一口咬定有第三个人在场。福雷斯特小姐说听见有人在迷宫里跑：可那没准儿就是托伦斯自己。你记得的，她说自己在迷宫里的时候，最难分辨声音来自哪个方向。”
“这套理论怕是不大好推翻，乡绅，只要你能解释清楚一点。托伦斯干完了那桩事，气枪怎么处置了？案发之后，迷宫里并没有找到气枪。凶手总归是把它处理掉了。”

===Original===
“I see no great difficulty there,” Wendover pointed out at once. “Look at the time Miss Forrest spent in wandering up and down in the Maze, unable to find her way out. If Torrance knew the labyrinth, he could easily make his way through it, get out to the river bank, chuck his gun into the water, and sprint back again into the Maze before she noticed his absence.”
He thought for a moment before adding:
“In fact, I don’t see why he mayn’t have got rid of the gun in the interval between the last murder and the moment he gave the alarm—the time when he shouted out that he’d found the body.”
He paused again. Then a further flash of insight threw a fresh light on the case.

===Chinese===
“我看这里没什么大难处。”温多弗立刻指出，“想想福雷斯特小姐在迷宫里来回打转、找不到出口，耗掉了多少工夫。要是托伦斯认得这迷宫，他大可以轻而易举地穿出去，走到河岸上，把枪往水里一扔，再一路飞跑回迷宫，赶在她察觉他不在之前。”
他想了想，又补上一句：
“其实，我不明白，他为什么就不能在最后一桩命案之后、到他报警那一刻之间的空当里把枪处理掉——所谓报警，就是他大喊发现尸体的那一刻。”
他又顿了顿。接着，又一道洞见的闪光，给案子投来新的光亮。

===Original===
“Why, of course, that would account for the running man. He would be rushing to the river bank and back again as quick as he could go, for the essential thing would be to get rid of the gun before anyone met him in the Maze.”
Sir Clinton had dropped all his air of superior criticism.
“That’s remarkably neat, Squire. I shouldn’t be surprised if it doesn’t touch the root of the business—at one or two points, at the least.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，当然啦，这就把那个奔跑的人解释通了。他是在拼着命赶去河岸再赶回来，因为顶顶要紧的是，赶在有人在迷宫里撞上他之前，把枪处理掉。”
克林顿爵士早已收起了那副居高临下的批评架势。
“这可巧极了，乡绅。哪怕它没有全然触到这桩案子的根子，我也不会吃惊——至少，它总触到了一两个点。”

===Original===
Curiously enough, the Chief Constable’s comment produced a complete change in Wendover’s mental outlook. He had fallen upon the Whistlefield case with all the enthusiasm of the irresponsible amateur. The mystery of it had caught his imagination, and he had thrown himself into the chase for a solution with an eagerness which he had hardly realised himself. He felt no more responsibility than if he had been attempting to follow clues in a detective story. Even the characters involved in the affair failed to give him any particular emotional background. He had never been intimate with the Shandon group; and some of the party he had not so much as seen before the tragedy occurred. Consequently, though he had used the real names of the various people concerned in the affair, they had borne no more significance than if he had said “Mr. X” or “Mr. Y.” The atmosphere in which he had worked had been that of a chess problem rather than an affair in real life.
And now, at Sir Clinton’s change of attitude, he caught a glimpse of a fresh side. It seemed that the line of thought which he had suggested might lead to something definite. It was no longer a case of idle speculation about the criminality of Mr. X or the guilt of Mr. Y. Instead, it was a question whether that rather decent young fellow Howard Torrance was going to find his neck in a noose one of these fine mornings. His own speculations might be the starting-point for a fresh line of detection. It came upon him with something of oppression that in his position with regard to Sir Clinton, his speculations might be put to practical use. Situated as he was, it was hardly so irresponsible a position as he had supposed.
But at this point in his train of thought a fresh idea occurred to him.
“Clinton said he knew who the murderer is. So my speculations don’t matter much. But it would have been a bad business if I’d turned suspicion on young Torrance. He might have had a lot of difficulty in clearing himself, if Clinton had taken up that line.”

===Chinese===
说来奇怪，郡警察局长这句评语，让温多弗的心境整个变了样。他原是怀着不负责任的业余爱好者那股热心，一头扑在惠斯特菲尔德案上的。案子的神秘攫住了他的想象，他投身于追索谜底的劲头有多大，连他自己都几乎不曾意识到。他觉得自己所担的责任，不比读侦探小说循着线索推演时多上一分。就连案子牵涉的那些人，也没能给他添上多少感情的底色。他同香农那一伙人素来不亲厚；其中几位，惨案发生之前他连见都没见过。因此，他虽然叫的都是案中人的真名实姓，那些名字在他心里的分量，跟“X 先生”“Y 先生”没有两样。他一直在其中工作的那股氛围，与其说是一桩真实生活中的案子，不如说是一道排局棋题。
而现在，克林顿爵士态度一变，他窥见了事情的另一面。他方才提的那条思路，看来可能会引出什么确凿的东西。这不再是闲磕牙地臆测 X 先生是否作恶、Y 先生是否有罪。它变成了一个问题：那位相当正派的小伙子霍华德·托伦斯，会不会在哪天晴朗的早晨，把脖子伸进绞索里去。他自己的这些臆测，可能成为一条新侦破路线的起点。他心头感到一阵压抑：以他相对于克林顿爵士的位置，他的臆测说不定会被派上实际用场。处在他这个位置上，实在不像他先前自以为的那样，可以不必负责。
可就在他思路走到这里时，一个新的念头冒了出来。
“克林顿说过，他知道凶手是谁。所以我的臆测关系不大。可要是我把疑心引到小托伦斯身上，那就是坏事了。倘若克林顿顺着那条线查下去，他要洗清自己，怕是千难万难。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton broke in at this moment.
“You don’t suspect Miss Forrest, I suppose!”
“No.”
All the amusement had gone out of the game, so far as Wendover was concerned; but Sir Clinton seemed to have no inkling of this, and pursued his way through the list.

===Chinese===
恰在这时，克林顿爵士插了话。
“你总不会怀疑福雷斯特小姐吧！”
“不怀疑。”
就温多弗而论，这场游戏的兴味已荡然无存；可克林顿爵士似乎对此毫无觉察，照旧顺着名单一路数下去。

===Original===
“Then that leaves Costock,” he pointed out.
“I don’t think Costock did it,” Wendover declared. He felt inclined to turn his criticism into the other camp now. “What have you against Costock? Can you bring any evidence to show that he had curare in hand? Or that he had an airgun? Or even that he was in the Maze at all at the time of the murders?”
“If that’s your line,” said Sir Clinton, with a noncommittal gesture, “we’ll say no more about it. I’ll look after Costock. Now there’s one name left—Ardsley. You’d better leave Ardsley to me, Squire. You’re far too apt to see red on that subject. You couldn’t produce an unbiased view of him if you tried.”

===Chinese===
“那就剩科斯托克了。”他指出。
“我不认为是科斯托克干的。”温多弗宣称。此刻他觉得，该把批评的炮口调向另一座营垒了，“你拿什么跟科斯托克过不去？你能拿出证据，证明他手里存着箭毒吗？能证明他有气枪吗？甚至能证明案发当时他到过迷宫吗？”
“既然你持的是这条线，”克林顿爵士说着，做了个不置可否的手势，“这事咱们就不再多说了。科斯托克交给我来照管。现在还剩一个名字——阿德斯利。阿德斯利你最好留给我，乡绅。一沾这个题目，你太容易火冒三丈。你就是想，也拿不出一份不带偏见的看法。”

===Original===
“Have you any evidence about his movements that afternoon?” Wendover asked, perfunctorily.
Sir Clinton also seemed to have grown tired of the business.
“You’ll find Ardsley’s name pretty prominent in the Whistlefield business when it’s all cleared up, I think. But I’m not prepared at present to say exactly what his part in the affair may turn out to be in the end.”
Wendover was only too glad to let the matter rest at this point. Irresponsible speculation is one thing; speculation which may lead up to a death sentence is something quite different. Suppose his ingenious reasoning—he had to admit that some of it was ingenious—were to lead to a wrongful conviction? He hadn’t quite seen it in that light before. It was all very well for Clinton to go in for theorising. It was his job to find the criminal and convict him. But Wendover had begun to feel that it was hardly for an amateur to step in and take a hand. Why, already he had light-heartedly thrown out suspicions against several people; and obviously some, at least, of these suspicions must be baseless. He would keep out of the field in future, he resolved.

===Chinese===
“关于他那天下午的行踪，你手里有证据吗？”温多弗心不在焉地问。
克林顿爵士似乎也对这套把戏倦了。
“等这案子水落石出，我想你会发现，阿德斯利的名字在其中相当扎眼。但他到头来究竟在其中扮演什么角色，眼下我还说不准。”
事情到此为止，温多弗求之不得。不负责任的臆测是一回事；可能通向死刑判决的臆测，就完全是另一回事了。倘若他那套巧思推出来的理路——他不得不承认，其中有些确属巧思——竟导致一桩冤狱呢？这层他从前没有好好看到。克林顿去搞理论推演，那是他的本分。他的差事就是找出罪犯、将其定罪。可温多弗已渐渐觉得，一个外行实在不宜插上一手。要知道，他已经轻飘飘地把疑点撒向了好几个人；而这些疑点，显然至少总有一些必定是无根无据的。往后他要置身局外，他这样下了决心。

===Original===
But there was still one point in connection with the Whistlefield case which had given him a good deal of perplexity. It threw no suspicion on anyone. He decided to clear it up if possible.
“There’s one thing I’ve been thinking over,” he began. “Why did you pretend you’d forgotten those darts on the museum mantelpiece, when all the time you’d left them there deliberately? You acted the part pretty well, Clinton. You took me in completely at the first rush. I thought it was real vexation over a genuine mistake. But when I’d had time to think about it, I saw plainly enough that you’d done it on purpose. You’re not the sort that makes silly mistakes of that kind.”
Sir Clinton came out of his reserve at once.

===Chinese===
不过，惠斯特菲尔德案里还有一点，一直叫他大惑不解。那一点不牵涉对任何人的怀疑。他决定尽可能把它弄个明白。
“有一件事我琢磨了好久。”他开了头，“你为什么装出忘了陈列馆壁炉台上那盒飞镖的样子？其实你从头到尾是故意把它们撂在那儿的。你那出戏演得真不赖，克林顿。乍一上来，你把我骗了个十足。我只当那是一桩真失误引来的真懊恼。可等我腾出工夫一想，就看得明明白白：你是故意的。你可不是会犯那种蠢失误的人。”
克林顿爵士立刻收起了那副深藏不露。

===Original===
“I’m not fooling now, Squire,” he said gravely. “I’m absolutely serious. I’ve staked my main case on that affair. I’m not able to tell you how or why at present. But you mustn’t breathe a word about it to a living soul, *no matter what happens next*.”
Wendover, in that moment, had a glimpse of a rarely-displayed side of Sir Clinton’s character. It convinced him, without further argument.
“Very good. Nobody will learn it from me.”
“You may find it pretty difficult to hold your tongue, Squire; but I trust you to do it. The temptation will probably be very strong before long. I’m hoping for the best; but I warn you that I’m expecting some pretty black work at Whistlefield before we’re through with this business. I couldn’t help seeing the funny side of Ernest Shandon’s affair; but the next one may not have much fun about it. You can take my word for it that Tragedy’s in the wings, now, waiting for its cue. So, *no matter what happens*, keep a tight grip on your tongue. You’re the only one who could spot that I was acting then. Nobody at Whistlefield knows anything about me. They took me for a blundering idiot. And that’s precisely what I wanted.”

===Chinese===
“这回我不是在耍笑，乡绅。”他郑重地说，“我是十二万分的认真。我把主案全押在那件事上了。眼下我还不能告诉你怎么回事、为什么。但你不许向任何一个活人漏出半个字，*无论接下来出什么事*。”
就在这一刻，温多弗窥见了克林顿爵士性格中罕有展露的一面。它说服了他，无须再多争辩。
“很好。没有谁会从我这里打听出它去。”
“你会发觉，守住舌头相当难，乡绅；可我信得过你。用不了多久，诱惑多半会来得非常凶。我往最好的方面盼着；可我警告你，这案子没了结之前，我预计惠斯特菲尔德还会出些相当凶黑的事。欧内斯特·香农那桩事，我没忍住，看出了它好笑的一面；可下一桩未必有多少可笑之处。你只管信我的话：悲剧这会儿正候在侧幕里，单等它上场的提词呢。所以，*无论出什么事*，把你的舌头攥紧了。你是唯一一个识破我当时是在做戏的人。惠斯特菲尔德没有人了解我的底细。他们把我当成了一个尽干蠢事的傻瓜。而那恰恰正是我要的。”

## XII The Fourth Attack / 十二 第四次袭击

===Original===
“I see the coroner’s jury brought in a verdict of wilful murder against some person or persons unknown,” Wendover remarked. “I suppose it’s the only verdict that would fit the case. They seemed to think you’d been rather slack in not having it all cut and dried for them, Clinton. Quite obviously they wanted the murderer’s head on a charger, and they were disappointed when you couldn’t produce the article.”

“I think they were disappointed that we hadn’t given them more evidence than we did,” Sir Clinton suggested with a certain indifference in his tone. “They seemed to imagine that the whole affair had been got up for their amusement—so that half of them could take on the post of Sherlock-on-the-Pounce. They can pounce away to their heart’s content if they wish. I’m not stopping them. But it isn’t my business to supply them with springboards, though they seemed to think so. All I wanted was to get the formalities through without too much jaw. And the coroner, decent man, saw to that for me.”

“What about your own swoops and pounces, before you wax ironical about these unfortunate yokels? It doesn’t seem to me that you’ve got very much farther than they’ve done, after all. What about it, Clinton?”

Sir Clinton laughed teasingly.

“The Hackleton case is dragging along still,” he said, with the obvious intention of changing the subject. “Shandon’s junior isn’t making much out of it, so far as I can see. Old man Hackleton has every reason to be content with the removal of Neville Shandon. He’s having it all his own way in the case now—far too clever for the poor barrister. He’ll get off scot-free, or I’m much mistaken.”

===Chinese===
“我看死因研讯的陪审团作出了裁定：系身份不明之人所为的蓄意谋杀。”温多弗说道，“我猜这是唯一与案情相合的裁定了。他们似乎觉得你有些怠慢，没把整桩案子办得现现成成端到他们面前，克林顿。再明白不过，他们想要的是用盘子盛来呈上的凶手脑袋，你交不出这件货色，他们便大失所望。”

“我看他们是失望在我们提交的证据不够多。”克林顿爵士接口道，语调里带着几分淡漠，“他们仿佛以为，这整桩案子是为了给他们解闷才张罗出来的——好让半数人都当上‘扑食的夏洛克’。他们愿意，尽管扑个痛快，我不会拦着。可给他们提供跳板不是我的分内事，虽说他们似乎不这么想。我只想把程序走完，别费太多口舌。验尸官是个正派人，替我把这一层办妥了。”

“先别急着挖苦这些倒霉的乡巴佬，你自己那些俯冲扑击又怎么样呢？依我看，说到底你也没比他们多走出多远。怎么样，克林顿？”

克林顿爵士揶揄地笑了。

“哈克尔顿案还在慢慢拖着呢。”他说道，明摆着是想岔开话题，“依我看，香农原先那位副手律师在这案子里捞不出什么名堂。老哈克尔顿对内维尔·香农的退场，有一百个理由心满意足。如今这案子随他翻云覆雨——那位可怜的出庭律师远不是他的对手。他会毫发无伤地脱身，不然我就是大大看走了眼。”

===Original===
Wendover refused to be led away on this fresh trail.

“Seriously,” he said, “you don’t seem to be doing much on this Whistlefield case. You’ve just been loafing about these last few days.”

Sir Clinton did not defend himself. In fact, he went out of his way to underline Wendover’s complaint.

“And tonight I’m actually dragging you off to play bridge at Whistlefield, eh? Well, the invitation didn’t originate with me. It came from Miss Hawkhurst. I admit that I angled for it in a somewhat unprincipled way—gave her to understand that the company of a sour old bachelor was getting on my nerves here, that I’d welcome a little bright feminine society, and that the society of herself and Miss Forrest had just the very kind of brightness that the case needed.”

===Chinese===
温多弗不肯被引上这条新岔道。

“说正经的，”他说，“惠斯特菲尔德这桩案子上，你好像没使什么劲。这几天你不过是四处闲晃罢了。”

克林顿爵士没有为自己辩护。实际上，他还偏偏给温多弗的抱怨添了着重号。

“今晚呢，我居然还要拖你到惠斯特菲尔德去打桥牌，嗯？这个邀请可不是我起的头，是霍克赫斯特小姐发来的。我承认，我用了一点不大讲原则的手段把它钓了过来——让她明白，同一位脾气乖僻的老单身汉作伴，我在这边已经有些神经受不了了；我欢迎一点明快的女性社交；而她本人同福雷斯特小姐的陪伴，恰好具备眼下这种情形所需要的那种明快。”

===Original===
“She must have felt flattered!” Wendover commented ironically.

“Oh, of course it was put in my most delicate vein.”

Then Sir Clinton became suddenly serious.

“I’m not very happy in mind about things, Squire; and I want to get a footing in that house apart from purely professional visits. Hence the angling. Otherwise, the thing would be in the worst of taste, I quite admit.”

Wendover pricked up his ears.

“Are you expecting more trouble even now? Nothing’s happened. …”

===Chinese===
“她一定觉得受宠若惊了！”温多弗讥诮地评论道。

“哦，那番话自然是照着我最雅致的笔调说的。”

随后克林顿爵士忽然正色起来。

“我心里对眼下的局面不太踏实，乡绅；除了纯粹公事性的造访，我还想在那座宅子里立住一只脚。所以才有那番垂钓。不然的话，这事实在俗劣到顶了，这一点我完全承认。”

温多弗竖起了耳朵。

“都到这个地步了，你还指望再出乱子？可什么都没发生……”

===Original===
“Since the last time? No, it’s rather a curious point which you may have noticed, Squire. Nothing ever does happen between the last time and the next time. That I should say was an almost invariable rule in life.”

“You evidently lost the chance of a good job when the Sibyls went out of business,” said Wendover in a disappointed tone. “You could have written up their books for them in the very best style. You’re a past master in the art of seeming to say something important and really saying nothing whatever.”

“It often comes in useful,” said Sir Clinton. “But why say anything at all? It seems just about the time when we ought to be starting for Whistlefield. Suppose we take the hint.”

He refused to discuss the Whistlefield case during the drive across, or even to give Wendover an inkling of why he wished to get a footing in the house at all. The Squire was not quite satisfied. To him, it appeared rather like a breach of hospitality for them to go there with anything in their minds beyond the game for which they had been invited. He disliked the idea of Sir Clinton Driffield introducing his alter ego the Chief Constable into a neighbour’s house by this indirect method.

===Chinese===
“自上一次以来？没有。这里头倒有个相当古怪的关节，你兴许留意过，乡绅：从上一次到下一次之间，从来什么事都不会发生。要我说，这几乎是人生一条万世不易的规律。”

“西比拉女巫们（the Sibyls）歇业的时候，你显然错失了一份好差事。”温多弗用失望的口气说，“你本可以替她们把神谕录润色得漂漂亮亮。看似言之凿凿、实则空无一物，这门艺术里你是个中大师。”

“这门功夫常常派得上用场。”克林顿爵士说，“不过，又何必点破呢？看来差不多该动身去惠斯特菲尔德了。咱们就领这个提示吧。”

一路驱车过去，他不肯讨论惠斯特菲尔德的案子，甚至连他到底为什么想在那座宅子里立脚，也不肯给温多弗一丝口风。乡绅心里不太痛快。在他看来，受了人家打牌之邀、心里却装着牌局以外的念头登门，未免有负主人的款待。克林顿·德里菲尔德爵士用这种迂回的法子，把他的另一个自我——郡警察局长——引进邻人的宅子，这个主意他很不喜欢。

===Original===
When they arrived they found only four of the Whistlefield party awaiting them. Arthur Hawkhurst was busy with the loudspeaker, from which he was evoking weird oscillation-notes in the course of his endeavours to pick up different stations. Ernest Shandon was sitting drowsily in a corner of the room; and Wendover noticed with distaste that he had a spirit decanter and syphon on a table beside him. As the Chief Constable and Wendover were announced, Sylvia came forward.

“So glad you’ve come, Sir Clinton. We’re looking forward to some decent bridge. …”

A weird howl from the loudspeaker drowned the remainder of her words. Ernest lifted himself from his chair with an effort and approached them.

“Are you much of a bridge-player?” he inquired apathetically. “I never cared enough for the game to do much good. It’s such a lot of trouble, you know. All this business of struggling for the declaration, and all that. And if one gets keen on it one’s apt to get very keen; and perhaps then one spends a lot of time over it. And one might spend that time in other ways, perhaps better, don’t you think? But perhaps you like it? Some people do.”

===Chinese===
到达时，他们发现惠斯特菲尔德这边只有四个人在等候。阿瑟·霍克赫斯特正忙着摆弄扩音喇叭，在试着接收各个电台的当口，喇叭里被他唤出种种怪异的振荡啸音。欧内斯特·香农昏昏欲睡地坐在房间一角；温多弗厌恶地注意到，他身边一张小桌上摆着盛烈酒的玻璃酒瓶和苏打水瓶。郡警察局长和温多弗被通报进屋，西尔维娅迎上前来。

“您来了真叫人高兴，克林顿爵士。我们都盼着打几局像样的桥牌呢……”

扩音喇叭里一声怪嚎，把她后半截话淹没了。欧内斯特费了把劲从椅子里撑起身，踱了过来。

“桥牌你打得好吗？”他无精打采地问，“我对这游戏从来热乎不到能打出名堂的程度。你知道，它太麻烦了。抢着叫牌啦，这一套那一套的。人要是一上心，很容易就上大心；说不定就在它身上花掉好多时间。而那时间本可以用来干别的，说不定还更好，你说是不是？不过也许你喜欢它？有些人喜欢的。”

===Original===
“Uncle was never a rap of good at it,” Sylvia explained with a faint suspicion of a smile. “So naturally he doesn’t like it. Same as the non-dancing man who can’t dance, you know.”

“Now Stenness is a good player,” Ernest went on. “And I can’t think why he finds it amusing. He’s got all the cards docketed in his head, you know, just like a lot of papers in pigeonholes. That seems to me too much like work—making a toil of pleasure and all that sort of thing. But tonight he won’t be playing. He’s busy in the study with some papers I asked him to look over. And Torrance is practising shots in the billiard room, so he won’t be playing, either. Arthur! Are you going to play?”

Arthur looked up crossly from his task.

“No!” he snapped. “Can’t you see this affair’s gone out of gear and I’m trying to put it right?”

Another shriek from the instrument emphasised his words.

Sylvia put her hands over her ears.

===Chinese===
“舅舅打这个从来就不灵光。”西尔维娅解释道，唇边浮起一丝若有若无的笑意，“所以他自然不喜欢。跟那个不会跳舞便索性不跳舞的男人一个道理，你知道的。”

“斯滕内斯倒是打得好。”欧内斯特接着说，“可我想不通他怎么会觉得这有趣。他把所有的牌都在脑子里编了号，你知道，就像一格一格鸽笼里插着的文件。在我看来这太像干活了——把消遣变成苦役那一套。不过今晚他不打。他正在书房里忙我请他过目的一些文件。托伦斯在台球室练球，所以他也不打。阿瑟！你打吗？”

阿瑟正忙着活计，没好气地抬起头来。

“不打！”他厉声说，“你没看见这玩意儿乱了套，我正想法子把它弄好吗？”

那台器械又是一声尖啸，给他的话作了注脚。

西尔维娅双手捂住了耳朵。

===Original===
“Will you be long over it, Arthur?” she demanded. “These howls are terrible.”

“Can’t you see I’m doing the best I can?” her brother retorted snappishly. “There’s nothing so aggravating as to have someone standing over one the whole time asking: ‘Will it be all right soon?’ and ‘When do you think you’ll have it in order?’ or ‘D’you know what’s wrong with it?’ I’m doing the best I can with the thing.”

Sylvia was evidently used to her brother’s outbreaks of temper. With a slight gesture she reassured Arthur that he would not be interrupted again; and then she turned to getting the bridge-table arranged. She and Wendover were to play Sir Clinton and Vera Forrest.

“I don’t care much for this room at this time of the evening,” she said, as she took the cards from their box. “The window’s almost level with the ground, and that bank of rhododendrons is so close that it blocks the best part of the view.”

“Not much view left at this time of night, Miss Hawkhurst,” Wendover said, glancing out. “The dusk’s so deep that one can hardly see anything in it now.”

===Chinese===
“你还要弄多久，阿瑟？”她质问道，“这些嚎叫太难听了。”

“你没看见我已经在尽力了吗？”她哥哥抢白道，“天底下最叫人冒火的，就是有人一直站在你背后，一会儿问：‘快好了吗？’一会儿问：‘你看什么时候能弄妥？’再不然问：‘你知道哪儿出毛病了吗？’我正尽全力弄这东西呢。”

西尔维娅显然习惯了哥哥的脾气发作。她轻轻做了个手势，让阿瑟放心，不会再有人打搅他；随后便转去张罗摆桥牌桌。她和温多弗搭档，对阵克林顿爵士和维拉·福雷斯特。

“傍晚这个时辰，我不大喜欢这个房间。”她一边从牌盒里取牌一边说，“窗子差不多同地面齐平，那道杜鹃花堤又贴得那么近，把最好的一片景致都挡去了。”

“夜里这个时辰，景致也剩不下多少了，霍克赫斯特小姐。”温多弗朝外瞥了一眼，说道，“暮色这么浓，如今外头几乎什么都瞧不见了。”

===Original===
Ernest who had been shuffling about the room in an aimless fashion for a few moments suddenly uttered a complaint.

“It’s very stuffy in here. Don’t you find it so, Sir Clinton? And you, Miss Forrest? It’s a rather hot night. Very close. I do like fresh air; they sometimes laugh at me and call me a fresh air fiend, you know; but I do like a breath of fresh air. Anybody object to the window being opened a bit from the bottom? Let some cooler air in here, then.”

Sylvia looked up from her game.

“We’re right in front of the window, uncle. Perhaps some of us might object to possible draughts.”

But Ernest refused to allow his desires to be sidetracked in this way.

“You don’t object, Miss Forrest? No? And you people don’t, either? You see, Sylvia, nobody minds. I’ll just open it a bit.”

He went forward and threw open the lower sash to its highest range.

===Chinese===
欧内斯特在屋里漫无目的地蹭来蹭去了一阵，忽然发起牢骚来。

“这里头太闷了。你不觉得吗，克林顿爵士？还有你，福雷斯特小姐？今晚上相当热。闷得很。我就是喜欢新鲜空气；他们有时笑话我，管我叫新鲜空气迷，你知道的；可我实在喜欢吸一口新鲜空气。有人反对把窗子从底下开一点儿吗？也好放些凉快气儿进来。”

西尔维娅从牌局上抬起头来。

“我们可就坐在窗子正前方，舅舅。只怕我们中间哪一位，会介意那说不定的过堂风。”

可欧内斯特不肯让他的心愿就这样被人岔开。

“你不反对吧，福雷斯特小姐？不？你们几位也不？你瞧，西尔维娅，没人在意。我就开它一条缝。”

他走上前去，把下面那扇窗一路推到了顶。

===Original===
“There! That’s much better!” he ejaculated, as he retired to his corner again. “It won’t get so stuffy now. That’ll be a great improvement, you’ll see. I never could stand stuffy rooms. I remember …”

Whatever he remembered was drowned by the loudspeaker. Arthur had at last completed his repairs and the jazz music of the machine filled the room.

“There! That’s all right now,” the mechanic announced at the pitch of his voice in an endeavour to make himself heard. “I’ll just leave it on, if you don’t mind. I want to see if it’s properly fixed up.”

He left the room unobserved by the bridge-players, who were intent on their game. Ernest gave a sour look at the loudspeaker; and after bearing it with obvious distaste for some minutes, he also rose.

===Chinese===
“瞧！这下好多了！”他一面退回自己的角落，一面脱口说道，“现在不会再那么闷了。这将是一大改善，你们瞧吧。闷屋子我从来受不了。我记得……”

他记得的不管是些什么，都被扩音喇叭淹没了。阿瑟终于修完了活，机器里的爵士乐灌满了整个房间。

“瞧！这下好了。”这位修理匠扯足嗓门宣布，好让人听得见，“各位要是不介意，我就让它这么开着。我要看看它到底修利索了没有。”

他离开了房间，专注牌局的牌手们谁也没有留意。欧内斯特朝扩音喇叭投去酸溜溜的一瞥；怀着明明白白的厌恶忍了几分钟之后，他也起身了。

===Original===
“I’m going into the winter-garden,” he explained, as he passed the bridge-table. “I can’t stand the racket that machine makes. It makes my head ache; it gives me a regular piercing pain in the ear to sit near it. I’ll just rest quietly in the winter-garden and come back again when Arthur’s finished with his tinkering at the affair.”

He stooped over Sir Clinton’s shoulder and added in an undertone:

“I’ve been very careful lately. I’ve taken your advice and kept inside the house as much as possible—so as to run no unnecessary risks, you know.”

He nodded with the air of one who confirms a weighty decision and lumbered off out of the room, leaving Sir Clinton staring after him.

“My advice!” the Chief Constable reflected with a certain dry amusement. “Well, I like his cheek in foisting that on to my shoulders!”

Wendover was glad that the bridge precluded much conversation. He felt that Sir Clinton had drawn him into a false position that evening; and he had to exert himself so as not to betray his feelings in the matter. Once they sat down, however, the play turned out very even; and he had not much mental energy left for anything beyond his game, which tended to reconcile him to his visit. Both the girls played better than the average; and he was beginning to forget his dissatisfaction as time went on.

===Chinese===
“我到冬季花园去。”他经过桥牌桌时解释道，“那台机器吵出来的动静我受不了。它闹得我头疼；坐在它近旁，我耳朵里像针扎一样地疼。我就在冬季花园里安安静静歇着，等阿瑟把他那套敲敲打打收拾完了再回来。”

他俯在克林顿爵士肩头，压低嗓子添了一句：

“我近来一直非常小心。我听了你的劝，尽量待在宅子里头——免得冒不必要的风险，你知道的。”

他点了点头，一副敲定了一项重大决策的人的气派，迈着笨重的步子出了房，撇下克林顿爵士在他身后望着发怔。

“我的劝告！”郡警察局长在心里品咂，带着几分干巴巴的兴味，“嘿，他把这笔账硬栽到我头上，脸皮可真够厚的！”

温多弗倒庆幸打桥牌说不成多少闲话。他觉得那天晚上克林顿爵士把他拽进了一个尴尬境地；他只得打点精神，免得心里的这点情绪露了出来。不过一落座，双方竟打得旗鼓相当；除了手上的牌，他匀不出多少心思想别的，这倒渐渐让他对这趟做客心平气和起来。两位姑娘打得都比一般水准好；随着时间过去，他也开始把自己的不痛快忘在脑后了。

===Original===
“That’s game and rubber,” said Sir Clinton, at length, as he looked up from the marker lying beside him.

Sylvia glanced at her wristwatch.

“Shall we play another?” she asked. “There’s plenty of time, unless you wish to get away early.”

As she spoke she stretched out her arm to lift the marker; but in the middle of the gesture she gave a sharp cry of pain and started up from her chair. Then, as she mechanically brought her hand down again on to the table, Wendover saw a spurt of blood from her right wrist, and, at its source, the brown feathering of one of the poisoned darts embedded in her white skin.

===Chinese===
“这一盘和这一局都结束了。”克林顿爵士终于说道，一面从手边的记分牌上抬起眼来。

西尔维娅看了一眼腕表。

“再打一局？”她问道，“时间还多得很，除非你们想早些回去。”

说话间，她伸出手臂去拿记分牌；可这动作做到一半，她猝然发出一声痛呼，从椅子上弹了起来。随后，当她的手不由自主地又落回桌面时，温多弗看见一股血从她右腕迸出；血的源头，是一支扎进她白皙皮肤里的毒飞镖，露出棕色的镖羽。

===Original===
For an instant the group around the bridge-table was stricken into immobility, while the blood jetted from Sylvia’s wrist and stained the cards across which her hand had fallen. The swift incursion of tragedy upon the scene had taken them unawares. A moment or two earlier they had been sitting in safety, intent upon their game. Then, out of the night the tiny missile had sped to its mark; and the King of Terrors had come among them. There had not even been the warning of the airgun’s report; for it must have been drowned by the noise of the loudspeaker which still continued to pour out its incongruous flood of dance music.

Wendover frozen in his chair, took in the scene almost without knowing that he was observing it; the pain-shot face of Sylvia, the horror in Vera Forrest’s eyes, the trickle of blood across the littered cards, and the cool visage of Sir Clinton as he leaned over the table towards the wounded girl. Then, as he watched, Sylvia’s expression changed. She had seen the poisoned dart in her wrist and now she understood what it meant. Her lips opened as though saying something, then her face grew suddenly white, and she slipped back in her chair.

Sir Clinton rose swiftly and lifted the unconscious girl across the room to one of the couches. Wendover noticed that, even in the haste, the Chief Constable took care to use his own body as a shield, keeping it between Sylvia and the window until he had reached a point which seemed out of range of the assassin.

“After the brute, Wendover!” Sir Clinton ordered, raising his voice above the clamour of the loudspeaker. “You may be able to spot him before he gets clear away. And shut that window behind you.”

Galvanised into action by the curt directions, Wendover suddenly ceased to be a mere spectator. Without a word he swung himself through the open window and out into the darkness. Somewhere in the gloom, the unknown murderer must be lurking, waiting perhaps to make sure of his victim with a second shot. Wendover was filled with an anger wholly alien to his usual temperament, and he peered eagerly into the obscurity around him in the hope of glimpsing a shadow moving among the shades.

===Chinese===
桥牌桌四周的人霎时被击成了泥塑木雕，血从西尔维娅的腕上喷出，染红了她手跌落处的那片纸牌。悲剧突临这场面之迅疾，令他们措手不及。一两分钟之前，他们还安安稳稳地坐着，一心扑在牌局上。转瞬之间，那枚小小的飞弹破夜而出，直取目标；恐怖之王驾临到他们中间。连气枪一声枪响的预警都不曾有；那声响想必早被扩音喇叭的噪音淹没了——喇叭还在往外倾泻着格格不入的连绵舞曲。

温多弗僵在椅中，几乎浑然不觉地看尽了眼前这一幕：西尔维娅那张被痛楚穿透的脸，维拉·福雷斯特眼中的惊骇，散乱的纸牌上蜿蜒的血迹，还有克林顿爵士俯身桌前、朝受伤姑娘探过去的那张冷静的脸。接着，他眼看着西尔维娅的神情变了。她看见了自己腕上的毒飞镖，此刻她明白那意味着什么了。她嘴唇张开，像要说什么话，随即脸色骤然惨白，整个人从椅子里滑了下去。

克林顿爵士霍然起身，抱起不省人事的姑娘，穿过房间放到一张躺椅上。温多弗注意到，即便在那样仓促之间，郡警察局长仍旧留神拿自己的身体作盾，一路隔在西尔维娅与窗子之间，直到抵达一处似乎在刺客射程之外的位置。

“去追那畜生，温多弗！”克林顿爵士喝令道，嗓门压过扩音喇叭的喧闹，“他逃干净之前，你说不定能撞见他。随手把那扇窗关上。”

这几句简短的指令把温多弗激得行动起来，他忽然不再是单纯的看客。他一言不发，翻身钻过敞开的窗子，跃入黑暗。昏暗中的什么地方，那个身份不明的凶手必定潜伏着，说不定正等着再补一镖，好坐实他的猎物。温多弗满腔怒火，这与他平素的性情全然不合；他急切地朝四周的暗处张望，巴望在重重阴影之间瞥见一道移动的黑影。

===Original===
The murder of the two Shandons and the attack upon Ernest had left him emotionally untouched to any real extent. The two Shandons had been hard men, from all he knew of them; and the fate which had overtaken them did not seem altogether out of keeping with people of their type. The attempt on Ernest had been unsuccessful and had made little impression on Wendover’s feelings. But this last outrage was in a different category. Even yet he could hardly realise that a deadly effort had been made to injure Sylvia. Sylvia! It was hardly possible for him to feel sure that anyone would attempt to bring down a girl in that terrible fashion. A man, somehow, was different; but he revolted against the idea of cutting short a life like Sylvia’s. The aimlessness of it seemed appalling to his mind; and his anger against the hidden assassin rose to a white heat.

He moved forward in the direction from which he supposed the shot had come; but in a few steps he ran right into the belt of rhododendrons which stretched parallel with the house on this front. As he did so, the loudspeaker was suddenly shut off and he halted to listen for sounds of movement. Nothing seemed to be stirring. He circled about the rhododendrons, but found no one there.

He retraced his steps towards the window. A single dim light shone at the other end of the winter-garden, but except for it the house-front was dark. The bridge-table showed every detail under the lamps of the room beyond the window—an ideal target for the eye of anyone posted in the darkness.

Suddenly Wendover’s eyes were dazzled by a blaze of light as the whole of the winter-garden lamps were switched on.

“I say,” demanded a cautious voice, “what does all this mean? What’s all this about, I say? Who are you, out there?”

===Chinese===
香农家两兄弟的被杀，对欧内斯特的那次袭击，都未曾真正触动过他的感情。就他所知，香农家那兄弟俩本是心肠硬的人；落到他们头上的那种下场，跟他们那类人似乎也不算全然不相称。对欧内斯特的那次谋害没有得手，在温多弗心上也没压出多少分量。可这最近的一次暴行却是另一码事。直到此刻，他几乎还没法相信，有人当真下过那样的毒手去害西尔维娅。西尔维娅！他简直没法让自己相信，竟会有人企图用那种可怕的方式撂倒一位姑娘。男人，不知怎的，另当别论；可一想到斩断西尔维娅这样一条性命，他打心底里抗拒。这桩事的无缘无故，在他想来骇人听闻；他对那藏匿的刺客的怒火，升腾到了白热。

他朝自己估计飞镖飞来的方向走去；可没迈几步，就一头撞进了那道与宅子正面平行的杜鹃花堤。就在这时，扩音喇叭戛然关掉了，他停下脚步，听有没有走动的声响。四下似乎毫无动静。他绕着那道杜鹃花堤兜了一圈，里面一个人也没有。

他循原路折回窗前。冬季花园另一端亮着孤零零一盏昏黄的灯，除此之外，宅子正面一片漆黑。窗内房间的灯光下，桥牌桌连细处都显得一清二楚——对潜伏在黑暗中的任何人来说，都是个理想的靶子。

突然，冬季花园的灯全数拧亮，一片光焰晃得温多弗睁不开眼。

“喂，”一个小心翼翼的声音发问，“这都是什么意思？我说，这到底是怎么回事？外头那个，你是什么人？”

===Original===
Wendover’s eyes, after an instant or two, grew accustomed to the glare. Looking towards the speaker, he saw Ernest Shandon’s figure at the nearest door of the winter-garden. Ernest evidently meant to run no risks; for he was holding the door almost closed and had taken shelter behind it while he called out his demand for explanations. Wendover’s lips curled contemptuously as he noted the shrinking figure under the lights.

“I’m Wendover,” he announced.

Ernest opened the door another inch, though with manifest reluctance.

“What’s it all about?” he reiterated, with almost pathetic anxiety. “Is there any danger? What are you running around like this for? Where’s Driffield? What’s happened? Can’t you answer, man?”

Wendover was still more disgusted by the obvious poltroonery of the man who was, nominally at least, his host.

===Chinese===
一两秒之后，温多弗的眼睛适应了那片强光。朝说话人望去，他看见欧内斯特·香农的身影立在冬季花园最近的门口。欧内斯特分明一点风险也不打算冒；他把门拉得只剩一线，人躲进门后，才喊出他那一连串要人解释的质问。温多弗看着灯光下那副缩头缩脑的身影，嘴角鄙夷地撇了撇。

“我是温多弗。”他自报家门。

欧内斯特把门又推开一指宽，虽然分明老大不情愿。

“到底是怎么回事？”他追问不休，焦灼得近乎可怜，“有什么危险吗？你这样跑来跑去干什么？德里菲尔德在哪儿？出了什么事？你就不能答一句话吗，伙计？”

此人至少名义上还是他的主人，这一副明明白白的懦怯相，愈发叫温多弗作呕。

===Original===
“Miss Hawkhurst has been shot with one of those poisoned darts. Come along and see if there’s anything we can do.”

Ernest was quite evidently reduced to the last stage of moral prostration by the news. He had not even sufficient nerve left to cover up his cowardice.

“Eh? What’s that? Come out there and be shot at myself? I won’t!”

“Well, stay there, then!” Wendover growled, continuing his way back to the window through which he had come.

“I tell you what I’ll do,” he heard Ernest’s voice again. “I’ll go into the house by the other door of the winter-garden and come round to where you are. I’ll be under cover the whole way if I do that.”

The sound of the winter-garden door closing and the turning of the key in the lock came to Wendover’s ears as he reopened the window and climbed through, shutting it behind him.

===Chinese===
“霍克赫斯特小姐中了那种毒飞镖的一镖。快过来，看看有什么我们帮得上忙的。”

这消息分明把欧内斯特打垮到了精神崩溃的最后一地步。他连遮掩自己懦怯的那点胆量都不剩了。

“嗯？你说什么？叫我出去挨那一镖？我不干！”

“那你就待在那儿吧！”温多弗吼道，继续朝他进来的那扇窗走回去。

“我告诉你我打算怎么办。”他又听见欧内斯特的声音，“我从冬季花园的另一扇门进宅子，绕到你们那儿去。这么走，我一路都有遮蔽。”

温多弗重新推开那扇窗爬了进去、又随手关上时，耳边传来冬季花园的门关上、钥匙在锁孔里转动的声响。

===Original===
Sylvia was still lying on the couch, evidently unconscious. Sir Clinton was beside her and, much to Wendover’s surprise, some lint and bandages had been laid out on the bridge-table which had been pulled across the room.

“Miss Forrest,” the Chief Constable said curtly, “will you bring some warm water? Get it yourself. These maids are no use in an emergency. And tell them to get Miss Hawkhurst’s room ready for her—immediately. A hot-water bottle as quick as they can—and some brandy.”

Vera was so quick that she had to pause at the door for his last directions.

“You Wendover,” went on Sir Clinton, “get Ardsley on the phone at once. Tell him I want him here at Whistlefield.”

Wendover halted for a moment.

===Chinese===
西尔维娅还躺在躺椅上，显然不省人事。克林顿爵士守在她身旁；让温多弗大吃一惊的是，桥牌桌已被拖到房间这头，桌上摆出了敷布和绷带。

“福雷斯特小姐，”郡警察局长简短地说，“请你端些温水来好吗？你亲自去。这些女仆到了紧急关头是不顶用的。再叫她们把霍克赫斯特小姐的房间给她收拾出来——立刻。热水袋，越快越好——还有白兰地。”

维拉手脚太麻利，人已到了门口，还得停下来听他最后的吩咐。

“你，温多弗，”克林顿爵士接着说，“马上打电话找阿德斯利。告诉他我要他到惠斯特菲尔德来一趟。”

温多弗顿了一顿。

===Original===
“Hadn’t I better tell him what he’s wanted for? He may be able to bring something with him.”

“It’s all arranged. Damnation man! *Will* you hurry up!”

Wendover, electrified by the vehemence of the tone, hurried off without a word. When he returned he found that Vera Forrest had carried out her instructions and had come back to see if anything more could be done. Ernest had also found his way into the room and stood staring vacantly at the form of his niece lying so limply on the couch. He was evidently about to open his mouth when Sir Clinton looked up.

“Everything all right? Thanks, Miss Forrest. You got Ardsley, Wendover? Good so far, then.”

He was busy bathing the wound with warm water as he spoke.

===Chinese===
“要不要告诉他为了什么事找他？他兴许能随身带些什么来。”

“都安排好了。见鬼，伙计！你*倒是*快点！”

这话音的疾厉把温多弗震得像过了电，他一言不发，匆匆去了。他回来时，发现维拉·福雷斯特已把吩咐一一办妥，又转回来看还能帮上什么忙。欧内斯特也已摸进了房，呆呆瞪着外甥女软瘫在躺椅上的那副身形。他分明正要张口，克林顿爵士抬起了眼。

“都办妥了？多谢，福雷斯特小姐。阿德斯利你找到了吧，温多弗？那么到目前为止，还算顺利。”

他说话时，正忙着用温水清洗伤口。

===Original===
“There’s just a chance we may be able to do something,” he explained, going on with his task. “By the merest luck, the dart hit the chain of her watch-bracelet. It got down between the links and made a nasty wound all the same; but it didn’t quite embed itself in the flesh. So there’s just the chance that the dose of poison injected may not reach the fatal amount. I can’t say. Ardsley will know better when he arrives.”

He bathed the wound again, then turned to Wendover.

“You saw no one?”

Wendover shook his head.

“It’s practically pitch dark tonight. I could see nothing.”

Sir Clinton thought for a moment.

===Chinese===
“还有一线指望，我们或许能有作为。”他一面继续手里的活计一面解释，“全凭天大的运气，那支飞镖打在了她腕表手链的链子上。它顺着链环间的缝隙钻了下去，仍旧造成了一个不轻的伤口；可它到底没有完全嵌进肉里。所以，注入的毒量未尝没有一线可能，够不上致命的分量。我说不准。阿德斯利来了会更清楚。”

他又清洗了一遍伤口，然后转向温多弗。

“你谁也没看见？”

温多弗摇了摇头。

“今晚简直是伸手不见五指。我什么也看不见。”

克林顿爵士想了一想。

===Original===
“You’ll find a flash-lamp in my overcoat pocket. Get it, Wendover, and hunt round that bank of rhododendrons to see if you can find the airgun. The brute may have dropped it in the hurry, this time. Don’t mind if you make a mess—the gun’s more important than any tracks you may obscure in your search.”

As Wendover moved towards the door, Ernest seemed to come to life.

“I suppose I ought to help,” he said, “but it seems to me taking a needless risk, sending anyone out into the dark like that. For all we know the fellow may be out there yet, with his gun. I don’t think anyone should go. I’m not going,” he concluded simply.

Sir Clinton glanced up for a moment and scanned Ernest with eyes that made no effort to conceal their contempt.

“I didn’t ask you to volunteer. Go on, Wendover. I’ll come and give you a hand as soon as Ardsley arrives.”

===Chinese===
“我大衣口袋里有支手电筒。去拿来，温多弗，再到那道杜鹃花堤四周搜寻一番，看能不能找到那支气枪。这一次，那畜生仓促之间说不定把它失落在那儿了。踩得一塌糊涂也不必在意——枪比你搜查时可能踏乱的任何痕迹都要紧。”

温多弗朝门口走去时，欧内斯特像是还了魂。

“按说我理应帮一把手，”他说，“可依我看，像这样打发人到黑地里去，是冒不必要的风险。说不定那家伙还带着枪在外头呢。我看谁都不该去。我就不去。”他干干脆脆地下了结论。

克林顿爵士抬眼扫了一下，用毫不掩饰轻蔑的目光把欧内斯特打量了一遍。

“我可没请你自告奋勇。去吧，温多弗。阿德斯利一到，我就来搭把手。”

===Original===
As Wendover turned to leave the room Stenness’s figure appeared at the door. It was evident that the secretary had been put on the alert by the hurrying to and fro in the house, and had come to see what was amiss; but apparently he had had no inkling of the real state of affairs. Wendover saw him glance from one to another in the room until at last his eyes lighted upon the limp figure of Sylvia stretched on the couch. Then a flash of expression crossed his features, something which betrayed an intense emotion; but Wendover, at the moment, was unable to interpret it. He stored it up in his memory for future consideration, and then left the room.

“And now,” said Sir Clinton, “I think we’d better take Miss Hawkhurst up to her room. We can manage it well enough; and she’d better be there rather than here when she comes to herself again.”

Under his directions this was carried out. On reaching Sylvia’s room, Sir Clinton looked round and then, going over to the window, he endeavoured to scan the surroundings; but it was obviously too dark to see much.

“I think we’ll shift this bed,” he suggested, when he came back. “It had better be brought over into this corner. Then there will be no possibility of any shot reaching it from the window. One never knows …”

He paused for a moment.

“Now I think Miss Forrest and I had better wait here till Miss Hawkhurst comes out of her faint; or at any rate till Dr. Ardsley turns up. But we mustn’t have a crowd here just now.”

===Chinese===
温多弗转身要出房时，斯滕内斯的身影出现在门口。显然，宅子里跑来跑去的动静惊动了这位秘书，他是来看看出了什么乱子；可他对真实的情况显然还毫不知情。温多弗看见他的目光在屋里众人身上挨个扫过，最后落在瘫在躺椅上的西尔维娅那副绵软的身形上。随即，一丝神情掠过他的面容，泄露出某种强烈的情绪；只是温多弗一时无从解读。他把它记在心里，留待日后琢磨，随后便出了房。

“现在，”克林顿爵士说，“我想我们最好把霍克赫斯特小姐抬到她自己房里去。我们几个抬得动；她再醒过来时，在那儿总比在这儿好。”

在他的指挥下，这事办妥了。到了西尔维娅房里，克林顿爵士环视一圈，随后走到窗前，设法查看四周的情形；可天显然黑得看不出什么。

“我想我们得挪一挪这张床。”他走回来时提议道，“最好把它搬到这个角落里来。那样，从窗子射来的任何一镖就都够不着它了。谁也说不准……”

他顿了一顿。

“现在，我想福雷斯特小姐和我最好在这儿守着，等霍克赫斯特小姐从昏迷中缓过来；至少也要等到阿德斯利博士到。只是眼下这里容不得聚一堆人。”

===Original===
His manner, rather than his words, cleared the room of his late assistants; and he and Vera Forrest were left alone. Sir Clinton, after feeling Sylvia’s pulse, succeeded in giving her a few drops of brandy. Soon she stirred faintly. Sir Clinton left the bedside and returned to the window. Down below, at a short distance, he could see Wendover busy with the flash-lamp. Quite obviously he had not yet found anything.

As Sir Clinton turned away from the window Vera Forrest beckoned him aside.

“What do you think, Sir Clinton? Is there any chance of her getting over it?”

Sir Clinton’s grave face showed the anxiety which was at work in his mind.

“I really can’t say anything, Miss Forrest, for I don’t know anything. The wound isn’t as deep as in the other cases. That’s always something. She hasn’t collapsed immediately, as her uncles did. That’s something also. But we’ll need to wait for Dr. Ardsley; and even when he comes, I doubt if we shall learn much. He’ll at least be able to give her any special treatment that there is. We can only hope for the best.”

===Chinese===
清空这间房的，与其说是他的言语，不如说是他的做派；方才那几位帮手都被请了出去，房里只剩下他和维拉·福雷斯特。克林顿爵士给西尔维娅诊了脉，又给她喂进了几滴白兰地。不多时，她微微动了动。克林顿爵士离开床边，回到窗前。下方不远处，他看得见温多弗正打着手电筒忙活。显然他还什么都没找到。

克林顿爵士从窗前转过身来，维拉·福雷斯特把他招呼到一旁。

“您怎么看，克林顿爵士？她有没有熬过去的指望？”

克林顿爵士凝重的脸上，透出正在他心头啃噬的焦虑。

“我实在说不上什么，福雷斯特小姐，因为我什么都不知道。伤口不像前几案扎得那么深。这总算是一点。她没有像她两位舅舅那样当场倒下。这也算一点。可我们得等阿德斯利博士；就算他来了，能不能问出多少名堂，我也犯疑。他至少还能给她用上现有的什么特效处置。我们只能往最好处指望。”

===Original===
It was clear from his tone that he did not take a light view of the case. He had hardly ceased speaking when they heard the sound of someone racing up the stair. The door was opened brusquely; and Sir Clinton had just time to interpose himself when Arthur Hawkhurst came into the room. The boy was evidently in high excitement. He had learned of the affair downstairs and had rushed up on the spur of the moment.

“ ’Sh!” said Sir Clinton, angrily. “Don’t break in here like a wild bull!”

He led the boy gently outside into the hall.

“Your sister has been shot at like your uncles,” he explained. “So far, the thing hasn’t killed her; but you needn’t take any optimistic view. I’ve sent for Dr. Ardsley. He knows about that poison; and perhaps he may be able to do something.” Arthur seemed unable to control his excitement.

“But who’d do a thing like that?” he demanded.

===Chinese===
从他的语气听得分明，他对这桩案子并不往轻里看。话音才落，他们就听见有人蹬蹬蹬奔上楼来。门被猛地推开；克林顿爵士刚来得及横身挡住，阿瑟·霍克赫斯特便闯进了房。这少年显然激动已极。他在楼下得了信，一时情急便冲了上来。

“嘘！”克林顿爵士恼火地说，“别像头蛮牛似的往里闯！”

他把少年温和地带出去，到了门厅里。

“你姐姐跟你两位舅舅一样，挨了那种飞镖。”他解释道，“到目前为止，这东西还没有要她的命；可你也用不着往乐观里想。我已经派人去请阿德斯利博士。他懂那种毒药；或许他能有点办法。”阿瑟似乎管不住自己的激动。

“可谁会干出这种事？”他质问道。

===Original===
“Don’t make a row,” Sir Clinton ordered, bluntly. “We can’t stand here holding a committee meeting. There’s plenty of time for discussion later on. She’s just coming out of a faint—at least it looks like that. Shock of seeing what had hurt her, no doubt, was what sent her off. Nothing to be done now until Ardsley comes. … Ah, here he is. Now, Hawkhurst, we’ll go; and leave the expert to the business.”

Ardsley was ascending the stair, carrying a bag with him. He nodded a curt greeting to the two at the head of the stair, gave another interrogative nod as if inquiring which room he should enter, and then disappeared, closing the door behind him. Arthur seemed amazed that Sir Clinton had said nothing as the doctor passed.

“Aren’t you going to tell him about it?” he demanded anxiously.

“He knows all about it,” Sir Clinton assured him, but he added no explanations. “One moment, before we go.”

He waited for a minute or two, then the door of Sylvia’s room reopened and Ardsley came out. His ordinarily impassive face had an expression of unusual gravity; and in answer to Sir Clinton’s interrogation he shook his head doubtfully.

“One can’t tell,” was all he would vouchsafe. “Get these nurses at once.”

And with this he turned on his heel and reentered the room.

===Chinese===
“别闹哄哄的。”克林顿爵士直截了当地吩咐，“咱们不能站在这儿开委员会。要议论，往后有的是工夫。她正要从昏迷中醒过来——至少看着像。无疑是一眼看见了伤了自己的东西，受了惊才昏过去的。阿德斯利来之前，现在无事可做……啊，他来了。好了，霍克赫斯特，咱们走；正事留给专家。”

阿德斯利正拾级而上，手里提着一只药箱。他朝楼梯口的两人短促地点头致意，又探询地点了点头，像是在问该进哪个房间，随即不见了人影，随手带上了门。医生经过时克林顿爵士一声不吭，阿瑟似乎大为惊讶。

“您不打算跟他说一声是怎么回事吗？”他焦急地追问。

“他全都知道了。”克林顿爵士向他担保，却没有多作解释，“稍等片刻再走。”

他等了一两分钟，随后西尔维娅的房门重又打开，阿德斯利走了出来。他平日不动声色的脸上，带着异乎寻常的凝重；应着克林顿爵士的探问，他迟疑地摇了摇头。

“说不准。”他只肯赏这一句。“马上去请护士。”

说罢，他掉转身子，重新进了房。

===Original===
Sir Clinton put his hands into his pockets and stood for a moment or two as though lost in thought. Then suddenly coming to life again, he made his way to the telephone box, where he shook himself free from Arthur on the plea of an urgent call.

When he had given his message through the telephone, the Chief Constable returned to the room in which the attack had been made. Wendover was apparently still busy with his search among the rhododendrons; Vera Forrest was with Sylvia; but the rest of the Whistlefield group were there, waiting to hear the latest news of the victim.

Ernest Shandon’s nerves had evidently suffered severely from this fresh shock. He was sitting in his original seat at the back of the room, his head sunk forward and his eyes staring apathetically at the carpet before him; while in his hand he held a glass of neat whisky which he had just poured out from the decanter beside him. Sir Clinton noticed that the curtains had been drawn in front of the window through which the attack had been made; and he was not far out in believing that this precaution was due to Ernest. It was, in fact, the first thing he had done, once he had found leisure for it.

Howard Torrance and Stenness were standing together near the fireplace. Howard, manifestly, was still in ignorance of some details of the tragedy; and he was endeavouring to extract them from Stenness by a series of eager questions. But the secretary, for once, seemed to have lost his efficiency. He was obviously replying almost at random; and his whole bearing was that of a man disturbed by a trivial interruption while in the midst of some intense preoccupation with another subject. His appearance suggested that of a man suddenly oppressed by an unexpected and intolerable calamity. Sir Clinton’s eyes narrowed as he swept his glance over the secretary’s face.

“He seems to be the most anxious of the lot,” he commented to himself.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士双手插进衣袋，站了一两分钟，仿佛出了神。随即他猛然回过魂来，走向电话间，在那儿借口有个紧急电话，把阿瑟甩脱了。

电话里交代完话，郡警察局长回到了行凶的那间房。温多弗显然还在杜鹃花堤间忙着搜寻；维拉·福雷斯特陪着西尔维娅；惠斯特菲尔德一伙其余的人都在，等着听伤者的最新消息。

这新的一惊，显然把欧内斯特·香农的神经狠狠挫伤了一回。他坐在屋里后部自己原先的座位上，头垂在胸前，两眼呆呆地瞪着面前的地毯；手里端着一杯不掺水的威士忌，刚从手边的玻璃酒瓶里斟出来的。克林顿爵士注意到，行凶的那扇窗前已经拉上了窗帘；他猜想这番防备出自欧内斯特，料得八九不离十。事实上，一腾出空来，他头一件事办的就是这个。

霍华德·托伦斯和斯滕内斯一道站在壁炉近旁。霍华德分明还不知道这场悲剧的若干细节，正一连声急切追问斯滕内斯，想把它们挖出来。可这位秘书，破天荒头一遭，像是失了他的干练。他分明几乎是随口应答；他整个人的神态，活像一个正深深惦念着另一桩事、却被区区一点打岔搅扰了的人。他的模样，又像一个骤然被一场始料未及、无法承受的灾祸压倒的人。克林顿爵士的目光扫过秘书的脸庞，双眼眯了起来。

“这一伙人里，数他最焦灼。”他心里评了一句。

===Original===
Arthur Hawkhurst had been standing at the window with his back to the room, but as Sir Clinton came in he swung round. His face seemed disfigured by a tumult of emotions: anger, distrust, and anxiety were clearly written on it.

“Well,” he demanded sharply, “can you tell us any more?”

“You heard what Ardsley said yourself,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “I haven’t seen him since then.”

Arthur glared at him with unconcealed fury.

“It’s easy enough to see that it isn’t your sister that’s lying at death’s door! You mightn’t be so cool about it then.”

He turned back to the window again, and stared out into the night.

===Chinese===
阿瑟·霍克赫斯特一直背对房间站在窗前，克林顿爵士一进来，他便霍地转过身。他那张脸仿佛被一团翻腾的情绪搅得变了形：愤怒、猜忌与忧急，清清楚楚写在上面。

“怎么样，”他尖利地质问道，“你还有什么能再告诉我们的？”

“阿德斯利的话你自己也听见了。”克林顿爵士指出，“打那以后，我没再见过他。”

阿瑟毫不掩饰怒火，瞪着他。

“躺在鬼门关口边的又不是你的姐姐，这还看不出来吗！换了你，未必还这样沉得住气。”

他又转回窗前，瞪视着外面的黑夜。

===Original===
“What *has* happened?” Howard Torrance demanded. “You’re the only one here who saw it all, Sir Clinton.”

“Someone took advantage of the music from the loudspeaker to steal up close to the window, there, which Mr. Shandon insisted on opening. An airgun dart was fired into the room and struck Miss Hawkhurst. Luckily, it happened to hit her wrist just where there was some protection—the chain of her watch-bracelet; and that prevented it from going as deep as it might. But if any poison has got into the wound, it may be a serious matter—most serious. That’s all I know, except that I got Dr. Ardsley over immediately, and he has her in his charge.”

“Is there any hope that it won’t be fatal this time?” Howard Torrance asked, anxiously.

Sir Clinton shook his head.

“I know as little as you do. I got the dart out almost immediately, so perhaps the poison hadn’t time to get in its work. That seems to offer some chance of escape. But you’ll need to wait for the expert’s views. I really know nothing.”

“And you don’t seem to be doing anything,” snarled Arthur from the window.

===Chinese===
“到底*出了*什么事？”霍华德·托伦斯追问，“这里头只有您一个人从头看到了尾，克林顿爵士。”

“有人借着扩音喇叭的音乐掩护，摸到了那扇窗子近旁——就是香农先生非要打开的那扇。一支气枪飞镖射进房来，打中了霍克赫斯特小姐。万幸，它恰好打在她手腕上有点遮护的地方——腕表手链的链子上；这才没让它扎得像原本可能的那样深。可只要有毒进了伤口，事情就严重了——严重至极。我知道的就这些，另外就是我立刻把阿德斯利博士请了过来，病人现在归他照管。”

“这一次有没有指望不致命？”霍华德·托伦斯焦急地问。

克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

“我知道的不比你们多。我几乎是当下就把飞镖取了出来，所以毒也许还没来得及发作。这似乎给脱险留了一线指望。可你们得等专家的意见。我真的什么都不知道。”

“可你呢，倒像是什么也不做。”阿瑟在窗边恶声吼道。

===Original===
Before Sir Clinton needed to reply, the door opened and Wendover hurried into the room. He was dishevelled, his tie was loose, and his dinner-jacket showed in some places smears of green and brown which he had evidently picked up during his prolonged search. But in his hand he carried the thing Sir Clinton wanted—the airgun.

“Good man!” the Chief Constable commented, as his eyes rested on the weapon.

At the exclamation Arthur turned back towards the room. His face changed as he caught sight of the thing that Wendover carried.

“Where did you get that, eh? That’s my best airgun!”

“That’s the thing that may have killed your sister, then,” said Wendover, looking mistrustfully at Arthur’s disturbed face. “I found it in that clump of rhododendrons out there. It had been jammed right into the middle of the bushes; that’s why it took so long to find.”

He looked Arthur up and down for a moment; then, disregarding the owner’s outstretched hand, he passed the airgun to Sir Clinton, who took it from him without a word. Arthur stepped forward angrily as though to recover his property; but at that moment a fresh interruption occurred. Again the door opened, but this time the grim figure of Ardsley appeared on the threshold. He waited for a moment until he saw that he had secured the attention of them all, then he turned towards Sir Clinton and gave him his verdict.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士还不及答话，门开了，温多弗匆匆进了房。他头发蓬乱，领带松垮，晚宴服上东一块绿西一块棕的污渍，分明是长时间搜寻蹭上的。可他手里提着的，正是克林顿爵士要的东西——那支气枪。

“好样的！”郡警察局长赞了一句，目光落在这件武器上。

听得这声喝彩，阿瑟转回身来。一看见温多弗手里提的东西，他的脸色变了。

“你从哪儿弄来的，嗯？那是我最好的一支气枪！”

“那么说，这就是那件差点儿送了你姐姐命的东西。”温多弗说，一面带疑虑地望着阿瑟那张失态的脸，“我在外头那丛杜鹃花里找到的。它被人硬塞进了灌木丛正当中；所以才找了那么久。”

他把阿瑟上下打量了片刻；随后，并不理会主人伸出的手，把气枪递给了克林顿爵士，爵士一言不发地接了过去。阿瑟怒冲冲抢上一步，像要夺回自己的东西；可就在这时，又来了一重新的打断。门再度打开，这一回，门槛上出现的是阿德斯利那神情严峻的身影。他等了一等，直到看清自己抓住了所有人的注意，才转向克林顿爵士，宣布了他的判断。

===Original===
“This is a bad business! Of course, she’s still alive; and there’s a chance yet. It’s a pity you didn’t think of a tourniquet at the moment—prevent any risk of the stuff spreading, since it’s an isolated limb. But there’s no use grumbling now. We can only wait and see if she pulls through. It’s a bad business!”

Sir Clinton nodded.

“Have you everything you need? The nurses will be here as soon as possible.”

“Miss Forrest will do in the meantime. One thing—there must be absolute quietness in the house. I can’t have my patient disturbed in the slightest degree. She’s unconscious again; but there must be no risk of disturbing her later on. Complete quiet, or I won’t answer for anything.”

He turned and left the room without waiting for any questions. The gravity of his expression was enough to show them that he had no great hope for Sylvia’s safety.

===Chinese===
“事情不妙！当然，她还活着；机会也还有。可惜你当时没想到用止血带——既然伤的是孤立的一条胳膊，那本可以防住毒物扩散的一切风险。不过现在抱怨也无用了。我们只能等着看她的造化，看她挺不挺得过来。事情不妙！”

克林顿爵士点了点头。

“需要的东西你都有了吗？护士会尽快到的。”

“眼下有福雷斯特小姐顶一顶就行。只一条——宅子里必须绝对安静。我的病人不能受丝毫惊扰。她又昏迷过去了；但往后也决不能冒一点惊扰她的风险。完全安静，不然出了任何事，我概不负责。”

他不等任何人发问，转身出了房。他神情之凝重，足以让众人明白：对西尔维娅能否脱险，他不抱多大指望。

## XIII The Dart / 十三 飞镖

===Original===
The period immediately following the attack upon Sylvia was one of intense inquietude in Wendover’s mind. Up to that point he had persuaded himself that the affairs at Whistlefield would eventually prove to be linked up in some way with the Hackleton case. The connection of some of the incidents—the attack on Ernest Shandon, for one—had certainly been obscure; but Wendover had nursed an irrational belief that in the end all the threads would lead back to Hackleton, and that the whole mystery would find a simple explanation which would bring it within the borders of normal motives and sane sequences of actions.

The latest tragedy, however, could not be squared with any of his preconceived ideas. What possible relationship could exist between Hackleton and Sylvia which would make her removal essential to the financier? It was hardly likely that either she or Ernest had been the repository of Neville Shandon’s secrets.

But if Hackleton dropped out of the piece, then the whole affair seemed to lose any thread of purpose and to become a mere massacre perpetrated by some being urged on by motives which lay outside the bounds of reason. Instead of a coldly calculating criminal, Wendover seemed to find himself confronted by a creature beyond the pale of humanity, a thing that slew at random out of sheer lust for death. His own normal mind revolted from such a monster; and he strove hard to piece the evidence together again in some way which would eliminate this nightmare figure and replace it by a criminal actuated by motives which sane intellects could grasp.

As soon as he got Sir Clinton alone after the tragedy at Whistlefield, he had done his best to extort information; but in this he had failed completely. Every one of his inquiries was met by a curt denial of any ulterior knowledge, though it was manifest that Sir Clinton was concentrating his whole mind on the latest developments in the Whistlefield affair. Despite this blank negation, however, the Squire got the impression that the Chief Constable’s anxiety centred round Sylvia rather than the Whistlefield case as a whole. From an unguarded word he inferred that Sir Clinton had, somehow or other, taken a risk; and that the results had been very different from what he had expected. Something had cut across Sir Clinton’s schemes and had shaken his confidence.

===Chinese===
袭击西尔维娅之后的那些时日，温多弗心中焦灼不安。在那之前，他一直让自己相信，惠斯特菲尔德的种种事端到头来总会以某种方式与哈克尔顿案挂上钩。其中若干事件的关联——譬如对欧内斯特·香农的那次袭击——固然始终暧昧不明；但温多弗怀抱着一种没有来由的信念，认为最终所有线索都会引回哈克尔顿，整桩疑案会得到一个简单的解释，被收进正常的动机与神志清明的行为序列的疆界之内。

然而，这最新的一场悲剧，与他任何先入之见都对不上榫。哈克尔顿与西尔维娅之间能有什么样的关系，竟会让这位金融家觉得非把她除掉不可？说她或欧内斯特是内维尔·香农机密的保管人，这也几乎无从设想。

可要是哈克尔顿从这出戏里退了场，整桩事情似乎便失了任何目的的线索，沦为一场纯粹的屠戮，出自行凶者那越出理性疆界之外的动机的驱策。温多弗觉得自己面对的仿佛不再是一个冷酷盘算的罪犯，而是一头流落在人性藩篱之外的生物，一件纯然出于嗜杀欲而随手行凶的东西。他那颗正常人的心对这样一头怪物深恶痛绝；他竭力想把证据重新拼合出个说法，好把这道噩梦般的影子排除掉，换上一个动机尚能为神志清明的头脑所理解的罪犯。

惠斯特菲尔德的悲剧发生后，温多弗一逮着单独面对克林顿爵士的机会，便竭力想从他嘴里撬出些内情；可这一层他完完全全地失败了。他的每一步追问，都换来一句干脆的否认——再无所知；尽管明摆着，克林顿爵士正把全副心思都押在惠斯特菲尔德案最新的事态上。然而，纵有这一片斩钉截铁的否定，乡绅仍得到一个印象：郡警察局长的忧急，与其说是围着整桩惠斯特菲尔德案，不如说是围着西尔维娅一个人打转。从一句失了防备的口风里，他推断出克林顿爵士不知怎么竟冒了一回险；而结果与他所预期的迥然不同。有什么东西横截断了克林顿爵士的盘算，动摇了他的自信。

===Original===
Even when he abandoned his fruitless inquisition and went to bed, Wendover was unable to free himself from the latest tragedy. His mind insisted on conjuring up pictures: some of them memories, others imaginary scenes in which the unknown murderer played his part. He saw the bridge-table at the end of the rubber, with the cards of the last trick lying still ungathered, Sir Clinton putting down the marker, a cigarette smouldering on the ashtray, Vera Forrest shuffling the pack for the next deal. Nothing could have been more peaceful. Then, in a flash, came the transformation scene. He lived again through the nightmare moment when the lethal dart sped in upon them from the outer dark, changing their fancied security into a thing of horror and peril. And from this his imagination passed to that lurking monster in the gloom beyond the window: a vague, featureless figure, crouching among the rhododendrons, lifting the thin barrel of the airgun in search of the appointed victim. In uneasy visions such as these, his night dragged slowly on.

Morning brought Wendover no release from his anxiety. Before he had come downstairs, Sir Clinton had been busy with the telephone; and his face was sufficient to show that he had had bad news. Wendover hardly dared to ask what it was; for his guest’s features plainly betrayed that the worst might be expected.

“Ardsley’s been telephoning,” Sir Clinton explained briefly. “She’s much worse. There was a bad collapse in the early morning and they just managed to pull her through. Luckily the nurses were on the spot, so everything was done that could be done. But Ardsley seems to have very little hope now. He thinks the dose of the poison must have been bigger than we thought.”

He bit his lip, seemed on the verge of saying something else, then ended by changing his mind and choosing other words: “We must go across there after breakfast, Squire, I must see Ardsley. You’ve no idea how this affair worries me.”

===Chinese===
即便撇下那场徒劳的盘问上了床，温多弗仍摆脱不开这最新的一场悲剧。他的脑子执意要变出些画面来：有些是记忆，有些是想出来的场景，那不知名的凶手在其中扮演着他的角色。他看见一局打完时的桥牌桌，最后一墩的牌还摊着没收，克林顿爵士放下记分牌，一支香烟在烟灰缸里幽幽地燃着，维拉·福雷斯特正洗着下一局的牌。再没有比这更安宁的景象了。随后，电光石火间，换景的一刻来了。他再度经历那噩梦般的瞬间：致命的飞镖从外面的黑暗里飞射而入，把他们自以为的安稳化作一件恐怖与凶险之物。由此，他的想象又移向窗外幽暗里那头潜伏的怪物：一个模糊的、没有眉目的身影，蹲伏在杜鹃花丛中，抬起气枪细瘦的枪管，搜索那注定的牺牲者。就在这类不安的幻象里，他的黑夜磨磨蹭蹭地挨了过去。

早晨并未把温多弗从忧虑中解脱出来。还没等他下楼，克林顿爵士已经打了好一阵电话；他的脸色足以表明消息不妙。温多弗几乎不敢问是什么；因为他这位客人的面容明白地显出，最坏的情形恐怕要来了。

“阿德斯利来过电话了。”克林顿爵士简短地解释道，“她情况恶化得很厉害。凌晨虚脱了一回，情形很糟，他们好歹把她抢救了回来。幸亏护士们就在跟前，能做的都做了。可如今阿德斯利似乎不抱什么指望了。他认为进入体内的毒量，准是比我们原先想的要大。”

他咬了咬嘴唇，像还有别的话要说，临了却变了主意，另拣了一句：“早饭后我们必须过那边去一趟，乡绅，我得见见阿德斯利。这桩事情多么让我焦心，你是想象不到的。”

===Original===
“I think I have a fair notion,” Wendover replied. “I’ve had a pretty bad night over it myself. It’s a damnable affair.”

Sir Clinton nodded absentmindedly. He was evidently lost in his thoughts. By the set of his mouth, Wendover could guess that they were anything but pleasant.

Though he hardly admitted it to himself, Sir Clinton’s behaviour was another factor which had loosened Wendover’s grip on the normal world. Hitherto the Chief Constable had seemed so sure of his case that he had treated it almost lightly; but now it was self-evident that something had gone wrong. Things had not worked out according to plan. The tragedy which he had predicted had forced itself into being; but now that it had come he appeared unable to act the part of the deus ex machina which he seemed to have meant to play. This sudden change disturbed Wendover deeply. The man on whom he had been relying to clear up the mystery appeared to be perplexed and anxious instead of cool and resolute.

===Chinese===
“我想我体会得到几分。”温多弗答道，“为着它，我自己也熬过了一个不折不扣的苦夜。这桩事可恶透顶。”

克林顿爵士心不在焉地点了点头。他分明是陷在沉思里了。从他嘴部的神情，温多弗猜得出那些念头决计不是什么愉快的。

克林顿爵士的举止，是松开了温多弗攥着正常世界的那只手的又一个因素，尽管他自己几乎不肯向自己承认。迄今为止，这位郡警察局长对案子仿佛成竹在胸，几乎拿它不当回事；可现在，事情出了岔子已是有目共睹。事态并没有照着计划发展。他预言过的那场悲剧硬是逼着自己成了真；可它既已来临，他却仿佛演不动那个解围之神（deus ex machina）的角色了——他原先似乎正是打算扮这么一角的。这骤然的变化让温多弗深感不安。他一直指望这个人来揭开谜底，可此人如今显得又困惑又焦灼，全不是先前那副冷静果决的样子。

===Original===
When they reached Whistlefield, Ernest Shandon was the first person who came to meet them.

“This is a terrible business!” he lamented, as he came into the study where they were. “It’s a dreadful affair, really. A dreadful affair! Ardsley’s very down about it, very down. You know, he wouldn’t do for a doctor in practice. He’s most unsympathetic. Most doctors are careful: they don’t blurt things out in the callous sort of way that Ardsley does. He doesn’t think about one’s feelings in the slightest. One expects a little decent circumlocution from a doctor; but there’s none of that about him. I asked him this morning if Sylvia had passed a good night; and he just glared at me and snarled that she was lucky to be alive at all; snarled it out as if she had been one of the dogs he cuts up. Is that the way to break bad news to a relation? I call it beastly. He never thinks of what it means to us. It’s just a case to him, I suppose. But look what it means to us. Sylvia runs the house so well. I don’t know what we’ll do without her.”

Sir Clinton had let him run on; but quite evidently he had no intention of wasting much time listening to Ernest’s lamentations.

===Chinese===
他们到达惠斯特菲尔德时，头一个迎上来的便是欧内斯特·香农。

“这真是件可怕的事！”他一边哀叹，一边走进他们所在的书房，“这桩事骇人，实在骇人！阿德斯利为这个垂头丧气得很，垂头丧气得很。你们知道，他根本不配出来挂牌行医。他最没有同情心。大多数医生都谨慎：不会像阿德斯利那样，把事情没心没肺地脱口而出。他对人的感受丝毫不放在心上。谁找医生，总指望得到一点像样的委婉曲折；可他身上一丝一毫也没有。今天早晨我问西尔维娅夜里睡得好不好；他竟瞪着我，恶声恶气地说她能活着就算走运；那话说得咬牙切齿，倒像她是他解剖的一条狗似的。这就是向亲属通报噩耗的方式吗？我说这简直恶劣透顶。他从来不想想这对我们意味着什么。我猜，这在他不过是一个病例罢了。可你瞧瞧这对我们意味着什么。西尔维娅把这个家操持得那么好。没有她，真不知道我们可怎么办。”

克林顿爵士由着他说下去；但显而易见，他并不打算多费工夫听欧内斯特的哀叹。

===Original===
“Miss Forrest must be resting just now, I suppose?”

“Yes,” Ernest assured him, “she was up helping Ardsley until the nurses came; and after that she didn’t seem able to sleep, so she sat up for a while. Ardsley came down and found her in the early morning, so he sent her off to bed. So he told me. I had gone to bed myself some time before.”

Sir Clinton made no comment and Ernest proceeded with his complaints.

“What I feel is that the police aren’t doing anything. Why haven’t you arrested somebody? My nerves are beginning to wear thin under this strain, I tell you. Here we have some murderer haunting the neighbourhood. He kills my brothers; he attacks me; he brings my niece to death’s door—and all the time the police look on with their hands in their pockets. What are they paid for? That’s what I ask you. Why don’t they lay hands on the fellow? What sort of a life do you think I’m leading just now? Every time I go outside the house I have the feeling that the scoundrel may be lurking behind the next bush, getting his gun ready. That’s a pretty state of things. And not a finger do you lift to help!”

“I offered you a guard of constables for Whistlefield not so long ago, Mr. Shandon. You refused it then. I’m sorry it isn’t available now. I have other work for my men at present.”

===Chinese===
“福雷斯特小姐这会儿想必正在歇着吧？”

“是啊。”欧内斯特向他担保，“护士们来之前，她一直起着身帮阿德斯利的忙；过后她像是睡不着，便又坐了一阵。凌晨时分阿德斯利下楼来撞见了她，就打发她上床去了。这是他告诉我的。我自己呢，比这早些时候就睡了。”

克林顿爵士未作评论，欧内斯特接着数落下去。

“我心中的感受是：警察什么也没干。你们怎么还不抓人？我告诉你们，在这种折磨底下，我的神经已经开始撑不住了。眼下就有那么一个杀人犯在这一带出没。他杀了我的两个哥哥；他袭击了我；他把我的外甥女逼到了鬼门关口——而警察呢，自始至终袖着手站在一边看。他们的薪水是拿来讲什么的？我倒要请问你。他们为什么还不动手拿人？你想想我这阵子过的是什么日子？每次一出这宅子，我就觉得那恶棍说不定正蹲在下一丛灌木后头，把他的枪都端好了。这算什么样的一种光景。可你们连一根指头也不抬来帮帮忙！”

“不久前我提出过给惠斯特菲尔德派一队警士守卫，香农先生。那时候你回绝了。很抱歉，现在办不到了。我的人手眼下另有差事。”

===Original===
Ernest was somewhat taken aback by this reminder.

“So you did, so you did. I’d forgotten that.”

Sir Clinton seemed inclined to accept this as an apology.

“I should like to see Mr. Stenness for a moment in private, if you don’t mind, Mr. Shandon. Could you send him to me?”

Ernest evidently felt that he had let his tongue run away with him. Possibly some faint realisation of the display of cowardice which he had made was dawning upon his mind. At any rate, he hastened to meet Sir Clinton’s wish.

“I’ll hunt him up and send him to you,” he announced with surprising conciseness; and he left the room without further talk.

===Chinese===
这一提醒让欧内斯特有些下不来台。

“你确实提过，确实提过。我把这个给忘了。”
