克林顿爵士似乎愿意把这当作一句道歉收下。

“要是你不介意的话，香农先生，我想单独见斯滕内斯先生一会儿。能请你让他到我这里来吗？”

欧内斯特分明感到自己失了口。或许，一丝对自己那副贪生怕死嘴脸的模糊领悟，正在他心里透出光来。无论如何，他赶忙去照克林顿爵士的意思办。

“我去把他找到，打发他到您这儿来。”他宣布得出奇地干脆；随后不再多话，径自出了房。

===Original===
While they were waiting for Stenness the door opened and Arthur Hawkhurst came in. Rather to Wendover’s surprise he showed no trace of the ill-feeling which he had displayed so strongly on the previous night. Instead, he seemed rather shamefaced; and he opened in an unexpected vein.

“I behaved like a young cub last night, Sir Clinton,” he admitted frankly. “I daresay I said a lot of things that I shouldn’t have said. But you know quite well”—his teeth showed in an engaging smile—“I was badly upset. Anyone might be, I think. Poor Sylvia! I’m deuced fond of her, you know. She’s about the only person in the world that matters a tinker’s curse to me. So naturally I wasn’t quite levelheaded; and I daresay I said things I shouldn’t have said.”

“That’s all right,” Sir Clinton assured him. “I understood perfectly how you felt. Forget it, and don’t worry. You’ve trouble enough without bothering about trifles just now.”

Arthur nodded a gloomy acquiescence.

===Chinese===
他们等着斯滕内斯的当口，门开了，进来的是阿瑟·霍克赫斯特。颇让温多弗意外的是，昨夜他表现得分外强烈的敌意，此刻竟无影无踪。相反，他倒像是有些惭愧；而且一开口就出了个想不到的调子。

“昨晚我的举动像个愣头愣脑的毛头小子，克林顿爵士。”他坦白地承认，“我许是说了好些不该说的话。可您也完全明白”——他龇出牙齿，绽出一个讨人喜欢的笑——“我当时难过极了。换了谁都一样，我想。可怜的西尔维娅！您知道，我是着实地疼她。这世上我真正放在心上的，拢共也就她一个人。所以我自然有点儿昏了头；说了什么不该说的话，多半也是有的。”

“没什么。”克林顿爵士宽慰他道，“你的心情我完全明白。忘了它吧，别放在心上。眼下你的烦恼已经够多了，犯不上再为这点小事操心。”

阿瑟郁郁地点头认可了。

===Original===
“Have you any notion why the thing was done?”

Sir Clinton was careful not to give a direct answer.

“We’re doing our best.”

Arthur’s eye lighted up.

“I wish you’d let me take a hand. Perhaps I could be of some use?”

“Not just at present, I’m afraid.”

Arthur took the rejection badly.

===Chinese===
“这事为什么有人要干，您有眉目吗？”

克林顿爵士小心着不直接作答。

“我们正在尽力。”

阿瑟的眼睛亮了起来。

“我巴不得您让我搭一把手。兴许我还能派上点用场？”

“眼下恐怕还不行。”

这回绝让阿瑟很不受用。

===Original===
“Nothing to hinder my working on my own, then, is there? You can’t prevent that. And if I come across the brute you needn’t expect to be allowed to butt in then, you know. I’ll tackle him myself. Hanging’s too good for him.”

“I agree with you there,” Sir Clinton said unguardedly. Then he added with a faint smile: “We’re speaking quite unofficially, of course.”

Arthur looked up suspiciously.

“I’m not quite sure what you mean. But what *I* mean’s quite plain and can be put into plain English. If I can lay my hands on the man who tried to murder Sylvia, he’ll wish for a decent hanging before I’m done with him. I’ll …”

“That’s enough, Mr. Hawkhurst,” Sir Clinton interrupted sharply. “We don’t want to hear about it.”

===Chinese===
“那么，单干总没人拦得了吧？这个你们可管不着。真叫我撞上那畜生，到时候您也别指望能插手，我把话放在这儿。我要亲手收拾他。绞死他都嫌便宜了他。”

“这一点我赞成你。”克林顿爵士一时未加提防地说道。随即他带一丝淡淡的笑意补了一句：“当然，咱们这话全然是非官方的。”

阿瑟狐疑地抬起了眼。

“我不大明白您的意思。可*我*的意思一清二楚，用大白话也说得出来。只要我能拿住那个谋害西尔维娅的家伙，等我对付完了他，他该会巴望痛痛快快被绞死才好。我要……”

“够了，霍克赫斯特先生。”克林顿爵士厉声打断，“这个我们不想听。”

===Original===
Arthur’s temper boiled up at the words. Wendover, glancing at his face, saw the features contorted in hardly-restrained fury. With an effort, the boy fought down his anger until he could speak.

“If anything happens to Sylvia I’ll get the brute yet; and then he’ll wish he’d never been born. That’s that!”

He swung round on his heel and left the room.

Sir Clinton sighed slightly as the door closed.

===Chinese===
这话让阿瑟的火气腾地冒了上来。温多弗瞥了一眼他的脸，只见那张面孔在勉强按捺的狂怒中扭得走了形。这少年费了好大的劲，才把怒火压下去，压到自己能开口说话。

“西尔维娅要是有个三长两短，我迟早要逮住那畜生；到那时候，他就会懊悔自己不该生到这世上来。就是这样！”

他脚跟一转，旋风似的出了房。

门关上时，克林顿爵士轻轻叹了口气。

===Original===
“Oh, Lord!” he exclaimed softly, as if to himself. “I hadn’t reckoned on *that*. This is growing devilishly complicated.”

Wendover had pricked up his ears.

“What’s the trouble now?”

Sir Clinton seemed to realise that he had spoken his thoughts aloud.

“It’s another factor that I hadn’t allowed for,” he admitted. But he refused to divulge anything further; and Wendover had to content himself with the cryptic phrase.

Stenness did not keep them waiting long. When he came into the study, Wendover was surprised to see the change which the night seemed to have made in the secretary’s appearance. He was heavy-eyed; and his features had a drawn expression as though he had passed through some great strain.

===Chinese===
“唉，天哪！”他轻轻地叹道，像是说给自己听，“*这一层*我可没算到。事情变得复杂得要命。”

温多弗竖起了耳朵。

“现在又出了什么岔子？”

克林顿爵士像是意识到自己把心里话说出了口。

“那又是一个我没算计到的因素。”他承认道。但他拒绝再吐露半个字；温多弗只得拿这句没头没尾的话聊以自安。

斯滕内斯没有让他们久等。他走进书房时，温多弗吃惊地看到，一夜之间这位秘书的容颜仿佛换了副样子。他眼皮沉重；面容上带着一种紧绷绷的憔悴，像是刚熬过一场巨大的折磨。

===Original===
“I suppose we all look a bit like that, after this affair,” Wendover commented to himself. “Clinton’s half killing himself with anxiety; young Hawkhurst’s far from normal; and I suppose I must look a bit white about the gills myself. It’s only to be expected.”

Sir Clinton wasted no time on preliminaries, but came to the point at once.

“Mr. Shandon told us that you knew the contents of Roger Shandon’s will. Can you give me the gist of it? It’s not a confidential document now, of course.”

“There’s a copy of it in the safe here,” Stenness explained. “You can look it over if you like.”

“Thanks. But if you can remember the main points it may save me the trouble of reading through it.”

Stenness took a key from his pocket and went across to open the safe which was built into the wall of the study.

===Chinese===
“经过这桩事，想必我们哪个的脸色都有几分不好看。”温多弗心里评道，“克林顿焦心得快要把自己熬干了；小霍克赫斯特也远非正常；我估摸着我自己的脸色想必也发白。这原是意料中事。”

克林顿爵士不在客套上费工夫，开门就见山。

“香农先生告诉我们，罗杰·香农的遗嘱内容你是知道的。能把大意讲给我听吗？当然，如今这已不是什么机密文件了。”

“这里保险柜里就有一份副本。”斯滕内斯解释道，“您愿意的话可以过目。”

“多谢。不过你若记得要点，或许能省我通读一遍的工夫。”

斯滕内斯从口袋里掏出一把钥匙，走过去打开嵌在书房墙壁里的保险柜。

===Original===
“The will’s simple enough. All the property is to be divided equally between Neville Shandon, Ernest Shandon, Miss Hawkhurst, and Arthur Hawkhurst. There’s the usual provision about heirs and survivors of that group.”

“What I particularly want to know is whether there’s any residuary legatee mentioned, anybody who takes the remainder of the estate after all other legacies have been paid in full.”

“I don’t remember any provision of that sort,” Stenness admitted, searching among the papers in the safe. “Here’s the copy of the will if you’d care to look at it.”

He handed it over to Sir Clinton who unfolded it and began to read.

===Chinese===
“遗嘱简单得很。全部财产由内维尔·香农、欧内斯特·香农、霍克赫斯特小姐与阿瑟·霍克赫斯特四人平分。照例还有一条规定，言及这一组人的继承人与余存者。”

“我特别想知道的是，遗嘱里提没提剩余遗产继承人——就是在其他各项遗赠全部付清之后，承受产业余数的那个人。”

“我不记得有过那样的条款。”斯滕内斯承认道，一面在保险柜里的文件中翻找，“这是遗嘱副本，您要不要过目。”

他把它递给克林顿爵士，爵士展开来，读了起来。

===Original===
“He left you nothing, did he?” the Chief Constable asked casually, as he continued his study of the document.

Stenness was plainly surprised by the question.

“No. Why should he? I’ve only been with him a year or two. I’m not an old family retainer who’s earned a pension. As a matter of fact, there are no bequests of the kind.”

“So I see,” Sir Clinton agreed when he had finished his reading. “It’s a very short will, not complicated by any of the provisions they often put into these things.”

He seemed to ponder over the matter for a moment or two.

“I had rather expected to find a residuary legatee in the thing somewhere; but you’re quite right, there’s nothing of the sort mentioned. You don’t happen to know anything about Neville Shandon’s will, do you? It wouldn’t fall into your province.”

===Chinese===
“他什么也没留给你，是吧？”郡警察局长一边继续研究那份文件，一边随口问道。

这个问题显然让斯滕内斯吃了一惊。

“没有。他为什么要留给我？我跟他不过一两年。我又不是挣下了养老金的老家仆。事实上，这份遗嘱里没有任何那一类的遗赠。”

“我看到了。”克林顿爵士读完，附和道，“这份遗嘱很短，没有被人们常往这类文书里塞的那些条款搅得复杂。”

他就这事似乎沉吟了片刻。

“我原本满指望在这份东西里能见到一位剩余遗产继承人；可你说得对，只字未提。内维尔·香农的遗嘱，你碰巧知道些什么吗？这本不在你的分内。”

===Original===
Stenness shook his head.

“I never read it. But I witnessed it, as it happens. And the impression I got from a glance at the last page was that it may have run on the same lines as Roger’s. You can easily get a copy of it once it’s filed, if you need it.”

Sir Clinton handed back the will and rose to his feet as the secretary restored the document to the safe.

“I see you have a key of that thing?”

===Chinese===
斯滕内斯摇了摇头。

“我从没读过它。不过碰巧，我在上面做过见证人。我从最后一页扫过一眼所得的印象是，它说不定与罗杰那份如出一辙。您若需要，等它一备案，弄一份副本是很容易的。”

克林顿爵士把遗嘱递还回去；秘书把文件放回保险柜时，他也站起了身。

“我看你这柜子是配有钥匙的？”

===Original===
Stenness closed the safe and put the key back into his pocket.

“Yes, Mr. Shandon told me to keep this one. I’ve been arranging the papers for him and it was more convenient that I should have the key. It saved him the bother of always handing it over when I needed it.”

“You hadn’t a key in Roger Shandon’s time?”

“No, Roger was rather a different sort of person.”

“By the way, Mr. Stenness, are you staying on here as secretary to Ernest Shandon?”

===Chinese===
斯滕内斯锁上保险柜，把钥匙放回口袋。

“是的，香农先生吩咐我把这把钥匙留在身边。我一直替他整理文件，钥匙由我拿着更便利些。省得我要用时，回回都得劳他交付一道。”

“罗杰·香农在世的时候，你没有钥匙？”

“没有，罗杰那个人可是另一种做派。”

“对了，斯滕内斯先生，你还打算留在这儿，给欧内斯特·香农当秘书吗？”

===Original===
Stenness seemed slightly taken aback by the question.

“There’s no definite arrangement, so far. I’m staying until the estate affairs have been cleared up; but after that I doubt if I shall remain here. I can do better than this.”

“I suppose you could,” Sir Clinton agreed indifferently.

He looked at his watch.

===Chinese===
这个问题似乎让斯滕内斯微微一怔。

“到现在还没有一定的安排。我会留到产业的事务清理妥当；在那之后，我怀疑自己还会不会留在这儿。我能找到比这更好的去处。”

“想必能。”克林顿爵士淡淡地附和了一句。

他看了看表。

===Original===
“I want to see Dr. Ardsley now. I’m rather in a hurry at present; but there are one or two more questions I want to put to you sometime, Mr. Stenness. Will you be free after dinner tonight? Very well, I’ll come across then. Now, if you could let Dr. Ardsley know I’m here.”

Stenness was evidently a prompt messenger, for Ardsley appeared almost at once. Wendover scanned his face eagerly as he came into the room. Here was the person who might be able to set their minds at ease. But Ardsley’s countenance gave him no cause for raising his spirits. It betrayed nothing but gloom and anxiety.

===Chinese===
“我现在要去见阿德斯利博士。眼下我相当赶时间；不过，还有一两个问题我日后要向你提一提，斯滕内斯先生。今晚晚饭后你有空吗？很好，到时候我过来一趟。现在，麻烦你通知阿德斯利博士一声，我来了。”

斯滕内斯传话显然利落，阿德斯利几乎立刻就到了。他走进房间时，温多弗急切地端详他的脸。就是这个人，或许能让他们的心放下来。可阿德斯利的面容没有给他半点振作的理由。那张脸上除了阴郁与忧急，什么也没有。

===Original===
“She’s much worse. I’d hoped for a rally after that attack in the night, but she hasn’t pulled herself together.”

“Tell us plainly what you think,” demanded Sir Clinton. “You needn’t beat about the bush where we’re concerned.”

Ardsley’s face seemed to grow, if anything, graver than before.

“I can hold out no great hope. Frankly, I think it will be all up soon—tonight, perhaps.”

No one seemed inclined to speak. Wendover was trying to force himself to face what now seemed inevitable. Death often came swiftly; but the circumstances of Sylvia’s tragedy gave it a quality which ordinary deaths do not possess. He could hardly assure himself that the whole thing was not a nightmare. There seemed to be something so aimless in the whole business, the killing of a young girl against whom no one could conceivably harbour any personal grudge. The inhuman purposelessness which had cut Sylvia down on the threshold of her life seemed more terrible to him than any planned scheme would have done; for a calculated crime would imply a motive, whereas this deed seemed to have arisen out of mere chaos—something outside normal things.

===Chinese===
“她恶化得很厉害。夜里那次发作之后，我原指望她能缓过一口气回升，可她始终没能缓上来。”

“直截了当地告诉我们你的想法。”克林顿爵士要求道，“跟我们打交道，用不着拐弯抹角。”

阿德斯利的脸色，如果说还有什么变化的话，是比先前更凝重了。

“我给不出多大的指望了。坦白说，我想这一切很快就完了——或许就在今晚。”

谁也不像是想开口。温多弗正强迫自己去面对那如今似已无法避免的结局。死亡常常来得迅疾；可西尔维娅这桩悲剧的境况，赋予它一种寻常死亡所不具备的性质。他几乎无法让自己相信，这整件事不是一场噩梦。这桩事情从头到尾仿佛如此漫无目的——杀害一个年轻的姑娘，而谁也无法设想会有什么人对她怀有半点私怨。正是这种把西尔维娅斩落在人生门槛上的非人的无目的，在温多弗看来，比任何有预谋的计划都更可怕；因为精心算计的犯罪总还意味着一个动机，而这桩罪行却仿佛纯然从混沌中滋生——出自正常事物之外的什么东西。

===Original===
Sir Clinton took a step towards the door and then seemed to change his mind.

“Do you think you could get some vinegar and some washing soda?” he asked, turning to Ardsley. “There’s something I’d like to be sure about; and it might be as well that an expert should see it.”

Ardsley had no difficulty in procuring what was wanted. As the doctor in charge of Sylvia, he had only to ask for anything. A couple of tumblers and a water-carafe were brought as well, at Sir Clinton’s request.

“Now you can put your back against the door, Squire. We don’t want any visitors.”

From a tiny glass bottle which he drew from his pocket, the Chief Constable extracted one of the ill-omened darts.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士朝门口迈了一步，随即像是变了主意。

“你看你能弄到一些醋和一些洗涤碱吗？”他转向阿德斯利，问道，“有件事我想弄个确实；让一位专家亲眼看看，兴许不无好处。”

阿德斯利毫不费力地弄到了所要的东西。作为西尔维娅的主治医生，他要什么只需开口。应克林顿爵士的要求，还一并送来了两只平底大玻璃杯和一只长颈水瓶。

“现在你可以拿背抵住门了，乡绅。咱们可不想有客上门。”

郡警察局长从口袋里摸出一只小小的玻璃瓶，从里面取出一支带着凶兆的飞镖。

===Original===
“This is the one which wounded Miss Hawkhurst,” he explained, as he dropped it into a glass of water. “Now we’ll need to give it time.”

He stirred it round occasionally; and gradually a faint bluish tinge communicated itself to the water. Ardsley was scrutinising the glass with deep interest, but his face showed nothing of the thoughts in his mind.

“Now we add a drop of vinegar, Squire,” said Sir Clinton, suiting the action to the word.

As the vinegar mixed with the solution, Wendover saw a change in the tint—a pale red replaced the original blue.

===Chinese===
“这就是打伤霍克赫斯特小姐的那一支。”他解释着，把它投进一杯水里，“现在咱们得给它点时间。”

他不时搅它一搅；渐渐地，一抹淡淡的蓝色渗进了水里。阿德斯利怀着浓厚的兴趣端详那只杯子，可他脸上丝毫看不出心里想些什么。

“现在咱们加一滴醋，乡绅。”克林顿爵士说道，说到做到，随即动手。

醋与溶液混和，温多弗看见颜色变了——原先的蓝被一抹淡红取而代之。

===Original===
“Now some washing soda, for a change,” said Sir Clinton, dropping in a crystal and swirling the liquid round in the glass. As he did so, the blue tinge returned to the solution.

Ardsley nodded approvingly.

“Litmus, obviously. That clinches it. You must be a bit of a chemist to have hit on that tip.”

Sir Clinton made no reply, but he cautioned Wendover to bear the test in mind.

“If that’s all you want, I’ll go back to Miss Hawkhurst,” Ardsley said, as soon as Sir Clinton ceased speaking.

===Chinese===
“现在换洗涤碱试试。”克林顿爵士说着，投进一小块晶粒，把杯中液体旋了又旋。随着他的动作，那抹蓝色又回到了溶液里。

阿德斯利赞许地点了点头。

“显然是石蕊。这一下就板上钉钉了。能想出这一招，您准是懂点化学的。”

克林顿爵士没有答话，只是叮嘱温多弗把这个试验记在心里。

“您要的若是仅此而已，我就回霍克赫斯特小姐那边去了。”克林顿爵士话音一落，阿德斯利便说。

===Original===
“We’re going back to the Grange, now,” Sir Clinton explained. “If you need me, you’ve only to ring up.”

“I thought you were in a hurry,” Wendover said in some surprise when he found that Sir Clinton seemed to have nothing on hand on their return to the Grange. “You broke off your talk with Stenness on that excuse. Why not have finished it at the time, instead of trailing over there again later in the day?”

“I’m worried about Miss Hawkhurst, Squire; and I prefer to get my news direct from Ardsley rather than over the phone.”

===Chinese===
“我们这就回农庄去。”克林顿爵士解释道，“需要我的时候，打个电话来就是。”

“我还当你有急事呢。”回到农庄后，见克林顿爵士手头仿佛并无一事，温多弗颇有些诧异地说，“你是拿这话头打断与斯滕内斯的谈话的。何不当时就把它谈完，倒省得晚些时候再磨一趟？”

“我惦记着霍克赫斯特小姐，乡绅；我宁可当面从阿德斯利口中听消息，也不愿隔着电话。”

===Original===
“You didn’t get much out of him this morning,” Wendover complained. “And I can’t think why you put that man into the business at all. It seems to me tempting Providence. Why, he’s quite possibly the source of the original curare, for all you know; he’s one of the suspects.”

“He’s not on my list of suspects, Squire; and if he’s on yours, you may score him off straight away. That’s definite. As to my using him, who could do the work better? What would a country G.P. make of Miss Hawkhurst’s case? Nothing whatever! You can’t expect rural medicos to be the last word in the study of out-of-the-way poisons. It’s not reasonable to ask it.”

===Chinese===
“今天早晨你可没从他嘴里问出多少东西。”温多弗抱怨道，“再说，你为什么要把那个人掺进这桩案子里来，我实在想不通。在我看来，这简直是在触犯天意。哎呀，说不定那批箭毒的头一个出处就是他，你哪里知道？他本来就是嫌疑犯之一。”

“他不在我的嫌疑名单上，乡绅；倘若他在你的名单上，你尽管把他一笔划掉。这是明确的。至于我用他——这活儿谁能干得比他更好？换了乡下的全科医生，能从霍克赫斯特小姐的病情里看出什么名堂？半点也看不出！你不能指望乡间大夫在冷僻毒物的研究上做到登峰造极。这样要求是没有道理的。”

===Original===
Wendover’s increasing disquietude found its relief in speech at last.

“I can’t see what your aim is in this affair, Clinton. You say you know the murderer. Why don’t you arrest him at once? You claimed to know him days ago; and yet you did nothing. And now you’ve let things drift; and the result has been this attack on Sylvia Hawkhurst. Why, you’re responsible for that! You were criminally careless with these poison darts, leaving them lying about for anyone to pick up.”

Sir Clinton made no defence. Instead, he turned Wendover’s vehemence into another channel.

“It’s easy to say ‘Arrest somebody!’ Suppose you were in my shoes, Squire, and you wanted to be absolutely on the safe side; whom would you arrest at this very moment?”

===Chinese===
温多弗与日俱增的不安，终于靠言语找到了一个出口。

“我看不出你在这桩案子里图的是什么，克林顿。你说你知道凶手是谁。那你为什么不立刻逮捕他？几天前你就声称认得他了；可你什么也没做。如今你又放任事态漂流；结果就是西尔维娅·霍克赫斯特遭了这场毒手。哎呀，这个你是有责任的！这些淬毒的飞镖，你处置得粗心大意，简直到了犯罪的 地步，随手乱放，谁都能捡了去。”

克林顿爵士没有辩白。他转而把温多弗的火气引向了另一条水道。

“说一句‘抓人！’当然容易。设若你处在我的位置上，乡绅，又想做到万无一失；此时此刻，你会逮捕谁？”

===Original===
Under the spur of the direct question, Wendover had a flash of illumination.

“Ernest Shandon,” he said. “I’ve just been thinking over things, and I’ve seen one or two points in a fresh light. Who was it opened the window last night and so made it possible for the murderer to shoot into the room? Ernest Shandon! Who was out of the room when the shot was fired? Ernest Shandon! Where was he? In the winter-garden, which has a door opening close to the bank of rhododendrons in which the murderer hid himself. Who had access to that stock of curare in the museum? Ernest Shandon!”

Sir Clinton failed to repress a smile, though he did his best.

“And who was attacked himself, in the Maze? Ernest Shandon! And who was sitting with a nail in his boot on the public highway that afternoon when his brothers were killed? Ernest Shandon! Let’s complete the tale, you know, before we begin to talk about arrests. The real truth of the matter is that Ernest Shandon has annoyed you by his cowardice and his general selfishness, and, therefore, you think he’d be all the better for a hanging. You’re beginning to see red here, just as you saw red in Ardsley’s case.”

Wendover sullenly admitted his blunder.

===Chinese===
受了这直截一问的刺激，温多弗脑中灵光一闪。

“欧内斯特·香农。”他说，“我方才把诸事从头想了一遍，有一两处看出了新的光亮。昨晚是谁开的窗，给凶手往屋里开枪创造了机会？欧内斯特·香农！开枪的时候是谁不在房里？欧内斯特·香农！他在哪儿？在冬季花园，那里有道门，开出去就紧挨着凶手藏身的那道杜鹃花堤。陈列馆里那批箭毒，是谁近得着？欧内斯特·香农！”

克林顿爵士虽然竭力忍住，还是没能压下一个微笑。

“可在迷宫里遭袭击的又是谁？欧内斯特·香农！他两位哥哥送命的那个下午，靴子里踩着一枚钉子、坐在公路边上的又是谁？欧内斯特·香农！要知道，谈逮捕之前，咱们得先把账记全。这件事的真底是：欧内斯特·香农的怯懦和他那无处不在的自私惹恼了你，于是你就觉得，他要是能挨上一回绞刑，那再好不过了。你在这里开始动无名之火了，一如你先前在阿德斯利的事上动火。”

温多弗绷着脸认了错。

===Original===
“But there’s another person who ought to be under observation—young Hawkhurst,” he continued. “That young beggar seems to me hardly sane at times. Look at him this morning! That cerebrospinal affair has affected him far more than I supposed …”

He broke off, struck by a fresh idea.

“Is he the person you have your eye on, Clinton? I never thought of that! Now that might account for the thing that’s been puzzling me—the damned aimlessness of all the Whistlefield affair. It’s just the sort of thing a lunatic would do. And they say that in a sleepy sickness case, if it turns to homicidal mania, the creature may go for the nearest relations. Just what’s happened at Whistlefield! And it was he who put on the loudspeaker last night and so covered any noise he might have made in getting into position outside the window. I hadn’t thought of that before. And it was his airgun that I found in the rhododendrons.”

This time, Sir Clinton did not smile.

===Chinese===
“不过还有一个人也该置于监视之下——小霍克赫斯特。”他接着说，“那个小家伙在我看来，有时简直神志不健全。瞧瞧他今天早晨的样子！那场脑脊髓的毛病对他的影响，比我原先想的深得多……”

他忽然住了口，被一个新冒出来的念头击中了。

“你盯上的人莫非是他，克林顿？这我可从来没想过！这一下，倒说不定能解释一直让我犯难的那一点——惠斯特菲尔德这一连串事情该死的漫无目的。这正是一个疯子干得出来的事。而且听说，嗜睡性脑炎的病例，若是一转成杀人狂，那病人兴许会向最亲近的亲属下毒手。惠斯特菲尔德发生的不正是一模一样的事！再说，昨晚是他开的扩音喇叭，正好把他摸到窗户外面就位时可能弄出的声响盖了过去。这一点我先前也没想到。还有，我在杜鹃花丛里找到的那支气枪，正是他的。”

这一回，克林顿爵士没有笑。

===Original===
“I don’t mind admitting to you, Squire, that young Hawkhurst is one of my difficulties.”

Wendover returned to his original charge.

“Well, I can’t understand what you’re driving at, Clinton. On the face of things, it seems to me that you’ve gambled away that poor girl’s life merely to get a case that you can prove; and now you’re no nearer it than you were before.”

Sir Clinton’s face grew very grave.

“You’ve touched a sore spot there, Squire. But did it never occur to you that I didn’t expect an attack on Miss Hawkhurst? What I did expect was something quite different. Didn’t it strike you as peculiar that I angled for that invitation to play bridge when it obviously wasn’t the sort of thing that one expects? I had to put on a pretty tough hide to wangle that with a straight face.”

===Chinese===
“我不妨向你承认，乡绅：小霍克赫斯特正是我的难处之一。”

温多弗又回到原先的指摘上。

“唉，我还是不明白你到底打的什么主意，克林顿。从表面看，你简直是拿那可怜姑娘的命做赌注押了出去，只为换一桩你能证明得了的案子；可到如今，你离它并不比原先更近一步。”

克林顿爵士的脸色变得异常凝重。

“你这话戳到了痛处，乡绅。可你从来没想到过吗——我并没有料到会袭击霍克赫斯特小姐？我料到的是全然不同的另一回事。我去钓那场打桥牌的邀请，明明不是常人会有的举动，你没觉得这事蹊跷吗？为了板着面孔把那件事糊弄到手，我不得不把脸皮磨得相当厚。”

===Original===
“Yes,” Wendover confirmed, “it was a piece of rank bad taste and I was surprised at your doing it.”

“It was. And I’m not usually celebrated for that kind of thing. Don’t you see what I was driving at, Squire? I expected the next attack to be made on myself—and I took good care to make an opportunity for it by going on to the murderer’s own ground. The whole bridge-party affair was a plant of mine to make myself a good target for the airgun expert.”

“My godfathers!” Wendover ejaculated in surprise, “I never thought that was what you were after. You’ve got fair nerves, Clinton, to offer yourself up like that to be shot at.”

===Chinese===
“是啊。”温多弗证实道，“那可是桩恶俗透顶的事，你居然做得出来，我当时就很吃惊。”

“的确是。而我素来并不以那一类做派闻名。你还看不明白我的用意吗，乡绅？我料定下一次袭击会落到我头上——于是我特意着意制造了一个机会，亲自踏到凶手的家门口去。那场桥牌局，从头到尾是我设下的一个局，为的是把自己做成那位气枪行家一个现成的靶子。”

“我的老天爷！”温多弗惊呼出声，“我从来没想过你图的是这个。把自己这样献出去挨枪子儿，你的胆子可真够可以的，克林顿。”

===Original===
“I’d rather take it when I was ready for it than have it unexpectedly—hence the bridge-party. I felt he’d hardly be able to resist the chance of a sitting shot.”

“H’m! I don’t know that I’d have been able to screw myself up to that point.”

“Of course you would! You didn’t hesitate over the risk of going after that fellow, through the window.”

“Yes,” Wendover admitted, “but that was in hot blood, which is rather different.”

Sir Clinton brushed this aside.

===Chinese===
“与其冷不防地挨一下，我宁愿趁自己有备时接下它——桥牌局因此而来。我料想，这么一个打坐靶的机会，他是很难抗拒的。”

“哼！换了我，能不能把自己逼到那一步，可说不准。”

“你当然能！冒着险从窗户出去追那家伙的时候，你可没有半点犹豫。”

“是啊。”温多弗承认道，“可那是一时血气上涌，情形大不相同。”

克林顿爵士把这话撇在一边。

===Original===
“The trouble is that I didn’t get what I wanted, after all. Miss Hawkhurst was hit. But you may remember that just when the brute pulled the trigger, she leaned slightly forward and put out her hand, whilst I happened to lean back. The dart went past you, and it struck her arm; but I can’t for the life of me be sure whether that was an accident or not. If I knew whether that shot was meant for me or for her, I’d know rather more about the case than I do; and I’d be in an easier frame of mind, I can tell you.”

A fresh point seemed to occur to him.

“By the way, Squire, your surmise about the fate of the airgun in the first attacks turns out to be correct. My men have been dragging the river near the bank at the boathouse; and we’ve got the airgun that killed the two Shandons. The murderer must have pitched it into the water just as you suggested.”

===Chinese===
“麻烦在于，我终究没能拿到我想要的东西。挨镖的是霍克赫斯特小姐。可你或许记得，那畜生扣扳机的当口，她恰好微微前倾、伸出了手，而我恰好往后一仰。飞镖擦过你身边，射中了她的手臂；可它究竟是不是一桩意外，我死活拿不准。倘若我知道那一镖瞄的是我还是她，我对这案子的了解就会比现在多上几分；我的心境也会松快得多，这我可以告诉你。”

像是又有一个新的关节浮上了他的心头。

“对了，乡绅，关于最初几场袭击里那支气枪的下落，你的揣测证明是对的。我的人一直在船屋附近的河岸边打捞；我们把杀害两位香农的那支气枪捞上来了。凶手准是照你所推测的那样，把它扔进了水里。”

===Original===
Wendover was distinctly pleased at this tribute to his acuteness.

“Is there anything identifiable about it?” he demanded.

“It seems to have come from the Whistlefield armoury,” Sir Clinton replied. “Confound them, I wish they hadn’t gone in so strong for airguns. It makes things more difficult.”

===Chinese===
这一番对其敏锐的褒奖，让温多弗分明高兴起来。

“它有什么可供辨认的地方吗？”他问。

“它似乎出自惠斯特菲尔德的枪械库。”克林顿爵士答道，“真见鬼，他们当初要不是这样下血本收气枪就好了。这让事情更难办了。”

## XIV The Forged Cheque / 十四 伪造支票

===Original===
Sir Clinton had yet another surprise in store for his host. Just before dinner, he apparently made up his mind to ring up Whistlefield; and to Wendover’s astonishment he suggested that the Squire should accompany him to the telephone.
“You’ll hear only one side of the conversation,” he said, with a rather grim expression, “but I think it may interest you. And perhaps it will be just as well to have a witness to testify about my end of the wire. I wish we had two receivers, for then you would have heard the whole thing.”
He got the connection in a moment or two and then astounded Wendover by asking for Ernest Shandon instead of Ardsley. After a few minutes, Wendover heard the beginning of the conversation.
“Sir Clinton Driffield speaking. Mr. Shandon, you must treat this as absolutely confidential. … Absolutely for yourself. Not a breath of it to anyone else, you understand? … I want you to keep an eye on your secretary … Yes, Stenness. I want him kept under observation. If you see him leave the house, ring me up immediately … Yes, at once … It won’t be for long. I’m coming across very shortly … I didn’t catch that … Yes, you weren’t far out in your suspicions. Most fortunate you mentioned the matter of the cheque … Anything further? Do you mean about the murderer? … Oh, I think I’ll have him tomorrow, quite possibly—if he doesn’t bolt. If he doesn’t bolt, I said. That’s the only thing I’m afraid of. … Yes, I’m sure that would interest you. After all, one’s skin is one’s dearest possession. Good night. We shall be across shortly after dinner.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士给他的主人还备着又一桩出人意料的招数。晚饭前不久，他显然打定了主意要往惠斯特菲尔德挂个电话；而让温多弗吃惊的是，他竟提议乡绅陪他一同到电话跟前去。
“这通话你只能听到一头。”他说道，神情颇为冷峻，“可我想它或许会让你感兴趣。再者，电线我这头说了些什么，有个见证人可以作证，兴许不是坏事。但愿咱们有两只听筒，那样你就能听个全须全尾了。”
不出一两分钟他便接通了线路，随后他指名要找的不是阿德斯利、而是欧内斯特·香农，这叫温多弗吃了一惊。过了几分钟，温多弗听到了这场谈话的开头。
“我是克林顿·德里菲尔德爵士。香农先生，底下这些话，你必须当作绝密。……绝对只许你一人与闻。对任何旁人都不得透半点口风，你明白吗？……我要你盯住你的秘书……对，斯滕内斯。我要让他处于监视之下。你若见他离开宅子，立刻打电话给我……对，马上……为时不会太久。我很快就要过来……我没听清……是的，你的疑心离谱不远。多亏你提起了支票那桩事……还有别的吗？你是问凶手？……哦，我想明天我就能拿住他，很有这个可能——只要他不跑。我是说，只要他不跑。那是我唯一担心的事。……是的，我敢说那准会让你感兴趣。毕竟，人这条命是他最宝贵的家当。晚安。晚饭后不久我们就过去。”

===Original===
Wendover had been able to gather the gist of the conversation from the side which he had heard.
“You’re afraid of Stenness doing a bolt? And you think he’s the man you’re after?”
“Really, Squire, you must take a reef in your questions,” Sir Clinton said, reproachfully. “I stretched a point to let you hear that talk; and I certainly didn’t intend to stand a cross-examination about it. You must make what you can out of it for yourself. And that reminds me, I’m afraid you can’t be present when I interview Master Stenness. You’ll just have to be a private caller this evening and wait for results till later.”
Wendover was not particularly pleased with this last news. He had evidently counted on hearing what Sir Clinton had to say to the secretary. However, he realised that he was in the hands of the Chief Constable and must do as he was told; so when they arrived at Whistlefield, he asked for Ernest Shandon while Sir Clinton went into the study to interview Stenness.

===Chinese===
凭着听到的这一头，温多弗已能把这番谈话的大意拼凑出来。
“你是怕斯滕内斯撒腿开溜？还认为你要抓的就是他？”
“说真的，乡绅，你问话可得收一收帆了。”克林顿爵士责备道，“我破例让你听了那番谈话；却断然没打算再受一场关于它的交叉质询。你能从里头听出些什么，须凭你自己的本事。这倒提醒了我——我盘问斯滕内斯少爷的时候，恐怕你不能在场。今晚你只好做一回私人访客，且等晚些时候再听结果。”
最后这个消息并没有让温多弗多么高兴。他分明原本指望，能听到克林顿爵士对秘书说些什么。不过他也明白，自己已捏在郡警察局长掌心，只能照吩咐行事；于是到了惠斯特菲尔德，他便去求见欧内斯特·香农，克林顿爵士则进书房盘问斯滕内斯去了。

===Original===
The secretary arrived in a few moments. He was still looking very anxious, perhaps even more anxious than in the morning. Sir Clinton wasted no time but came to grips with the subject at once.
“Now, Mr. Stenness, I’ve one or two questions to put to you. I may as well caution you that anything you say may be used against you if you are put on your trial.”
Stenness’s face betrayed less surprise than might have been expected.
“You say ‘if,’ but perhaps you mean ‘when’?”

===Chinese===
不多一会儿，秘书便到了。他看上去依旧满面忧煎，只怕比早晨还更甚一层。克林顿爵士不在工夫上耽搁，一上来便攥住了正题。
“好了，斯滕内斯先生，我有一两个问题要向你提。我不妨先警告你一句：你所说的任何话，日后你若被送上法庭，都可能被用作不利于你的证据。”
斯滕内斯面上显露的惊讶，比人们可能预想的要少。
“你说的是‘如果’，可你想说的或许是‘到时候’吧？”

===Original===
“I’m picking my words with some care,” Sir Clinton assured him. “I mean ‘if.’ The point’s still in doubt; but I want to play the game with you and take no improper advantage.”
The imperturbable face of the secretary showed neither relief nor depression.
“It’s very good of you,” he said in a colourless voice. Sir Clinton considered for a moment. Stenness moved over to a chair and sat down.

===Chinese===
“我的措辞是斟酌过的。”克林顿爵士向他保证，“我的意思就是‘如果’。这一点尚在未定之天；可我要跟你光明正大地来，不占一分不正当的便宜。”
秘书那张宠辱不惊的脸上，既不见宽慰，也不见沮丧。
“您真是宽厚。”他用一种没有颜色的声音说道。克林顿爵士思量了片刻。斯滕内斯挪到一把椅子跟前坐下了。

===Original===
“I think I can put my cards on the table in your case, Stenness,” the Chief Constable said at length. “Nothing I’m going to tell you will be news to you; and there seems no reason why I shouldn’t say it.”
Stenness looked up indifferently. His mind seemed to be occupied with something quite apart from the affair in hand.
“Go on,” he said, apathetically.
“Here are the facts, then,” Sir Clinton began. “You were employed here as Roger Shandon’s secretary. In that capacity, you seem to have had access to his chequebooks. It’s not a usual thing; but I have sound reasons for supposing that it was so in your case.”

===Chinese===
“我想，在你这桩案子上，我可以把牌摊到桌面上来了，斯滕内斯。”郡警察局长终于说道，“我要告诉你的一切，对你都不会是新闻；而我似乎也没有理由不说出口。”
斯滕内斯漠然抬眼。他的心思仿佛叫一件与手头之事全不相干的东西占住了。
“讲下去。”他兴味索然地说。
“那么，事实便是这些。”克林顿爵士开腔道，“你受雇在此，当罗杰·香农的秘书。以这个身份，你似乎近得着他的支票簿。这本不是常有的事；可我有可靠的依据认定，你的情形便是如此。”

===Original===
Stenness nodded his assent.
“I don’t deny that,” he admitted.
“You have the key of the safe, haven’t you? Would you mind seeing if you can find the chequebook that Roger Shandon used last?”
Stenness walked over to the safe, opened it, and after a few moments’ search he unearthed the chequebook.

===Chinese===
斯滕内斯点头认了。
“这一点我不否认。”他承认道。
“保险柜的钥匙在你手上，是吧？能不能麻烦你找找，罗杰·香农最后用的那本支票簿还在不在？”
斯滕内斯走到保险柜前，打开柜门，寻了不大一会儿，便把那本支票簿翻了出来。

===Original===
“Now,” Sir Clinton went on, “would you mind turning up the counterfoil numbered 60073?”
Stenness looked up without showing any emotion on his features.
“There’s no such counterfoil in the book,” he admitted.
“But you find 60072 and 60074 there?”

===Chinese===
“现在，”克林顿爵士接着说，“能不能请你把编号 60073 的存根翻出来？”
斯滕内斯抬起头来，眉目间不带一丝波澜。
“簿子里没有这样一张存根。”他承认道。
“可 60072 和 60074 两张，你都在簿子里找到了？”

===Original===
“Yes.”
“Rather a peculiar state of affairs, isn’t it?”
“It is.”
Sir Clinton turned to another subject.

===Chinese===
“是。”
“这情形未免蹊跷，不是吗？”
“是蹊跷。”
克林顿爵士转到了另一桩事上。

===Original===
“There’s a bundle of returned cheques in that drawer of the writing-desk, isn’t there?”
“There is. Do you want it?”
Sir Clinton seemed to disregard the question.
“Would it surprise you, Stenness, if you learned that one of these cheques has been abstracted and that it can’t be found? The bank returned it in due course for all that.”
Stenness gazed stonily at his interlocutor.
“It wouldn’t surprise me in the slightest.”

===Chinese===
“那张书桌的抽屉里有一沓银行退回的支票，是吧？”
“是有一沓。您要它吗？”
克林顿爵士像是把这问题晾在了一边。
“斯滕内斯，倘若你得知，这沓支票里有一张已被人抽走、再也寻找不见，你会觉得意外吗？饶是如此，银行当时可是如期把它退了回来的。”
斯滕内斯铁石一般地凝视着问话的人。
“丝毫也不会。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton paused for a moment before continuing. When he spoke again it was in a different vein.
“These are all plain facts. Now we come to hypothesis; and of course the ground’s not quite so firm. I think, if you don’t mind, we might put it in the form of one of these John Doe and Richard Roe cases, lest you should think …”
He left the sentence incomplete.
“Now,” he began briskly, “let’s suppose that John Doe is a rich man who has made his money in rather peculiar ways—like the late Roger Shandon, for example. He employs a secretary. I think one may reasonably suppose that a secretary in that case would need to be somebody who could shut his eyes when necessary, and who wouldn’t be apt to judge things too rigidly. In fact, Stenness, he would need to be a fairly unscrupulous fellow himself.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士停了片刻才继续。再开口时，腔调已经换了一副。
“这些全是明摆着的事实。现在咱们来到假设的地界；当然，这里的地面就不那么坚实了。我想，你若不介意，咱们不妨把它摆成约翰·多伊（John Doe）与理查德·罗伊（Richard Roe）那类假想案子的模样，免得你以为……”
这句话他没有说完。
“现在，”他轻快地起了个头，“咱们姑且假设，约翰·多伊是个有钱人，而这钱来得路数相当蹊跷——比如，像已故的罗杰·香农。他雇着一名秘书。我想人们可以有根有据地设想，在那等人家当秘书，须是个必要时闭得上眼睛、又不惯把事情苛察得太紧的人。说实在的，斯滕内斯，此人自己就得是个相当不择手段的家伙。”

===Original===
Stenness nodded indifferently.
“Go on.”
“I’m putting a hypothetical case, remember,” Sir Clinton cautioned him. “This is what might be said—I don’t necessarily accept it myself. I’m only trying to show you how it could be made to look, you understand? Well, then, this secretary, Richard Roe, sooner or later sees the chance which Providence has thrown in his way. His employer is in the habit of drawing bearer cheques for large amounts—some thousands—from time to time. And, rather carelessly, he has dropped into the way of getting his secretary to cash them for him and bring him the money. So the bank is accustomed to paying over these things to the secretary, and no questions asked.”
Stenness gave no sign of special interest. His normal reserve was sufficient to veil his thoughts.

===Chinese===
斯滕内斯漠然点了点头。
“讲下去。”
“记住，我摆的是一桩假设的案子。”克林顿爵士提醒他，“这些是可能被人说出的说法——我自己未必一概认可。我不过是想让你看看，这件事可以被装扮成什么模样，你明白吗？好，那么，这位秘书理查德·罗伊，迟早看中了天意抛到他面前来的机会。他的雇主有个习惯，时不时开几张巨额的不记名支票——数额巨大，动辄数千。而且，说来也大意，他渐渐养成了让秘书替他把支票兑成现款、再把钱带给他的做法。于是银行也惯于把这些支票如数付给秘书，从不多问一声。”
斯滕内斯没有露出特别的兴味。他素常的矜持，已足以掩住他的心思。

===Original===
“The secretary, we may assume, is an acute fellow. I think we may take it that he may see a chance when it comes his way. But forgery requires a certain amount of manual skill if it is carried out in some ways; and possibly the secretary is sufficiently acute to distrust his powers as a forger. But it’s always possible to trace a signature.”
Sir Clinton pulled out his cigarette case and lit a cigarette before going on. He seemed determined to infuse informality into the proceedings.
“It’s always possible to trace a signature,” he continued. “But one needs a model signature for that—a signature from a cheque, of course, because sensible people don’t use their letter signature on their cheques. They have a special one with some specific trick in it—the position of a dot, or something of the sort. I hope I’m not boring you with these elementary things.”

===Chinese===
“咱们不妨假定，这位秘书是个精明人。我想可以认定，机会一旦来到他面前，他是识得出来的。可伪造一行，走某些路子是颇须手上功夫的；而这位秘书兴许精明得足以不敢相信自己冒名签字的本事。不过，描摹一个签名，是任何时候都办得到的事。”
克林顿爵士掏出烟盒，点上一支烟，才继续说下去。他像是拿定了主意，要给这场程序搀进几分家常随意的气氛。
“描摹一个签名，是任何时候都办得到的事。”他接着说，“可这么干得有个签名范本——当然得是支票上的签名，因为明白人签支票，从不用自己书信上的那个签字。他们另备一个专用的，里头藏着某处独有的机关——一个点落在什么位置，或诸如此类。但愿这些粗浅的东西没有让你起腻。”

===Original===
“Not at all,” said Stenness, with a certain show of polite interest.
“The model, in the case of the secretary Richard Roe,” went on Sir Clinton, “could easily be chosen from one of the old cheques returned by the bank. He had access to these, we may suppose. But then comes in a point which is sure to strike his acute mind. A man never writes his signature twice in precisely the same way; there’s always a faint difference between any two signatures. Hence, if two cheques turn up with identical signatures, a sharp detective might suspect something wrong. You follow me?”
Stenness nodded in silence.
“The acute secretary, Richard Roe, therefore traces his employer’s signature from one of these old cheques. And to cover his trail, to make certain that the thing cannot be shown to be a traced signature, he then destroys the old cheque. Thus there are not two identical signatures in existence; and the only thing missing is a cancelled cheque—not a thing anyone is likely to make a fuss about at the worst, even if its disappearance is noted. I make myself clear?”
“Quite,” said Stenness, still with his air of formal interest.

===Chinese===
“一点儿也不。”斯滕内斯说道，摆出几分客气周到的兴致。
“至于范本，在秘书理查德·罗伊这桩案子里，”克林顿爵士往下说，“从银行退回的旧支票里挑出一张来，是再容易不过的。这些东西他都近得着，咱们不妨这样假定。可跟着就来了一个关节，他那颗精明的头脑准会看在眼里。一个人签起名来，从来不会两次分毫不差；随便哪两个签名之间，总有一丝细微的差别。因此，倘若有两张支票露了面，签名却一模一样，眼尖的侦探便可能疑心里头有诈。你跟得上我吗？”
斯滕内斯默然点头。
“于是，那位精明的秘书理查德·罗伊，便从其中一张旧支票上把雇主的签名描摹了下来。而为了掩盖踪迹，为了叫人无从证明那是个描摹出来的签名，他接着便把旧支票毁了。这样一来，世上便不存在两个一模一样的签名；唯一短缺的，只是一张注销的支票——即便有人注意到它失踪，往最坏处说，也不是会有人为之大惊小怪的东西。我说得够明白了吗？”
“够明白了。”斯滕内斯说，仍旧带着那副一本正经的兴致。

===Original===
“So far, then,” Sir Clinton went on, “all is plain sailing. But now comes a sticky bit. In fact, *the* sticky bit of the whole affair. Every cheque has its counterfoil; and Mr. John Doe, the employer, has had an awkward habit of always filling in his counterfoils. Hence when Mr. Richard Roe traces his employer’s signature on, let us say, cheque No. 60073, he has to do something about the counterfoil of that cheque. If he leaves it blank, it will catch the attention of the good Mr. Doe the next time he uses the chequebook. If the acute secretary fudges an entry on counterfoil No. 60073, then Mr. Doe, who is by no means a dull fellow, may spot the thing and cause trouble. What is to be done? The obvious thing is to remove counterfoil No. 60073 from the chequebook and trust that its absence will not be noticed. I think that is the course I’d have followed myself if I had got into that fix.”
Sir Clinton seemed for a moment to lose interest in his narrative. He sat for a time in silence, eyeing the secretary as though he hoped to surprise something. But Stenness showed no sign of either guilt or confusion.
“I congratulate you on your nerves, Stenness,” Sir Clinton began once more. “Now that’s an hypothesis which I should not be very loath to adopt as an explanation of this affair of the cheques. It seems to me to cover the ground neatly. In fact, I’m quite convinced that it’s a good hypothesis so far as it goes. But some people might be prepared to carry it a stage further. I’ll just sketch out what they would say.”

===Chinese===
“那么，到此为止，”克林顿爵士往下说，“一路都是顺风行船。可现在来了个黏手的地方。事实上，是整桩事情里*最*黏手的地方。每张支票都有它的存根；而雇主约翰·多伊先生有个麻烦的习惯：回回都把存根填得齐齐整整。因此，理查德·罗伊先生在——姑且说——第 60073 号支票上描好雇主的签名之后，对那张支票的存根便不能不有所处置。他若留着它一片空白，下回好心的多伊先生用起支票簿来，就会叫它扎了眼。这位精明的秘书若在第 60073 号存根上做假填上一笔，那么多伊先生——他决计不是个迟钝的人——便可能瞧出蹊跷，惹出麻烦来。可怎么办呢？最现成的一招，就是把第 60073 号存根从支票簿里除去，指望它的缺席不被人留意。我想，倘若我陷进那步田地，我自己走的也是这一条路。”
有那么一刻，克林顿爵士像是对自己讲述的事失了兴味。他默默坐了一阵，端量着秘书，仿佛指望猝然捉出点什么来。可斯滕内斯身上，既不见半点心虚，也不见半分慌乱。
“你的神经，我佩服，斯滕内斯。”克林顿爵士重新开了腔，“方才那套假设，若拿它来解释这桩支票的事情，我不至于很不情愿采纳。在我看来，它把地面铺盖得干净利落。事实上，就其所及的范围而论，我全然信服它是个好假设。可有些人兴许还打算把它再往前推一步。我把他们会说的话，勾个轮廓给你听。”

===Original===
At this point Stenness seemed to find some interest in the matter. He sat up and looked across at the Chief Constable.
“Please go on,” he requested.
“We have assumed that Richard Roe is an acute person. Now an alert mind might quite conceivably see a further step which would bring him on to safer ground. If things took their course, the forgery would be spotted in a very short time. One can’t take thousands out of a man’s account without raising inquiry. So, normally, the reasonable thing to do would be to bolt and chance getting out of the country with the cash. That’s what would occur to most people at once. But there’s another way of making sure of things.”

===Chinese===
到了这一步，斯滕内斯像是当真对这件事起了几分兴味。他坐直了身子，朝郡警察局长望过去。
“请讲下去。”他请求道。
“咱们已经假定，理查德·罗伊是个精明人。而一个警醒的头脑，尽可以想见它还会看出更远一步，一步能把他引上更稳当的地面。倘若听任事情顺流而下，伪造不出多久便会被人看破。从一个人的账户里取走几千，不惹来查问是不可能的。所以，照常理，合算的做法便是开溜，携着现款碰运气逃出国境去。多数人头一个涌上心来的准是这个。可还有另一条把事情弄稳妥的路子。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton’s voice took on a graver tone.
“Let us suppose that immediately the cheque has been cashed, the employer happens to die. What evidence of forgery is left then? None whatever, if the tracing of the signature has been decently executed. The supposed writer is dead; and no one else can deny his signature. And the cheque, we assume, has been cashed before the death takes place. On that basis, there would be no need for any flight on the part of the forger. He would simply have to sit tight and behave normally.”
Sir Clinton surprised a fresh look on Stenness’s face. It was only a fleeting change; but it was quite unmistakable. But the secretary remained obstinately mute and waited for the rest of the argument.
“That’s assuming a natural death of the employer. But such coincidences are rather rare. An acute mind would not count on a chance like that. However, rare as such coincidences may be, they are not beyond possibility, if a human agent should happen to take a hand in the business. Suppose that the acute Richard Roe perceived this, and decided that it was worth his while to produce that coincidence by murdering his employer. …”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士的声音沉了下来。
“咱们不妨假设，支票刚一兑成现钱，雇主便恰巧死了。那时还留下什么伪造的证据呢？半点也没有——只要签名的描摹做得像样。那个据称落笔的人已经死了；再没有旁人能否认那是他的签名。而这张支票，咱们假定，是在死亡发生之前兑付的。在这个基础上，伪造者根本无须出逃。他只消稳坐不动、举止如常便够了。”
克林顿爵士冷不防在斯滕内斯脸上窥见一副新的神情。那变化只是电光石火的一闪；却绝对错认不得。可秘书执拗地缄默着，只等这番论证的下半段。
“那是假定雇主死于自然。可这样的巧合相当少见。一颗精明的头脑不会把宝押在那种运气上。然而，这样的巧合再怎么少见，也并非绝无可能——倘若碰巧有一只人手在这桩买卖里插上一脚的话。不妨设想，那位精明的理查德·罗伊看破了这一层，认定这巧合值得他不惜谋害雇主、亲手把它炮制出来……”

===Original===
Sir Clinton swung round in his chair, surprised by the opening of the door. Ardsley stood on the threshold, and a glance at his face showed that something serious had happened.
“It’s all up, Sir Clinton. They can pull down the blinds.”
“Miss Hawkhurst?” was all Sir Clinton could say.
Ardsley made a gesture of despair.
“Some things are beyond us,” he said despondently.

===Chinese===
房门忽然开了，克林顿爵士吃了一惊，从椅子上旋过身去。阿德斯利立在门口，看一眼他的脸色便知道：出了严重的事。
“全完了，克林顿爵士。百叶窗可以放下来了。”
“霍克赫斯特小姐？”克林顿爵士只说得出这一句。
阿德斯利做了个绝望的手势。
“有些事情，本非人力所能及。”他沮丧地说。

## XV The Secretary’s Affairs / 十五 秘书的私事

===Original===
Sir Clinton received Ardsley’s news almost as if he had feared that the end was inevitable. He made no attempt to express his feelings, however.

“I think you’d better let the others know,” he suggested.

Ardsley agreed, with a faint grimace of reluctance for the task, and left the room.

Stenness had listened to the interchange between the two with an air of a man trying to persuade himself that he is in a dream and that by a violent effort he may be able to shake off his nightmare. At last he seemed to master his feelings.

“It’s all over, is it?” he asked in a choked voice, as though hoping even at the last moment to be reassured by good news.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士听阿德斯利报来消息时的神情，几乎像是他早已害怕这结局无可避免。不过，他并没有试图表露自己的心情。

“我想你还是让其他人知道的好。”他建议道。

阿德斯利答应了，脸上掠过一丝不情愿担这差事的苦相，随即离开了房间。

这两个人的一问一答，斯滕内斯全听在耳里，那副神情，像是一个人正竭力说服自己此刻身在梦中，只消猛一使劲，便能把这噩梦抖落。最后，他像是把情绪克制住了。

“都完了，是吗？”他声音哽咽地问，仿佛还指望在这最后关头，能换来一句让人安心的好消息。

===Original===
“It’s all over,” Sir Clinton admitted, gravely.

Stenness seemed to pull himself together.

“Then in that case,” he said, “there seems to be no reason why I shouldn’t make a clean breast of things. Nothing matters much, now; and you may as well get the true story. It’ll make no difference to me.”

Sir Clinton made a vague gesture of assent, but refrained from speaking. After a moment or two, Stenness began.

===Chinese===
“都完了。”克林顿爵士沉重地承认道。

斯滕内斯像是重新振作了起来。

“那么，事到如今，”他说，“我似乎再没有什么理由不把事情和盘托出了。现在什么都无关紧要了；你不妨听听真实的内情。对我横竖没有分别。”

克林顿爵士含糊地做了个允准的手势，却没有开口。过了一两分钟，斯滕内斯说了起来。

===Original===
“This is how it happened. Not so long ago, I was a cub with no near relations to look after me and keep me straight. I’m not whining; I’m simply explaining. I had a few thousands of capital; and naturally a good deal of it got frittered away. I learned something about the world in the process, so perhaps it wasn’t a total loss.”

Sir Clinton noticed that even at this stage Stenness retained his conciseness and stuck to the main facts. The secretary was sparing him useless details; and, as he had said, he was not whining over his losses.

“When I had been at it for a year or two, I had run myself down to a little over five thousand pounds. That’s a good enough nest-egg. But I hadn’t the sense to see it in that light. I wanted a good deal more than three or four hundred a year. So I looked about for some way of increasing my capital.”

A faintly contemptuous expression crossed his face.

===Chinese===
“事情的原委是这样的。就在不久以前，我还是个毛头小子，没有一位近亲来照管我、管束我走正路。我不是在诉苦；我只是把情形交代清楚。我手里原有几千镑本钱；自然，其中一大部分零零碎碎地散掉了。在这个过程中我也见识了些世道，所以或许也不算全盘落空。”

克林顿爵士注意到，即便到了这个地步，斯滕内斯依旧保持着他的简练，紧抓着主要事实不放。这位秘书不拿无用的细节来耗费他的工夫；而且正如他自己所说，他并不为自己的损失哀哀切切。

“这么混了一两年，我把自己折腾得只剩五千镑出头。那份家底，本来已经够拿得出手了。可我当时没有那份见识，不肯这样看。我要的远不止一年三四百镑的进项。于是我四处寻找让本钱增值的门路。”

一丝微微轻蔑的神色掠过他的脸。

===Original===
“I must have been a very green hand in those days. I had a sort of trustfulness which I’ve lost since then. To make a long story short I was swindled out of that five thousand. I was so green that at the time I didn’t realise who was at the back of the swindle. All I met were agents of the big fish in the background. They cleaned me out, almost completely.”

He shifted slightly in his chair as though the recollection made him uncomfortable.

“I had to do something for a living; and somehow I dropped into secretarial work—the kind where it’s more important that a man should have a decent appearance than that he should know his work. But by that time I realised that I’d have to work for a living, and I sobered up. I took things seriously and picked up all I could. I turned into quite a decently-efficient secretary.”

Sir Clinton nodded. It was no more than Stenness’s due.

===Chinese===
“那些日子，我准是个嫩透了的生手。我有一种轻信，后来早就丢掉了。长话短说，那五千镑叫我着了骗局的道。我嫩到当时竟没看出骗局背后究竟是何人。我照面的，全是幕后那条大鱼手下的代理。他们把我刮得几乎一干二净。”

他在椅子里微微挪动了一下，仿佛这段回想让他浑身不自在。

“总得寻个营生糊口；不知怎么，我就干上了秘书这一行——那一行当，人长得体面，比人懂行更要紧。不过到那时，我已经明白这世上得靠做事吃饭，人也清醒了。我做事认真，能学的都学到手，成了一名相当称职的秘书。”

克林顿爵士点了点头。这本就是斯滕内斯应得的评价。

===Original===
“I drifted about from post to post, until a couple of years ago I dropped into Roger Shandon’s place. I learned a lot with him. It was a perfect education—on certain lines.”

“I can quite imagine that,” Sir Clinton interjected.

“He was a damned scoundrel,” Stenness pronounced, without heat. “But I picked up a lot about the seamy side of affairs from things that passed through my hands. It was interesting, even at first. And then, it got more interesting.”

He shifted again in his chair so as to look Sir Clinton in the face.

===Chinese===
“我漂来漂去，一个差事换一个差事，直到两年前，落脚到了罗杰·香农手下。跟着他，我学到了许多。那是一段十全十美的教育——在某些方面。”

“这我完全想象得出。”克林顿爵士插话道。

“他是个不折不扣的恶棍。”斯滕内斯下了断语，语气里并无火气，“不过，从经我手的事务里，我领教了不少这一行的阴暗面。起初便已觉得有趣。到后来，就越发有趣了。”

他又在椅子里挪了挪身子，好正面对着克林顿爵士。

===Original===
“I came across a name in his correspondence, the name of one of the fellows who had helped to rook me of that last five thousand. That put me on the alert. I began to hunt things up. It took me a good while; and none of it was in any way explicit, you understand; but I had sense enough to put two and two together and fill up the blanks. My late employer was the man who had been behind the ramp that cleared me of the last of my cash.”

“You couldn’t have expected me to guess that,” Sir Clinton said, as though defending himself. “I knew there was more behind this business than appeared on the surface, but naturally I’d no inkling of anything of that sort.”

Stenness paid no attention to the interruption.

“I suppose my training under Roger Shandon had taken the refined edge off any honesty I had. Or else it had left the honesty but blunted my respect for the conventions, if you like it better that way. It seemed to me, anyhow, a simple enough state of affairs. This fellow Shandon had picked my pocket—at least that was what it amounted to in practice, though I doubt if I could have charged him with fraud and brought it home to him. Well, I saw no particular reason why he should get away with my money. He’d taken advantage of my stupidity or trustfulness, or whatever you like to call it. I decided to pay him back in his own coin. I might have milked him of a fair extra sum as a fine; but that didn’t suit my book. I’ve got a peculiar brand of conscience; and I made up my mind that I’d take precisely the cash that he cheated me out of. No doubt the odd figures on the cheque surprised you.”

===Chinese===
“我在他的往来信函里撞见了一个名字，正是当年帮着把我最后那五千镑骗走的家伙之一的尊姓大名。这一下我警觉了。我开始暗中查证。这费了我好长一段时间；而且你要明白，哪一条线索都不是明摆在纸面上的；好在我还算有脑子，能把零星迹象串起来，把空白处一一填满。我那位已故的东家，正是当年那场刮尽我最后一文现钱的骗局的幕后主使。”

“这你可不能指望我猜得出来。”克林顿爵士说道，像是在替自己申辩，“我早知道这桩事情表面之下另有文章，可那样的内情，我自然连一点风声也没得到。”

斯滕内斯没有理会这句打岔。

“我想，在罗杰·香农门下的历练，已经把我身上哪怕一点诚实的锋刃都磨钝了。或者换个你听着顺耳的说法：诚实还留着，只是对世俗成规的敬畏磨钝了。不管怎么说，在我看来，这是一桩再简单不过的事。这个香农摸了我的腰包——至少实际上就等于是这么回事，尽管我很怀疑，我能否以诈骗相控、把罪名坐实到他头上。总之，我看不出有什么特别的道理，该让他揣着我的钱逍遥自在。他利用了我的愚蠢，或者轻信，随你怎么称呼它。我决意以其人之道，还治其人之身。我本可以再多榨他一笔，充作罚金；可那不合我的账。我的良心是独一无二的一个牌子；我拿定主意，只取他骗走我的那笔现钱，分毫不差。支票上那个零头数目，想必让你吃了一惊。”

===Original===
“No,” Sir Clinton objected. “I simply took it that Shandon wasn’t in the habit of drawing cheques for round thousands and that you filled in an odd figure so as not to make the cheque look uncommon.”

“I’d have done that in any case, of course,” Stenness explained, “but as it happened, the exact sum he took from me originally made a likely enough figure; and I stuck to it. I didn’t even fine him a sovereign for his swindling. I contented myself with taking back exactly what I’d lost. I saw nothing wrong in it; and I see nothing wrong in it now. My conscience doesn’t trouble me a rap in the matter. Legally, of course, it’s quite a different question.”

“Quite,” said Sir Clinton, but his tone gave no clue to his views on the matter.

===Chinese===
“倒没有。”克林顿爵士提出异议，“我只是想，香农没有开整千数目支票的习惯，你便填了个带零头的数，免得这张支票显得惹眼。”

“当然，无论如何我也会那样做的。”斯滕内斯解释道，“不过赶巧，他当初从我手里拿走的确切数目，本身就像个说得过去的数字；我便照它写了。我甚至没有为他的骗局多罚一个金镑。我只满足于取回我损失的那笔钱，分毫不差。当年我不觉得这有什么错；如今也不觉得。在这件事上，我的良心一丝一毫也不受煎熬。当然，从法律上讲，那就完全是另一个问题了。”

“那当然。”克林顿爵士应道，但语气里探不出他对这桩事的看法。

===Original===
“As to the actual business, I needn’t go over it; for you put your finger on it quite correctly up to a point, not ten minutes ago. I forged his signature, destroyed the cancelled cheque, cut the counterfoil out of the chequebook, and cashed the forged cheque. Nobody suspected anything.”

“There was no reason why they should—at the time.”

“No. But now I come to the point where you made a further suggestion. You brought out the idea that I’d murdered Shandon to cover the trail.”

“I suggested it as an hypothesis that some people might be inclined to put forward,” corrected Sir Clinton. “If you remember, I refrained from supporting it myself.”

===Chinese===
“至于实际的手法，我不必赘述了；因为不出十分钟之前，你已经在相当程度上一语点破了。我伪造了他的签名，销毁了那张作废的支票，从支票簿上剪去了存根，然后把伪造的支票兑成了现款。没有任何人起过疑心。”

“当时，他们也没有理由起疑。”

“是的。可现在，我要说到你再进一步的那个推想了。你提出了一种看法：我为了掩盖行迹，谋害了香农。”

“我是把它当作一个别人或许乐于提出的假说来说的。”克林顿爵士更正道，“你如果还记得，我本人并没有表示赞同。”

===Original===
Stenness reflected for a moment.

“That’s true. But murder never entered into my plans at all. Bear in mind that I don’t feel a criminal in this affair. All I’ve done is to take my own money out of the hand of a fellow who had picked my pocket. You’d recover your own purse if you caught a thief red-handed with it; and you wouldn’t call yourself a robber for doing so. Well, no more do I.”

“Go on,” said Sir Clinton, in unconscious plagiarism.

“That being so,” Stenness continued, “murder was the last thing that would have entered my mind. Why should I murder him? I’d squared the account; I’d got my money back again. What would be the point in putting my neck into a noose? None whatever! All I needed was a clean getaway. I planned that carefully enough.”

===Chinese===
斯滕内斯沉吟了片刻。

“这话不假。可谋杀压根儿就没有进过我的计划。你要记住，在这件事里，我不觉得自己是个罪犯。我做的全部，不过是从一个摸过我腰包的家伙手里，取回我自己的钱。倘若你逮住一个小偷，他人赃并获地攥着你的钱袋，你也会把它收回来；你不会因此管自己叫强盗。我也一样。”

“讲下去。”克林顿爵士说道，不自觉地拾人牙慧。

“既然如此，”斯滕内斯接着说，“谋杀是我脑子里最不可能冒出的念头。我为什么要杀他？账目已经两讫；钱已经重新回到我手里。把脖子伸进绞索，于我有什么好处？半点也没有！我需要的只是干干净净地脱身。这一层，我筹划得够周密了。”

===Original===
“That’s no particular business of mine at present,” Sir Clinton reminded him. “But one might ask what you’re doing here, since it’s evidently not according to plan.”

“It’s easy to account for that. I had planned to get away on the evening of the day when the Shandons were murdered. I was in the middle of clearing up preparations for a bolt … and suddenly came the affair in the Maze. Could I bolt then? Not likely. I’d have been marked down as the murderer if I’d stirred a step. And look what face would have been on things if I’d cleared out. It would have added the last touch of substance to the very hypothesis you put forward. The whole forgery business would have been raked up to furnish a motive. I couldn’t have faced it—for I hadn’t an alibi. Nobody could swear that I was in my room—I was packing up—at the time the murders were done. It would have been a clear enough case for any jury.”

Sir Clinton’s face showed that he agreed with this reading.

===Chinese===
“眼下这还轮不到我特别去过问。”克林顿爵士提醒他，“不过倒可以问一句：事情显然没有照计划走，你却还在这里做什么？”

“这好解释。我原计划就在香农兄弟遇害的那天傍晚动身逃走。我正做着行前的收尾……迷宫里的案子就突然爆发了。那时候我还能开溜吗？想也别想。我但凡挪动一步，就会被指认作凶手。再想想，倘若我真的一走了之，事情会成一副什么嘴脸。那恰恰会给你提出的那个假说补上最后一分坐实的分量。整桩伪造支票的勾当都会被翻出来，充作凶案的动机。我可招架不住——因为我拿不出不在场证明。凶案发生的时候，谁也不能起誓说我在自己房里——我当时正在收拾行李。在任何陪审团眼里，那都会是一桩再清楚不过的案子。”

克林顿爵士的神色表明，他认可这一层分析。

===Original===
“There’s one point that hasn’t come out, though,” he said. “What’s the meaning of this sudden collapse on your part? If your conscience is clear—and I don’t doubt your account of it—why do you throw up the sponge like this? That’s not very clear.”

Stenness’s face showed that Sir Clinton had touched him on the raw. He had some difficulty with his voice as he replied.

“I may as well put all the cards on the table. You know what Miss Hawkhurst was like? Any man might have fallen in love with her. I did, at any rate.”

“Were you engaged?”

===Chinese===
“不过，还有一点没有弄明白。”他说，“你这突然之间全盘垮掉，是什么缘故？倘若你问心无愧——我并不怀疑你在这上面的自陈——那你为什么又这样缴械投降？这一点不很清楚。”

斯滕内斯的神色表明，克林顿爵士戳到了他的痛处。他作答时，嗓音有一阵不听使唤。

“我不妨把所有的牌都摊到桌面上。你知道霍克赫斯特小姐是位什么样的人？任何男人都可能爱上她。反正我是爱上了。”

“你们订过婚吗？”

===Original===
“No. I’ve got some sort of pride, even if I am a forger. Miss Hawkhurst had an income of her own. What had I? Nothing. Anyone might have supposed I was after her money.”

“Hardly the money alone, surely—Miss Hawkhurst herself would account for the attraction without that.”

“Well, I’m not that sort,” said Stenness, abruptly. “I’m not the kind of man who can live on his wife’s money. I can’t explain it. It is so.”

“Your conscience is a rum contrivance,” Sir Clinton commented, not unkindly.

===Chinese===
“没有。哪怕我干过伪造支票的事，我也有我的一份自尊。霍克赫斯特小姐自己有一份进项。我有什么？什么也没有。谁都会以为我图的是她的钱。”

“总不至于单单是钱吧——霍克赫斯特小姐本人，无须那一项，就足以解释这份倾慕了。”

“反正，我不是那种人。”斯滕内斯突兀地说，“我不是那种能靠妻子的钱过活的男人。我说不出个所以然。事情就是这样。”

“你这份良心，可真是个古怪的造物。”克林顿爵士评论道，语气并不刻薄。

===Original===
“It’s in good working order, at any rate,” Stenness retorted. “Now, isn’t the thing clear enough to you? I meant to recover my money, clear out, work hard and make enough for my purposes. I reckoned that a couple of years would do it, if I took risks. And before I went, I was going to take the biggest risk of all. I was going to tell Sylvia the whole story and see what she had to say.”

Sir Clinton could not repress his surprise.

“You’re a rum card, Stenness. Be thankful I’ve had a large experience of liars and know when a man’s speaking the truth; for that yarn wouldn’t be believed by one person in a hundred.”

“It’s the truth for all that,” returned Stenness, doggedly. “I’ve told you before that I see nothing wrong in what I’ve done—nothing morally wrong, I mean. He swindled me. I take my money back again. What’s wrong in that?”

===Chinese===
“不管怎么说，它运转良好。”斯滕内斯回敬道，“现在，这事你还不明白么？我打算取回我的钱，远走高飞，埋头苦干，挣下我需用的数目。我盘算过，只要肯冒险，两年工夫也就够了。而在动身之前，我还要冒上最大的一次险。我要把全部实情告诉西尔维娅，看看她怎么说。”

克林顿爵士抑制不住自己的惊讶。

“你是个怪人，斯滕内斯。幸而我说谎的人见得多了，分得清一个人什么时候讲的是真话；要不然，你这套说辞，一百个人里也未必有一个肯信。”

“纵然如此，它也是真话。”斯滕内斯执拗地答道，“我早就告诉过你，我不觉得自己做的事有什么错——我是说，道义上没有什么错。他骗了我。我取回我的钱。这有什么错？”

===Original===
“I wish I had your simple way of looking at things.”

Sir Clinton sat in silence for a few moments, evidently pondering over the case.

“You’re a problem, Stenness,” he said at last. “I don’t really know what to do with you.”

“Oh, arrest me!” Stenness exclaimed, bitterly. “Nothing matters now. She’s dead. It’s all over; and I don’t much care what happens.”

===Chinese===
“我倒真希望有你那副看待事物的简单法子。”

克林顿爵士默然坐了片刻，显然在心里掂量着这桩案子。

“你是个难题，斯滕内斯。”他终于开口，“我还真不知道该拿你怎么处置。”

“咳，把我抓起来吧！”斯滕内斯悲苦地喊道，“如今什么都无所谓了。她死了。一切都完了；往后会怎么样，我也不大在乎。”

===Original===
“Pull yourself together, man,” said Sir Clinton, curtly. “That sort of chatter does all right on the stage. Nobody with a backbone takes a knock like that. If you wake up three years hence in a prison cell, you’ll look at things in a different light, and be very fed-up that you’ve lost your liberty as well as other things. Some things are inevitable. Others aren’t. Don’t behave like a child.”

Stenness took the rebuke sullenly.

“Well, what does it matter?” he demanded. “You have enough in your hands to convict me if you want to—and I don’t care. Do as you like. I’ll write it out for you now, if you think it’ll save you trouble. I’m not inclined to wriggle at the last moment.”

Sir Clinton gave no sign that he had heard him. Instead, he seemed engrossed on some problem. At last he lifted his head.

===Chinese===
“振作起来，老兄。”克林顿爵士简截地说，“那种台词，在戏台上说说还成。有脊梁骨的人，没有谁会挨那样一记闷棍就垮掉。倘若三年之后你在牢房里醒来，你看待事情的眼光就不同了，还会懊恼不堪：除了一切之外，连自由也一并搭了进去。有些事在所难免。有些不是。别像个孩子似的。”

斯滕内斯郁郁地受了这顿训斥。

“唉，那又有什么要紧？”他质问道，“你手里握着的东西，想给我定罪绰绰有余——我不在乎。随你的便。你要是觉得省事，我现在就可以写一份供状给你。我不打算在最后关头再左躲右闪。”

克林顿爵士没有露出听见了的样子。他倒像是全神贯注于某个难题。最后，他抬起了头。

===Original===
“I can’t follow that intricate conscience of yours, Stenness. It’s beyond me. But I can sympathise to some extent with your analogy of the pickpocket caught red-handed. That was very apt. I’m going to give you a chance. I know well enough that you’re speaking the truth about this business. Besides, I can get it checked if necessary. On the basis of ethics, I think you’ve some right to the money. You have it here in notes, I suppose?”

“It’s upstairs.”

“Very well. Bring it down. Put it in the safe. Seal it up in an envelope with your name on it before putting it away. I’ll see what’s to be done about it tomorrow. Now, do that at once. Don’t argue.”

“The money means nothing much to me now,” said Stenness angrily.

===Chinese===
“你那套百转千回的良心，我实在跟不上，斯滕内斯。它超出我的理解。不过，对于你那个人赃并获的小偷的比方，我倒能在某种程度上报以同感。那比方打得很贴切。我打算给你一个机会。你在这件事上讲的是真话，这一点我知道得很清楚。何况，必要的话，我可以让人查证核实。从伦理上说，我认为这笔钱你有几分权利。它就在这里，以现钞的形式在你手上吧？”

“在楼上。”

“很好。把它拿下来。放进保险柜。先封进一只写着你名字的信封，再收进去。明天我再看该怎么处置。现在就去办。不许争辩。”

“这笔钱如今对我没有多大意义了。”斯滕内斯忿忿地说。

===Original===
“Then hand it back to the people it legally belongs to,” Sir Clinton said coolly. “If you don’t want it, other people may have a use for it. It’s a fair test of all that high falutin’ stuff you laid off a minute or two ago.”

Stenness made no reply, but rose and went towards the door.

“Oh, just another thing, Stenness. Meet me at the front of the house here at … at. … Have you a timetable?”

Stenness produced the A.B.C. from a shelf and Sir Clinton turned over its pages before continuing.

===Chinese===
“那就把它退还给它在法律上所属的人。”克林顿爵士冷冷地说，“你不要它，别的人也许还用得着。对你一两分钟前发的那一通高论，这倒是个公道的检验。”

斯滕内斯没有作声，起身朝门口走去。

“哦，还有件事，斯滕内斯。到这宅子的正门来见我，时间是……是……你手头有时刻表吗？”

斯滕内斯从架子上取来A.B.C.铁路时刻表，克林顿爵士翻了几页，才接着说下去。

===Original===
“The first train’s at 7:10 a.m.,” he said. “Meet me out at the house-door at half-past six sharp tomorrow morning. Now don’t fail!”

Stenness was plainly bewildered, and in his astonishment he gave a grudging assent.

“That’ll do, then,” Sir Clinton went on. “Just put that cash in the safe, now. And, by the by, send Ardsley to me as you go out.”

Stenness nodded dully and moved towards the door. Now that he had made a clean breast of things, his mind seemed to have gone back to his loss; and his whole bearing was eloquent of his utter despair. Sir Clinton watched him leave the room.

===Chinese===
“头班车是早上 7 点 10 分。”他说，“明天早晨六点半整，到宅子门口外头见我。千万不能失约！”

斯滕内斯显然被弄糊涂了，惊愕之中勉强应承下来。

“那就这样定了。”克林顿爵士接着说，“现在就把那笔现款放进保险柜。对了，出去的时候，顺带叫阿德斯利到我这里来一趟。”

斯滕内斯木然点了点头，向门口挪动。既然已经把事情和盘托出，他的心思像是又回到了他的丧痛上；他整个人的姿态，把彻头彻尾的绝望表露无遗。克林顿爵士望着他走出房去。

===Original===
“A tough bullet for the poor devil to bite on,” he thought to himself. “Well, ‘joy cometh in the morning,’ it says somewhere or other. Perhaps he’ll find it so.”

He lit a fresh cigarette and seemed to dismiss Stenness from his mind.

“I wonder if that devil is wound up far enough to make a final effort?” he reflected in some perplexity. “I believe he is. At any rate, I’ll take the chance of it. He’s had it all his own way so far, and this ought to encourage him.”

Ardsley came into the room and smiled on seeing that Sir Clinton was alone.

===Chinese===
“这颗子弹，让那可怜的家伙去咬，未免太硬了。”他心里想，“唉，‘早晨便必欢呼’——不知什么地方是这么说的来着。也许他会发现果然如此。”

他点上一支新烟，像是把斯滕内斯从心里撇开了。

“不知那个魔鬼的发条上得够不够紧，肯不肯再拼最后一把？”他略带困惑地思忖着，“我相信够了。无论如何，我要冒这个险。到目前为止，他一路随心所欲，这一着该能给他鼓劲。”

阿德斯利走进房来，见克林顿爵士独自一人，脸上现出笑意。

===Original===
“I’m in a hurry, Ardsley. Stenness has taken up a lot of valuable time with his woes. What I wanted is this. Will you be at the house-door here at half-past six o’clock tomorrow morning without fail? I’ll need you, perhaps.”

He paused, then as an afterthought apparently, he added:

“I don’t suppose you could give a death certificate in the case of a death by misadventure, could you?”

Ardsley shook his head.

===Chinese===
“我赶时间，阿德斯利。斯滕内斯的那些伤心事，占去了不少宝贵的工夫。我要说的是这一件：明天早晨六点半，你能务必在这宅子门口吗？我兴许用得着你。”

他顿了顿，随后像是临时又想起什么，补上一句：

“倘若是意外死亡（death by misadventure）的案子，你大概开不出死亡证明书来吧，能不能？”

阿德斯利摇了摇头。

===Original===
“I’d need to know something about the case before I could venture on that sort of thing. The Coroner would want to have a say in business of that kind.”

Sir Clinton reluctantly agreed.

“Now suppose we pick up Wendover in the other room. No one will want to see Miss Hawkhurst tonight?”

“I’ll see to that,” Ardsley assured him. “Another doctor will need to be called in, and so forth. Until he turns up, I think no one need go into that room.”

===Chinese===
“我得先了解案子的详情，才敢贸然去碰那一类事情。那种事，验尸官是要过问一声的。”

克林顿爵士勉强同意了。

“现在，我们到另一间屋里去接温多弗吧。今晚不会有人要探望霍克赫斯特小姐了吧？”

“这个我会料理妥当。”阿德斯利向他保证，“还得再请一位医生来，诸如此类。在他到之前，我想谁也不必进那个房间。”

## XVI The Last Attack in the Maze / 十六 迷宫里的最后一次袭击

===Original===
When Ardsley and the Chief Constable entered the other room they found Wendover with Ernest Shandon and Arthur. In the stress of his emotion, Ernest seemed to have flown to his usual comforter, for he had a decanter and a syphon at his elbow. Arthur Hawkhurst seemed to be endeavouring to restrain his feelings to the best of his ability; but it was obvious at a glance that his nerves were all on edge.

“Miss Forrest isn’t here?” Sir Clinton inquired, though the question was needless.

“No,” Ernest hastened to explain. “She’s not here. I think she must be somewhere else—upstairs in her own room, perhaps, or else somewhere about the house. Or she may have gone out with Torrance. He’s gone for a walk. Quite possibly she went out with him. Very thoughtful of them to leave us to our grief, very thoughtful. I don’t know how we’ll get over this; I really don’t know. Sylvia was so useful about the place—made things run so smoothly, you know. We’ll miss her terribly.”

He drank some of his whisky and soda.

===Chinese===
阿德斯利和郡警察局长走进另一间屋子时，发现温多弗正同欧内斯特·香农和阿瑟在一起。情绪的重压之下，欧内斯特像是又投奔了他素来依赖的安慰剂——他肘边正摆着一只玻璃酒瓶和一支苏打水瓶。阿瑟·霍克赫斯特像是正竭尽所能地克制着自己的情绪；可一眼便看得出，他的神经全绷在了刃口上。

“福雷斯特小姐不在？”克林顿爵士问道，尽管这一问本属多余。

“不在。”欧内斯特赶忙解释，“她不在这儿。我想她准是在别处——也许在楼上她自己房里，再不就在宅子里什么地方。要不然，就是跟托伦斯出去了。他散步去了。她跟他一道出去，也很有可能。把我们撂给悲伤，他们想得真周到，真周到。这一关我们怎么熬过去，我不知道，真的不知道。西尔维娅在这个家里那么有用——各样事情都给她打点得顺顺当当，你是知道的。没有她，我们会想她想得厉害。”

他喝了口威士忌苏打。

===Original===
“Where’s Stenness?” demanded Arthur, as though to show that he had himself under control.

“He’s busy,” Sir Clinton explained.

“He’s lucky to have something to be busy with,” Arthur commented. “I wish I’d something to do to take my mind off this business just now.”

Ernest drank some more whisky and soda thoughtfully, then put his hand into his pocket and seemed to feel for something.

“I’ve lost my cigar-case,” he announced disconsolately. “Really, everything seems to be going wrong together, these last few days.”

===Chinese===
“斯滕内斯呢？”阿瑟质问道，像是要显示自己把持得住。

“他正忙着。”克林顿爵士解释道。

“他有事可忙，倒是走运。”阿瑟评道，“这会儿我倒盼着有点什么事做做，好把心思从这桩事上岔开。”

欧内斯特若有所思地又呷了几口威士忌苏打，随后把手伸进口袋，像是在摸索什么。

“我的雪茄烟盒丢了。”他悻悻然宣布，“真的，这几天，样样事情像是商量好了似的，一齐出了岔子。”

===Original===
Wendover opened his mouth to speak, but closed it again as he caught Sir Clinton’s frown and remembered the Chief Constable’s caution to him about the case.

“Lost your case?” Sir Clinton inquired. “That’s a nuisance.”

Ernest was still feeling vaguely in his pocket as though he expected to unearth the cigar-case in some remote corner.

“It’s been missing for a day or two,” he complained. “I can’t think what’s become of it. I’ve hunted through all my other suits, and it isn’t there. And I’ve searched all over the house, too; and yet I can’t find it. I suppose I’ll have to buy another. And that’s such a nuisance, you know. One gets accustomed to the thing one uses. A new one won’t feel the same for long enough.”

===Chinese===
温多弗张口欲言，可一撞见克林顿爵士皱起的眉头，又想起郡警察局长就本案给他的告诫，便把话咽了回去。

“烟盒丢了？”克林顿爵士问道，“那可是件麻烦事。”

欧内斯特还在口袋里恍恍惚惚地摸索，仿佛指望那烟盒会从哪个僻远的角落里自行现身。

“它已经丢了一两天了。”他抱怨道，“我想不出它出了什么事。我其余那几套衣裳全翻遍了，没有。整座宅子我也搜了个遍，还是找不着。看来我只得再买一只了。你知道，那可是件麻烦事。人用惯了自己的东西。新的一只，得过上好一阵子才用得顺手。”

===Original===
“You can’t remember where you put it down last, I suppose?” Sir Clinton asked. “It’s always a good plan to go back to the time you can remember using it last. If I’m not mistaken, you had it with you in the Maze when you were attacked. You told me you took out a cigar then. Does that suggest anything? You may be able to pick up the thread now and remember using it again after that.”

Ernest Shandon’s face lighted up with a certain dull satisfaction.

“No. Now I remember quite well. You’ve reminded me of it. Isn’t it funny how one can forget a thing and then, if one gets a jog to one’s memory, the whole thing comes back again? I often find that, quite often.”

“So you know where it is now? Well, that’s always a relief.”

===Chinese===
“你记不起最后一次把它放在哪儿了，我猜是吧？”克林顿爵士问道，“回到你记得最后一次用它的时候，向来是个好法子。如果我没弄错，你遭袭击那会儿，它是随你带在迷宫里的。你告诉过我，你当时还掏出一支雪茄来着。这有没有让你想起什么？你兴许能就此把线头接上，记起在那之后又用过它。”

欧内斯特·香农的脸上亮起了一种呆滞的得意。

“不。现在我记得清清楚楚了。是你提醒了我。一件事竟能忘个干净，可只要有人往你的记忆上捅一下，整桩事又都回来了——你说怪不怪？我常碰到这种事，常常碰到。”

“这么说，你现在知道它在哪儿了？好，那总算让人松口气。”

===Original===
Ernest’s face fell again.

“Yes, I remember where I dropped it. But I can’t get it tonight, that’s the worst of it. I dropped it in the Maze when I was shot at. I was sitting there in Helen’s Bower, and when I jumped up the thing fell off my knee. It must be lying there yet. I’d forgotten all about it. Those cigars won’t be much good now,” he ended, regretfully.

“Why not go and get it?” Wendover asked, with a tinge of malice. He had not forgiven Ernest for his pusillanimous display on the night that Sylvia was shot.

Ernest looked round at him, wide-eyed in astonishment. He took off his glasses, polished them carefully, replaced them on his nose, and continued his staring examination of Wendover.

“Well, really,” he managed to say at last, “that’s a very strange suggestion, Wendover, very strange. Do you imagine that I’d go out in the dark, down to the Maze, and hunt about for my case? Why, it would be foolhardy, positively tempting Providence, to do that. This murderer fellow may be lurking outside the house-door, for all we can tell; and you calmly propose that I should walk straight out and put myself in his way! Well, really …”

He turned to the decanter at his side and poured out a fresh stiff glass.

===Chinese===
欧内斯特的脸色又耷拉了下来。

“是啊，我记得我把它掉在哪儿了。可今晚我拿不回来，最糟就糟在这里。我挨枪的那会儿，把它掉在迷宫里了。我当时正坐在海伦亭里，跳起来的时候，那东西从我膝上滑了下去。这会儿它一定还躺在那儿呢。我把它忘了个一干二净。那些雪茄如今也要不成了。”他懊恼地收了尾。

“那为什么不去取回来？”温多弗问道，话里带着一丝恶意。西尔维娅中镖的那一夜，欧内斯特那副贪生怕死的嘴脸，他还没有原谅。

欧内斯特瞪圆了眼睛，愕然回头看他。他摘下眼镜，细细擦拭一番，重新架回鼻梁，继续盯着温多弗上下打量。

“哎呀，真是的，”他好歹总算憋出了一句，“这可是个非常古怪的建议，温多弗，非常古怪。你莫非以为，我会摸着黑出门，下到迷宫里去，为一只烟盒翻来找去？哎呀，那样做简直是莽撞行事，分明是在触犯天意。这个杀人犯家伙说不定就埋伏在宅门外，咱们谁说得准；你倒好，气定神闲地提议我径直走出去，把自己送到他的道上！哎呀，真是的……”

他转向身畔的酒瓶，又给自己满满斟上一杯。

===Original===
Arthur Hawkhurst had listened to his uncle’s exhibition of caution with unconcealed contempt; and he now broke in with all the brutality of youth.

“Cold feet, eh?”

Ernest seemed to resent the imputation with a certain dull animosity.

“It seems to me only common sense, Arthur. Why should I run any risks? I’ve been attacked once; and since they didn’t succeed in damaging me then, they’ll obviously be waiting for another chance. I think it would be a stupid move to put myself in the way, a very stupid move indeed. And I think most sensible people would agree with me. If you don’t, and if you want something to do, you might go down to the Maze and get me my cigar-case yourself. That would be better than sitting there, sneering at your elders.”

He assumed the air of one who had just administered a well-merited rebuke; but his dignity was slightly diminished by the necessity of putting his glasses straight. Arthur seemed to take his uncle’s protest as a taunt.

===Chinese===
舅舅这一番谨慎的表演，阿瑟·霍克赫斯特一直听着，脸上不加掩饰地满是轻蔑；此刻他插了进来，拿出青年人特有的那种不留情面。

“吓破胆了，嗯？”

对这句指控，欧内斯特像是怀着一种迟钝的敌意。

“在我看来，这不过是常识，阿瑟。我为什么要去冒什么险？我已经被袭击过一回了；那一回他们没能伤着我，往后他们显然还会等下一次机会。我认为自己往他道上凑是愚蠢的一招，实在是愚蠢得很的一招。而且我想，大多数明白人都会同意我。你要是不同意，又想找点事做，大可以自己下到迷宫去，替我把雪茄烟盒取回来。那总比你坐在那儿讥笑长辈强。”

他摆出一副刚刚训诫过人、而对方罪有应得的架势；可惜他不得不扶正眼镜，这份尊严也就折损了几分。阿瑟却像是把舅舅这一番分辩，当成了对他的挑衅。

===Original===
“Think I’m in a funk, too? I’ll go and get your case if you like.”

Ernest appeared to be horrified at the suggestion.

“I couldn’t think of it!” he exclaimed, almost with animation. “Why, anything might happen out in the dark there. You’re not to go, Arthur. I forbid it.”

Arthur’s lips shut tight for a moment as he looked at his uncle.

“It doesn’t matter a damn to me whether you forbid it or not. I’ve offered to go; and I’m not going to draw back now and let people think I was only bragging. Besides, how do you know anyone’s after me? They’re after you all right; they’ve attacked you already. But that’s no reason why they should worry about me, is it? I guess you’re the one they’ve marked down, uncle.”

===Chinese===
“当我也吓慌了？行啊，你乐意的话，我就去把你的烟盒取来。”

这个提议似乎把欧内斯特吓坏了。

“这个我连想都不能想！”他嚷道，几乎来了精神，“哎呀，外头黑地里什么事都可能出。你不许去，阿瑟。我禁止你去。”

阿瑟望着舅舅，双唇紧紧抿了片刻。

“你禁不禁止，关我什么事。我已经开了口要去；这会儿可不能缩回去，让人家当我只会吹牛。再说，你怎么知道有人要害我？要害的是你，没错；他们已经袭击过你了。可这并不是说他们就会来找我麻烦，对不对？我琢磨着，他们记上名册的那一个，是你，舅舅。”

===Original===
“Oh, indeed, do you think so?” said Ernest, uncomfortably. “I don’t think that’s a very nice thing to say, Arthur. It’s one of these things that may have too much truth in them to be altogether a joke, you know. I wish you wouldn’t say them. I don’t like them, I really don’t. And I don’t want you to go out of the house tonight. Suppose anything were to happen to you!”

He paused for a moment, then added as a final argument:

“We’ve got trouble enough on our hands just now.”

Sir Clinton was watching Arthur keenly; and the boy turned round in time to catch the expression on the Chief Constable’s face.

“You think I was just bragging? All right, you’ll see. I’ll take one of the cars down to the Maze and I’ll be back in ten minutes.”

He turned on his heel and walked out of the room. To Wendover’s surprise, Sir Clinton made no attempt to detain him. Ernest’s face showed marked annoyance. Evidently he could not help seeing that his super-caution had been brought into the limelight once again.

===Chinese===
“哦，当真，你这么想？”欧内斯特说道，浑身不自在，“我看这话可不大中听，阿瑟。你知道，这类话里头，说不定实情的成分太重，全当玩笑看待不得。我劝你别说这些话。我不爱听，真的不爱听。还有，今晚我也不想让你出这座宅子。万一你有个三长两短怎么办！”

他顿了片刻，随后抛出最后一项论据：

“咱们眼下的麻烦已经够多了。”

克林顿爵士正锐利地注视着阿瑟；这少年转过头去，恰好逮住了郡警察局长脸上的神情。

“你们当我光是吹牛？好，走着瞧。我开一辆车下迷宫去，十分钟就回来。”

他脚跟一转，出了房门。让温多弗诧异的是，克林顿爵士竟没有半分挽留的意思。欧内斯特的脸上显出明显的恼怒。他显然不能不看到：自己那份过了头的谨慎，又被端到了聚光灯下，又是一回。

===Original===
“I think that’s a silly trick,” he complained. “What’s the good of running unnecessary risks? I can get along without my cigar-case till tomorrow. And here he goes off, posing to himself as a young hero—while really, you know, he’s only a foolhardy young ass. But he was always like that. I wish Sylvia had been here; she could manage him. I never seemed to have any influence with him, somehow.”

Ardsley, obviously bored, rose and left the room. Sir Clinton took the opportunity of changing the subject.

“While your nephew’s away, Mr. Shandon, I think I’d better take the opportunity of giving you some information. You remember telling me something about a cheque and a missing counterfoil?”

“Yes,” Ernest admitted, apparently not averse to the fresh subject. “I thought I ought to tell you about that! It may not have been important; but I thought you ought to have all the information about everything, even if it didn’t seem to be anything very vital, you understand? One never knows how one thing may hinge on to another, if you see what I mean? And it certainly seemed a strange thing to me—very rum.”

“I’ve looked into the matter,” Sir Clinton went on, “and I think I’ve succeeded in doing what’s probably the most important thing from your point of view. I’ve recovered a few thousand pounds, which somebody might have got away with. You’ll find it in your safe tonight. It will be in notes. You’d better take the numbers of them. I haven’t had time to do that; and it might be as well to know them.”

===Chinese===
“我看这是个愚蠢的把戏。”他抱怨道，“冒不必要的险，图的是什么？没了雪茄烟盒，我照样能挨到明天。可他倒好，就这么跑了出去，自顾自扮起少年英雄来——其实呢，你知道，他不过是一头莽撞的小蠢驴。他向来是这个德行。西尔维娅要是在这儿就好了；她管得住他。不知怎么，我在他身上好像从来就没有过半点威信。”

阿德斯利显然乏味透了，起身出了房。克林顿爵士趁此机会换了个话题。

“趁你外甥不在，香农先生，我想我不妨借这个机会向你通报一点情况。你还记得吧，你跟我说过一张支票和一份缺失存根的事？”

“记得。”欧内斯特承认道，对换上的新话题显然并不排斥，“我当时就觉得，这事我应当告诉你！它也许算不得要紧；可我想，样样情况你都应该掌握，哪怕看上去无关痛痒，你明白吗？谁也说不准一件事会在哪儿扣着另一件事，你懂我的意思吧？何况那件事在我看来确实蹊跷——怪得很。”

“这件事我已经查办过了。”克林顿爵士接着说，“我想，从你的角度看，我大概已经办成了最要紧的一件事。我追回了几千英镑——那笔钱，本来是可能叫人卷走的。今晚你就会在你那只保险柜里见到。都是钞票。你最好把号码记下来。我一直没腾出工夫；知道号码，总归是有用的。”

===Original===
Ernest’s eyes lighted up when he heard the result of Sir Clinton’s work; but the brief illumination died and was followed by a depressed expression.

“Does this mean there’ll be a prosecution; and I’ll have to give evidence? That’ll be a troublesome business.”

Sir Clinton reassured him with a gesture.

“Well, perhaps we’d better not start crossing bridges till we come to them. Let’s leave the matter for the present. It’s not for me to advise you whether to force on a case or not. I’ll even refrain from mentioning the name of the man who took the money.”

“Oh, I’ve a pretty good notion of that,” Ernest protested, with a certain air of low cunning which sat ill on his dull features. “I may not be very clever, you know; but I can put two and two together all right.”

“Then we’ll leave it at that,” said Sir Clinton, and his tone closed the discussion on that point.

===Chinese===
听到克林顿爵士这番工作的结果，欧内斯特的眼睛亮了一下；可那点短暂的光亮随即熄灭，跟着换上了一副沮丧的神情。

“这是不是意味着要提起公诉；我还得去作证？那可是件麻烦事。”

克林顿爵士做了个手势，示意他宽心。

“这个嘛，桥还没到跟前，咱们最好先别忙着过。这事眼下就搁一搁。要不要把案子强推下去，这不是我该向你进言的事。我甚至连拿走钱的那人的姓名都绝口不提。”

“哦，那人是谁，我心里颇有数。”欧内斯特抗议道，带着一副市井式的狡黠，那份神气安在他呆滞的面相上很不相称，“我知道，我兴许算不得聪明；可把两下里一搭，我还是推断得来的。”

“那么，这事就到此为止。”克林顿爵士说道，他的语气把这一点上的讨论封了口。

===Original===
For a time they sat in silence. Wendover could not quite understand Sir Clinton’s manoeuvres. Quite obviously he had given Arthur the last spur which had driven him into this expedition to the Maze; and Wendover was inclined to agree with Ernest that it was a foolhardy business. He waited with some anxiety for the boy’s return.

All at once they heard the sound of hasty footsteps in the hall, and the door was flung open, and Arthur hurried into the room. Wendover noticed that, though excited, he was in no panic.

“You were right enough, uncle!” he exclaimed, still standing with the handle of the door in his hand. “The beggar had a shot at me just at the entrance to the Maze.”

Ernest nodded his head with an attempt at sapience.

“I told you so,” he said. “I told you so! But of course you wouldn’t believe me. No, you knew more about it than I did. But you see now …”

===Chinese===
有一阵子，他们默默坐着。温多弗不大看得透克林顿爵士的手脚。显而易见，正是他给了阿瑟最后一鞭，把他策进了这趟迷宫之行；而温多弗倒倾向于同意欧内斯特的看法：这是件莽撞的勾当。他怀着几分焦灼，等那少年回来。

猛然间，他们听见门厅里一阵急促的脚步声，房门被一把推开，阿瑟急匆匆闯了进来。温多弗注意到，他虽然兴奋，却毫无惊慌失措。

“舅舅，你的话一点不错！”他还站在门口，一只手握着门把手，嚷道，“那家伙就在迷宫入口那儿，冲我开了一枪。”

欧内斯特点了点头，摆出一副先知的架子。

“我早说过吧，”他说，“我早说过吧！可你当然不肯信我。不，你比我懂嘛。可现在你瞧见了……”

===Original===
“Come along, the lot of you,” Arthur cried. “We’ll nab the beggar this time. He can’t be far away yet.”

“Sit down!” Sir Clinton ordered calmly. “He’s had any amount of time to get clear away. We’d never catch him in the dark. I must hear how it happened, first of all. Now give me every detail you can think of.”

Arthur seemed sobered by the matter-of-fact air of the Chief Constable. He sat down and began his story without more ado.

“I took out the two-seater from the garage,” he explained, “and bucketted down to the Maze as quick as I could. It’s a dark night outside, not even a star showing. I left the headlights on when I stopped the car; and I left the engine running as well. It wasn’t going to take me any time to get to Helen’s Bower. I got out, and crossed over to the entrance to the Maze that’s nearest the road. It was pretty dark; but I could find my way all right.”

“You heard nobody about?” asked Wendover.

“Nothing but the beat of the engine. Just as I got to the entrance and was going inside, I heard an airgun go off quite close to me—no distance at all, I should say—and I felt something hit me about the breast-pocket.”

===Chinese===
“你们全都来呀。”阿瑟喊道，“这回咱们把他逮住。他这会儿还跑不远。”

“坐下！”克林顿爵士平静地下令，“他有的是工夫跑得干干净净。摸着黑，咱们怎么也抓不着他。首先，我得听明白事情是怎么发生的。现在，你想到的每一个细节，都讲给我听。”

郡警察局长这副就事论事的气派，似乎让阿瑟清醒了几分。他坐下，二话不说，讲起了经过。

“我从车库里开出了那辆双座车，”他解释道，“一路颠簸着，尽快往迷宫赶。外头夜色浓黑，连一颗星都不露。停了车，我没关前灯；发动机也由它转着。到海伦亭去，用不了我什么工夫。我下了车，朝离大路最近的那处迷宫入口走过去。天相当黑；可我认路还不成问题。”

“你没听见附近有人？”温多弗问。

“除了发动机的声响，什么也没有。我刚到入口，正要进去，就听见一支气枪在我近旁开了火——照我看简直近在咫尺——又觉得什么东西打在我胸前口袋那一带。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton leaned forward.

“Don’t touch!” he said, pointing to the side of Arthur’s dinner-jacket. “You hadn’t a coat on, I see?”

Arthur looked down. The feathering of one of the lethal darts was protruding from his jacket.

“Oh, it stuck, did it?” he said. “I thought it had failed to get through the cloth. It’s driven into my leather cigar-case, I expect.”

Sir Clinton made a rapid examination and then cautiously withdrew the dart. Inspection of the cigar-case showed that the point of the missile had embedded itself in one of the cigars.

“That saved you a nasty prick,” was all the comment the Chief Constable made. “Let’s hear the rest.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士倾身向前。

“别碰！”他指着阿瑟晚宴服的一侧说道，“我看，你外面没套大衣？”

阿瑟低头一瞧。一支致命飞镖的镖羽，正从他上衣上戳出来。

“哦，它钉住了，是吧？”他说，“我还当它没能穿透衣料呢。我猜，它是扎进我那只皮雪茄烟盒里了。”

克林顿爵士飞快地查看一番，随即小心翼翼地把飞镖起了出来。一验雪茄烟盒，只见那枚飞镖的镖尖，深深扎进了其中一支雪茄里。

“这只烟盒替你挡了一记狠扎。”郡警察局长评论了这么一句，“接着讲吧。”

===Original===
“I was just going to start after the beggar,” Arthur went on, “when suddenly there was a yell from the road. When I looked round, there was old Mrs. Thornton having a fit of hysterics or something over on the road, right in the beam of the headlights.”

“Who is Mrs. Thornton?” inquired Sir Clinton.

“She’s the wife of the lodge-keeper at the East Gate.”

“There’s nothing wrong with the Thorntons,” Wendover hastened to interject. “I’ve known them for twenty years and a decenter old couple you couldn’t find anywhere.”

===Chinese===
“我正要拔腿去追那家伙，”阿瑟接着说，“大路上忽然爆出一声尖叫。我回头一看，桑顿老太太正在大路上犯歇斯底里还是怎么的，恰恰站在前灯的光柱里。”

“桑顿太太是谁？”克林顿爵士问。

“她是东门看门人的老婆。”

“桑顿两口子不会有问题。”温多弗赶忙插话，“我认识他们二十年了，再正派的老夫妻，你打着灯笼也找不出第二对。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton made a gesture as though brushing aside the interruption.

“And then?” he demanded.

“Well, there she was, screaming her head off,” Arthur continued. “She’d heard the sound of the airgun; and of course airguns aren’t liked hereabouts nowadays. So she’d just started to yell at once. She thought it was meant for her, it seems. I hustled her into the car and drove her home, full tilt. Then when I’d got rid of her, I put up the hood and side-curtains and came back here again, hell-for-leather. I guessed that the side-curtains would stop anything, if the beggar had another try; and I had the throttle about full open as I passed the Maze, so he hadn’t much of a mark, anyway.”

Wendover’s opinion of Arthur went up considerably during this narrative. The youngster seemed to have had sense enough to take precautions, once he was convinced of the reality of the danger. And there was no doubt about the attack. Wendover had seen the depth to which the dart had penetrated the cigar-case and its contents. It must have been fired at very close range indeed.

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士做了个手势，仿佛把这句插话拂到一旁。

“后来呢？”他追问。

“唉，她就在那儿，没命地尖叫，”阿瑟继续说，“她听见了气枪的响声；这一带如今当然一听见气枪就犯忌讳。于是她当场就嚷开了。看样子，她当那一枪是冲她去的。我连搡带劝把她塞进车里，一路全速把她送回了家。打发掉她之后，我支起车篷和侧帘，没命地往回赶。我盘算着，那家伙若再来一下，侧帘总归挡得住；而且路过迷宫那一段，我把油门几乎踩到了底，反正他没捞着个像样的靶子。”

这一番叙述，让温多弗对阿瑟的评价大涨。这少年一旦认定了危险是实实在在的，倒还有足够的头脑采取防备。而这次袭击也确凿无疑。飞镖扎进雪茄烟盒和盒中之物有多深，温多弗亲眼见过。那一镖，必是在极近的距离内射出的无疑。

===Original===
