“H’m!” said Sir Clinton. “Now I think we’ll take your advice and get down to the Maze.”

Much to the surprise of them all, Ernest got to his feet with the rest. He evidently saw their expressions, for he seemed rather shamefaced.

“I think I’ll come along with you,” he said, diffidently. Then, with an assumption of confidence he added: “I know you’ve all been sneering at me for taking care of my skin. I’ll just show you that it was caution and not because I was afraid. I can take the same risks as the rest of you!”

On reflection, Wendover was hardly so much impressed by this offer. Obviously, the murderer, having made his attack, would at once set about getting away from the neighbourhood of the Maze; and by this time he would probably be far enough away to avoid pursuit. Ernest, therefore, was risking very little by joining the party.

“Take out the limousine, Arthur,” he suggested. “It’ll hold the lot of us—and the glass will give us good cover.”

Wendover smiled at the return of Ernest’s caution, though he admitted that the choice was sound enough. They hurried to the garage and Arthur drove them down to the Maze.

===Chinese===
“哼！”克林顿爵士道，“现在我想，咱们不妨听你的，下迷宫去一趟。”

让众人大为诧异的是，欧内斯特竟也随大家一同站起了身。他分明瞧见了众人的神色，因为他显得相当讪讪。

“我想，我跟你们一道去。”他怯怯地说。随即又装出一副笃定的口气补上一句：“我知道，你们全都在讥笑我惜命。我正好让你们瞧瞧，那是谨慎，不是因为怕。你们冒得起的险，我也一样冒得起！”

细一想，温多弗对这份壮举却不怎么动容。显而易见，凶手一击得手，必定立刻着手离开迷宫一带；到这会儿，多半已经逃得够远、追之不及了。因此，欧内斯特此时入伙，实在冒不了什么险。

“把那辆大轿车开出来，阿瑟。”他建议道，“咱们全都坐得下——那玻璃也能给咱们很好的遮蔽。”

见欧内斯特的谨慎又回来了，温多弗微微一笑，尽管他承认这一选择相当在理。他们赶到车库，阿瑟载着众人直下迷宫。

===Original===
Once there, Ernest seemed to feel that he had perhaps been over-courageous.

“Somebody ought to look after the car,” he suggested. “If we leave it here the fellow may steal up and go off with it, suppose he is lurking about. And where should we be then? He’d have got clean away and left us standing. I think I’d better sit in the car while you hunt about, and then we’ll know. …”

At the sight of the open contempt on Sir Clinton’s face, he let his proposal die away before it was completed, and crawled reluctantly out of the car with the others. He even made a show of eagerness and led the way to the Maze entrance.

“You’re off the line a bit, uncle,” Arthur pointed out.

“I can’t see very well in the dark,” Ernest complained. “And this grass is simply soaked with dew. I’ve got my feet all wet. Such a nuisance. …”

He tripped over something and came heavily to the ground. A heartfelt oath reached their ears.

===Chinese===
一到地方，欧内斯特像是觉得，自己方才或许勇敢过了头。

“总得有人看着车。”他建议道，“咱们要是把它撂在这儿，那家伙万一还在附近潜着，说不定就摸上来把它开跑了。到那时咱们可怎么办？他跑了个干净，把咱们撂在原地干站着。我看，你们四下搜寻的时候，我还是坐在车里好，这样咱们就有数了……”

一见克林顿爵士脸上那份毫不掩饰的轻蔑，他后半截话没说完便让它自生自灭，只得同大伙儿一道，老大不情愿地从车里爬了出来。他甚至还装出一副踊跃的样子，领头往迷宫入口走去。

“舅舅，你偏了道了。”阿瑟指出。

“黑地里我看不大清楚。”欧内斯特抱怨道，“再说这草简直叫露水泡透了。我两只脚全湿了。真是麻烦……”

他被什么东西绊了一下，重重扑倒在地。一声发自肺腑的咒骂传进众人耳中。

===Original===
“I’m wet all down the front, now,” Ernest wailed. “I fell over some damned thing or other and I’ve hurt my toe. I hope it hasn’t split the nail. What *is* this thing, damn it?”

He seemed to be feeling about in the dark.

“Why! It’s the airgun!”

Sir Clinton’s flash-lamp suddenly shot out its glare; and in the cone of illumination they saw the grotesque figure of Ernest kneeling on the ground with the airgun clutched in his hand. He rose to his feet laboriously.

“I’m soaking with that dew. Very heavy it’s been tonight. Wasn’t it a godsend that I had a spot of whisky just before coming out? That’ll keep a cold away. I’ll have another one—a whisky hot—when I get back again.”

Sir Clinton paid no attention to Ernest’s babble. He took the airgun gingerly from its discoverer’s hand and held it out to Arthur in the glare of the flash-lamp.

===Chinese===
“这下我前襟全湿了。”欧内斯特哀嚎道，“我绊上了哪个天杀的东西，脚趾也碰伤了。但愿趾甲没劈裂。这*到底*是个什么东西，见鬼？”

他像是正在黑暗里摸索。

“咦！是气枪！”

克林顿爵士的手电筒倏地射出光来；在那一锥亮光里，他们看见欧内斯特那副怪诞的身影——跪在地上，手里死死攥着那支气枪。他费了好大的劲才直起身来。

“那场露水把我浇了个透。今晚的露水真重。幸亏出门前我喝了一小口威士忌，这可不是天赐的吗？那能顶住感冒。等回去我再要一杯——热威士忌。”

克林顿爵士对欧内斯特的絮叨不予理会。他小心翼翼地从发现者手里接过气枪，举到手电筒的亮光里，递给阿瑟。

===Original===
“One of the local armoury, I suppose?”

Arthur examined it for a moment.

“Yes, that’s one of ours.”

The honours of discovery, however, seemed destined to fall to Ernest.

“Here,” he demanded, “turn that light over this way, will you? There’s something round my foot.”

They could hear him kicking in the obscurity. Sir Clinton swung the beam round and stooped down.

===Chinese===
“该是本地枪械库里的家伙吧？”

阿瑟端详了片刻。

“对，是我们家的。”

不过，发现的荣光，似乎注定还要落到欧内斯特头上。

“喂，”他要求道，“把灯往这边照照，行不行？我脚边有个什么东西。”

他们听得见他在暗处乱踢。克林顿爵士把光柱转过去，俯下身来。

===Original===
“It’s a bit of black thread you’ve got tangled up in. Wait a jiffy.”

He freed Ernest from the fibre and began to trace along the thread with his light. It seemed to be merely the end of a long tentacle extending out from the entrance to the Maze.

“Ariadne’s clue!” exclaimed Wendover, when he saw the direction in which the filament lay.

Sir Clinton nodded briefly.

“You people had better get back to the car,” he said. “I don’t want the ground trampled here. We can look at it in the morning. I’m just going to follow up this thread. I’ll be back in a minute or two.”

Holding his light low, he disappeared into the intricacies of the Maze, while Wendover shepherded the others back to the car. Once round a corner or two and well out of sight of the rest, Sir Clinton ceased to trouble about the thread and made his way direct to one centre of the Maze. He sought about for a time, evidently fruitlessly; and then made his way to the other centre. Here his search was more successful. Among some bushes in the enclosure, he unearthed a suitcase.

===Chinese===
“是一截黑线，缠在你脚上了。稍等一下。”

他把欧内斯特从那根细丝里解出来，开始打着灯沿线追索。那似乎只是一条长长的触须的末梢，从迷宫入口一路伸展出来。

“阿里阿德涅的引线！”温多弗看见那根细丝卧倒的方向，脱口喊道。

克林顿爵士简短地点了点头。

“你们几位还是回车里去好。”他说，“我不想这里的地面叫人踩乱。明早咱们再来看。我就去把这条线追下去。一两分钟就回来。”

他把灯压得低低的，消失在迷宫的重重幽径之中；温多弗则把其余几人拢回车里。转过一两个弯、确知旁人再也望不见自己之后，克林顿爵士便不再理会那条线，径直朝迷宫的一处中心走去。他四下搜寻了一阵，显然一无所获；随后又奔另一处中心去。这一趟搜寻却有了结果。在围场里的几丛灌木间，他刨出了一只手提箱。

===Original===
“Well, *that* was a long shot,” he admitted to himself, though with evident satisfaction. “He’s evidently not too sure that he’s taken me in with his soft sawder, and he’s provided for contingencies. Let’s see.”

He opened the suitcase and scrutinised one or two of the garments in it.

“Complete change of clothes and no marking on so much as a handkerchief. Quite right!”

He re-closed the suitcase and put it back into the hiding-place in which he had found it. Then he retraced his steps in the Maze until he came to the black thread which he proceeded to follow to the end.

===Chinese===
“唉，*这一趟*原是碰运气的瞎猜。”他自认自道，语气里却透着明显的得意，“他显然拿不准他那套甜言蜜语有没有把我哄住，便为万一备下了后手。来瞧瞧。”

他打开手提箱，细细查验了里面的一两件衣裳。

“全套换洗衣裳，连一方手帕上都找不到标记。很好！”

他重新扣好手提箱，放回原先起获它的藏匿处。随后他在迷宫里循原路折返，回到那截黑线旁，顺线一路追到头。

===Original===
“Now we’ll go back to the house,” he proposed, when he rejoined the others at the car. “That thread led me to the boathouse.”

“So the attack was made from the river?” Wendover asked.

“It’s strange that he didn’t pitch his gun into the water, isn’t it?” Sir Clinton said. “One might have expected him to get rid of it in the easiest way.”

“I expect he got a bit of a shock,” Arthur suggested. “He must have known that he hit me squarely and yet nothing happened. That would be a bit of a surprise to him, wouldn’t it. Perhaps he got rattled.”

“Lucky for you there wasn’t a second shot,” was Sir Clinton’s comment. “You could hardly expect your cigar-case to save you twice running.”

===Chinese===
“现在咱们回宅子去。”他回到车边与众人会合时提议道，“那条线把我引到了船屋。”

“这么说，那一枪是从河上打来的？”温多弗问。

“他没把枪扔进水里，这事有点怪，不是吗？”克林顿爵士说，“换作谁，都会料想他用最省事的法子把它处理掉。”

“我看他是受了一场惊吓。”阿瑟揣测道，“他准知道自己结结实实打中了我，可我竟安然无恙。这对他总归是桩意外，对不对？兴许他乱了阵脚。”

“算你运气，没有第二枪。”克林顿爵士评论道，“你总不能指望你的雪茄烟盒连着两回救你的命。”

===Original===
When they reached Whistlefield again, they found Ardsley talking to Torrance, who had returned from his walk. He had been out alone, it appeared. Vera had gone to her own room when Ardsley had given his news about Sylvia and had not reappeared again.

Sir Clinton took Ardsley aside for a moment.

“You’ve got a nurse upstairs in that room?”

Ardsley assented.

“One of them’s going to watch all night. There’s a superstition some people have that one shouldn’t leave a dead person alone. I don’t mind being superstitious for once, if it’s in a good cause.”

===Chinese===
他们回到惠斯特菲尔德时，看见阿德斯利正和托伦斯说话——托伦斯散步回来了。看样子，他是一个人出去的。阿德斯利把西尔维娅的消息一宣布，维拉便回自己房里去了，再没有露面。

克林顿爵士把阿德斯利拉到一旁说了几句。

“楼上那间房里，你们安排了护士？”

阿德斯利点头称是。

“其中一位要守一整夜。有些人有种迷信，说不该让死者独处。若是为着正当的缘由，我倒不介意破例迷信这一回。”

===Original===
“No one must get into the room, of course.”

“No one shall,” said Ardsley, definitely.

Sir Clinton seemed to be satisfied; and Ardsley left the room. The Chief Constable had one more private conversation still to carry through. He took Ernest Shandon into the study and closed the door.

“I can put my hand on the murderer now, Mr. Shandon, so you needn’t be nervous about that. But I’m rather troubled about one point. This is going to lead to the devil of a scandal if I arrest him. Are you anxious for that?”

Ernest seemed staggered by this way of looking at things.

===Chinese===
“当然，谁也不许进那间房。”

“谁也进不去。”阿德斯利斩钉截铁地说。

克林顿爵士似乎满意了；阿德斯利离了房。郡警察局长还有一场密谈尚待进行。他把欧内斯特·香农领进书房，关上了门。

“如今我一伸手就能拿住凶手了，香农先生，这一点你不必担心。倒是有个地方让我颇为犯难。我若逮捕他，这事要惹出一场天大的丑闻。你很巴望落到那一步吗？”

这样一种看事情的路数，似乎把欧内斯特打了个晕头转向。

===Original===
“Well, really, I don’t quite see what you mean. It’s a bit obscure, isn’t it? I must confess I don’t quite follow you, if you understand me?”

“I’ll put it this way. I could arrest the fellow tonight. I know where he is. I’d have no trouble over that. But I think I can make surer of him if I wait till tomorrow morning. I’ve got to risk his bolting. I’ve that possibility in view. He might get away. But if he got away, would you worry much? Think of the scandal it would save—and it’s going to be a big one. And the trial will be a most laborious affair, too. What do you think? Shall I arrest him now, or wait till the morning and risk his getting away?”

Ernest pondered over the problem, but he seemed incapable of giving any help.

“I really don’t know,” he said. “You’re too deep for me, really. I can’t make out what you’re driving at.”

Sir Clinton’s face showed disappointment.

“There are some things that a police official can’t put into plain words, you know. I can’t say outright that I’d be glad to see the beggar off the premises. Can’t you see what I mean?”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，真是的，我不大明白你的意思。这话有点绕，是不是？我必须承认，我实在跟不上你，你理解我吗？”

“我这么说吧。今晚我就可以逮捕那家伙。我知道他在哪儿。这毫无难处。可我想，若是等到明天早晨，我能把他拿得更稳。他畏罪潜逃的风险，我只好担着。这个可能性我已计在盘内。他也许真会跑掉。可他若是跑了，你会很放在心上吗？想想那能省下的丑闻——这丑闻还不小。再说，审判也将是桩极其劳民伤财的事。你怎么看？我是现在就抓他，还是等到明早，冒他跑掉的这个险？”

欧内斯特把这问题掂量了又掂量，可他像是全然帮不上一点忙。

“我真的不知道。”他说，“你太深了，真的。我摸不透你打的到底是什么主意。”

克林顿爵士的脸上现出失望。

“有些话，一个警务官员没法说成明白话，你知道。我没法直截了当地说，我巴不得那家伙从这片宅子里滚蛋。你就看不明白我的意思吗？”

===Original===
But Ernest shook his head dully; and Sir Clinton gave up the effort.

“Oh, very well,” he said. “The responsibility’s mine in any case. I’ll wait till tomorrow and chance it. But I can’t say I’ve got much help from you, Mr. Shandon. When the scandal comes, you needn’t blame me.”

He seemed to consider something for a moment, then he added:

“By the way, I suppose you won’t mind putting me up for the night? I’m taking all the responsibility I’ve any use for, as it is; and I think I ought to be able to say I was here tonight.”

Ernest seemed to be rendered completely owlish by this last request; but he assented willingly enough.

“And Wendover, too, of course,” added Sir Clinton.

He paused for a moment as though in doubt, before speaking again.

===Chinese===
可欧内斯特呆呆地摇了摇头；克林顿爵士只好死了这条心。

“哦，很好。”他说，“责任横竖都在我。我就等到明天，碰这个运气吧。不过，从你这里得到的助力，我可说不上有多少，香农先生。丑闻闹出来的时候，你用不着怪我。”

他像是掂量了片刻什么，随后又添上一句：

“对了，留我在这里过一夜，你大概不会见怪吧？我如今揽下的责任已经够我受用的了；而且我想，今晚我人在这里这件事，我应该能够亲口说得出来。”

这最后一个请求，似乎把欧内斯特弄得彻底晕头转向，瞪着眼一副茫然；可他还是相当爽快地应承了。

“当然，还有温多弗。”克林顿爵士补了一句。

他迟疑了片刻，像是有些拿不准，才又开口。

===Original===
“I think I’d better ring up the police station, if you don’t mind, Mr. Shandon. I’ll do it now.”

Ernest, with a shameless curiosity, followed him to the instrument and waited until he got the connection.

“Sir Clinton Driffield speaking. Sergeant, will you be good enough to buy me another tin of Navy Cut—same as the last you got—first thing tomorrow morning? I’ve run out of tobacco. Send a man up with it, will you? Yes, Navy Cut. Thanks.”

Sir Clinton turned away from the instrument and noticed his host hovering close beside him.

“It’s a handy thing to be a Chief Constable, isn’t it? I’d run out of tobacco and I won’t have time to go down to the village tomorrow morning. I shall arrest that fellow first thing after breakfast; and the formalities may take some time, you know.”

===Chinese===
“要是你不介意，香农先生，我想我最好给警察局挂个电话。我现在就打。”

欧内斯特揣着一副不知羞的好奇心，跟到电话机旁，守到线路接通。

“我是克林顿·德里菲尔德爵士。警佐，劳你的大驾，明天一早头一件事，再替我买一听 Navy Cut——跟你上回买的一样——好不好？我的烟抽完了。派个人送上来，行吗？对，Navy Cut。谢谢。”

克林顿爵士从电话机前转过身来，发觉主人正紧紧挨在旁边打转。

“当个郡警察局长，是桩便利的事，不是吗？我的烟抽完了，明早又腾不出工夫下村去。一吃过早饭，头一件事我就要逮捕那家伙；那些手续，你知道，兴许要费上些时间。”

## XVII The Siege in the Maze / 十七 迷宫围困

===Original===
Wendover was awakened next morning in an unfamiliar bedroom to find Sir Clinton at his side. The Chief Constable’s eyes were tired, as though he had had very little sleep; but otherwise he seemed as alert as usual.
“Come on, Squire!” he said. “Dress as quick as you can. You needn’t mind shaving for once. You’ve been clamouring for the arrest of the murderer for long enough now, so I thought you’d like to be in at the death. I’ve got an appointment with him this morning; so you’d better hurry up if you want to see the last scenes in the Whistlefield dramatic entertainment.”
Wendover had been rubbing his eyes rather sleepily when he awoke; but Sir Clinton’s words stimulated him into activity.
“Go on with your dressing and don’t talk,” Sir Clinton replied to his questions. “I haven’t time to explain things just now. There’ll be a good deal of explaining to be done in the end,” he added, gloomily, “so we may as well make one bite at it!”
Wendover hurried over his toilet, and soon he and Sir Clinton descended the stair and made their way to the front door. The figure of Stenness was plainly visible in the light of the early morning.
===Chinese===
第二天早晨，温多弗在一间陌生的卧室里被人唤醒，睁眼便见克林顿爵士立在床畔。郡警察局长眼里带着倦意，像是几乎不曾合眼；可除此之外，他看上去仍同素日一样警醒。
“快起来，乡绅！”他说，“能穿多快穿多快。这一回，胡子也免了罢。你嚷着要抓凶手，嚷得也够久了，所以我想，临了毙命的那一幕，你总乐意在场。今天早晨我跟他有个约会；你要是想看惠斯特菲尔德这出大戏的最后几幕，最好麻利些。”
温多弗醒来时，还睡眼惺忪地揉着眼睛；克林顿爵士这一席话，却把他激得行动起来了。
“只管穿衣裳，别说话。”克林顿爵士答他的问话道，“眼下我没有工夫解释。到头来反正有一大堆解释要做，”他阴郁地补了一句，“咱们索性一口把它嚼完得了！”
温多弗匆匆料理了梳洗，不多时便同克林顿爵士下了楼，径往正门去。晨光里，斯滕内斯的身影看得清清楚楚。

===Original===
“So it was Stenness? Somehow I thought it might have been he,” Wendover whispered, while they were still at a distance.
“I’ve got appointments with several people this morning,” Sir Clinton said, sharply. “Ardsley’s another of them. You needn’t start suspecting everyone, Squire, or you’ll have a busy time. No! No questions till later!”
He stepped forward and greeted the secretary.
“Where’s the gun-room, Stenness? It may be as well to pick up something useful!”
===Chinese===
“原来真是斯滕内斯？不知怎么，我先前还当多半就是他。”离得尚远时，温多弗低声道。
“今天早晨我有好几个约会。”克林顿爵士厉声道，“阿德斯利也是其中之一。你犯不上见人就疑，乡绅，不然有你忙的。不！有问题也留到回头再问！”
他跨上一步，向秘书招呼了一声。
“枪械室在哪儿，斯滕内斯？顺路带上几件顶用的东西，兴许不无好处！”

===Original===
The secretary led them down some passages. If he was surprised by Sir Clinton’s methods, he showed no visible sign. When they reached the gun-room, each selected a shotgun and ammunition under Sir Clinton’s orders. Wendover noticed that the Chief Constable picked out a couple himself.
“One’s for Ardsley,” he explained in answer to Wendover’s glance of surprise. “Come along! He’ll be waiting for us outside, I expect.”
When they reached the front door again, Ardsley was just stepping out of his car. Sir Clinton motioned him back into the driving seat and directed the others to get into the motor.
“The Maze, if you please, Ardsley,” he said, when they had seated themselves.
===Chinese===
秘书领着他们穿行了几条走廊。纵然克林顿爵士的做派叫他吃惊，他面上也不露分毫。到了枪械室，各人依克林顿爵士的吩咐挑了一支猎枪和弹药。温多弗注意到，郡警察局长自家挑了两支。
“一支是给阿德斯利的。”他解释道，算是回应温多弗惊讶的一瞥。“走吧！我料想他正在外头候着咱们。”
他们再回到正门前时，阿德斯利正跨出汽车。克林顿爵士示意他坐回驾驶座去，又吩咐其余的人上车。
“劳驾，去迷宫，阿德斯利。”待众人坐定，他说道。

===Original===
Wendover was completely at sea for a few moments. It was plain that both Ardsley and Stenness must be regarded as cleared in the eyes of the Chief Constable, or he would not have brought them there and taken the trouble to arm them. But if they were excluded, the murderer must belong to a very small group. And suddenly Wendover saw his way through the whole intricacy of the Whistlefield case.
“Of course! Young Torrance! He’s the man!”
But he was careful not to utter his views aloud, fearing to draw the fire of Sir Clinton, who was sitting beside him with drawn brows. Wendover felt it better to pursue his line of thought silently.
===Chinese===
有好一阵，温多弗全然如坠五里雾中。事情很清楚：在郡警察局长眼里，阿德斯利和斯滕内斯必定都已洗刷清白，不然他不会带他们上这儿来，还费事给他们配上枪。可这两人一经排除，凶手便只能属于一个小得可怜的圈子了。刹那间，温多弗豁然贯通，看破了惠斯特菲尔德全案的重重纠葛。
“当然了！小托伦斯！就是他！”
可他谨守着不把见解说出口，生怕招来克林顿爵士的排头——爵士正蹙着眉坐在他身旁呢。温多弗觉得，还是闷声把自己的思路推演下去为妙。

===Original===
“What an ass I’ve been! Young Torrance was in the Maze when the two Shandons were killed. He was somewhere or other about, probably, when Ernest was attacked. Clinton has most likely tracked him down without saying anything about it. And when Sylvia was shot, he wasn’t with us in the room; they said he was playing billiards by himself. Quite likely he sneaked out of the billiard room window, crept round, did the shooting, and got back under cover while we were all too taken aback by the business to do anything. If I’d been a shade quicker, that night, I’d have got him! And last night, when Arthur was attacked, Torrance had gone for a walk alone. It’s obvious! And like an idiot, I didn’t see it. All one needed was a pencil and paper and a list of the people who were actually on hand on each occasion; and then, by elimination, one was bound to get at him straight away.”
He pondered over his own obtuseness for a time, while the car ran down the road towards the Maze; but his train of thought was interrupted by Ardsley pulling up at a word from Sir Clinton. A uniformed constable stepped forward from the shelter of a clump of bushes, and Wendover saw with surprise that he had a revolver in his belt.
===Chinese===
“我这头蠢驴！两位香农遇害的时候，小托伦斯就在迷宫里。欧内斯特遇袭那回，他多半也在左近什么地方转悠。克林顿十有八九不动声色，早已把他追查出来了。西尔维娅中枪那阵，他没同我们待在屋里；他们说他独自打台球来着。极可能他是从台球室的窗户溜出去，绕到外头开了枪，再趁我们全都惊得措手不及的当口掩了回去。那晚我但凡再快上一分，就逮着他了！还有昨夜阿瑟遇袭的时候，托伦斯又偏偏独自散步去了。这不是明摆着么！我却像个傻瓜，愣没瞧出来。只消一支铅笔一张纸，把每一回确确实实在场的人开列成单，再一路排除，势必一下就能逮着他。”
汽车沿路朝迷宫驶去的一路，他把自己这份愚钝寻思了一阵；可克林顿爵士一声吩咐，阿德斯利把车刹住，他的思路便被打断了。一名穿制服的警察从一丛灌木的掩蔽后跨上前来，温多弗吃惊地看见，他腰带上别着一支左轮手枪。

===Original===
“Everything all right?” demanded Sir Clinton, as the constable saluted.
“Yes, sir. It went exactly as you told us. About a quarter of an hour ago we saw him hurrying along the road.”
“Just so,” Sir Clinton interrupted. “I watched him leaving the house.”
“He went into the Maze, sir; and as soon as he was well inside, we followed your orders and put the padlocks on all the gates. He’s tried to get out once, sir; but as soon as he saw us he ran back into the Maze.”
“You didn’t try to catch him, of course?”
“No, sir. Your orders were strict about that; and we kept to them.”
===Chinese===
“一切都妥当？”警察举手行礼，克林顿爵士问道。
“是，长官。一切都照您的吩咐分毫不差。约莫一刻钟前，我们看见他匆匆顺着大路过来了。”
“正是。”克林顿爵士插话道，“他离开宅子时，我亲眼盯着。”
“他进了迷宫，长官；等他进到深处，我们便遵照您的命令，给各道门都上了挂锁。他试过一回要出来，长官；可一瞧见我们，就又缩回迷宫里去了。”
“你们当然没动手抓他？”
“没有，长官。您那道命令说得严；我们照办了。”

===Original===
“Quite right. Now you’ve got the stuff up, haven’t you?”
“It’s over there, sir.”
“Well, bring me the megaphone. We’ll need to talk to him before we can do anything further.”
While the constable was fetching the instrument, they got out of the car. Wendover, when he found himself on the road, gazed across at the green barriers of the Maze, behind which the murderer was lurking. Sir Clinton’s tactics were plain enough in their final phase, though Wendover could not understand how the Chief Constable had been so sure of running the miscreant down in the particular way he had chosen. He turned at the sound of steps, to find the constable had come back with the megaphone, a battered instrument which had probably seen service at police sports in the past. Sir Clinton took it from his subordinate and then called the attention of the group about the car.
“I want you people to take careful note of what happens, from now onwards. You may have to give evidence about it, so please pay attention to everything that happens.”
===Chinese===
“很好。东西都运上来了，是吧？”
“在那边，长官。”
“那么，把传话筒给我拿来。不先跟他搭上话，什么进一步的举动都谈不成。”
警察去取那件家伙的当口，他们下了车。待站到路上，温多弗凝望着迷宫那几道绿色的壁垒——凶手就潜藏在后头。克林顿爵士的战术到了收网的阶段，已是彰彰明甚；只是温多弗不明白，郡警察局长凭什么那样有把握，认定那恶徒必定落进他选中的这一路套子。听见脚步声他回过头去，见警察已拿着传话筒回来了——一件饱经风霜的旧物，从前多半在警察运动会上效过力。克林顿爵士从部下手里接过它，随即唤起车旁一干人的注意。
“从现在起，诸位要把发生的事一件件看仔细了。你们将来或许得当堂作证，所以请留意每一桩经过。”

===Original===
Wendover noticed that Sir Clinton’s voice had lost its usual tinge of humour. Quite obviously he regarded the situation as grave; and his tone was that of a man who sees difficulties ahead, but means to overcome them if possible. As soon as he was certain that all the group were on the alert, the Chief Constable raised the megaphone and spoke towards the Maze.
“Ernest Shandon! I have a warrant here for your arrest. I call on you to surrender. Come to the gate nearest the road within five minutes and give yourself up.”
“He’s got a pistol, sir,” the constable hastened to add to his previous report, “and an airgun, too. He had them in his hands as he went into the Maze.”
Sir Clinton raised the megaphone again.
“Before you come to the gate, you must throw your weapons over the hedge. You can’t get away, Shandon, you may as well come out quietly.”
===Chinese===
温多弗注意到，克林顿爵士的声音失了惯常那点诙谐的味道。他把局势看得严峻，这是再明显不过的；他的语调，是一个望得见前头艰难、却打定主意要设法闯过去的人的语调。待确信这一干人个个警醒了，郡警察局长便举起传话筒，朝迷宫说话。
“欧内斯特·香农！我手里有逮捕你的拘捕令。我命你投降。五分钟之内，到离大路最近的那道门来，束手就擒。”
“他带着一支手枪，长官，”警察赶忙在先前的报告上补了一句，“还有一支气枪。他进迷宫时，两样都提在手里。”
克林顿爵士重新举起传话筒。
“到门跟前来之前，须先把凶器从树篱上扔出来。你逃不脱的，香农，还是安安静静出来为好。”

===Original===
His voice echoed across the lawns, but from the recesses of the Maze came no reply.
“Five minutes from now,” Sir Clinton said finally, and put down the megaphone. He glanced at his wristwatch as he did so.
“He won’t come out, of course; but I’m anxious to do everything in a justifiable way,” he explained. “He’s had fair warning.”
They stood uneasily about, furtively consulting their watches until the five-minute period had elapsed; but no sign came from the Maze. Wendover was completely puzzled by the turn of events. How could Ernest Shandon be the murderer? When the attempt had been made on Arthur, Ernest Shandon had been sitting within ten feet of Wendover himself, under the eye of Sir Clinton; and the attack had been carried out here, at the entrance to the Maze. Then, floating through his mind, came a recollection of Sir Clinton’s hint that a man might be “on both halves of the map simultaneously.” But that was impossible! No man could be in two places at once. The whole affair seemed to verge on a nightmare inconsistency. And yet, Sir Clinton had evidently foreseen the attempt to escape and had taken precautions to prevent it being successful. And undoubtedly it must be Ernest Shandon in the Maze, for the local constables must have recognised him from their hiding-places as he went in.
When the five minutes’ grace had elapsed, Sir Clinton turned round; but as he did so, his eye was caught by a new figure which was advancing over the lawns.
===Chinese===
他的声音在草坪上空回荡，迷宫深处却无半点回应。
“五分钟，从现在数起。”克林顿爵士末了说道，放下传话筒，顺势瞥了一眼腕表。
“他自然不会出来；可我事事都要做得站得住脚。”他解释道，“他该得的警告已经给足了。”
众人局促地四下站着，偷偷对表，直到五分钟的期限走完；迷宫里却毫无动静。事态这一转，把温多弗彻底弄糊涂了。欧内斯特·香农怎么会是凶手？袭击阿瑟的那一回，欧内斯特·香农分明坐在离温多弗不出十英尺的地方，正落在克林顿爵士的眼皮底下；而那次袭击，就发生在此地、迷宫的入口。随后，一段记忆浮过他的脑海：克林顿爵士曾暗示，一个人可能“同时落在地图的两半上”。可那不可能！没有人能同时身在两处。整桩事情仿佛濒于噩梦般的自相矛盾。然而，克林顿爵士分明预见了这场出逃，早布下了防它得逞的手段。而且，困在迷宫里的必是欧内斯特·香农无疑，因为他进去的时候，本地警察从各自的藏身处必定认出了他。
五分钟的宽限一到，克林顿爵士转过身来；可就在这当口，一个新的人影闯入他的眼中，正穿过草坪走来。

===Original===
“Oh, damnation!” he exclaimed angrily. “Here’s the very thing I wanted to avoid.”
Wendover, following Sir Clinton’s glance, recognised Arthur Hawkhurst hurrying towards them; and as he approached, the Squire could see that he was carrying a sporting rifle in his hand.
“You gave me the slip,” Arthur said reproachfully, as he came up to them. “But I spotted what you were after. Heard you moving about and dressed in next to no time. I’m a bit out of breath with the hurry.”
Sir Clinton looked at him sternly.
===Chinese===
“哦，该死！”他恼怒地脱口而出，“我正要避免的，偏偏来了。”
温多弗顺着克林顿爵士的目光望去，认出是阿瑟·霍克赫斯特正匆匆赶来；待他走近了，乡绅才看清他手里提着一支运动步枪。
“你们把我甩下了。”阿瑟走到近前，埋怨道，“可我瞧出了你们要干什么。听见你们走动，转眼间就穿戴停当。赶得太急，这会儿还有点喘。”
克林顿爵士严厉地打量着他。

===Original===
“If you come here at all, Mr. Hawkhurst, you come under my orders. If you can’t agree to that, I’ll have to see that you’re sent back to the house.”
Arthur frowned heavily; then, after a moment’s thought, he evidently made up his mind to accept the inevitable.
“Very well, then. If you put it like that, there’s no more to be said. But if the beggar attempts to escape, I suppose I may wing him?”
He touched his rifle as he spoke.
“You’ll do exactly as you’re told.”
===Chinese===
“你既然来了，霍克赫斯特先生，就得听我号令。这条你若应不下，我就只好叫人把你送回宅子里去。”
阿瑟重重皱起眉头；继而略一思量，他显然打定主意，认了这桩逃不掉的事。
“那么，好吧。你既然把话说到这份上，我还有什么可讲的。只是那家伙要是想跑，我总可以赏他一枪，打伤他吧？”
说话间，他碰了碰自己的枪。
“你只消叫你怎么做就怎么做。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton evidently had no wish to be distracted from his main problem. His voice had a ring in it which impressed even Arthur.
“What’s it all about?” he demanded from Wendover, in a lowered tone.
“Your uncle’s the murderer, it seems; and Sir Clinton’s got him trapped in the Maze.”
Arthur looked at him in amazement.
“I say, you know, Wendover, that’ll take a bit of thinking over, won’t it?”
He said no more; and Wendover could believe that Arthur, like himself, was conning over the whole of the Whistlefield case, and being brought up against the apparent impossibilities of the Chief Constable’s solution of the problem. At length Arthur lifted his head again.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士显然不愿让正事分了心。他的声音里有一种音调，连阿瑟也为之一凛。
“这是唱的哪一出？”他压低嗓门向温多弗打听。
“看样子，凶手是你舅舅；克林顿爵士把他困在迷宫里了。”
阿瑟愕然望着他。
“我说，温多弗，这可得费点脑筋好好想想，是不是？”
他再没言语；温多弗可以想见，阿瑟同自己一样，正在心里把惠斯特菲尔德全案从头到尾默诵一遍，一头撞上郡警察局长那套解法表面上的种种不可能。末了，阿瑟重新抬起头来。

===Original===
“Well, if he didn’t do it, he has only to come out and say so. If he didn’t do it. …”
His voice died away into silence. Then he added:
“I promised myself to square up for Sylvia, and I’ll do that, no matter who the man is.”
He dropped his rifle to the order and waited patiently for the next move, keeping his eyes fixed on the impenetrable hedges of the Maze.
===Chinese===
“唉，他要是不曾干，只消出来道一声不曾干。他要是不曾干的……”
他的声音渐渐低下去，归于沉寂。随后他又说：
“我给自己许下过愿，要替西尔维娅讨还这笔账；那人是谁都罢，这个愿我一定还。”
他依着号令把枪口垂了下来，耐着性子等候下一步动静，一双眼睛只顾盯住迷宫那几道莫测高深的树篱。

===Original===
Sir Clinton waited for a few minutes longer. Then he seemed to have exhausted the limit which he had set for himself.
“You see the situation?” he demanded, turning to the others. “He’s in there. He refuses to come out. We’ve got to get him.”
“I suppose you’re going to starve him out?” Wendover inquired, as Sir Clinton stopped short.
“Too risky. He might get away in the dark when night comes on. I don’t say he could; but I’m going to take no risks.”
“Then I suppose we’ll need to go into the Maze and dig him out,” Wendover suggested, philosophically.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士又等了几分钟。随后，他像是熬到了自己划定的期限的尽头。
“形势你们都看清了？”他转向众人，“他在里头。他拒不出来。咱们得把他拿到手。”
“我猜，你是打算把他困饿出来？”克林顿爵士话音一顿，温多弗问道。
“太悬。入夜天黑，他兴许就溜了。我不是说他溜得掉；可我一步险也不打算冒。”
“那么我猜，咱们就得进迷宫，把他掏出来了。”温多弗达观地提议道。

===Original===
Sir Clinton dissented with a nod.
“I’m responsible for the lives of my constables,” he said. “I can take some brands of responsibility quick enough; but I won’t shoulder the liability of sending good men to hunt armed vermin through a maze like that. They know nothing about its paths. He knows every inch of it. It would be sheer murder if I gave orders of that sort. No, there’s a better way. But bear in mind that I gave him the chance of surrender first of all.”
Arthur fidgeted with his rifle.
“I know the Maze as well as he does,” he pointed out. “I could get him out easily enough if you’d let me take on the job.”
Sir Clinton negatived the suggestion curtly.
“I’ve got a better ferret than you, my boy. If he gets driven into the open and looks like escaping, I’ll let you wing him. But that’s all you are to do.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士一点头，否了这主意。
“我手下警察的性命，责任在我身上。”他说，“有几样责任，我担起来倒也不含糊；可打发好人到那样的迷宫里去搜捕带枪的害兽，这个干系我不背。他们一点不认得里面的路径。他却每一寸都烂熟于心。我要是下那样的命令，那简直就等于蓄意谋杀。不，有个更好的法子。不过你记住：头一样，我给过他投降的机会。”
阿瑟把枪摆弄个不停。
“那座迷宫，我同他一样熟。”他指出，“你要肯把这差事交给我，把他弄出来不费什么力气。”
克林顿爵士三言两语，否决了这个提议。
“小伙子，我手里有一只比你高明的雪貂。他若被赶到了明处、眼看要逃，我便准你赏他一枪。可你份内的事，也仅止于此。”

===Original===
He wetted his finger and held it up in the air.
“Couldn’t be better. There’s just a faint drift in the air. You’ve got the stuff over by the boathouse?” he added, turning to the constable. “Right. We’ll go across, then. But keep well away from the Maze as we go; for the beast may be hiding behind the outermost hedge trying to draw a bead on us as we pass.”
Considerably mystified, Wendover followed Sir Clinton towards the river bank. When they reached the neighbourhood of the boathouse, he was still more astonished to find a number of sacks lying on the grass, evidently filled with some material. Three spades were grouped close by. Again Sir Clinton held up his moistened finger and gauged the direction of the light airs that were blowing. Then, seeing the surprised faces of his companions, he pointed to the sacks:
“My ferret!”
Ardsley had gone over and inspected one of the bags. He rubbed his finger on the outside of one and then inspected the skin with interest. Then, suddenly, he laughed grimly:
===Chinese===
他舔湿一根手指，举到空中。
“再好不过。空气里正有一丝极轻的流动。东西都在船屋那边？”他转向警察，补了一句，“好。那咱们就过去。只是路上须离迷宫远远的；那畜生可能正藏在外层树篱后头，等咱们经过时好拿准星套咱们。”
温多弗满腹狐疑，跟着克林顿爵士朝河岸走去。到了船屋左近，他愈发吃惊地发现，草地上躺着好些麻袋，显然装着什么材料。三把铁锹拢在一旁。克林顿爵士又一次举起润湿的手指，估量那阵微风的去向。随后，眼见同伴们一脸惊异，他指向那几只麻袋：
“我的雪貂！”
阿德斯利早走过去查验了其中一袋。他在袋外抹了一指头，随即饶有兴味地端详那根手指。忽然间，他冷冷地笑了：

===Original===
“Sulphur! That’s a cute notion. A ferret!”
Sir Clinton acknowledged the discovery with a smile that had more than a touch of the sinister in it.
“Simple, isn’t it?”
“Quite the Mikado touch,” Ardsley commented.
Sir Clinton said no more, but busied himself with giving orders to his subordinates. Wendover had grasped the meaning of the interchange between Ardsley and the Chief Constable. Sir Clinton meant to use the simplest poison gas of all—the fumes of burning sulphur. The light air-currents would drift them over the Maze; and Ernest Shandon would soon find his fastness converted into a deathtrap. He would have to come out into the open; and by that time he would be in such a state that he could be easily captured.
===Chinese===
“硫磺！好个刁钻的主意。一只雪貂！”
克林顿爵士含笑领受了这项发现，那笑意里的阴狠远不止一星半点。
“简单得很，不是吗？”
“颇有几分日本天皇的手笔。”阿德斯利评道。
克林顿爵士不再多说，只忙着向部下发号施令。阿德斯利与郡警察局长之间这番对答的深意，温多弗已经领会了。克林顿爵士打算动用最简单的一种毒气——燃烧硫磺的烟气。轻微的气流会把烟气吹送过迷宫；欧内斯特·香农很快便会发现，他的这座堡垒已化作一座死亡陷阱。他将不得不出到明处来；而到那时，他已给熏成那副样子，轻易便能拿住了。

===Original===
Sir Clinton had issued his directions, posting his constables at points from which they could converge on the various entrances to the Maze if necessary.
“Take no risks,” he added, finally. “I don’t want any fancy exploits today. The man who gets Shandon and suffers no damage in getting him, will be the man who gets a good mark from me. I’ll not have anyone hurt, understand!”
He dismissed the constables to carry his orders to their comrades, and then swung round to the rest of the group.
“You can get over yonder, to that clump of rhododendrons, Hawkhurst. If he shows up anywhere within your zone of fire, you’re to wing him. You’re not to kill him. I’m trusting you to play the game, remember.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士发下指示，把警察分派在各处要点，必要时便可向迷宫的各个入口合围。
“一步险也不许冒。”末了他又添一句，“今天我不要任何花哨的英雄戏。谁拿下香农而又毫发无伤，谁就在我这儿记上一功。我不许任何人挂彩，明白吗！”
他遣警察们去把命令传达给同僚，随即转过身来，面对其余众人。
“霍克赫斯特，你到那边那丛杜鹃花后面去。他只要在你的火力范围里露头，你就开枪打伤他。不许打死他。我信得过你会光明正大，记住。”

===Original===
Arthur nodded, and betook himself to his post.
“Two more guns will cover all but this side of the Maze,” Sir Clinton went on. “You go over to the road, Wendover. From there,” he pointed, “you can cover young Hawkhurst’s dead ground. I’ll do the same on the other side. But first of all, we’ve got to get this stuff spread around a bit.”
He cut the twine at the mouth of the sacks, tilted out the sulphur, and began to distribute it with a spade. The others hurried to assist him, and spread the yellow lumps under his directions.
“Now we can light up,” Sir Clinton said, at last, when things had been completed to his satisfaction. “These fireworks will give the thing a good start if you spray the sparks to and fro over the surface of the stuff.”
He produced some fireworks from a paper parcel as he spoke and set an example to his companions. Soon the sulphur caught fire; and Wendover, incautiously working on the leeward side, began to cough violently.
===Chinese===
阿瑟点头，自去上岗了。
“再添两支枪，迷宫除这一面之外便都看住了。”克林顿爵士接着说，“温多弗，你到大路上去。从那儿，”他一指，“你可以照应到小霍克赫斯特的射击死角。我到另一面照应。可头一件事，咱们得先把这些东西撒开来。”
他割断袋口的麻绳，倒出硫磺，操起一把铁锹开始摊撒。旁人赶忙上前帮手，依着他的指点把那些黄色的块块摊开。
“现在可以点火了。”末了，待一切布置得称了他的心，克林顿爵士才说，“这些烟花，你把火星往这东西的表面来回一洒，火头起得就顺。”
说话间，他从一只纸包里掏出几支烟花，给同伴们做了个示范。不多时硫磺便着了火；温多弗一时大意，在下风头干着活，登时咳得翻天倒海。

===Original===
“Keep to the windward side of the stuff,” Sir Clinton advised. “Get it alight here and there. The flame will soon spread over the whole surface. Now, I’m off.”
He picked up his shotgun and skirted the Maze at a respectful distance as he made his way to his post. For a minute or two Wendover stolidly continued to tend the “ferret”; but he could not help wondering whether this method of criminal-hunting really came within permitted bounds. His mind inclined more readily to active measures; and the devil’s cookery was, as he phrased it to Ardsley, “hardly the game.”
The toxicologist showed no sympathy with his point of view.
“Driffield’s quite right. Suppose tonight found him with a dead constable on his hands—perhaps a widow and some fatherless children to face? Would you care to be in his shoes? I wouldn’t! Play the game? Bosh! It’s not playing the game to chuck away your men’s lives unnecessarily.”
He dug up some burning sulphur and distributed it over an unkindled patch.
===Chinese===
“守在这东西的上风头。”克林顿爵士指点道，“这儿那儿多点几处。火苗很快就会蔓过整个表面。好了，我走了。”
他提起猎枪，远远绕开迷宫边缘，往自己的岗位去了。有一两分钟，温多弗闷头照管着这只“雪貂”；可他不禁犯嘀咕：这种缉捕罪犯的法子，当真还在许可的界限之内么。他的心思更偏向干脆的手段；至于这魔鬼的烹调，用他对阿德斯利的话说，“未免算不上光明正大”。
毒理学家对他的见解毫无同情。
“德里菲尔德说得一点儿不错。设想到了今夜，他手上摊着一名死去的警察——说不定还要面对一个寡妇和几个没了爹的孩子呢？你愿意站到他的位置上去吗？我可不干！光明正大？废话！白白糟蹋部下的性命，那才叫不光明正大。”
他铲起几块烧着的硫磺，摊到一片还没点着的去处。

===Original===
“Whew! This is a suffocating job! If it’s like this now it’ll be fairly stifling on the lee side in half an hour.”
Stenness said nothing. Wendover, who was not in the secret of the secretary’s love affairs, could not understand why Stenness looked almost as grim as Sir Clinton. The Squire woke up all at once to the fact that he ought to be elsewhere; and he made his way to his appointed post.
As he reached it, the report of the sporting rifle came from the clump of rhododendrons.
“It’s all right,” he heard Arthur’s voice raised in a shouted explanation. “I saw him at the gate, trying to climb over; so I let him have it above his head. That scared him. He’s gone away now, back into the Maze. He’s got a pistol in his hand.”
===Chinese===
“嚯！这活儿真呛人！现在尚且如此，半个钟头后背风那面准闷死人。”
斯滕内斯一言不发。温多弗并不知悉秘书那桩情事的底细，弄不明白斯滕内斯的神色为何严酷得几乎不亚于克林顿爵士。乡绅猛然醒悟，自己本该在别处；于是他便往派定的岗位去了。
他刚到岗位，杜鹃花丛那边便传来一声运动步枪的枪响。
“没事儿！”他听见阿瑟扬声喊话解释，“我看见他在门口，想翻过来；我就照他头顶上方给了一枪。把他吓回去了。他这会儿退走了，又回迷宫里去了。他手里攥着一支手枪。”

===Original===
Almost at once they heard Sir Clinton’s shotgun fired.
“Trying the entrances one by one to see if he can slip out,” commented Arthur, with a certain malicious glee in his tone.
Some minutes passed, then, to Wendover’s astonishment, he heard the crack of the automatic pistol, and a bullet sang past him.
“Not much sign of surrender about that,” he admitted, as he got hurriedly under cover. “The beggar must have marked me down and tried a long shot through the hedge.”
===Chinese===
几乎紧跟着，他们听见克林顿爵士那边的猎枪响了一声。
“他在挨个试探那些入口，看能不能溜出去呢。”阿瑟评道，话音里带着几分不怀好意的快意。
又过了几分钟，温多弗愕然听见自动手枪一声脆响，一颗子弹嗖地从他身旁掠过。
“这可瞧不出多少投降的意思。”他一面赶忙寻掩护，一面承认道，“那家伙准是把我盯上了，隔着树篱试了一记远射。”

===Original===
Time flowed slowly on. Occasionally, with a slight change in the faint wind, some of the sulphur fumes drifted down to Wendover and caught his lungs.
“It must be pretty thick in the Maze, if a mere whiff of it gives one gyp like that,” he reflected. “The beggar must be half-dead with the fumes by this time, for he gets the full blast of it.”
Another interval of quietness gave Wendover time to think over the situation. Up to that point, he had gone through the business almost without trying to bring the morning’s work into touch with normal life. The whole affair had had an impersonal quality; for from start to finish he had not set eyes on the man they were hunting. But that peculiarity had enhanced the rather unreal character of the adventure, lending it a touch of the fantastic in his mind. And the extraordinarily methodical procedure which had governed the transactions helped in some way to accentuate his feelings. It had all been as logical as a nightmare seems to be while one lives through it.
Yet another shotgun report—from the sulphur station this time—showed that the murderer was still making attempts to slip away. After that, another period elapsed without any further alarms. The sulphur fumes were now so dense that Wendover, though out of the main line of drift, was becoming seriously troubled by them. He could hear Arthur coughing continuously in the rhododendron clump; but the muzzle of the rifle was never lowered even in the worst paroxysm. Quite obviously Arthur meant to miss no chance of squaring the account for Sylvia.
===Chinese===
时间缓缓地挨过去。微风偶尔稍稍一转向，便有几缕硫磺烟气飘到温多弗这里，呛他的肺。
“外头沾这么一星半点儿，就呛得人这样受罪，迷宫里头想必浓得可以了。”他暗自寻思，“那家伙这会儿准叫烟气熏得半死了——他受的可是全份儿的。”
又一段寂静给了温多弗回味局势的工夫。直到那时为止，他经历这桩事情，几乎不曾试着把今晨的作为同寻常日子接上茬。整件事自带着一种非个人的性质；因为从头到尾，他都不曾亲眼见过他们围猎的那个人。可这一层特别，反倒加深了这场冒险恍如隔世的味道，在心里给它添了一笔奇幻的色彩。而支配着这一连串行动的那套井然有序到出奇的程式，又在某种程度上把他的感受推得更深了。这一切，就像一个人身在噩梦之中时，觉得噩梦句句都在理一样。
又一声猎枪响——这回是硫磺火那边打的——表明凶手仍在设法脱身。此后又过了一阵，再没有新的动静。硫磺烟气这时已浓到这般地步：温多弗虽在烟尘漂流的主道之外，也渐渐被呛得苦不堪言。他听得见阿瑟在杜鹃花丛里咳个不住；可纵是咳到最凶的当口，那枪口也从不曾放低。不言而喻，阿瑟决意不错过任何一个替西尔维娅清算这笔账的机会。

===Original===
As that thought crossed his mind, Wendover seemed to see the whole Whistlefield case in a fresh light. Instead of the mysterious murderer lurking behind green walls of the Maze, his mental vision threw up a picture of the real personality, Ernest Shandon. He could see him with his mind’s eye wandering along the paths of the Maze, choking, desperate, seeking for some outlet to safety and beaten back each time by the warning of the guns. And minute by minute the poison-cloud was growing denser over his place of refuge, bringing nearer the inevitable end of the drama.
And as this picture forced itself upon Wendover, he began to feel the nightmare growing more intense. It seemed almost incredible that Ernest Shandon, a creature despised by everyone for his shiftlessness and futility, could have planned and carried through this murderous work. Wendover, since he had been brought into intimate contact with Ernest, had felt nothing but boredom and derision. The man’s dullness, his cowardice, his selfishness, had all impressed themselves strongly on the Squire; and had produced a definite feeling of repulsion and contempt. Now he had to readjust his ideas. The dullness must have been merely an exaggeration; the cowardice had been a sham, since the murderer had no reason to fear anything for his own skin; and the selfishness—why, that was only a manifestation of callousness without which no planned murder could be carried through. Instead of the insignificant figure which he had hitherto encountered, Wendover began to see instead a fresh personality hidden behind the mask: something going coldly to its deadly work, unrestrained by any normal feelings of humanity or even kinship, a modern Minotaur in the labyrinth of the Maze.
Almost appalled by the vividness of the portrait which his mind had conjured up, Wendover stared across the grass at the wall of greenery which concealed from his gaze the actual form of the murderer. Then, as he gazed, there came once more the report of the automatic pistol—a single shot.
===Chinese===
这个念头掠过心头时，温多弗仿佛换了一副新的眼光，去看整个惠斯特菲尔德案。他心中的眼帘里浮起的，不再是潜藏在迷宫绿墙后面的那个神秘凶手，而是那个真实的人：欧内斯特·香农。他仿佛看见此人在迷宫的小径间踉跄徘徊，呛着，绝望着，寻一条逃向活路的出口，却一回回被警告的枪声逼退。而那片毒云，正在他的藏身之上一分一寸地变浓，把这出戏不可避免的落幕，一步一步拉到眼前。
这幅图景强加到温多弗眼前，他便觉出那噩梦愈发逼真了。欧内斯特·香农——一个因散漫无志、百无一用而人人轻贱的人物——竟能谋划出这桩凶案并贯彻到底，这几乎叫人难以置信。温多弗自打同欧内斯特有了切近的接触，对他便只余厌烦与讥诮。此人的迟钝、他的怯懦、他的自私，都在乡绅心里打下了深深的印子，生出一种确凿无疑的憎嫌与鄙夷。如今他不得不重新校正自己的成见了。那份迟钝，想必只是刻意夸大出来的一层表象；那怯懦原是装出来的，因为这凶手犯不着为自己这条命担半点怕；至于那自私——嘿，那不过是铁石心肠的一种显形，而没有铁石心肠，任何预谋的凶案都无从办成。温多弗眼中浮现的，不再是先前打交道的那个无足轻重的人物，而是面具背后另藏的一个崭新人格：一样冷冰冰走向致命勾当的东西，全然不受寻常人性乃至骨肉之情的拘束——这迷宫之中的一头现代弥诺陶洛斯。
他心中召来的这幅画像鲜活至此，温多弗几乎为之悚然；他隔着草坪，死死盯住那道绿墙——凶手的真身就遮在他的视线之外。正望着，自动手枪又一次响起——单单一声。

===Original===
And once more the waiting recommenced, unbroken by any incident.
At last Sir Clinton appeared, with his gun at the trail, round the corner of the Maze. He signalled to Wendover and Arthur to rejoin him.
“I think that’s the end of the business,” he said with stern satisfaction, as they came up. “If I’m not much mistaken, that last shot was for himself. The game was up; and he must have been half-dead with the fumes by now.”
He turned to Arthur.
“Do you see now why I wouldn’t let you touch him? If you had, then we’d have had all the bother of a trial for manslaughter at the least; and I don’t guarantee that things would have gone smoothly in it. As it is, he’s suicided; and no one’s to blame but himself. And if you wanted to put the screw on him, could you have given it a harder turn than this?”
He pointed towards the sulphur station.
===Chinese===
等候重新开始，再没有什么动静来打破它。
末了，克林顿爵士斜提着猎枪，从迷宫的拐角处现身。他打手势唤温多弗和阿瑟回到他身边去。
“我想，这桩事到此收场了。”待他们走近，他带着严峻的满足说道，“倘若我没大错，最后那一枪是给他自己的。戏已散场；再说，到这会儿，他也准叫烟气熏得半死了。”
他转向阿瑟。
“现在你可明白，我为什么不许你碰他了？你若碰了，咱们少说也得吃一场过失杀人罪的官司；而且我不担保那官司能顺顺当当。如今这样，他是自尽的；怪不得旁人，只怪他自己。再说，你原就想给他上上螺丝的——你还能拧得比这一遭更狠么？”
他朝硫磺火那边一指。

===Original===
Arthur saw the point.
“I expect you’re right,” he admitted, coughing as a fresh cloud of fumes drifted down upon them. “He must have had his dose before he gave in. You think that there’s no doubt that he’s shot himself?”
Sir Clinton’s face showed what he thought.
“Well, I tell you what,” Arthur proposed. “There’s a tree near the riverbank which overlooks part of the Maze if one gets high enough in it. Suppose I swarm up it and see if I can spot anything? Now that he’s out of action, it’s safe enough.”
Sir Clinton welcomed the idea.
===Chinese===
阿瑟听懂了这话里的分量。
“我想你是对的。”他承认道，一股新的烟云飘落下来，呛得他咳嗽，“他认输之前，准已吃足了他那份儿。你认为他开枪自尽，这是没有疑问的了？”
克林顿爵士的脸色道出了他的想法。
“这么着吧，我有个主意。”阿瑟提议道，“河岸边上有棵树，爬得够高，便能俯瞰迷宫的一角。我去爬上去瞧瞧，看能不能望见什么，怎么样？他既已不中用，那尽够安全。”
克林顿爵士对这个主意表示欢迎。

===Original===
“That’s a good scheme. He might be shamming, after all; and I won’t take any risks by sending anyone into the Maze till I’m sure. If you keep well inside the leaves you’ll be safe enough in any case. And if you find he’s dead, it’ll save us a long wait.”
Arthur dropped his rifle and went off to put his project into execution. After he had gone Sir Clinton turned to Wendover.
“Not much family affection left in the Whistlefield circle, Squire. But can you wonder, after all. Friend Ernest wasn’t the sort of man who’d attract much liking at the best; and that boy was very keen on his sister, undoubtedly. I have a certain sympathy with his feelings. And I can tell you I’ve been on pins and needles for some time back lest young Hawkhurst should find out who the murderer really was and take the law into his own hands at once. He wouldn’t have been hanged, of course; that mental instability of his would have saved him. But it would have been a case for Broadmoor; and that’s not much better than the drop in his state. It’s a relief to get it all over as easily as we have done.”
In a few minutes, Arthur came back with his report.
===Chinese===
“这是个好办法。他到底也可能是装死；拿不准之前，我谁也不往迷宫里派，一步险不冒。你只要好好藏在枝叶里，横竖尽够安全。你要是发现他死了，也省得咱们干等。”
阿瑟放下枪，去把他这计划付诸实施。他走后，克林顿爵士转向温多弗。
“惠斯特菲尔德这一圈子里，骨肉之情所剩无几了，乡绅。可细想想，这又何足怪。欧内斯特朋友那个人，往好里说，也不是个招人喜欢的料；而那孩子疼他妹妹，这是无疑的。他的心情，我有几分同情。我还可以告诉你，先前好一阵子，我一直如坐针毡，就怕小霍克赫斯特查出凶手究竟是谁，当下动起私刑来。他当然不至于被判绞刑；他那精神上的失稳自会救下他。可那就得送去布罗德莫尔（Broadmoor）了；以他那状态，这也不比上绞架好多少。这桩事能像咱们这样轻易了结，真是一桩宽慰。”
不出几分钟，阿瑟回来复命。

===Original===
“He’s lying on the ground in Helen’s Bower. I couldn’t see very clearly; but from the way he’s lying, I think he’s done for.”
“Well,” said Sir Clinton, “I’d better get into the Maze and make sure of things.”
He reflected for a moment, then added:
“I think perhaps it would be as well if all of us went in together. It’s quite safe; I’m pretty sure of that. And there’s something there, I suspect, that I shall want witnesses for.”
He summoned Ardsley and Stenness and explained the state of affairs to them.
===Chinese===
“他躺在海伦亭的地上。我望不太真切；可照他躺的那副样子，我看他是没救了。”
“那么，”克林顿爵士说，“我最好进迷宫去，把事情坐实。”
他沉吟片刻，又补充道：
“我想，或许咱们大伙儿一齐进去更妥当。相当安全；这一点我十拿九稳。而且我疑心，那里头有一样东西，我要用到见证人。”
他唤来阿德斯利和斯滕内斯，向两人说明了情由。

===Original===
“If we dowse the sulphur, the wind will clear away the fumes fairly soon. We can get water from the river and put it out. There’s sure to be something in the boathouse which we can use for bringing water—a bucket or a bailer of some sort!”
When it seemed safe, they entered the Maze and soon reached Helen’s Bower. No danger awaited them. Ernest’s body lay sprawling on the grass with a bullet wound in the head and the automatic pistol still clutched in the hand. Sir Clinton crossed over and knelt down beside the corpse.
“We’d better search the body now and be done with it,” he said, beginning the task as he spoke.
===Chinese===
“咱们把硫磺火浇熄，风很快就会把烟气吹散。咱们可以从河里取水，把火扑灭。船屋里准有能提水的家什——桶啊，瓢啊，总有一样！”
待觉得安全了，他们便进了迷宫，不多时就到了海伦亭。没有什么危险等着他们。欧内斯特的尸身四仰八叉摊在草地上，头部一个弹孔，那只自动手枪还攥在手里。克林顿爵士走过去，在尸体旁边跪了下来。
“咱们现在就把尸身搜上一搜，一次了结。”他一面说，一面动手办这件事。

===Original===
“Here’s something bulky in the breast-pocket,” he explained as he extracted it. “H’m! An envelope with your name written on it, Stenness. Your property, perhaps?”
Stenness glanced at the paper and a look of intelligence passed between him and Sir Clinton.
“That’s mine,” he confirmed. “It ought to have notes in it!”
The Chief Constable thrust his fingers into the envelope and, pulling out some of the contents, exhibited them to the company.
“It’s full of notes—must be a fair sum. I’ll keep this for the present, Stenness. You don’t mind? It’s best to do things formally; and you can always have it later on when you want it.”
===Chinese===
“胸前口袋里有件鼓鼓囊囊的东西。”他一边往外掏，一边解释，“嗯！一只信封，上面写着你的名字，斯滕内斯。或许是你的东西？”
斯滕内斯瞥了一眼那张纸，他同克林顿爵士之间交换了一个心照不宣的眼色。
“那是我的。”他证实道，“里头应该装着钞票！”
郡警察局长把手指伸进信封，抽出一部分内容物，展示给众人看。
“满满一信封钞票——数目准是不小的一笔。这只信封我先收着，斯滕内斯。你不介意吧？事情照章程办最稳妥；往后你要用，随时可以取回。”

===Original===
“Oh, keep it, certainly,” Stenness agreed, with a certain dull indifference.
Sir Clinton continued his search; but the only thing of interest was a tin box which he opened carefully and held out for inspection.
“More of these darts, you see?”
He counted them rapidly.
“Yes, that’s right. This represents the balance of the lot after deducting the ones he used in the attack on Miss Hawkhurst and last night.”
===Chinese===
“哦，收着吧，当然。”斯滕内斯应道，带着一种发木的淡漠。
克林顿爵士继续搜检；唯一值得一看的东西是一只铁盒，他小心打开，递出去给众人过目。
“瞧见没有，又是些飞镖？”
他飞快地数了一遍。
“不错，对得上。这一盒，扣掉他袭击霍克赫斯特小姐那次和昨夜用掉的，正是全数的余额。”

===Original===
He replaced the cover on the box and transferred the thing to his pocket.
“That’s all we need do. You’d better have a look at the body, Ardsley. We’ll need your evidence at the inquest. I’ll send a constable in here to look after things until we can get the body removed.”
When Ardsley had finished his examination, they left the Maze.
“Go back to the house now,” Sir Clinton directed, as they emerged from the entrance gate. “I’ll have to pilot my men in to the centre, you know. You can send the car down to meet me if you like.”
He turned back with the constables, while the rest of the party got into the motor. No one seemed inclined to talk, as they made their way up to the house. The events of the morning had been too bizarre to merge easily into everyday affairs. To Wendover, it seemed as though he had spent the last hour or two in some world lying far beyond the normal bounds of probability—already the siege of the Maze was passing into the realm of the unreal in his mind; and he had difficulty in forcing himself to remember that it had cost the life of a man.
===Chinese===
他把盒盖盖回，将铁盒收进了口袋。
“咱们要做的就这些。你最好验一验尸身，阿德斯利。死因研讯上用得着你的证词。我会派一名警察进来照看现场，等咱们能把尸体运走。”
阿德斯利验毕，他们便离开了迷宫。
“现在回宅子去吧。”他们走出入口大门时，克林顿爵士吩咐道，“你知道，我还得领我的人进到中心去。愿意的话，可以叫车子下来接我。”
他同警察们折返回去，其余的人则上了汽车。一路驶回宅子，谁也不想开口。今晨的种种过于离奇，一时难以融进日常的世事。在温多弗看来，他仿佛把方才一两个钟头，消磨在一个远出常理之外的什么世界里——迷宫的这场围困，在他心里已在往虚幻之境滑去；他得费一番力气，才能强迫自己记起：它是拿一条人命作抵的。

===Original===
But even while he reflected on this, a fresh surprise came upon him. As the car swept round in sight of the main entrance to Whistlefield, he heard a sharp intake of breath from Stenness and a suppressed exclamation from Arthur.
He raised his eyes, and for a moment could hardly believe what he saw. There, at the top of the steps, stood Torrance and Vera Forrest, while between them was Sylvia, safe and sound except for the sling which supported her right arm.
“Rather a surprise, I suppose,” said Ardsley dryly, as he pulled up the car at the front door.
===Chinese===
可他正这般想着，一桩新的意外又临到他头上。汽车一拐，惠斯特菲尔德的正门映入眼帘，他听见斯滕内斯猛地倒抽一口气，还有阿瑟一声压住的惊呼。
他抬起眼来，有一瞬间几乎不敢相信所见。台阶顶上立着托伦斯和维拉·福雷斯特，两人之间是西尔维娅——她安然无恙，只是右臂还兜在吊带里。
“想必是个不小的意外吧。”阿德斯利把车停在大门前，干巴巴地说。

## XVIII The Truth of the Matter / 十八 真相大白

===Original===

Wendover brought out cigars and cigarettes, and endeavoured to make his guests as comfortable as possible without too much fuss. It was some days after the affair at the Maze which had ended in Ernest Shandon’s suicide; and Sir Clinton had asked his host to invite Stenness and Ardsley to dinner. The Squire had at first objected to the toxicologist, for his prejudices were quite unabated; but Sir Clinton had made it clear that Ardsley’s presence was essential from his point of view, and Wendover had given way without too much argument. Dinner had passed off without any friction, for the Squire was not the sort of person to abuse his position as a host.
Sir Clinton picked out a cigar and cut it with some care.
“I hear we may congratulate you on your engagement, Stenness,” he said. “I suppose it’s rather early to ask when you’re going to be married?”

===Chinese===

温多弗取出雪茄和香烟，尽力让客人们各得其适，又不显得过分张罗。迷宫那桩以欧内斯特·香农自尽收场的事件，此时已过去几天；克林顿爵士请主人邀了斯滕内斯和阿德斯利来吃饭。乡绅起初对毒理学家表示反对，因为他的成见丝毫未减；但克林顿爵士言明，在他看来，阿德斯利的到场必不可少，温多弗也没多费口舌，便依了。一顿饭吃得毫无龃龉——乡绅毕竟不是那种仗着东道身份失了体统的人。
克林顿爵士拣了一支雪茄，细细剪了切口。
“听说我们要恭喜你订了婚，斯滕内斯。”他说，“如今就问你们打算几时成婚，怕是早了点儿吧？”

===Original===

Stenness looked across the room, rather as though he were in doubt about the intention behind the question.
“I’ll have to make some money first,” he answered. “One can’t live on somebody else’s income.”
Sir Clinton laughed.
“Some people don’t seem to find it so hard as all that. But if that’s your difficulty, perhaps I could do something for you. I’ve some small influence in South Africa, and it so happens that a man out there asked me to look round for someone to fill a post. The pay’s good enough to marry on. I’ll come across tomorrow to Whistlefield in the morning and talk to you about it. My impression is that it would suit you. And it has one big advantage. It would take you both out of your old surroundings and make a clean break with all this affair, which would hang round your neck if you stayed in this country. People will talk, no matter what happens; and Miss Hawkhurst couldn’t help knowing they were talking if she stayed on here.”

===Chinese===

斯滕内斯的目光越过房间望过来，神情像是拿不准这句问话背后有何用意。
“我得先挣出些钱来。”他答道，“总不能靠别人的进项过日子。”
克林顿爵士笑了。
“有些人倒好像不觉得这事有那么难。不过你若为这个犯难，我兴许能帮上点忙。我在南非有几分薄面，恰巧那边有位先生托我物色一个人补一个位子。那份薪水，成了家也尽够用。明天上午我到惠斯特菲尔德去一趟，同你细谈。我印象中那位于你相宜。它还有一大好处：能把你们两个都带出旧环境，同这桩事一刀两断——你们若留在这个国家，它是要一辈子缠在你们脖子上的。无论事情如何了结，人们的舌头总要动的；霍克赫斯特小姐若继续留在此地，人家背地里的议论，她想不知道也难。”

===Original===

He paused to light his cigar before he continued.
“And that brings me to my reason for getting you people together tonight. None of you are likely to talk; but you’d be hardly human if you didn’t think about this Whistlefield affair. And it’s on the cards that you might fall into misapprehensions over it, which might lead to difficulties. I’ve come to the conclusion that it will be better if you know all that I know myself about it; and I think that will clear the whole thing up and let you get it out of your minds. Once a puzzle’s solved, no one troubles about it any more; but even a trace of mystery will keep one worrying spasmodically and so one can’t put the matter aside for good.”
He glanced round the group and he could see that his suggestion met with the approval of them all.
“Very well. You must bear in mind, first of all, that I’m going to give you a mixture of facts and theories. I can’t guarantee that every detail will be absolutely accurate, for some of it’s guesswork on my part.”
“Go ahead,” said Wendover. “We understand that well enough.”

===Chinese===

他停下来点上雪茄，才接着往下说。
“这就说到我今晚把你们几位聚拢来的缘由了。你们谁都不至于往外说；可你们若不惦记这桩惠斯特菲尔德的事，也就算不得血肉之躯了。何况你们保不齐会在这上头生出误会，误会又可能生出枝节。我得出的结论是：我所知道的底细，最好让你们也全知道；我想这样能把整桩事彻底了断，让你们把它从心里放下去。谜一解开，便再没人把它挂在心上；可哪怕只余一丝疑云，也会教人隔三差五地悬一回心，这桩事便永远撂不下了。”
他环视一圈众人，看得出这个提议得了他们一致的赞成。
“很好。首先你们要记住：我接下来讲的，是事实与推想搀在一起的。我没法担保每个细节都分毫不差，因为有些不过是我个人的猜测。”
“只管讲。”温多弗说，“这一点我们心里有数得很。”

===Original===

“One thing that most people forget when they read about a police case in the papers,” continued Sir Clinton, “is the handicap of local knowledge. On the face of it, any one of you three had a better chance than I had of getting to the bottom of the Whistlefield affair. All of you had some knowledge of the characters of the people involved; each of you at any rate knew his own part in the business: but a detective coming in from the outside sees nothing before him but a group of strangers with totally unpredictable qualities. He has all that leeway to make up before he even starts level with you—and he hasn’t much time to pick up his information.”
“That’s true,” said Stenness, thoughtfully. “I’d never looked at it in that light. The police have a harder job than I thought.”
“There’s a countervailing advantage, of course,” Sir Clinton hastened to admit. “A detective comes to a case with no preconceived ideas about character. The actors are all strangers to him; and he has to depend on his wits and his judgment entirely. That was my position when I came into the Whistlefield case. I knew none of you personally, and I was quite free from prejudices about you.”
“Facts are more important than opinions in a case of this sort, so that really leaves a balance in your favour,” Ardsley suggested.

===Chinese===

“人们从报上读到一桩警方案子时，最容易忘掉的一件事，”克林顿爵士接着说，“就是侦探在本地人情上吃的亏。表面看来，你们三位中随便哪一位，把惠斯特菲尔德这桩事查个水落石出的机会都比我大。你们对涉案各人的脾性都有几分了解；至少各人都清楚自己在事情里扮的是什么角色。可一个从外面进来的侦探，眼前只见一群脾性全然无从捉摸的陌生人。他要先补齐这一大截，才谈得上同你们并肩起跑——而他能用来摸情况的时间又少得可怜。”
“这话不假。”斯滕内斯若有所思地说，“我从没从这个角度看过。警方的差事，比我想的要难。”
“当然，也有一桩相抵的好处。”克林顿爵士忙承认，“侦探接手案子，对人的品性不带半点先入之见。这出戏里的角色，在他全是陌生人；他只能全凭自己的头脑和判断。我接手惠斯特菲尔德这桩案子时，正是这个处境。你们哪一位我都不认识，对你们也毫无成见。”
“在这样一桩案子里，事实比见解要紧，这么一算，天平到底还是偏向你这边。”阿德斯利说。

===Original===

“Quite true—once you’ve got your facts,” Sir Clinton agreed. “Now let’s take the facts in the order that they presented themselves. The things I saw when I was called to the Maze were plain enough. An airgun had been used to fire poisoned darts. Roger Shandon had been hit in the shoulders and the neck; Neville Shandon had been struck rather lower down in the body. There had been no attempt at robbery, except for the tearing away of the sheet of notes in Neville’s hand. Loopholes had been cut in the hedge, evidently beforehand—for no one would start cutting loopholes with his victim a few yards away. A box of darts had been spilt at the loophole from which Roger Shandon was shot. The murderer had managed to dodge either Miss Forrest or young Torrance, or both of them, as he made his way out of the Maze. Finally, my dog proved that the murderer had followed a very roundabout track in leaving the Maze. He got out near the river bank. He went across to a clump of trees, on one of which there was a mark about three feet off the ground. Then he had gone across the grass to the road. And when he reached the road, his trail stopped short.”
“Yes,” Wendover interjected, “you talked a lot of rot about the murderer getting into his private aeroplane and flying away, I remember.”
Sir Clinton smiled slightly.
“I was near enough for all practical purposes, as you’ll see, Squire. Now we come to what I thought it reasonable to infer from the facts. First of all, an airgun can be fired by either a man or a woman, so the weapon didn’t even suggest the sex of the criminal. The poison was obviously going to be a good clue; for it made the crime abnormal, so to speak; and the more uncommon the method is in a murder, the more you limit the possibilities in the identity of the murderer. Next, it was clear enough that Roger had been shot while he was sitting in his chair with his back to the murderer. In that position, only his shoulders and neck would be exposed as targets and it was there that he was hit. Neville, on the other hand, had been shot from the front or slightly to the side. That suggested the possibility that Roger might have been killed in mistake for Neville, whereas Neville could not have been mistaken for Roger, since the murderer, shooting from the front or side, could see his face as he fired.”

===Chinese===

“一点不错——前提是先把事实拿到手。”克林顿爵士表示同意，“那咱们就按事实出现的先后顺序来捋。我应召赶到迷宫时见到的东西，够清楚的了。凶手用一支气枪发射了淬毒的飞镖。罗杰·香农中的是肩头和颈子；内维尔·香农中镖的部位要靠下一些。除了从内维尔手里撕走那页笔记，不见图财的迹象。树篱上修剪出了暗孔，显然是事先所为——谁也不会等到被害人只隔几步远时才动手剪暗孔。射罗杰·香农的那个暗孔边，翻洒了一只飞镖盒。凶手离开迷宫时，还避过了福雷斯特小姐或小托伦斯中的一个，也可能把他们两位都避过了。最后，我的狗证明：凶手离开迷宫，走的是一条极尽迂回的路线。他从近河岸处出去，走到一小片树丛，其中一棵树上留着离地约三英尺的一道印记。随后他穿过草地上了大路。而一上大路，他的踪迹便戛然而止。”
“是啊，”温多弗插嘴道，“我还记得，你当时胡诌了半天凶手登上私人飞机飞走的话。”
克林顿爵士微微一笑。
“乡绅，往后你就明白了，就实际效果而言，我当时说得已八九不离十。现在说说从这些事实里我认为可以合理推出的东西。首先，气枪男人放得，女人也放得，这件凶器连凶手的性别都暗示不出来。那毒物显然会是一条好线索；可以说，它使这桩罪显得反常；而杀人手法越罕见，凶手身份的可能范围便削得越窄。其次，相当清楚，罗杰是背对凶手坐在椅子上中的镖。在那个姿势下，能充作靶子的只有他的肩头和颈子，而他恰是中在那两处。内维尔则相反，是正面或略偏侧面中的镖。这便提示了一种可能：罗杰也许是错被当成了内维尔；而内维尔却不可能被误认作罗杰，因为凶手从正面或侧面开枪，扣扳机时看得清他的脸。”

===Original===

“Did you lay much stress on that in your mind?” Stenness asked.
“Not much at the time. It suggested that Roger might have been killed first of all, by mistake, and that Neville was the man the murderer was really after. But alone, it amounted to very little. Then comes the fact that nothing seemed to have been removed from either body, except the notes torn out of Neville’s hand. Of course, I’d been following the Hackleton case; and it was clear enough that Neville Shandon might have been put out of the way to keep him from examining Hackleton. That’s been done before—remember how Maître Labori was shot in the back as he was going into Court to examine General Mercier during the Dreyfus case. I merely docketed that in my memory and kept an open mind on the point. I hadn’t enough data to make it worth while doing more.
“The next point was the discovery of the loopholes. That established premeditation—the crime had been thought out and prepared for beforehand. And that meant, further, that the murderer was someone who knew that one or other of the Shandons was likely to be in the Maze that afternoon or at least at some time thereabouts. That looked like a local criminal at first sight. But one has to be judicial; and it was clear enough that a premeditated crime might have been preceded by a good deal of quiet spying; and thus an outsider might have got to know the Shandons’ habits. One couldn’t lay much stress on that.”

===Chinese===

“这一点，你当时很放在心上么？”斯滕内斯问。
“当时谈不上。它提示：罗杰可能是头一个被错杀的，凶手真正要的是内维尔的命。可单凭这一点，分量轻得很。再往下，便是两具尸体上似乎什么都没被拿走，只有内维尔手里那页笔记被撕去了。当然，哈克尔顿案我一直留意着；内维尔·香农有可能是被人除去，好拦住他去盘诘哈克尔顿，这一点够清楚的。这种事早有先例——记得德雷福斯案里，拉博里律师（Maître Labori）正要进法庭盘问梅西埃将军（Mercier），背上就中了一枪。这一条我只往记忆里立了个档，对这一点存而不论。手头的材料不足，不值得再往下做文章。”
“再一个要点，是暗孔的发现。这坐实了预谋——这桩罪是事先想定、备好手脚的。这进一步意味着：凶手是个知道某一位香农那天下午多半会在迷宫里、至少前后某时会在迷宫里的人。乍一看，这像是本地作案。可看事得持平；预谋之罪，事先完全可能经过一番不动声色的窥探，外人也可能由此摸清香农家的习惯。这一条，同样做不得多大的文章。”

===Original===

“So at that point you didn’t know whether the Hackleton case came in or not?” Stenness asked.
“No. I simply kept an open mind on the point. Now the next thing was the box of darts which Skene found scattered about. That was easy enough to read. The murderer must have fumbled while he was shooting Roger—because the box was at Roger’s loophole. He was in a deadly hurry, or he’d have picked them up then. Evidently he’d something else to do in a hurry and he meant to come back for the darts. Isn’t it clear enough that when he’d shot Roger, he saw the face after he’d fired; and he realised he’d hit the wrong man. Neville had still to be reckoned with—and it looks to me as if the murderer had counted on Neville being in the Maze just then. I expect he had private information. So he grabbed three darts from the ground and rushed off to finish Neville, which he did. Neville may have been alarmed by something, which would account for his standing up when he was shot at. Then the murderer proposes to go back for his lost darts. But now he finds someone else in the Maze. He hears voices. Probably he finds his road back to Helen’s Bower blocked by these strangers. So he runs as hard as he can to get rid of his airgun, which is the deadly evidence against him. But he gets into difficulties in avoiding these unknown people in the Maze and it takes him some time to get out.”
“Did you suspect anyone in particular at that point?” Wendover interrupted.
“It might have been young Torrance, of course,” Sir Clinton admitted. “He might have doubled his own part with that of the murderer. I kept an open mind.”
“I suppose it might have been Miss Forrest, if you take everyone into account,” Ardsley commented.

===Chinese===

“这么说，到那一步，你还不知道哈克尔顿案搭不搭得上边？”斯滕内斯问。
“不知道。这一点我只是存而不论。再往后，便是斯基恩发现散落一地的那盒飞镖。这一层不难解读。凶手射罗杰时准是失了手——因为盒子就翻在罗杰那个暗孔跟前。他当时急得要命，否则当场就会把飞镖拾走。分明他另有急事要办，打算回头再来取。他射倒罗杰之后、开过枪才看清那张脸，才明白自己打错了人——这还不够清楚么？内维尔还等着料理——而依我看，凶手分明是指望内维尔恰在那时就在迷宫里。我料他手里握着私下的消息。于是他从地上抓起三支飞镖，奔去结果内维尔——也真叫他得手了。内维尔多半是被什么惊动了，这才能解释他中镖时为何正站着。随后凶手打算折回去取丢失的飞镖。可这时他发觉迷宫里另有旁人。他听见了人声。多半是回海伦亭的路，叫这几个生人堵住了。于是他没命地跑，先要把气枪脱手——那是对他最致命的罪证。可要避开迷宫里这些素不相识的人，他又陷入难处，费了好些时候才脱身。”
“那时候，你对哪个具体的人起过疑心么？”温多弗打断道。
“当然，小托伦斯也不是没有可能。”克林顿爵士承认道，“他大可以把自己的角色同凶手的角色一并兼了。我那时不作定论。”
“要是把人人都算进去，福雷斯特小姐想必也可能。”阿德斯利评道。

===Original===

“I didn’t speculate much at the time,” Sir Clinton answered. “What really started me thinking definitely was the clue that my dog gave us. He led us, you remember, by a very winding track through the Maze—evidently the turnings and windings were due to the murderer dodging someone in the alleys. Then we came near the river—that suggested that he flung away his airgun into the water as he passed. Then the dog led us to a tree in a small clump near by. Wendover noticed a mark on the trunk of the tree, about three feet off the ground, and he suggested that it had been made by the boot of the murderer while he was trying to climb the tree. But after that the trail went on and reached the road—and there it stopped dead. The dog simply baulked there; it found nothing further.”
Sir Clinton paused for a moment to let this point sink in.
“A trail can only stop dead in that way for either of two reasons. First, a man may stand still and wait. But since the man wasn’t there he obviously hadn’t waited. The only other way in which a thing like that could happen is by the man getting into the air off the road at that point.”
“Ha! The private aeroplane, I suppose,” said Wendover sarcastically.
Sir Clinton’s retort crushed the Squire slightly.
“Or the private motor—or even the humble push-bike. If you step into a car or get on to a bicycle your trail will stop so far as footsteps are concerned.”

===Chinese===

“我当时没怎么妄加猜测。”克林顿爵士答道，“真正教我开始往实处想的，是我的狗给我们的那条线索。你们记得，它领着我们沿一条七拐八绕的路线穿过迷宫——那些弯弯绕绕，分明是凶手在小径里躲避什么人所致。随后我们走近了河边——这说明他路过时把气枪抛进了水里。接着狗把我们引到近旁一小片树丛里的一棵树前。温多弗注意到树干上有一道印记，离地约三英尺，他猜那是凶手想爬树时，靴子在树上蹭出来的。可那之后踪迹还在延续，一直到了大路上——在那里断得干干净净。狗到那儿就卡住了，再嗅不出半点下文。”
克林顿爵士停了片刻，好让这一层沉进众人心里。
“一条踪迹这样断得干干净净，缘故只有两个。头一个：那人可能站定在原地等候。可人既不在那儿，他显然不曾等过。再一个可能，便是那人在那个地点离开地面、脱开了大路。”
“哈！我猜又是那架私人飞机。”温多弗讥诮道。
克林顿爵士这一驳，把乡绅噎得矮了半截。
“要么是私人汽车——再不就是那不起眼的自行车。你一跨进汽车或跨上自行车，单就脚步而论，踪迹自然就断了。”

===Original===

Wendover admitted the hit.
“What an ass I was not to see that at once. And of course the road was bone-hard and had no dust on it, so he left no track of his tyres?”
“None that I could swear to. Now that bicycle settled a good deal. It cleared at once Torrance, Miss Forrest, and most probably Costock as well. I may say at once that I never took Costock seriously. He’s a miserable creature who couldn’t screw himself up to murder if he tried. I had him watched; but I never really suspected him of anything beyond a futile attempt at blackmailing Roger Shandon. He hadn’t even the nerve for that. His pistol was really for self-defence against Shandon, I’m sure, just as he said it was.”

===Chinese===

温多弗认了这一击。
“我真是个蠢驴，居然没能一眼看破。还有，那条路面自然硬得梆梆响，面上又没浮土，所以他连车胎印也没留下？”
“没有——至少没有我敢具结作证的印迹。这辆自行车一冒头，许多事便定了。它当场洗清了托伦斯、福雷斯特小姐，多半也洗清了科斯托克。我不妨直说：科斯托克我从来没正眼当过回事。他是个窝囊废，就算打定主意去杀人，也攒不起那份狠劲。我派人盯过他的梢；可除了对罗杰·香农那场徒劳的敲诈，我从没真疑心过他别的。就连那点事，他都没撑到底的胆量。他那把手枪，我确信真如他自己所言，是防香农用的。”

===Original===

Wendover harked back to the problem of the track.
“You seem very sure it was a bicycle and not a motor car.”
“Isn’t it fairly certain?” Sir Clinton asked. “If the murderer had used a car, he’d have been seen by the lodge-keepers if he left the grounds—at least he’d have run that risk. But a bicycle can be carried off the road by hand and taken through a gap in a hedge quite easily. Since the murderer evidently would not want to be seen, the bicycle is the obvious thing. Call it a bicycle, anyway, for convenience just now. My trouble was that I couldn’t prove which way the bicycle went: whether it went towards the house or towards the East Gate. I left the matter alone for the time, hoping for something else to turn up. Of course, I set my men at work to hunt for any bicycle that had been concealed in the grounds; but they failed to find it.”
“Why didn’t you make inquiries about bicycles at Whistlefield?” Wendover demanded.
“Because I wanted to keep my thumb on the bicycle question. I didn’t want to get the name of being too clever—so far as the murderer was concerned. It was far better to let him think his method was undetected.”
“So at that point,” Stenness put in, “you didn’t know whether the murderer had gone back to the house or had gone outside the grounds?”

===Chinese===

温多弗又绕回踪迹的问题。
“你好像咬得很死：是自行车，不是汽车。”
“这不是相当可以肯定的事么？”克林顿爵士反问，“凶手若用了汽车，只要一出庄园地界，就会落在看门人眼里——至少要担这个风险。可自行车下了路、提在手里，从树篱一个豁口穿过去，易如反掌。凶手显然不愿露形迹，自行车便是明摆着的选择。眼下一图方便，姑且就叫它自行车。我的难处是：那辆车往哪个方向去了，我证明不了——是奔宅子去的，还是奔东门去的。这事我暂且搁下，指望旁的端倪自己冒出来。当然，我派手下人去搜庄园里藏着的车；他们没能找到。”
“那你当时为什么不在惠斯特菲尔德查问自行车的事？”温多弗追问。
“因为自行车这个问题，我要暗暗攥在自己手心里。至少在凶手眼里，我不想赚一个过分精明的名声。由他以为自己的手法无人识破，好处要大得多。”
“这么说，到那一步，”斯滕内斯插话道，“你还不知道凶手是回宅子里去了，还是出了庄园？”

===Original===

“No,” Sir Clinton admitted. “I didn’t. The next thing, if you remember, was my visit to the house and my interviews with various people, yourself included. Bear in mind that at that time, I didn’t know whether the murderer was one of the house group or had come in like a bolt from the blue from outside. I had to get that point cleared up as soon as possible; and it offered a good deal of difficulty.
“When I came to interview the various people at the house, it was simply a case of meeting a number of strangers for the first time. I had to pick up what I could, and at the same time take care not to be prejudiced by initial impressions. That’s more difficult than you’d think. Torrance and Miss Forrest were cleared already, so I did not need to pay much attention to them, apart from their evidence. You, Stenness, gave me a bit of trouble, I admit. I couldn’t quite make you out at that time.”
Stenness acknowledged this with a faint smile. Sir Clinton hastened on with his narrative without giving either of the others a chance of interrupting.

===Chinese===

“不知道。”克林顿爵士承认，“确实不知道。再往后一桩，你们记得，便是我到宅子里走访，同各色人等挨个谈话——你也在其列。记住，那时我还不知道凶手是宅子里这一伙人中的一个，还是从外面晴天霹雳般杀进来的。这一点必须尽快弄清；而它又着实难办。”
“到宅子里同各人谈话，说白了，就是头一回会见一大屋子陌生人。我得见缝插针地捞取情况，又得时时当心，别叫第一面的印象带出成见。这比你们想的要难。托伦斯和福雷斯特小姐那时已经洗清了，除了证词，我不必在他们身上多费神。你，斯滕内斯，说句实话，给我添过一点麻烦。那时我实在摸你不透。”
斯滕内斯报以淡淡一笑，算是认了。克林顿爵士加紧往下讲，不给另外两位插话的机会。

===Original===

“Arthur Hawkhurst caused me some thought, though. Stenness gave me a hint about his attack of sleepy sickness. He came in with an airgun in his hand. He seemed an irresponsible sort of boy. But that was all. There’s a big chasm between that and homicidal mania. I simply docketed him in my memory and left the matter there.”
At this point Sir Clinton seemed to find his narrative growing more interesting to himself. He pulled himself up in his chair and glanced round his audience before taking up the next part of his subject.
“Ernest Shandon was the final figure—for, of course, I dismissed Miss Hawkhurst at once. Now at first sight, friend Ernest was an unattractive fellow. First, he was obviously callous in the extreme. He didn’t seem sorry about his brothers’ deaths; his sore toe bulked far more prominently in his conversation. That seemed a bit grotesque to me at the time. It stuck in my memory on that account alone. Then, second, he seemed absolutely selfish. His ego seemed to be the only thing that really interested him. He wanted his tea; and he meant to have it, too. That seemed a bit abnormal, though one can’t hang a man for wanting his tea, of course. Third, he gave me the impression of being one of the dullest and stupidest men that one could wish to meet. Altogether, one would say, there wasn’t much to be got out of a person of that type: dull, selfish, callous, and stupid. And yet, if you look back now, you’ll see that the whole basis of the Shandon tragedies lies in just those qualities. It’ll be quite clear when we come to it.

===Chinese===

“不过，阿瑟·霍克赫斯特倒教我费了些思量。斯滕内斯向我提过一句他嗜睡性脑炎发作的旧事。他手里拎着一支气枪进来。看上去是个没心没肺的少年。可也就到此为止。从这到杀人狂之间，隔着万丈深渊。我把他往记忆里一归档，便撂开了手。”
讲到这里，克林顿爵士像是觉得自己的故事越讲越有味道了。他在椅子里把身子坐正，环视一圈听众，才拣起话头的下一截。
“最后剩下的一个，便是欧内斯特·香农——霍克赫斯特小姐嘛，我自然是一开头就排除的。乍一看，咱这位欧内斯特朋友是个不讨喜的家伙。第一，他显然冷漠到了极点。两位哥哥死了，他看不出有多伤心；他那根受伤的脚趾，在他言谈里占的分量反倒重得多。这在当时的我看来，有点荒诞。单凭这一点，他就在我记忆里生了根。第二，他显得自私透顶。他那个自我，似乎是他唯一真有兴趣的东西。他要他的茶；而且打定主意非喝到嘴不可。这也算一点反常——当然，人想喝口茶，总不能凭这个吊死他。第三，他给我的印象是：天底下再想寻出一个比他更乏味、更愚蠢的人，怕是难了。总而言之，人们会说，这种人身上榨不出多少东西：乏味、自私、冷漠、愚蠢。然而如今回头再看，你们会明白：香农家这一连串惨剧的整个根基，恰恰就埋在这几样品性里。讲到那儿，便一清二楚了。”

===Original===

“I’ve pointed out that my difficulty was to fix as soon as possible whether this was ‘an inside job’ or one carried through by an outsider. Also, quite possibly there might be more trouble at Whistlefield. Now I’d taken particular care to note that the murderer knew the Maze thoroughly. So as a wild shot I dropped the hint about Ariadne’s clue—the thread which would guide a man through the Maze if he didn’t know it very well. I flung that down casually. I hadn’t really much hope of doing much with it; for I hardly believed in further trouble then. But it would do no harm, so I dropped the suggestion in presence of some of the possible criminals.”
“H’m! Now I begin to see some light,” Wendover commented.
“The next point was the nature of the poison,” Sir Clinton went on. “The local doctor suggested you, Ardsley, as an expert; so I went over at once to get your views. Once I knew it was curare, I felt I’d got something definite enough to go on. That isn’t common stuff. Of course you probably had a stock yourself; and I didn’t feel inclined to interfere with you. I thought it fairly clear that if you had gone on the murder tack you’d have avoided a stuff which could be traced to you directly. So I asked about any other local source, and you put me on to the pot in the Whistlefield museum.
“That put a different complexion on the whole case. It was evidently essential to get hold of that supply immediately. If the murderer had drawn a private stock from the pot, we couldn’t help that. If he had just used enough to poison his darts, then we could stop further supplies by confiscating the pot. So I packed you and Wendover off to secure it.”

===Chinese===

“我说过，我的难处是尽快敲定：这桩案是‘宅内人干的’，还是外来者一手办成的。再者，惠斯特菲尔德多半还会再出乱子。而凶手对迷宫烂熟于心，这一点我特意记下了。于是我姑妄放出一炮，抛出了阿里阿德涅引线的话头——就是那种能领着不熟路径的人走出迷宫的线。我随口撂下这么一句。本来没指望它派多大用场；因为那时我还不大相信会再出事。可它并无坏处，我便当着几个有嫌疑的人的面，撂下了这话。”
“哼！这下我开始见着点光亮了。”温多弗评道。
“下一个要点，是那毒物的性质。”克林顿爵士接着说，“本地医生举你为行家，阿德斯利，我便立刻登门讨教。一知道那是箭毒，我便觉得手里有了够扎实的着力点。那可不是寻常东西。当然，你手头多半也存着一份；我也不想在你头上兴师动众。我想这一点相当清楚：你若真走上了杀人那条道，必会避开一种能径直追查到你头上的东西。于是我打听本地还有什么别的来路，你便把我指到了惠斯特菲尔德陈列馆里那只箭毒罐上。”
“这一来，整桩案子的面貌全变了。把那一份存货立刻控制在手，显然是头等要紧。凶手若已从罐里倒走了自存的一份，我们无可奈何。他若只取了淬飞镖够用的那一点，那我们没收那罐箭毒，便断了他的后援。于是我打发你同温多弗去把它取了回来。”

===Original===

“Wendover being to watch me, I suppose?” Ardsley put in with a grim smile.
“I won’t deny it. You’d have done the same in my place,” Sir Clinton pointed out.
“Why didn’t you come yourself?” Wendover inquired.
“Because I’d something else on hand that had to be done in a hurry. I must confess frankly that I’d nothing definite to go on. It was mere intuition, if you like; or you can call it a case of taking precautions against eventualities which one doesn’t expect to occur. It was rather like the business of Ariadne’s clue—a long shot which might come off. And I thought it had more chance of bearing fruit. These poisoned darts were clues of a sort. They were also weapons. So if the murderer got a chance of grabbing them, I thought he might be tempted. But I wasn’t going to let him have the real things, not likely. I gave him a substitute, quite similar in appearance, but really quite harmless. Then if he tried any more of his games, the chances were that he’d use the darts I gave him; and do no great harm with them. Even if he had one or two deadly darts left, he’d be sure to mix the lot together, and that reduced the risk of poison in a dart chosen at random from the mixture of deadly and harmless ones.

===Chinese===

“温多弗同行，为的就是看住我吧？”阿德斯利插进来，嘴角挂着一丝冷笑。
“我不否认。换你坐我的位置，你也会这么办。”克林顿爵士点破道。
“那你自己为什么不来？”温多弗问。
“因为我手头另有一桩急务要赶着办。我得坦白承认：那时并没有确实的依据。你们尽可以说，那不过是直觉；或者说，是对自己都不指望发生的不测预做防备。它同阿里阿德涅引线那桩事颇为相像——一记未必中的、或许能中的远射。而我估摸它结果实的指望更大些。这些淬毒的飞镖本身就是线索，同时也是武器。凶手若捞得着下手的机会，我想他未必把持得住。可真货我绝不肯交到他手上，想都别想。我给他备了替身，外表极像，实则全然无害。这样，他若再耍他那些把戏，多半用的便是我给的飞镖，也就闯不出大祸。就算他还剩一两支致命的，他也必定把两批掺作一堆；如此从真伪掺混的一堆里随手摸一支，带毒的风险便减了下去。”

===Original===

“So, when we were passing through the village, I got you to drop me, Wendover; and I bought some darts, drilled them like the deadly ones, faked them to match with Condy’s fluid, put some litmus in so as to make my lot easily recognisable by a simple test, and then I was ready. Incidentally, I think I got the reputation of being quite mad. I needed a tin to keep the deadly darts in—I wasn’t going to have them loose in my pocket—and I had to leave the real tin for the murderer to pick up if he wanted. So I sent out for some Navy Cut, used the tins for the lethal darts, and staggered the sergeant down there by presenting him with the tobacco. He’s still puzzling over it, I guess.
“You know what happened next. I left the sham darts on the mantelpiece of the museum purposely; and the murderer lifted them. Now as Ardsley didn’t touch them—he went out of the room in front of me and they were still lying there—and as Wendover wasn’t the murderer, that left only you, Stenness, and Miss Hawkhurst, young Hawkhurst and Ernest Shandon, as possible thieves. If the tin vanished, then I had got down to pretty narrow limits.”
“I see you’d ruled me out by that time,” Ardsley said.

===Chinese===

“所以，路过村子的时候，我让你把我放下，温多弗；我买了几支飞镖，照致命那批的样子钻了孔，用康迪氏药晶染得一般无二，又掺进些石蕊，好让我这批货经一道简单试验便能认出来，如此便万事齐备。顺带一说，我想我因此落了个十足疯子的名声。我需要一只铁听来装那些致命的飞镖——总不能任它们散在口袋里——而原先那只真听，又非留给凶手不可，随他捡去。于是我派人去买了些 Navy Cut，把烟听腾出来装致命的飞镖，又把烟丝奉送给底下那位警佐，惊得他晕头转向。我猜他至今还在纳闷。”
“后来出了什么事，你们都知道了。我有意把那批假飞镖撂在陈列馆的壁炉台上；凶手把它们顺走了。阿德斯利既没碰它们——他走在我前头出的门，飞镖还原样躺在那儿——温多弗又不是凶手，剩下可能下手的，便只有你斯滕内斯、霍克赫斯特小姐、小霍克赫斯特和欧内斯特·香农了。倘若那只铁听再失踪，范围便缩得相当窄了。”
“我明白了，到那时你已经把我排除在外了。”阿德斯利说。

===Original===

“Oh, practically,” Sir Clinton agreed. “Of course my convictions were quite fluid still. I was quite prepared to reconsider things at any time. The next point was to go over the alibis as well as I could. Wendover gave me a hand with that. Once one got into that matter, it was clear enough that the only possible suspects were yourself, Stenness, young Hawkhurst, you, Ardsley—because I knew nothing about your doings that day—and finally, somebody with a bicycle. But once you bring in the bicycle, Ernest Shandon’s alibi falls to bits. I’ll show you how.
“This is my reconstruction of the murders in the Maze: I don’t say it’s correct in every detail—only the main outlines are really important. Ernest Shandon went off with Miss Hawkhurst in the car. I suspect that he knew his brothers were going to the Maze that afternoon. He’d already taken his bicycle along and concealed it in a little plantation near the East Gate; and he’d hidden the airgun there as well.
“He got off the car at the East Gate, walked along the road quickly, and got through the hedge into the plantation. He took out his bicycle and the airgun and pedalled as hard as he could go for the Maze. His one risk was meeting someone on the road. If he’d done that, he’d have had to postpone his affair until another day. He met no one—it’s a road hardly anyone walks on, I believe. He got to the Maze, went in to his loophole commanding Helen’s Bower. There he saw someone whom he took to be Neville Shandon. I suspect that he had the Hackleton case at the back of his mind during the planning; and he meant to make it appear that Neville had been killed on that account. You know yourselves what a troublesome false trail it turned out to be at the start.

===Chinese===

“哦，实际上是的。”克林顿爵士应道，“当然，我心里的定见还流动得很，随时准备把整盘棋重摆。下一个环节，是把各人的不在场证明尽量过一遍筛子。温多弗搭了把手。一钻进这件事，便看得相当清楚：够格当嫌疑人的，只剩你斯滕内斯、小霍克赫斯特、你阿德斯利——因为你那天的行止我一无所得知——末了，再添上一个有自行车的人。可自行车一旦请进案来，欧内斯特·香农的不在场证明便碎成齑粉。我这就摆给你们看。”
“以下是我对迷宫那两桩命案的复原：我不敢说细节处处无误——真正要紧的只是大轮廓。欧内斯特·香农随霍克赫斯特小姐乘车外出了。我疑心两位哥哥那天下午要去迷宫，他是知情的。他事先已把自行车带了过去，藏进东门附近一片小树地；气枪也一并藏在了那里。”
“他在东门下了车，沿大路疾走，从树篱豁口钻进小树地。他取出自行车和气枪，拼出吃奶的力气往迷宫蹬。他唯一的风险，是在路上撞见人。真撞见了，这桩事便只好改日再办。他一个人也没遇上——那条路，我记得几乎没人步行。他到了迷宫，进到他那个俯瞰海伦亭的暗孔跟前。在那里，他望见一个他认作内维尔·香农的人。我疑心他筹划之初，心底便垫着哈克尔顿案；他要把局面装成内维尔是栽在这案子上。这条假线索起初给我们添了多少麻烦，你们都是亲历的。”

===Original===

“My belief is that he was sweating like a pig owing to his sprint up to the Maze—you must remember that his whole alibi depended on the time factor and he hadn’t a moment to waste. Very likely his nose was greasy and he had trouble with his glasses. You remember they were always going askew or falling off? However it happened, he didn’t recognise Roger from behind, and he shot him first of all. He shot him in the neck; and I am led to suppose that Roger didn’t cry out, because the heavy dose of curare paralysed his vocal muscles almost immediately.
“Then, as Roger fell, Ernest recognised that he’d blundered. He’d killed the wrong man; and the Hackleton business wouldn’t serve to complicate this case. I expect he got flustered—and no great wonder. I suppose he dropped the darts, his glasses fell off, and altogether he was in a pretty state of confusion. And there was still Neville to finish off.
“At that moment, he’d no idea that the Maze wasn’t empty except for Neville in the other centre. So he abandoned his darts after grabbing three from the ground; and he went off to kill his other brother before anything more happened. Bear in mind that speed was everything to him, and you’ll get some idea of the flurry he must have been in. I expect he meant to come back for the spilt darts and the tin as soon as he’d finished Neville.
“We know from the evidence of Torrance and Miss Forrest that the first murder passed unnoticed so far as they were concerned. They didn’t even pay any attention to the reports of the airgun in Roger’s case. Considering the queer acoustics of the Maze, there’s nothing wonderful in that. But I think it’s likely enough that Neville Shandon had heard something; and Ernest just got him as he was standing up and wondering if he shouldn’t hunt about for the cause of the funny noise he had heard.
“Whatever the details were, Ernest got him all right; but he shot him in the body and Neville was able to give a yell or two before he collapsed.
